===== introduction 49
   **The buyer's question is an architecture question**: an identity never resolved, a provenance never shown, a copy never reconciled.
   - **Chief and enterprise architects** of vehicle-data companies, dealer-software vendors and groups that run both.
   - **Product and engineering directors** of portals, lessors, insurers, remarketing businesses and dealer groups that build their own tooling.
   | **Identify** | What is this car, exactly? | VIN, plate assignments, edition and factory options | [[vehicle-identity]], [[specification-and-equipment]] |
   | **Check** | What happened to it? | Damage, odometer readings, title brands, inspections, ownership changes | [[vehicle-history]], [[registers-and-official-data]] |
   | **Value** | What is it worth? | Comparable market evidence, condition, battery health, the valuation model's confidence | [[market-data]], [[valuation-engine]], [[condition-and-damage]], [[ev-battery-health]] |
   | **Buy** | What should the dealer pay? | Value range, reconditioning cost, holding cost, target margin, VAT regime | [[vehicle-acquisition]] |
   | **Price** | What should the dealer ask? | Price position against the market, stand-time, demand | [[used-car-pricing]] |
   | **Advertise** | Where, and with what words and pictures? | The dossier, the resolved specification, the price, channel rules | [[merchandising]], [[listing-syndication]] |
   | **Transfer** | Who owns it now? | The deal, the registration transaction, proof from the register | [[deals-and-sales-documents]], [[registration-and-trade-services]] |
   **It belongs to the right car**: keyed to the physical vehicle, not to a plate that moves between cars or a stock number that changes when a car moves rooftop.
   **Its source is known**: who said it, when, under which licence and with what confidence.
   **It is fresh enough for the decision**: a register status for a sale must be live; a specification decoded last year is still good.
   **It is corrected everywhere it spread**: a supplier's fix reaches every valuation, report, advert and client copy built on the wrong value.
   | **What** | Vehicle identity and the vehicle key; the vehicle dossier and its facts; accounts, tenants and entitlements; usage metering; the licence and purpose register; the domain-event backbone | Release cadence; customer-facing workflows and screens; API contracts for external data clients; channel and supplier adapters as owned products |
   | **Why** | Same meaning in both halves; built twice, each one produces a failure pattern (two keys clone cars, two meters disagree) | They change at different speeds; sharing them couples a weekly dealer release to a five-year client contract |
   | **Where it is argued** | [[vehicle-identity]], [[inventory-and-vehicle-dossier]], [[identity-access-and-entitlements]], [[billing-and-metering]], [[data-licensing-and-governance]], [[events-webhooks-and-sync]] | [[vehicle-data-apis]], [[listing-syndication]], [[dms-integration]], [[modernizing-the-platform]] |
   | **Netherlands / Belgium** | The Netherlands has an open, plate-keyed register (the RDW, *Rijksdienst voor het Wegverkeer*) with open data, a transaction channel for providers the book calls authorized information providers ([[registers-and-official-data]] defines the term; it is not RDW's own title); data vendors there also sell dealer software.
   | **Germany** | A closed register at the Federal Motor Transport Authority (KBA), so no national mirror; two incumbent valuation and specification houses; DMS markets shaped by manufacturers. |
   | **United Kingdom** | One dominant portal that also sells data and software to retailers; open roadworthiness-test (MOT) history; a register, the Driver and Vehicle Licensing Agency (DVLA), with scarce live lookups. |
   | **United States** | No national vehicle register; history built by contributory networks and state title systems; a concentrated DMS market and the Driver's Privacy Protection Act on register data. |
   | **Preface** | What the book is for, how vehicle platforms fail, and which group the examples follow | This page, [[where-vehicle-platforms-go-wrong]], [[meet-axleby]] |
   | **[[part-1]]** MARKET | **Nobody sees the whole car**; the economics of dealers, data and software become latency, freshness, accuracy and metering requirements; map capabilities, share the key-carrying ones first, and draw boundaries where words change meaning | 1–4: ecosystem, economics, capability map, bounded contexts and sources of truth |
   | **[[part-2]]** VEHICLE | **Key the car, not the plate, and keep every fact with its source**; the golden record is a view | 5–10: identity, registers, specification, history, ingestion, provenance |
   | **[[part-3]]** VALUE | **A value is a typed, evidenced answer with a confidence, not a number**; de-listings, inspections, battery readings and connected-car data are evidence with a method, a source and rights, not facts | 11–15: market data, valuation, condition, battery health, connected cars |
   | **[[part-4]]** DEALER | **Every dealer action is a decision with a record**, and every hop to a portal, CRM, register or DMS must be confirmed, not assumed | 16–25: buying, stock, pricing, adverts, syndication, leads, deals, registration, DMS, B2B trade |
   | **[[part-5]]** SAAS | **The tenant, the entitlement, the meter and the licence are the commercial backbone**, shared by both halves and enforced on every request | 26–30: tenancy, identity, onboarding, billing, licensing |
   | **[[part-6]]** DISTRIBUTION | **Sell blocks with outcomes, publish facts as events, and back every push with a pull**; aggregation is not anonymization, and a free check must yield nothing worth reselling | 31–34: APIs, events, data products, consumer checks |
   | **[[part-7]]** OPERATIONS | **The platform's availability is borrowed from registers, suppliers, portals and DMS vendors**, so design around budgets, labeled copies and honest queues | 35–39: reliability, cost, observability, security, privacy |
   | **[[part-8]]** JOURNEYS | **Flows fail between systems, not inside them** | 40–43: inventory to portal, data correction, lead to DMS, sale to registration |
   | **[[part-9]]** LEADERSHIP | **The Chief Architect decides what to share, buy, reorganize and retire**, and in what order | 44–49: sourcing, portfolio integration, organization, modernization, AI, evolution |
   | **[[part-10]]** PRACTICE | **The public record is richest on contracts, access and outages and silent on engines** | 50–53: Cox Automotive, CDK Global, Auto Trader UK, VWE |
   | **Reference** | The coverage map, templates, definitions and sources | [[domain-coverage]], [[toolkit]], [[glossary]], [[bibliography]] |
   | **Documented fact** | A company's public statement, a filing, a regulation, a standard, a date, an official statistic | Linked inline, with the **year of publication** in the sentence, and listed in Sources and Evidence.
   | **Industry practice** | What vehicle-data and dealer-software businesses commonly do | Phrased as "commonly" or "a typical pattern is", with no invented percentages. |
   | **Reference-architecture inference** | How a system like this is plausibly built, reasoned from engineering principles | When it concerns a real company, it is labeled **INFERENCE, NOT DOCUMENTED**. |
   | **Recommendation** | My judgement as a Chief Architect | First person ("I would"), with the reasoning and the conditions under which it does not hold.
   | **Illustration** | An example set at Axleby | Labeled fictional in each chapter; its numbers agree across the book. |
   | **Labeled assumption** | An illustrative volume, latency target or cost that is neither documented nor an Axleby fact | Marked "assume…" in the text or *(assumption)* in a table.
   - **A domain model, used as a research map.** The book started from a Productscape domain model in my sibling repository that describes one real Dutch company, VWE Automotive, from public sources only: 28 product bricks (named business capabilities), 140 modules, 29 data assets, personas and stressors.
   - **The public record of the named companies**: filings, engineering posts, talks and developer documentation, each cited with its publication year.
   - **Standards, regulation and research**: ISO standards, EU and national legal texts, register and regulator documentation, and published research.
   - **The DMS's own domains**: dealer accounting, parts, workshop and service management.
   - **Lending and insurance**: credit decisions, regulated consumer-credit contracts and claims handling.
   - **New-car sales and manufacturer ordering.** Manufacturers appear as sources of build data, connected-car data and franchise requirements, not as a retail flow the book designs.
   - **Vehicle facts**: **The Cloned Identity**, **Spec Guesswork**, **Provenance Amnesia**, **The Sticky Error** and **The Confident Valuation**.
   - **Dealer workflow**: **The Zombie Listing**, **Feed Roulette**, **The Leaky Lead** and **DMS Hostage**.
   - **Commercial**: **The Tenant Blur**, **The Unmetered Call** and **Licence Blindness**.
   - **Platform**: **Borrowed Uptime**, **The Acquisition Archipelago** and **Customization Creep**.
===== where-vehicle-platforms-go-wrong 25
   | **The Cloned Identity** | Vehicles are keyed by plate or by a product's own stock id; one record mixes two cars, or one car appears as several. | Data | Share of records keyed by VIN; plate assignments per vehicle |
   | **Spec Guesswork** | Edition and options are inferred from model names or dealer input, so adverts, valuations and reports disagree about what the car is. | Both | Share of stock with a resolved edition and its source |
   | **Provenance Amnesia** | Facts are stored without source, observation date, licence or confidence; nobody can say where a number came from or whether it may be shown. | Data | Share of facts carrying source, observed-at and licence |
   | **The Sticky Error** | A corrected fact lives on in caches, reports, valuations, adverts and clients' copies, because nobody knows where it spread. | Both | Time from correction to the last affected copy |
   | **The Confident Valuation** | Valuations come without confidence, comparables or back-testing, and keep being trusted after the market moves. | Data | Back-tested error by segment, and weeks to detect drift |
   | **The Zombie Listing** | Sold or withdrawn cars stay live on portals because deletes are fire-and-forget and channel state is never reconciled. | SaaS | Median and worst time from sold to off every portal |
   | **Feed Roulette** | Dozens of channel and supplier adapters run without contracts, status feedback or monitoring; every format change is discovered by a dealer. | SaaS | Adapters with a contract, a health check and an owner |
   | **The Leaky Lead** | Leads are lost, duplicated or misrouted between portals, inboxes, CRMs and DMS, and every hop reports success. | SaaS | Leads acknowledged by the final system, per channel |
   | **DMS Hostage** | Critical dealer workflows depend on brittle or unsanctioned DMS access; when the DMS vendor changes terms or goes down, the dealer stops. | SaaS | Workflows that stop when one DMS is unavailable |
   | **The Tenant Blur** | Dealer groups, rooftops and users are bolted onto a single-dealer model; permissions are shared logins and tenant ids are optional. | SaaS | Tables, caches and topics without a mandatory tenant key |
   | **The Unmetered Call** | Billable events are reconstructed from logs at month-end; no-hit rules, bundles and credits differ by product and nobody can explain an invoice. | Both | Places that decide whether a call is billable |
   | **Licence Blindness** | Data is cached, combined or resold beyond what its source licence and the client's purpose allow, and nobody can prove what was used for what. | Data | Data API calls checked against a licence and purpose at request time |
   | **Borrowed Uptime** | The platform's availability is the product of every register, supplier, portal and DMS it calls synchronously; one outage stops the dealer. | Both | Synchronous external calls on each critical journey |
   | **The Acquisition Archipelago** | Acquired products keep their own logins, vehicle keys, billing and data; customers re-type plates and the group cannot see one customer. | Both | Logins, vehicle keys and billing systems per customer |
   | **Customization Creep** | Large customers get code forks, special fields and bespoke workflows instead of configuration and extension points, until every release is a migration. | SaaS | Customer-specific branches, flags and fields |
   **Why it happens.** Syndication is usually built as **commands**: insert, update, delete, sent once to each channel.
   | **Identify** | The platform resolves the wrong car, or the right car with the wrong edition | The Cloned Identity, Spec Guesswork | The Cloned Plate |
   | **Check** | A history report mixes cars, hides its sources or keeps a corrected error | Provenance Amnesia, The Sticky Error, The Cloned Identity | The Odometer Correction |
   | **Value** | A valuation is wrong and does not say how unsure it is | The Confident Valuation, Spec Guesswork | The EV Overvaluation |
   | **Stock** | The dossier and the DMS disagree, or the DMS is gone | DMS Hostage, The Acquisition Archipelago | The DriveDesk Outage |
   | **Advertise** | Portals show the wrong car, the wrong price or a sold car | The Zombie Listing, Feed Roulette, The Sticky Error | The Zombie Golf |
   | **Sell** | Leads are lost or shown to the wrong dealer | The Leaky Lead, The Tenant Blur | The Lost Leads of Velto, the Cross-Tenant Lead |
   | **Register** | Official transactions fail, duplicate or stall | Borrowed Uptime | The Month-End Register Outage |
   | **Trade and Insight** | Data is billed wrongly or used beyond its licence | The Unmetered Call, Licence Blindness | The No-Hit Credit, the Licence Breach |
   | **Under every stage** | Keys, logins, tenants and dependencies that do not fit | The Acquisition Archipelago, The Tenant Blur, Borrowed Uptime, Customization Creep | Five products, three logins, two vehicle keys |
===== meet-axleby 114
   **Axleby Group B.V.** is a privately held vehicle-data and dealer-software group headquartered in **Utrecht, the Netherlands**.
   A **rooftop** is one physical dealership location, the usual unit of tenancy and billing in dealer software ([[tenancy-and-dealer-groups]] owns the term).
   | **1996** | Axleby is founded in Utrecht to resell Dutch register data to insurers and leasing companies. |
   | **1999** | Axleby signs its data-supply and transaction-provider agreement with the Dutch register, becoming what this book calls an authorized information provider ([[registers-and-official-data]]). |
   | **2004** | Axleby Trade starts as a business line: register transactions for the Dutch trade, then documents, plates and registration-tax calculations. |
   | **2006** | **Connect XML**, a SOAP/XML API and report back end, goes live in a co-located Utrecht data center. |
   | **2009** | **Lotwise** is founded in Ghent; its monolith, now **Lotwise Classic**, dates from that year. |
   | **2014–15** | **Showroomly** is founded in Leeds (2014) and **Wertlauf** in Munich (2015). |
   | **2018** | **Odolo**, the consumer vehicle-check app, launches. |
   | **2019** | Axleby acquires Lotwise and merges it with its own dealer portal. |
   | **2020** | Lotwise Classic moves to **AWS eu-west-1**. |
   | **2021** | Axleby acquires Wertlauf; its models later become group valuation (**Vantage**). |
   | **2022** | **Harrowgate Capital** becomes majority owner; **JSON API v1** launches. |
   | **2023** | Axleby acquires **Showroomly**. |
   | **2024** | **Anchor** (identity graph) and **Switchboard** (lead router) are built; the Kraków hub opens. |
   | **2025** | Showroomly moves from Heroku to **AWS eu-west-2**. |
   | **2026** | **Ines Marchetti** joins as Group Chief Architect. |
   | **January 2027** | The Licence Breach; **Charter** is built during 2027. |
   | **March 2027** | The board approves the **One Dossier** programme; capability map v1 is presented. |
   | **June 2027** | The register transaction redesign goes live (14 June) after the Month-End Register Outage. |
   | **October 2027** | JSON API v2 lookups and the developer portal go live. |
   | **November 2027** | The DriveDesk Outage: twelve days without the DMS at 1,900 rooftops. |
   | **December 2027** | Capability map v2 is adopted; the event backbone design record is approved; Connect XML's retirement plan is approved. |
   | **31 December 2029** | Announced sunset of Connect XML. |
   | **Axleby Data** | Vehicle data services: identity, specification, history reports, valuations, market data, file enrichment, insight dashboards and data APIs.
   | **Lotwise** | Dealer SaaS for NL, BE and DE: appraisal, stock and dossier, pricing, adverts and syndication, websites and leads, sales administration, financing referral | Founded **2009**, Ghent; acquired **2019** | Monthly module subscriptions per rooftop (about half of revenue), plus group add-ons and extra seats, implementation and DMS-integration fees, financing-referral commissions and data usage beyond modul
   | **Showroomly** | UK dealer websites, lead management and syndication | Founded **2014**, Leeds; acquired **2023** | Monthly subscriptions per rooftop | **€12M** |
   | **Axleby Trade** | Dutch register transactions (registration in a new name, indemnification, stock-register actions), import and export documents, registration-tax calculations, plates and trade materials, and **Tradefloor**, a business-to-business (B2B) trade exchange with hand-off to auction partners | Business line since **2004** | Per-transaction and per-document fees, webshop sales, Tradefloor success fees | *
   | **Odolo** | Consumer vehicle-check app and website (NL, BE, UK): free basic check by plate, paid full report | Launched **2018** | Per-report consumer payments; referral fees | **€8M** |
   | **Wertlauf** | Valuation and market-data business in Germany; its models power group valuation (**Vantage**) | Founded **2015**, Munich; acquired **2021** | Reported inside Axleby Data | (inside Axleby Data) |
   **No-hit-no-fee** means a lookup that finds no record is not billed; [[billing-and-metering]] owns the rule and shows why it is harder to implement than to state.
   - **The data half sells by the unit, the workflow half by the month.** A rooftop on all five Lotwise modules pays €625 a month at list price; an API client pays per call, and a lookup that finds nothing must cost nothing.
   - **Some data costs Axleby money on every call.** Specbook fetches licensed build data per VIN at an average of **€0.46 a request**, five times the €0.09 list price of a plate-to-specification call.
   - **Axleby Trade turns data access into legal acts.** Its transactions change who keeps a car, must be correct, ordered and provable, and peak at month-end and quarter-end ([[registration-and-trade-services]]).
   - **Odolo sells to consumers on the same data dealers buy**, which creates a conflict of interest when a consumer checks a Lotwise dealer's car, and opens the door scrapers use ([[consumer-vehicle-checks]]).
   | **Total** | **12,400** | **8,550** | |
   - **Most rooftops belong to groups.** **410** dealer groups own two or more rooftops, and **58%** of Lotwise rooftops belong to one, so a tenant model built for one dealer with one login does not survive ([[tenancy-and-dealer-groups]]).
   - **Most Lotwise rooftops are independents.** Independent used-car dealers are **62%**, franchised dealers (who sell new cars for a manufacturer, usually on its approved dealer management system, DMS) **31%**, and lessors' remarketing desks, traders and exporters **7%**.
   - **API clients are a business of their own.** **640** organizations (software vendors, lessors, insurers, portals, fleet managers) call the data APIs; **210** still use only Connect XML, each a migration Axleby cannot do for them ([[modernizing-the-platform]]).
   - **DriveDesk is a concentration.** **1,900** Lotwise rooftops run DriveDesk, a fictional DMS, behind an adapter Axleby does not control ([[dms-integration]]).
   The **61% match rate** means four in ten market adverts cannot teach the valuation engine anything about a known car ([[market-data]]).
   - **Connect XML still owns money.** It runs the usage invoice in stored procedures, so every change to how a call is counted or credited is made twice until Meterline replaces both stacks ([[billing-and-metering]]).
   - **Lotwise Classic still owns the dealer's car.** Stock, dossier, adverts and sales documents live in one monolith whose RabbitMQ queue Relay shares, which is how failed updates could hold a delete back in the Zombie Golf ([[listing-syndication]]).
   - **The newer services are only half adopted.** Anchor, Switchboard and JSON API v1 exist, but older products call them selectively; a platform built next to the old one is not the platform until the old paths stop.
   - **Two vehicle keys.** Lotwise Classic keys a car by **stock id plus plate**, Connect XML by **plate**, Anchor by the **AVK**, which resolves to a VIN (vehicle identification number) with plate assignments as time-bounded edges.
   - **Three logins.** Business users log in through **Axleby Portal accounts** (SQL Server; the Axleby Data portal and Axleby Trade), **Lotwise users** (MySQL) or **Showroomly users** (Rails); Odolo's consumer accounts are separate, and five products keep five customer-account records for billing.

   | **Kilometra** | A fictional odometer and service-record data supplier that aggregates workshop readings, including for UK imports | — |
   | **Rijnstaal Verzekeringen** | A fictional insurer and Axleby Data API client | — |

   **The vehicle dossier is split**: reports, valuations and register transactions live in different screens and systems.
   **Syndication drift**: Relay does not reconcile what portals show, and sold cars linger *(The Zombie Listing, Feed Roulette)*.
   - **Axleby ID** (Keycloak, self-hosted on EKS, with organizations) as the one login, and **Gatekeeper** (relationship-based authorization on OpenFGA) for who may do what for which rooftop ([[identity-access-and-entitlements]]).
   - **Anchor as the only vehicle key** across products: every product refers to a car by its AVK ([[vehicle-identity]]).
   - The **Lotwise Dossier** shows history, valuation, condition and register transactions in one place ([[inventory-and-vehicle-dossier]]).
   - **Meterline** records a usage event for every billable act, in both API stacks and every product ([[billing-and-metering]]).
   - **Charter** in the request path of every data API ([[data-licensing-and-governance]]).
   - A **One Dossier event backbone** on MSK, with a transactional outbox in Lotwise Classic ([[events-webhooks-and-sync]]).
   - **Relay rebuilt as desired-state sync** with reconciliation against each channel ([[listing-syndication]]).
   - **JSON API v2** with webhooks: v2 lookups went live early, in October 2027; webhooks follow in Q3 2028 ([[vehicle-data-apis]], [[events-webhooks-and-sync]]).
   | **Hitch** | DMS integration layer: one adapter per DMS family, time-bounded cross-reference to stock item and AVK, commands with receipts, nightly reconciliation, per-rooftop degraded mode | Partner integrations (Hendrik Maes); built through 2027 | [[dms-integration]] |
   | **Inlet** | Shared ingestion pipeline: immutable landing zone, four validation layers, quarantine, stored match decisions, versioned survivorship | Specified July 2027 after the Odometer Correction | [[ingestion-and-entity-resolution]] |
   | **Provenance envelope v1** | Source, licence, confidence and two times on every fact, with append-only versions | July 2027, with Inlet | [[provenance-and-data-quality]] |
   | **Retrace** | Correction cases, impact adapters and the *disputed* fact status | Priya Raman's data operations; design record August 2027 | [[journey-data-correction]] |
   | **Pulse v2** | Market observations with listing lifecycles, market-vehicle clusters and inferred outcomes | Approved March 2027; live in stages April–October 2027 | [[market-data]] |
   | **Gauge** and **Lotwise Inspect** | Group condition service (write-once evidence, findings on a panel map, versioned rubrics) and its guided-capture app | Lotwise team, Ghent; Inspect piloted at Fleetmoor's return centers from 14 June 2027 | [[condition-and-damage]] |
   | **Mandate** and **Signalyard** | Consent and data-rights registry (one grant per vehicle) and purpose-scoped connected-car telemetry store | Fleetmoor odometer pilot live 6 September 2027 | [[connected-vehicle-data]] |
   | **Lotwise Dossier** and the **Dossier service** | One stock item per tenant and AVK per stay, with timeline, documents and module actions | Dossier service on AWS from Q4 2027; stock state leaves Lotwise Classic in 2028 | [[inventory-and-vehicle-dossier]] |
   | **Rebuilt Appraise** | Installable web app with offline capture, immutable offer versions and a versioned maximum-purchase-price calculator | 40 pilot rooftops from June 2027; all NL/BE rooftops October 2027 | [[vehicle-acquisition]] |
   | **Relay 2** | Syndication rebuilt as desired-state sync with reconciliation per channel listing | One Dossier, 2028 | [[listing-syndication]] |
   | **Sale-right register** and **Handback** | One exclusive, time-bounded right to sell per car across Tradefloor and auction partners; Fleetmoor's return-and-settlement workflow | Approved September 2027; Handback trial October–November 2027 | [[b2b-trade-and-remarketing]] |
   | **Runway** and **Baseline** | Onboarding orchestrator (a state machine per rooftop) and tenant configuration service (versioned, inherited settings and rules) | Platform organization; first half of 2027 | [[onboarding-and-configuration]] |
   | **Tenant directory** | Organization → legal entity → rooftop → department, versioned and effective-dated | Tenancy decision record July 2027 | [[tenancy-and-dealer-groups]] |
   | **Axleby ID** and **Gatekeeper** | One login with group federation and SCIM; relationship-based authorization | Velto migrated July–September 2027; Hartley pilot Q4 2027 | [[identity-access-and-entitlements]] |
   | **Meterline** | Usage events at the business moment, one rating engine | Shadow-rated beside the legacy runs from October 2027 | [[billing-and-metering]] |
   | **Charter** | Licence and purpose register with request-path checks and a recipient audit | Live May 2027; every external data call checked by December 2027 | [[data-licensing-and-governance]] |
   | **Folio** and **JSON API v2** | Contract registry; block-based lookup API with per-block outcomes | v2 lookups live October 2027; v2 webhooks Q3 2028 | [[vehicle-data-apis]], [[events-webhooks-and-sync]] |
   | **One Dossier event backbone** | Domain events through an outbox onto MSK, with a public webhook gateway | Design record 14 December 2027; Lotwise outbox Q1 2028 | [[events-webhooks-and-sync]] |
   | **Horizon** | Change-of-ownership propensity scores, redesigned as match-in on a client's own customers | DPIA March 2027; scores expire after 90 days | [[data-products-and-insights]] |
   | **Pitwall** | Support console: dossier timeline, channel status, lead trail, register status | Live September 2027 | [[observability-and-data-operations]] |
   | **Tidemark** | Retention and erasure service with an erasure ledger and a suppression list | Live December 2027 | [[privacy-and-retention]] |
   | **The Cloned Plate** | 2026 | Two Audi A4s carried the same Dutch plate; Tracewell merged the clone's damage events into the genuine car's history; a consumer complaint through Odolo exposed it.
   | **The Zombie Golf** | February 2027 | Velto sold a 2021 Volkswagen Golf at its Breda rooftop; the sold status from DriveDesk failed to sync; Relay's deletes queued behind failed updates; the car stayed live on **4** portals for **4 days**, producing **37** leads and **9** booked test drives. | The Zombie Listing | [[listing-syndication]] |
   | **The Month-End Register Outage** | 31 March 2027 | The Dutch register's transaction channel was unavailable to Axleby for **5 hours 40 minutes** on the last working day of the quarter; **3,100** transactions queued; the retry queue preserved order, but **214** were submitted twice after ambiguous timeouts and needed correction. | Borrowed Uptime | [[registration-and-trade-services]] |
   | **The Odometer Correction** | May 2027 | A Kilometra batch recorded miles as kilometers for **2,870** UK-imported vehicles; it was corrected after **11 days**, by which time **6,140** valuations, **412** history reports (**96** to consumers) and **1,180** live adverts carried the wrong mileage. | The Sticky Error | [[journey-data-correction]] |
   | **The Cross-Tenant Lead** | June 2027 | A cache key without a tenant id in Showroomly's lead-inbox BFF showed **112** leads of one dealer to users of **3** other dealers for **47 minutes**; reported to the UK Information Commissioner's Office (ICO) as a personal-data breach. | The Tenant Blur | [[tenancy-and-dealer-groups]] |
   | **The Lost Leads of Velto** | September 2027 | After Velto reorganized **46** rooftops into **9** regional hubs, routing rules for three closed rooftop codes, under which a hub's stock was still advertised, sent leads to an inactive DriveDesk CRM queue that accepted and then discarded them; **1,460** leads went unworked for **9 days**. | The Leaky Lead | [[journey-lead-to-dms]] |
   | **The DriveDesk Outage** | November 2027 | DriveDesk was hit by ransomware and offline for **12 days**; **1,900** Lotwise rooftops lost DMS sync; Lotwise served as the fallback for stock, adverts and sales documents. | DMS Hostage | [[dms-integration]] |
   | **The EV Overvaluation** | 2026 | After used battery-electric prices fell, Vantage overvalued used BEVs by **9.5%** on average for **14 weeks** before drift monitoring caught it; Fleetmoor and two dealer groups complained. | The Confident Valuation | [[valuation-engine]] |
   | **The No-Hit Credit** | 2026 | The no-hit-no-fee rule was implemented differently in Connect XML and JSON API v1; Axleby credited **€186,000** to **41** API clients. | The Unmetered Call | [[billing-and-metering]] |
   | **The Licence Breach** | January 2027 | A small lead-generation API client resold history-report data to a consumer website against its licence; it was detected through a customer complaint, not monitoring, and led to Charter. | Licence Blindness | [[data-licensing-and-governance]] |
   | **The Plate Enumeration Attack** | August 2027 | A scraper used **3,800** rotating IP addresses and leaked trial API keys to enumerate Odolo's free check and the JSON API: about **9 million** requests in 6 days. | (threat; see Licence Blindness) | [[vehicle-platform-security]] |
   **The Odometer Correction is the data-side spine**: one wrong unit in one supplier batch crosses history, valuation, adverts, reports and API clients, and the question is how far a correction can follow.
   **The Zombie Golf is the workflow-side spine**: one sold car crosses the DMS, the dossier, the publish queue, four portals and the lead inbox, and the question is who knows the car is gone.

   | **Official sources** | The Dutch register's information and transaction channels; the Belgian register through a partner; DVLA and DVSA interfaces in the UK | Turnstile team, Axleby Trade legal file |

   | **Insurers** | Damage claims, total losses, theft | Damage pools and claim registers, usually licensed for narrow purposes | Insurer-run registers; NMVTIS total-loss reports (US) |
   | **Workshops and inspection bodies** | Service records, repairs, odometer readings, inspection results | Workshop software exports, inspection databases, aggregators | DVSA MOT history (UK); Car-Pass (BE); workshop chains |

   | **Portals** | Asking prices, price changes, time on market, buyer interest | Data agreements, harvesting within legal limits, their own APIs | Auto Trader UK, mobile.de, AutoScout24, Marktplaats, Cars.com, CarGurus |
   | **Vehicle data providers** | Assembled facts: identity, specification, history, valuations, market data | They *are* the platform layer; they buy from everyone above | Carfax, HPI, DAT, Autovista, VWE, Autotelex |
   | **Valuation guides** | Reference values for trade, retail and residual | Licensed tables and APIs | Kelley Blue Book, Black Book, DAT, Schwacke, Autotelex, Cap HPI |
   | **DMS vendors** | The dealer's books: stock ledger, deals, accounting, service, parts, customers | Certified partner interfaces, file extracts, sometimes nothing | CDK Global, Reynolds and Reynolds, Tekion, Keyloop, Pinewood, incadea, CROSS |
   | **Customer relationship management (CRM), inventory and website vendors** | Leads, stock workflow, adverts, website behavior | APIs, feeds, ADF/XML lead e-mail | VinSolutions, vAuto, Dealer.com, Lotwise-type products |
   | **Dealers and dealer groups** | Purchase price, reconditioning cost, asking price, appraisal readings, sale | They type it into dealer software | Independents, franchised dealers, groups |
   | **Lessors and fleets** | Fleet lists, contract ends, return condition, residual values | Fleet files, APIs, returns platforms | Ayvens, Hertz, national lessors |
   | **Traders and exporters** | Cross-border purchases, import documents, registration tax | Trade platforms, import paperwork | B2B traders in NL, BE and DE |
   | **Lenders** | Finance applications, liens, contracts | Lender integrations, lien registers | Captive banks, consumer lenders |
   | **Consumers** | The plate they type, the car they own, the questions they ask | Free checks, paid reports, leads | Buyers, sellers, motorists |
   - A **register** is an official vehicle register, never a commercial provider: the Dutch RDW (*Rijksdienst voor het Wegverkeer*), the Belgian DIV (*Directie Inschrijvingen Voertuigen*), the German KBA (*Kraftfahrt-Bundesamt*), the UK's DVLA (Driver and Vehicle Licensing Agency) and DVSA (Driver and Vehicle Standards Agency, which holds the results of the MOT, the annual roadworthiness test), and the US state departme
   - A **vehicle data provider** sells facts and derived judgements about specific vehicles (identity, specification, history, condition, valuation, market data) by the call, the report, the file or the subscription.
   This book calls that business **vehicle data services**, or **the data half**.
   - **Dealer SaaS** is software that dealers use to buy, stock, price, advertise, sell and register cars, sold as a subscription.
   This book calls it **the workflow half**.
   - A **dealer management system (DMS)** is the dealer's enterprise resource planning system.
   - A **rooftop** is one physical dealership location.
   A **dealer group** is a company that owns two or more rooftops, often under several legal entities and brands. [[tenancy-and-dealer-groups]] owns the deep model.
   - An **independent** dealer sells used cars without a manufacturer franchise.
   **The same organization often plays several roles**: a portal is a channel, a market-data source and, in the UK, a valuation and software vendor; a dealer is a customer and a data source; a data provider such as VWE supplies software vendors and competes with them through its own dealer platform ([VWE API documentation, read October 2026](https://vwe.nl/api-documentatie/)).
   The productscape model behind this book calls this the **B2B2B channel conflict**.

   **Value names come from the guide era**: "trade-in value", *Händlereinkaufswert* (dealer purchase value) and the Dutch *koerslijst* (price list) are a guide's products that contracts, insurers and tax authorities refer to.
   **The DMS era made one system the owner of the dealer's books**: it started as accounting, and the stock ledger, deal file and manufacturer link grew around it ([[dms-integration]]).

   | **Independent used-car dealer** | One to three sites, 30–150 cars, does register paperwork in-house | Identify and Check, on the driveway | A hidden damage or rolled-back odometer wipes out the gross on a car | Lotwise (62% of rooftops); Autobedrijf Brandsma, 55 cars |

   | **Odometer and history evidence** | RDW odometer judgement on readings registered since 2014; insurer damage data | Car-Pass: 345.5 million readings received in 2025; document required at sale | No public odometer register; workshops, inspection reports and commercial providers | DVSA MOT history with odometer, weekly bulk and daily deltas | Title brands and odometer disclosures via NMVTIS; contributory history net

   | **Valuation references** | Autotelex *koerslijst* (also tax evidence for BPM), VWE, Indicata | Pan-European providers *(assumption)* | DAT and Schwacke (J.D.
   | **Dominant portals** | Marktplaats; AutoScout24 (with AutoTrack and Gaspedaal since 2025) | 2dehands/2ememain, AutoScout24 | mobile.de, AutoScout24 | Auto Trader UK, far ahead on time spent | Cars.com, CarGurus, Autotrader (Cox) |
   | **DMS landscape** | Brand-approved DMS for franchised dealers; local software for independents (Autoflex claims 2,400+ Dutch businesses) | Importer-influenced; not verified | Most OEM-shaped: CROSS held over 75% of German and Austrian VW dealers in 2019; Loco-Soft 3,400+ users | Independent vendors: Keyloop, Pinewood | Concentrated: in 2017 CDK held over 40% and Reynolds around 30% |
   | **Who integrates the ecosystem** | Register-connected data vendors that also sell dealer software (VWE, Autotelex) | Pan-Benelux and pan-European vendors | Data houses (DAT, Schwacke) plus portals; registration-service companies for transactions | The dominant portal sells data and retailer software | Full-stack groups (Cox) and DMS platforms (CDK, Reynolds) |
   | **The same car arrives as many records** | One car advertised on several portals, relisted, imported, re-plated; Axleby's Pulse matched only 61% of 2026 adverts to a VIN-keyed vehicle | Match rates per source and market | VIN-keyed identity graph; plates as time-bounded aliases; stored match decisions | [[vehicle-identity]], [[ingestion-and-entity-resolution]] |
   | **Two participants disagree** | A dealer's appraisal reading conflicts with an official one; two specification sources resolve to different editions | Conflict rules that flag rather than overwrite | Keep every source's fact with provenance; survivorship rules versioned | [[provenance-and-data-quality]] |
   | **A source is incomplete** | RDW open data has no VIN and withholds theft status; NHTSA's VIN decoder warns that a missing value does not mean "not equipped" | Explicit "unknown" and "not covered" states | Never turn absence into a fact; ask live for withheld facts | [[registers-and-official-data]], [[vehicle-history]] |
   | **A supplier sends wrong data** | Kilometra's May 2027 batch recorded miles as kilometers for 2,870 UK-imported cars | Distribution checks per source segment at ingestion | Quarantine; correction propagated to every derived product | [[journey-data-correction]] |
   | **A dependency goes down** | CDK Global's DMS, June 2024; the Dutch register's transaction channel unavailable to Axleby for 5 hours 40 minutes on 31 March 2027 | Synthetic checks and journey monitors | Degraded modes: labeled cached reads, honest queues for writes, reconciliation afterwards | [[reliability-and-dependencies]] |
   | **A participant changes access terms** | CDK closed its DMS to independent integrators in 2015; DVLA's Vehicle Enquiry Service is closed to new registrations (October 2026) | Contract and terms inventory with owners and review dates | Sanctioned interfaces only; adapters that can be replaced; a fallback source per critical fact | [[dms-integration]], [[build-buy-or-partner]] |
   | **A participant changes owner or merges** | AutoScout24 took over AutoTrack and Gaspedaal (2025); Carfax spun off with Mobility Global (2026); ATC sold to Autorola (2026) | Watch list of suppliers, channels and competitors | Adapters behind stable internal contracts; no single channel or source whose loss stops a product | [[evolving-the-vehicle-platform]] |
   | **Facts leak beyond their terms** | Axleby's Licence Breach, January 2027 | Purpose checks and access audit, not complaints | Licence and purpose register in the request path | [[data-licensing-and-governance]] |
   **Retries across organizations are dangerous where actions have legal effect**: Axleby's 214 double register submissions on 31 March 2027 came from blind resubmission after ambiguous timeouts ([[registration-and-trade-services]]).
   | **Pure data provider** | Carfax (history, contributory network) | Neutral supplier to every software vendor and portal | Distribution depends on others' software | Ingestion at scale, provenance, licensing, API products |
   | **Portal that sells data and software** | Auto Trader UK | Audience plus its own market data; bundling | Retailer pushback when bundled features change their workflow (Deal Builder, November 2025) | Taxonomy, stock sync, valuations from adverts, partner API |
   | **DMS-centered platform** | CDK Global | System of record; certified access fees | Access disputes and litigation; concentration risk | Partner programs, API contracts, resilience |
   | **Full-stack portfolio** | Cox Automotive | Data on both sides of the transaction | Years of integration across acquired products | Shared identity, vehicle key, data layer, federated APIs |
   | **Register-connected data vendor with dealer software** | VWE (NL); Axleby (fictional) | Register access as a moat; data pulled through workflow | One register as the main dependency; legacy XML APIs | Register gateway, dossier, metering, licence control |
   **The right one depends on which fact is scarce in the market**: the transaction connection where the register is open, the audience where one portal dominates, the contributory network where there is no register, and sanctioned access where one DMS holds the books.
   - **Keep an ecosystem map as a governed asset.** Every source, channel, partner and client class, with the facts exchanged, the contract owner, the licence terms that matter (cache, combine, resell, purpose), the interface, the review date and the fallback.
   - **Separate the common core from market adapters by design review, not by accident.** A new market should arrive as a set of adapters and rule packs.
   - **Treat every participant as a possible competitor, acquirer or casualty.** Portals buy each other, data houses change owners, DMS vendors change terms, registers close channels.
   - **Decide the channel conflict explicitly.** If the data half sells to software vendors who compete with the workflow half, the API contract, pricing and data access must be the same for the group's own products and for external clients, or the group will be accused of preferential treatment.
===== dealer-and-data-economics 47
   **Gross per unit** (often *gross profit per unit*, GPU) is the profit on one retailed car before overheads.
   - **Front-end gross** = retail price − (acquisition cost + reconditioning + transport + fees).
   - **Back-end gross** = income from finance and insurance products sold with the car (F&I): lender commission, warranties, service contracts, gap insurance.
   **Days-to-turn** is the number of days from stock-in (the moment a car enters the dealer's stock) to sale.
   | **Days-to-turn** (age at sale) | Sale date − stock-in date, averaged over sold cars | Axleby's 58 days for Lotwise stock (fictional) |
   | **Days listed** | Sale date − first advert date | AutoScout24: cars sold in Germany in Q4 2025 had been listed for an average of **59 days** ([AutoScout24 market report](https://www.autoscout24.de/unternehmen/daten/marktreport-q42025-gebrauchtwagenmarkt-schliesst-2025-stabil-ab-steigender-druck-zum-jahresstart/), February 2026) |
   | **Days' supply** | Stock ÷ daily sales over a stated window | Cox Automotive: **49 days** of US retail used supply in December 2025 ([Cox, January 2026](https://www.coxautoinc.com/insights/used-retail-vehicle-sales-december-2025/)); AutoNation: **38 days** on trailing calendar-month sales (10-K, 2026) |
   | **Time to line** | Acquisition → retail-ready ("frontline") | Trade-press estimates of 10–18 days, mostly vendor-sourced and from 2016–2023 |
   **Holding cost** is what each day in stock costs: depreciation, the cost of the money tied up, and smaller costs for space and insurance.
   | **Front-end gross** | **≈ €2,093** | |
   | **Holding cost** | **≈ €8.8 per day** | |
   | **Gross after holding cost** | **≈ €1,583** | before staff, premises, marketing and software |
   - **Central pricing and appraisal.** AutoNation describes "our One Price used vehicle centralized pricing and appraisal strategy" in its 2025 10-K.
   - **Moving stock between rooftops and channels.** An aging car may sell in another town or go to the wholesale (trade) channel before it loses more value.
   - **Mix over volume.** In 2025 Penske retailed 11.3% fewer used units while its used gross per unit rose 13.7%, which it attributed "primarily" to "a change in inventory management strategy internationally" ([Penske 10-K](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1019849/000162828026012830/pag-20251231.htm), February 2026).
   | **Owned, open or derived** | Dutch register mirror built from open data; Axleby's identity graph; valuations, condition grades and odometer judgements computed from several sources | Near zero: compute and storage | Volume, because fixed costs (ingestion, model training, data quality) are spread across calls |
   | **Rented per request** | Licensed build data per VIN; international damage records; a supplier's odometer readings | A fee on every upstream request, often whether or not it finds anything | Cache reuse permitted by the licence, hit rate, and price relative to fee |
   | **Rented per licence** | An annual licence to a specification catalog or a register data channel | Zero per call within the licence; step costs at licence renewal | Volume within the licensed scope and purpose |
   **Cost per lookup** is the fully loaded cost of answering one billable call:
   **No-hit-no-fee** is a pricing rule: a lookup that finds no record is not billed.
   | **Total** | **€1,110** | **96** | |
   | **Total to the group** | **€1,065** | of which Brandsma pays €885 |
   - **Gross revenue retention (GRR)**: this year's revenue from last year's customers, excluding expansion, divided by their revenue last year; it measures what leaks away.
   - **Net revenue retention (NRR)**: the same including expansion (modules, rooftops, price increases); above 100%, the base grows without new customers.
   - **Cost to serve**: the fully loaded cost of running one customer, including hosting, support, onboarding, channel and integration operations, and upstream data consumed.
   **On the dealer side** ([[inventory-and-vehicle-dossier]], [[used-car-pricing]]):
   - **Cost layers on the stock item**: acquisition, each recon line, transport and fees, each with a timestamp and source, plus the VAT regime fixed at purchase.
   - **Several clocks**: stock-in, retail-ready, first advert live and sold, plus, where it applies, a funding or curtailment date.
   - **Per-vehicle targets**: a gross or return target per car, or a potential tier, with the market snapshot that justified it.
   - **Price changes as events** with author (person or rule), reason and the market data used, so that the effect of a price drop on days and gross can be measured afterwards.
   - **A disposition decision** (retail or wholesale) recorded with its reasons, and the outcome fed back to appraisal.
   - **A usage event for every billable act**, created at the business moment (the report request, the transaction submission), carrying an idempotency key, tenant, rooftop, product, business key (VIN, stock number) and result class.
   - **Upstream cost joined to the call that caused it**: the supplier, the supplier's request id and the fee, so cost per lookup can be computed per client and product.
   - **The licence and purpose under which the answer may be stored or shown**, so cache reuse, which decides margin on rented data, is enforced in one place.
   - **Tenant and rooftop on every event and span**, set from the verified token, so revenue, cost to serve and noisy-neighbor load can be attributed ([[tenancy-and-dealer-groups]]).
   - **A governed metric catalog**: one definition each for days-to-turn, days listed, gross per unit, cost per lookup, GRR and NRR, with an owner and a version.
   | **Per rooftop per module per month** | Dealer SaaS core (Lotwise, Auto Trader, Carfax) | Adoption across rooftops; predictable revenue | Rooftop as a first-class tenant node; entitlements per rooftop and module; proration | Underprices heavy users; little link to value delivered |
   | **Per vehicle in stock or advertised** | Syndication, advertising | Growth in the dealer's volume | Daily stock counts per rooftop; disputes about what counted | Penalizes stock the dealer wants to sell; revenue falls when dealers cut stock (Auto Trader's stock lever) |
   | **Per call or per report** | Data APIs, history reports, register transactions | Usage; low entry price | Usage events with result class and upstream cost; no-hit-no-fee rules | Bill shock; disputes per VIN; margin depends on hit rate and cache reuse |
   | **Bundle with included volume** | Appraise with 25 reports; packages | Habit; simple invoices | Allowance tracking per rooftop and period; overage rating | Unused allowance hides low value; heavy users subsidized |
   | **Annual licence with volume tiers** | Insurers, lessors, portals | Commitment; low cost of sale | Volume tracking against tiers; purpose and licence enforcement | Zero marginal revenue on calls with real marginal cost |
   | **Success fee or revenue share** | Finance referral, trade exchange, auction hand-off | Outcomes | Attribution of the outcome to the platform; partner reconciliation | Cyclical revenue; regulated (credit intermediation); attribution disputes |
   - **Bundling data into the workflow** (as Auto Trader does in its packages) raises adoption and simplifies invoices, but hides upstream cost from the customer, so the platform must cap and alert on cost per rooftop internally.
   - **Finance-referral revenue** (about €10 million at Lotwise) rides on the dealer's sales volume and on regulation.
   **What stays common** is the unit model: cost layers, tax regime on the stock item, multiple clocks, per-vehicle targets, usage events with result classes and tenant-attributed cost.
   **What varies by market** is a rule set, not a fork: margin-scheme rules, register fees passed through, finance-commission retention, metric defaults, and e-invoicing of the platform's own invoices, mandatory for business-to-business sales in Belgium since 1 January 2026 ([FPS Finance](https://einvoice.belgium.be/en), accessed October 2026) and being phased in for German issuers from 2027 ([[billing-and-metering]]).
   **Require a unit-economics line for every new data product**: expected upstream cost, hit rate, reuse permitted by licence, and price.
===== vehicle-capability-map 50
   A **business capability** is what the business must be able to do to deliver value, described independently of the people, processes and systems that currently do it. "Value a used car" is a capability; Vantage is a system that implements it; Wertlauf is the team that built it; the appraisal screen in Lotwise is one place it is used.
   A **shared capability** is one that both halves of the platform, the data half and the workflow half, consume and that has one implementation and one owner.
   - **Nine capabilities were implemented two or three times.** Accounts and login had three implementations, billing three paths, vehicle data APIs two stacks, and vehicle identity was keyed three ways (Lotwise's stock id plus plate, Connect XML's plate and Anchor's Axleby Vehicle Key, AVK).
   - **Eight capabilities the group needed had no clear home in the reference model.** DMS (dealer management system) integration, events and webhooks, onboarding and configuration, observability and data operations, ingestion and entity resolution, provenance and data quality, connected-vehicle data and vehicle identity resolution as a capability of its own.
   - **The incidents clustered.** The EV Overvaluation (2026), the No-Hit Credit (2026), the **Licence Breach** (January 2027) and the **Zombie Golf** (February 2027, a sold Golf live on 4 portals for 4 days, drawing 37 leads) each sat in a capability that was either duplicated, unowned or missing from the model.
   | **Capability** | A stable ability the business needs to deliver value, named as a noun phrase about the business ("vehicle valuation", "register transactions") | A system (Vantage), a team (Wertlauf), a product (Lotwise Appraise) or a process step ("enter the mileage") |
   | **Level 1 (domain)** | A group of capabilities that serve one part of the value chain or one shared purpose; six in Axleby's v1 map | A business unit or brand |
   | **Level 2 (capability)** | The unit of ownership, investment and sharing decisions; 39 in v1 | A microservice; one capability may need several services |
   | **Level 3** | A sub-capability drawn only where a sharing or sourcing decision needs it | Detail for completeness; v1 drew none |
   | **Investment class** | Differentiating, parity or commodity (defined below) | The capability's current health |
   | **Condition** | How well the capability works today: green, amber, red or grey | Its importance |
   | **Scope** | Who consumes it: shared (both halves), data half, workflow half | Who happens to build it today |
   | **Market flag** | Whether the capability's rules, sources or partners differ by country | Whether it is sold in several countries |
   - **Differentiating:** decides whether a dealer or data client chooses the group; earns the best engineers, build as the default and its own accuracy metrics.
   - **Parity:** must match competitors to stay in the deal, but doing it better wins little; deserves exactly one good implementation, often on bought components.
   - **Commodity:** standardized; buy, rent or delegate to a partner.
   | **Vehicle data foundation** (8) | Register connector and mirror; vehicle identity and specification; vehicle history; EV battery health; damage calculation; market and transaction data; valuation; registration-tax calculation | Who and what the vehicle is, what happened to it, what it is worth |
   | **Dealer operations** (7) | Vehicle dossier and workflow hub; stock and pricing; trade-in appraisal; advert syndication; dealer website and lead capture; sales administration; financing referral | The dealer's car from purchase to delivery |
   | **Registration, trade and remarketing** (4) | Register transactions; plates and materials webshop; import and export documents; B2B trade exchange | Official paperwork and trader-to-trader sales |
   | **Data products and channels** (6) | Vehicle data API gateway; file enrichment; market insight dashboards; change-of-ownership scoring; consumer plate check; consumer travel documents | Data sold to enterprises, developers and consumers |
   | **Platform and commerce** (3) | Accounts, identity and entitlements; subscription and transaction billing; data licensing and governance | The shared commercial and compliance backbone |
   | **Vehicle identity resolution** | Plates move on import, re-registration and personalization; an identity graph keyed by vehicle identification number (VIN) is a capability, not a lookup | Every market studied keys registers by plate and history by VIN | [[vehicle-identity]] |
   | **Ingestion and entity resolution** | Registers, suppliers, portals and dealers send overlapping, late and wrong data; validation, quarantine and survivorship need one implementation | Register snapshots, supplier files and harvested adverts arrive in different shapes and rhythms | [[ingestion-and-entity-resolution]] |
   | **Provenance and data quality** | A fact without source, date, licence and confidence cannot be shown, corrected or defended | Corrections must reach every copy; reports carry liability | [[provenance-and-data-quality]] |
   | **Connected-vehicle data** | The EU Data Act applies from 12 September 2025 and gives users and their chosen third parties access to vehicle-generated data | Regulation (EU) 2023/2854 | [[connected-vehicle-data]] |
   | **DMS integration** | Franchised dealers keep accounting, deals and often stock in a DMS the platform does not control | CDK's 2024 outage and the Authenticom litigation show the stakes | [[dms-integration]] |
   | **Events and webhooks** | Stock, price, sold and corrected-fact changes must reach portals, DMS, clients and the warehouse in order | The model lists events on data assets but no capability publishes them | [[events-webhooks-and-sync]] |
   | **Onboarding and configuration** | Dealer groups arrive with hundreds of users, a DMS and channel accounts; configuration must replace code forks | Group onboarding is an integration project, not a sign-up | [[onboarding-and-configuration]] |
   | **Observability and data operations** | Health per tenant, per channel and per data source, and a support view across products | The model names availability KPIs but no capability measures them | [[observability-and-data-operations]] |
   **Lease contract administration** is a different business (fleet management software) that Axleby does not sell; **marketplace payments and escrow** stay with auction partners because Tradefloor hands deals off; **fraud prevention** is treated as a cross-cutting concern of security, not a capability a customer buys ([[vehicle-platform-security]]).
   | **Differentiating** | **9:** register access, identity resolution, history, provenance, market data, valuation, battery health, vehicle dossier, register transactions | **2:** connected-vehicle data, market insight | **2:** appraisal, pricing |
   | **Parity** | **8:** specification, ingestion, events, accounts, entitlements, onboarding, metering, licensing | **3:** data APIs, ownership prediction, consumer checks | **10:** damage, registration tax, merchandising, syndication, websites, leads, sales documents, DMS integration, import and export documents, trade exchange |
   | **Commodity** | **1:** observability | **1:** file enrichment | **2:** financing referral, plates and materials |
   **My reading** is that the order follows the capability dependencies: specification before valuation, valuation before trended valuation, specification and features before AI descriptions, all of them before transactions. [[case-auto-trader-uk]] develops this reading.
   | **The org chart in disguise** | Capabilities named after brands or teams ("Lotwise features", "Wertlauf models") | A capability with exactly one team and one product and no consumers outside it | Rename as a business ability; list consumers; merge where two names hide one ability |
   | **Duplicates under two names** | "Lead inbox" and "enquiry management" on the same map | Two capabilities with the same inputs, outputs and consumers | Merge; record the merge in the map's changelog |
   | **Conflicting owners** | Two directors claim valuation, or nobody claims provenance | The ownership column has two names or none | One accountable owner per capability, decided by the Chief Architect with the CTO; consumers are not owners |
   | **Incomplete map** | DMS integration, events or onboarding missing because the reference model lacked them | Systems or incidents that cannot be placed under any capability | Add the capability, mark it "added", state why it belongs |
   | **Everything is differentiating** | Every director classifies their own area as D | More than about a third of capabilities rated D *(assumption: a rule of thumb, not a standard)* | Ask "would a customer leave if this were merely as good as a competitor's?"; require a named competitor comparison for every D |
   | **The stale map** | A map from last year guides this year's sourcing | The map's date is older than the last major incident or acquisition | Revise on a fixed calendar and after every acquisition, incident review or market entry |
   | **Product portfolio map** (brands and modules) | What the group sells | Matches revenue lines and P&L ownership | Hides duplication across brands; no sharing signal | Commercial planning; investor reporting |
   | **Capability map with heat** (this chapter) | What the business must be able to do, how well, and how much it differentiates | Stable over reorganizations; exposes duplicates and gaps | Political; easy to let go stale; says nothing about runtime | Investment and sharing decisions; acquisition integration |
   | **Wardley map** | Components on a value chain by evolution stage | Shows drift toward commodity; good for sourcing timing | Harder to read for boards; needs a user need anchor | Strategy and sourcing over several years ([[build-buy-or-partner]]) |
   | **Bounded-context map** | Models, languages and sources of truth, with their relationships | Directly drives service and team boundaries | Too detailed for business prioritization | Design of contexts, contracts and anti-corruption layers ([[vehicle-bounded-contexts]]) |
   | **System landscape diagram** | What runs where and calls what | Operationally accurate | Encodes today's accidents; no business classification | Operations, migration and dependency analysis |
   | **Different registers and sources** | A1 register access, A3 specification, A4 history, A7 market data, E1 consumer checks | Register access regimes (RDW, DIV, KBA, DVLA and DVSA, US state registers), type codes, history sources, portal landscape | One capability, per-market adapters behind one contract ([[registers-and-official-data]]) |
   | **Different models and rules** | A8 valuation, D3 market insight, D4 ownership prediction | Price levels, segment definitions, disclosure and privacy limits | One engine, per-market models and rule sets |
   | **Different law** | A11 connected-vehicle data, B4 merchandising, B8 sales documents, B9 financing referral | EU Data Act (not UK), advert disclosures, VAT margin scheme, consumer-credit rules | Versioned market packs of rules and templates ([[onboarding-and-configuration]]) |
   | **Different partners** | B5 syndication, B10 DMS integration | Portals and DMS products per country | One capability, adapters per partner ([[listing-syndication]], [[dms-integration]]) |
   | **Netherlands only** | A12 registration tax, C1 register transactions, C2 import and export documents, C3 plates and materials | Dutch registration tax (*BPM*, *belasting van personenauto's en motorrijwielen*), the Dutch register's transaction channel | Market-specific capabilities; no generic version until a second market is entered ([[registration-and-trade-services]]) |
===== vehicle-bounded-contexts 68
   A **bounded context** is an explicit boundary within which a model and its language (the **ubiquitous language**, in Eric Evans's term) are consistent.
   | **Sold** | De-listed (market data); stock transition (dossier); deal posted (DMS); keeper changed (register); binding trade acceptance (business-to-business, B2B, trade) |
   | **Mileage** | Dealer-typed reading (appraisal); official reading (register inspection); supplier reading (history); surviving current value (golden record); valuation input (ledger) |
   | **Customer** | Paying organization (billing); tenant (tenancy); enquirer (leads); buyer on a contract (sales); DMS customer number (DMS) |
   | **Vehicle** | Physical car (identity); stock stay (dossier); advert (channel); market-vehicle cluster of adverts (market data) |
   A **subdomain** is a part of the business problem; a bounded context is a part of the solution.
   **Supporting subdomains** are specific to the business but not where it wins.
   An **entity** is a business object with identity and a lifecycle.
   | **Reference** | The owner's key, plus what the user typed | A stock item holds the AVK | Resolved through the owner; redirected on merge |
   | **Decision snapshot** | Id and version of a fact used for a decision | The appraisal used history report version 3 | Never updated; a correction adds "true now" beside "known then" |
   | **Observation** | Another party's claim, kept as evidence | DriveDesk's model text; a dealer's "service book complete" | Stored with source and time; may feed the owner's ingestion |
   | **Projection** | A read model rebuilt from the owner's events | Group stock list; register mirror replica | Rebuildable; lag measured and shown |
   | **Vehicle facts** | Vehicle identity | Core | Anchor | Utrecht |
   | **Value** | Market data | Supporting | Pulse | Munich |
   | **Dealer workflow** | Stock and dossier | Core | Lotwise Classic → Dossier service | Ghent |
   | **Commercial control plane** | Tenancy | Supporting | Tenant directory | Kraków |
   | **Governance** | Licensing | Supporting | Charter | Utrecht |
   **Pricing** is supporting because Axleby's pricing advantage comes from valuation and market data; the rules are copyable configuration.
   **Licensing** is supporting, but no fact context ships without Charter in the request path ([[data-licensing-and-governance]]).
   | **Vehicle** | AVK (opaque, 26 characters, never reused); VIN as evidence | Vehicle identity (Anchor) | References everywhere; the plate and VIN "as typed" beside them | Provisional → Confirmed ↔ Suspect → Merged (tombstone) or Split ([[vehicle-identity]]) |
   | **Plate assignment** | (country, plate, valid from, valid to) | Vehicle identity, fed by register access | Projections in search; never a foreign key | Opened and closed by register or document evidence |
   | **Register record** | The register's key (Dutch plate, UK registration mark) | The register; inside the platform, register access (Turnstile) | Mirror projection; theft and awaiting-inspection status asked live, never mirrored | Follows the register's snapshot or change feed |
   | **Specification** | AVK plus specification version | Specification (Specbook) | Decision snapshots (valuation, advert, deal); rendered advert text as a projection | Resolution states from identified to disputed ([[specification-and-equipment]]) |
   | **History event and odometer judgement** | Event id; AVK plus the source's key; judgement rule version | Vehicle history (Tracewell) | References in reports; judgements as decision snapshots | Events append-only, corrections supersede; judgements recomputed ([[vehicle-history]]) |
   | **History report** | Report id plus version | Vehicle history (snapshot store) | Reference in the dossier and in usage events | Issued → superseded by a new version |
   | **Current vehicle facts (golden record)** | AVK plus attribute | Each attribute's owning context; computed by Inlet survivorship rules | It *is* a projection, materialized beside Anchor for read speed | Recomputed from source records, never written |
   | **Condition report, battery observation** | Report id; observation id | Condition and battery (Gauge; battery-health service) | Decision snapshots in appraisal, trade listing, valuation | Superseded, never edited ([[condition-and-damage]], [[ev-battery-health]]) |
   | **Consent grant** | Grant id; one per vehicle and purpose | Connected data (Mandate) | References in telemetry and promoted readings | Requested → pending → active → revoking → ended ([[connected-vehicle-data]]) |
   | **Market observation and listing lifecycle** | Observation id; market-vehicle cluster id | Market data (Pulse) | Aggregates only outside the context | Live → Gone, Relisted, LiveElsewhere, Transferred, SoldProbable, SoldConfirmed ([[market-data]]) |
   | **Valuation** | Valuation id | Valuation (Vantage ledger) | Decision snapshots in dossier, deal, trade listing; outcome links appended in the ledger | Issued; superseded, never edited ([[valuation-engine]]) |
   | **Stock item and dossier** | Stock item id; one open per tenant and AVK per stay; dealer stock number as a label | Stock and dossier; field ownership per rooftop with the DMS | Projections (stock lists, websites); references in leads, deals, listings | Nine commercial states, Appraised to Delivered or Written off ([[inventory-and-vehicle-dossier]]) |
   | **Asking price** | Stock item id plus price event | Stock and dossier (pricing proposes) | Projections in channels and websites | Each change with author, reason and rule version ([[used-car-pricing]]) |
   | **Channel listing** | (stock item, channel); portal listing id; creation key | Syndication (desired state); the portal (actual state) | Observations of portal state; the dossier's advertising track | Desired present or absent, observed state reconciled ([[listing-syndication]]) |
   | **Trade listing, sale-right lease, trade deal** | Listing id; lease id with fencing token; trade deal id | B2B trade (Tradefloor) | The buyer receives a copy of agreed dossier facts at sale | Unoffered → Leased → Accepted → Released ([[b2b-trade-and-remarketing]]) |
   | **Lead and interaction** | Lead id; source ids as references | Leads (Switchboard) | Delivered copies in an external customer relationship management (CRM) system, with receipts | New → routed → worked → won or lost ([[leads-and-crm]]) |
   | **Prospect** | Per dealer legal entity | Leads | None across legal entities or tenants | Linked, never merged across entities |
   | **Deal and issued documents** | Deal id; invoice number per series | Sales and documents; the DMS for DMS-connected franchised rooftops | References from the dossier; accounting export | Draft → Quoted → Signed → Invoiced → Delivered → Closed, or Cancelled ([[deals-and-sales-documents]]) |
   | **Buyer (customer master)** | Per legal entity: DMS customer id or Sales Admin customer id | The dealer's system of record: DMS if connected, else Sales Admin | Snapshot on the deal | The dealer is controller; the platform processes |
   | **Register transaction** | Transaction id; idempotency key from the dealer's click | Register transactions (Axleby Trade ledger) | Dossier register track; proof by reference | Queued → Submitted → Confirmed, Rejected or Ambiguous, then probe ([[registration-and-trade-services]]) |
   | **Register authority** | Legal entity plus recognition | Register transactions (authority register) | Read by the access decision path as a gate | Granted, expired, withdrawn |
   | **DMS cross-reference** | (DMS, rooftop, external id) with validity interval | DMS integration (Hitch) | None | Edge opened and closed on transfer, restock, buy-back ([[dms-integration]]) |
   | **Tenant, legal entity, region, rooftop** | Opaque ids; rooftop ids global | Tenancy (tenant directory) | Projections in every context, refreshed by lifecycle events | Versioned, effective-dated; `rooftop.closed` with successor, `rooftop.transferred` ([[tenancy-and-dealer-groups]]) |
   | **User, API client, membership, relationship** | Subject id; client id; Gatekeeper tuple | Identity and access (Axleby ID, Gatekeeper) | Subject id in audit records; short-lived decision caches | Invited → active → disabled; provisioned by SCIM (System for Cross-domain Identity Management) where federated ([[identity-access-and-entitlements]]) |
   | **Entitlement** | (tenant, rooftop, product, feature, effective dates) | Identity and access (entitlement service), derived from subscriptions | Local caches filled by `entitlement.changed` | Effective-dated; derived, never edited |
   | **Configuration** | Setting key plus version at a hierarchy node | Configuration (Baseline) | Effective-config caches | Versioned, inherited, lockable ([[onboarding-and-configuration]]) |
   | **Usage event, invoice** | Usage id derived from client request reference, stock-keeping unit (SKU) and billing account; invoice number | Metering and billing (Meterline, rating, invoice run) | Rated usage references events; usage views are projections | Immutable; corrections are reversal events or credit notes ([[billing-and-metering]]) |
   | **Source licence, client grant, access decision** | Licence class; grant id; decision id | Licensing (Charter) | Decision ids on responses and usage events | Rules effective-dated; audit append-only ([[data-licensing-and-governance]]) |
   - **Conformist**: the downstream adopts the upstream model as is.
   - **Anti-corruption layer (ACL)**: "an isolating layer to provide your system with functionality of the upstream system in terms of your own domain model", translating "in one or both directions" ([Evans, 2015](https://www.domainlanguage.com/wp-content/uploads/2016/05/DDD_Reference_2015-03.pdf)).
   - **Open host service and published language**: a documented, versioned protocol many downstreams use.
   - **Customer–supplier**: the downstream's needs shape the upstream's plan.
   - **Vehicle facts went to one group in Utrecht**, with Munich keeping valuation and market data; Lotwise's own plate lookups and Connect XML's register tables ended with the Three Copies review.
   - **Dealer workflow stayed in Ghent, one team per context**, with Hendrik Maes's partner-integrations group owning the external adapters (channels, DMS, supplier connectors) as products.
   - **The control plane went to Marta Nowak's platform organization in Kraków**: tenancy, identity, configuration and the tagging standards that put tenant and rooftop on every flow.
   | **Product-shaped** | Each product or acquisition is a context with its own vehicle, customer and billing | Fast for each team; no coordination | Five records per car; the Acquisition Archipelago; every cross-product feature is an integration project | The first year after an acquisition, while the map is being drawn |
   | **Entity services** | One service per noun: vehicle service, customer service, stock service | Simple to explain; matches the database | Business rules span several services; transitions become distributed transactions; every change touches many teams | Rarely; only for true reference data with no lifecycle rules |
   | **Shared database, modules in code** | One schema, contexts as packages | Transactions across everything; no events to build | Any team can write any table; ownership is a convention nobody enforces; one migration blocks all | Small teams, one product, one release train |
   | **Capability-aligned contexts, shared identity and control plane** | Contexts around meanings and lifecycles; conform to AVK and tenancy; ACL at external edges | One join for the car and the customer; teams ship separately; external changes contained | Needs an event backbone, a governed map and a board willing to decide contested rows | A group with several products, two halves and external dependencies: Axleby's 
   | **What the plate identifies** | NL: the vehicle for its Dutch life; BE: the owner, who moves it to the next car | The keeper's registration; usually deregistered on sale | The vehicle, except private plates moved or retained | Varies by state |
   | **Register context's edge** | NL: open snapshot plus a provider channel; BE: through a partner | No open register; offices and services | MOT (roadworthiness test) history API with change feed | State Departments of Motor Vehicles (DMVs) plus NMVTIS (the national title information system), VIN-keyed |
   | **Who completes "registered"** | NL: the dealer's recognized entity, synchronously | Office or service, asynchronously | Keeper or motor trader online | Dealer or service provider at the DMV |
   | **DMS position at the dealer edge** | Long tail of local vendors; many independents without a DMS | Manufacturer-shaped DMS products per brand | Few vendors for groups, plus a tail | Concentrated; certified integration programs with fees |
   | **Registration tax context** | NL: registration tax (BPM); BE: regional tax on putting into use (BIV) | None at registration; annual vehicle tax *(general practice)* | Vehicle Excise Duty (VED) | State sales and title fees |
   **What is common** is every internal context and every entity in the table.
   - **Writers per field**, from the writer audit: a second writer is an incident in slow motion.
   - **Undeclared copies**: the census runs every quarter, not once.
   - **Heuristic joins** by plate and date or by name and postcode: each is a missing reference.
   - **Correction reach**: the share of consumers confirming recomputation within target per correction, the Sticky Error made measurable.
===== part-2 0
===== vehicle-identity 52
   The **vehicle identification number (VIN)** is a 17-character code that the manufacturer assigns to one vehicle and stamps into its structure.
   The **world manufacturer identifier (WMI)** is the first three characters: a code for a manufacturer or one of its divisions, issued through a national body under ISO 3780.
   **The model-year code in position 10 repeats every 30 years**: codes used for 1980–2009 were reused from 2010, and for North American cars and light trucks whether position 7 is a letter or a digit tells you which cycle applies ([Wikipedia, 2026](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vehicle_identification_number)).
   **Older vehicles are not standard**: the 17-character format became mandatory in the United States only for 1981 models, and earlier "VINs" range from 13-character codes to bare serial numbers.
   A **plate assignment** is this book's name for the fact that a given plate was attached to a given vehicle in a given jurisdiction for a given period: `(country, plate, vehicle, valid_from, valid_to, source, evidence)`.
   | **VIN** | One vehicle as built | Yes, except errors, re-stamping and cloning | The natural key for the vehicle |
   | **Plate** | A registration in one jurisdiction, for a period | No | Input at the counter and the lane; join to register data as of a date |
   | **Registration document numbers** (Dutch registration code, UK V5C serial, German registration certificate Part II number) | One issued document | No; replaced on every transfer | Proof of authority in a transaction, never identity |
   | **Type keys** (EU type-approval number with variant and version; German HSN/TSN manufacturer and type key numbers) | A type of vehicle, not one vehicle | Yes | Specification and insurance classes ([[specification-and-equipment]]) |
   | **Stock id** | One car's stay at one dealer | No; a car traded twice has two | The dealer workflow ([[inventory-and-vehicle-dossier]]) |
   | **Advert or listing id** | One advert on one channel | No | Syndication and market data ([[market-data]]) |
   | **Vehicle key** (Axleby: AVK) | The platform's record of one physical vehicle | Yes, by design; redirected after a merge | The key every product and event uses |
   The **vehicle key** is an internal surrogate identifier that the platform assigns to one physical vehicle when it first sees it, and that it never reuses.
   **Plate cloning** is putting copies of another car's legitimate plates on a vehicle, usually the same make, model and color, to avoid fines and tolls or to hide a stolen car.
   **Data errors** are the bulk: O and I typed for 0 and 1 (both letters are banned from VINs), missing or swapped characters, the dealer's trade plate recorded instead of the car's, an advert carrying the plate of another car in the same stock.
   An **identity graph** is the data structure that records which identifiers, registrations and records belong to which physical vehicle, over time, with the evidence for each link.
   - **Vehicle node.** One per physical vehicle, keyed by the AVK.
