   - **VIN observation.** A VIN exactly as received, plus a normalized form (upper case, trimmed, O, I and Q flagged, not silently replaced), with source, time, capture method (register, document scan, typing, feed) and validation result.
   - **Registration episode.** One registration in one jurisdiction: country, register, first-registration date, end date and end reason (export, scrapping, deregistration, transfer to a private plate).
   - **Plate assignment.** The time-bounded edge from a plate to a registration episode, with its source and evidence.
   - **Identity events.** Every creation, attachment, merge, split and status change, appended to a log.
   **VIN evidence beats plate evidence**: when a fact arrives with both and they disagree with the graph, the fact goes to the vehicle its VIN identifies, and the disagreement is recorded as a signal.
   | **Confirmed** | VIN and plate assignment from a register or an official document checked against one | Axleby's licensed Dutch register data; a captured registration certificate field (E) matching the register | Sell paid history reports and valuations; submit register transactions |
   | **Corroborated** | VIN from a non-official source agrees with an independent second source | Dealer-typed VIN whose last four digits match the Dutch *meldcode*; a dealer-typed VIN for which the UK MOT (annual roadworthiness test) history service returns the same registration | Same as Confirmed, with the source shown |
   | **Asserted** | One non-official source | A VIN typed at appraisal; a plate in a portal advert | Run dealer workflow (stock, adverts, pricing); not sell history as fact |
   | **Suspect** | Conflicting signals | Attribute mismatch, overlapping registrations, a VIN arriving under another car's plate | Hold: quarantine new facts, show "identity under review" |
   **They are a contract between the identity context and every product**: Tracewell will not assemble a paid report on an Asserted identity, Vantage marks such valuations provisional, and Lotwise lets a dealer advertise on one because the dealer answers for the advert.
   **Never cache an as-of resolution as current**: a lookup for an event in 2023 and a lookup for today are different keys.
   **Never cache "unavailable" as "not found"**: when a register or supplier cannot answer, return the last known assignment marked with its age, and do not store the failure as a fact (the Driver and Vehicle Standards Agency's (DVSA) MOT history API makes the distinction explicit with an `Unavailable` value for its recall flag).
   - **The Dutch register's open data is plate-keyed and has no VIN.** The RDW dataset *Gekentekende voertuigen* (registered vehicles) held about **16.87 million** rows on 22 October 2026, keyed by plate, with about a hundred columns and no VIN column ([RDW open data, n.d., accessed 2026-10-22](https://opendata.rdw.nl/api/views/m9d7-ebf2.json)).
   - **The US is VIN-keyed throughout.** States must report each VIN's brands, titled owner and odometer disclosures to NMVTIS at least every 24 hours ([28 CFR 25.54](https://www.law.cornell.edu/cfr/text/28/25.54)), and NHTSA's vPIC decodes any VIN pattern for free, with error codes ([NHTSA vPIC, n.d.](https://vpic.nhtsa.dot.gov/api/)).
   - **The EU registration certificate carries the VIN in a harmonized field.** Directive 1999/37/EC defines field (A) for the registration number, (B) the date of first registration and **(E) the VIN**, so a cross-border appraisal reads the same fields off any member state's certificate ([Council Directive 1999/37/EC, 1999](http://publications.europa.eu/resource/celex/31999L0037)).
   | **Duplicate vehicles**: one car under two AVKs after a VIN typo | VIN edit distance of one with matching plate and attributes; scheduled duplicate scan | Propose a merge; auto-merge only when the plate assignment is Confirmed for both | Tombstone and redirect; publish `vehicle.merged`; consumers re-key |
   | **Conflicting updates**: two sources assign the same plate to different VINs for overlapping periods | Overlap check on every new assignment | Keep both observations; mark the vehicle Suspect; the register's assignment wins for the current interval | Review; split if a second car is real; record the losing source's error rate |
   | **Incomplete records**: plate only, or a partial VIN | Missing VIN on observation | Create a Provisional node; allow workflow use, not paid reports | Fill the VIN from the register or the next document capture; confirm or merge |
   | **Retries**: the same observation delivered twice | Idempotency key on `(source, source record id, version)` | Ignore the duplicate | None needed |
   | **Partial failures**: a merge applied in Anchor but not by all consumers | Consumer acknowledgements and a lag dashboard per consumer | Re-deliver from the event log | Nightly reconciliation of AVKs referenced by each consumer against tombstones |
   | **External outage**: register or supplier cannot answer a plate lookup | Turnstile health and timeouts | Return the last known assignment with its age and tier; block register transactions and Confirmed-only products if no assignment exists | Refresh assignments touched during the outage when the source returns |
   | **Fraud signal**: VIN arrives under another car's plate | VIN comparison at ingestion | Quarantine the fact; mark Suspect; never merge on plate alone | Split or clear after review; feed the security and fraud team ([[vehicle-platform-security]]) |
   **A confident auto-merge is worse than a duplicate**: two AVKs for one car confuse a dealer; one AVK for two cars puts one car's damage into another's report.
   | **Plate as the key** | Every record keyed by country and plate | A single-market tool in the Netherlands or UK that never sells history | Breaks on imports, exports, private plates and every Belgian and German sale; makes clones invisible |
   | **VIN as the key** | Records keyed by normalized VIN | US-only platforms where the VIN is mandatory, checked and on every record | Cannot hold a car before its VIN is known; typos create duplicates; a clone and its victim become one record; changing a wrong VIN means re-keying everything |
   | **Surrogate key anchored to the VIN** (recommended) | An internal key per vehicle; VINs and plates as dated, sourced edges | Multi-market platforms selling history and valuation; any acquired portfolio | An identity service to run; every product must resolve through it; merges and splits must be propagated |
   | **Per-product keys with a mapping table** | Each product keeps its own key; a cross-reference joins them | A short transition while products are migrated | Every new consumer joins through the table; drift between products is never fixed |
   | **The plate follows** | The vehicle, for its Dutch life | The owner, to their next car | The keeper's registration; usually deregistered on sale | The vehicle, except private plates moved or retained on a V778 | Varies by state: vehicle, seller, or a new plate on sale |
   | **Plate unique within** | The country, for one car | The country, but reused across cars over time | The country, over time | The country; Q plates replace doubtful identities | One state only |
   | **VIN in public vehicle data** | No: open data is plate-keyed; *meldcode* gives the last four digits | VIN-keyed odometer history at Car-Pass; register data not public | No public register; access under §39 of the Road Traffic Act (StVG) needs a legal claim | No VIN from DVLA VES; MOT API answers by VIN or plate | Yes: NMVTIS and vPIC are VIN-keyed |
   | **Best plate-to-VIN bridge** | Licensed register channel; registration certificate | Registration certificate; insurer or Car-Pass data | Registration certificate Part I field (E); dealer capture | Captured VIN checked against MOT history by VIN, which returns the registration; V5C; licensed data | Title and NMVTIS records; plate data per state |
   | **VIN validation** | Length, characters, known WMI | Same | Same | Same, plus DVLA-issued replacement VINs | Check digit mandatory; position 10 gives model year |
   | **Typical identity transitions** | Import gets a new plate; export ends the plate | Owner moves plate to next car | Deregistration and re-registration on sale | Private plate transfers; Q plates; re-stamped VINs | Re-titling across states; title washing |
   **What stays common** is the model: one vehicle node, VIN observations, registration episodes, time-bounded plate assignments, confidence tiers, merge and split events.
   **What is market-specific** is a set of adapters and rules: how a plate is normalized (Belgian dashes, UK spacing, German district prefixes), how long an assignment may be cached before it is re-checked, which source confirms a VIN, and which transitions to expect.
===== registers-and-official-data 56
   | **Turnstile mirror** | Daily open-data snapshot, plus live lookups on the information channel | Daily | JSON API v1, Odolo, Specbook, Tracewell | Owner-change date, inspection expiry, export flag, recall flag, transfer-possible flag |
   | **Connect XML register tables** (SQL Server, Utrecht) | Own nightly loader, written in 2008, reading the same open data | Nightly; failed silently on 6 of 90 nights *(Axleby illustration)* | Connect XML clients (38% of API calls), the invoice run | Same fields, older column names |
   | **Lotwise Classic plate cache** (MySQL) | Filled on a dealer's first lookup of a plate | Only when a user reopened the dossier; median entry age **11 days** | Lotwise Appraise, the stock book, the advert editor | Whatever was true at the first lookup |
   A **vehicle register** is an official record, kept by a public body or under a statutory mandate, of the vehicles admitted to the roads of a jurisdiction and the people responsible for them.
   | **Registration** | Plate, first admission date, first registration in this country, document numbers | Defines the official identity and the documents needed to transfer | Once, then on re-registration |
   | **Technical** | Make, trade name, type-approval number, variant and version, mass, fuel, emissions, list price | Basis for tax, road access and type-approval checks | Rarely: conversions, corrections |
   | **Inspection** | Periodic roadworthiness test expiry (*APK* in the Netherlands, *Algemene Periodieke Keuring*; the MOT test in the UK) and results | Decides whether the car may be driven | Yearly or every two years |
   | **Status** | Exported, scrapped, stolen, awaiting inspection after serious damage, insured, transfer possible, open recall | Blocks or permits transactions and road use | Unpredictable; minutes matter for theft |
   | **Odometer judgement** | The register's verdict on the series of recorded readings (Dutch *tellerstandoordeel*), or readings at each test | Evidence in sales and fraud cases | With every reading |
   | **Ownership change** | Date of the last change of registered owner or keeper; keeper identity | Transfers liability for tax, insurance and fines | With every sale |
   | **Open data** | Bulk vehicle facts without personal data | None beyond the licence terms; no availability guarantee | RDW open data, CC0 licence; NHTSA's vPIC decoder | May be mirrored and resold; never the only source for time-critical status |
   | **Paid business access** | Per-vehicle non-sensitive data, sometimes with status the open data omits | Registered business, credential, per-lookup fee; often "per plate, not in bulk" | RDW's paid business lookup; DVLA's Vehicle Enquiry Service | A live call at the moment of decision; caching limited by terms |
   | **Provider and transaction access** | The right to send official transactions and receive their outcomes | Recognition of the legal actor, a certificate, contractual terms, audits | Dutch providers; Germany's large-customer interface; US electronic title programs | An operational dependency with legal identity attached to every message |
   | **Purpose-bound personal-data access** | Keeper name and address | A statutory purpose, shown per request; logs kept for years | German §39 of the Road Traffic Act (StVG); DVLA "reasonable cause"; permissible uses under the US Driver's Privacy Protection Act (DPPA) | Kept out of products by default; isolated, logged and purpose-tagged when used |
   **Open data** is "free and without restriction" for every user and excludes data that is privacy-, fraud- or competition-sensitive; stolen and missing status is not released ([RDW, n.d.](https://www.rdw.nl/over-rdw/dienstverlening/open-data/algemene-informatie)).
   - **The legal actor is the dealer, not the provider.** A dealer needs a basic recognition (*basiserkenning*, which requires a certificate of conduct and a Chamber of Commerce entry) and then specific recognitions such as trade stock (*bedrijfsvoorraad*) or registration in a customer's name.
   - **The certificate is the key.** Whether through a provider or directly, the dealer needs an RDW-issued certificate, "as it were the key to RDW's electronic front door".
   - **The register checks every transaction against its own state.** On a stock-in, the system checks the recognition, the company number, the data sent and the signals on the vehicle in the register.
   **Axleby's position, like any provider's, is a seat, not a licence to everything**: it reaches the register on behalf of others, and what it may do with what passes through is narrower than what it can technically see.
   The **register gateway** is the one component through which every read from and write to an official register passes.
   The **register mirror** is a local, queryable copy of the register data the platform may hold in bulk, with the source dataset, its version and the observation time on each fact.
   The **mutation feed** is a stream of changes to register records.
   | **Channel adapters** | Speak each register's protocol (bulk download, XML lookup, transaction messages, REST with OAuth) and translate to one internal model | Protocol mapping, verbatim request and response capture | Everything below |
   | **Snapshot differ and mirror loader** | Load bulk snapshots, guard against partial files, compute field-level changes | The mirror, snapshot versions | Mirror, mutation feed |
   | **Register mirror** | Serve plate and VIN lookups from bulk-permitted data with `observed_at` per fact | Mirrored register facts | JSON API, Connect XML, Lotwise, Odolo, Specbook |
   | **Mutation feed** | Publish `register-observed-change` events | Event schema and ordering per plate | Anchor, Tracewell, webhooks ([[events-webhooks-and-sync]]) |
   | **Live lookup broker** | Ask the register at decision time; apply quotas and priority; classify answers | Answer classes, quota budgets | Purchase gate, history assembler, transaction pre-checks |
   | **Transaction relay** | Submit official transactions under the dealer's credential and track outcomes | Transport and outcome capture; the business state machine stays in Axleby Trade | Axleby Trade back office ([[registration-and-trade-services]]) |
   | **Credential, session and quota monitor** | Track expiry, renewal, session health and quota use per credential | Credential register | SRE, partner integrations, dealers |
   | **Purpose check and audit** | Evaluate (legal actor, user, product, purpose) against the source's terms; record every read and write | Calls Charter ([[data-licensing-and-governance]]) | Compliance, register audits |
   - **Freshness is measured, not assumed.** Turnstile tracks, per field, how many changes arrive for each past date over the following days, and publishes the lag it observes as the mirror's freshness level ([[provenance-and-data-quality]] owns freshness SLOs).
   - **Diff events are not legal events.** An owner-change event derived from snapshots is evidence that a sale happened in a window.
   | **Duplicate snapshot load** | The same day's file is downloaded twice after a retry | Snapshot version already recorded | Events are idempotent on (plate, attribute, source version); the second load emits nothing |
   | **Truncated or partial snapshot** | A download stops at 11 million rows | Row count outside ±0.5% of the previous day *(assumption)*; schema or checksum mismatch | Quarantine; keep the previous snapshot live; mark the mirror's freshness as one day older |
   | **Mass change from a source change** | The register renames a field or changes a value vocabulary; every row "changes" | Change volume per field beyond a threshold | Hold the feed; map the new vocabulary; emit no events for a cosmetic change |
   | **Missed delta** | A DVSA daily delta is not available at 8am and is skipped | Sequence gap in applied deltas | Reload from the next Sunday bulk; mark affected MOT facts with their true age |
   | **Conflict between mirror and live answer** | The mirror says transfer possible; the live call says blocked | Divergence logged on every live call | Live answer wins for the decision; divergence rate is a mirror-quality metric |
   | **Incomplete records** | Open data reads "not provided in Open Data" or "not registered"; no VIN; imports with "no judgement" | Field-level null classes | Store the null class, not an empty value; show "not available from this source", never "none" |
   | **Ambiguous transaction timeout** | The register committed, the confirmation was lost | Timeout on the relay | Query state before resubmitting; the 214 double submissions of 31 March 2027 came from skipping this |
   | **Information channel outage** | Live status calls time out | Answer class "Unavailable" rises; probe fails | Screens use the mirror with age shown; purchase decisions wait; status published |
   | **Transaction channel outage** | Month-End Register Outage, 5 h 40 min | Session monitor; probe | Queue in order; never complete locally; the register forbids registering during a disruption |
   | **Credential expiry or revocation** | A dealer's certificate fails to renew; a DVSA secret expires | Credential register | Alert the dealer or the owning team before expiry; block that dealer's submissions with a clear reason |
   | **Quota exhaustion** | A batch drains the MOT quota; key restricted for 24 hours | Quota use per lane | Batch lane throttled first; mirror serves screens; never retry into a restriction |
   | **Access regime change** | A channel closes to new registrations; provider terms change | Contract owner; terms watch | Encode the change in Charter; plan the fallback before it is needed |
   | **Pass-through only**: every lookup goes live | Best | Highest per lookup; quota-bound | None: Borrowed Uptime | Low | Low | Low volume, no bulk regime, decision-time use only |
   | **Open-data mirror plus live calls for blocking facts** (Axleby's choice) | Screen: days; decision: live | Low for screens; paid only at decisions | Screens and products survive outages | Low if purpose-tagged | High: feed, files, webhooks | A bulk-open register exists and decisions are a small share of lookups |
   | **Mirror everything, including paid per-vehicle answers** | Looks fresh, ages silently | Low per lookup | High | **High**: per-plate access is "not in bulk" | High but fragile | Only where the terms explicitly allow retention and resale |
   | **Buy register data from another provider** | As the provider's | Provider margin per call | Provider's | Shared, but still the platform's duty | Low | Entering a market without a provider seat |
   **Integrating with an existing provider** is faster, but it puts a competitor in the path of every transaction, with that provider's outage behavior and pricing.
   | **Register body** | NL: RDW, national base register.
   | **Bulk vehicle data** | NL: open data, CC0, daily, plate-keyed, no VIN.
   | **Per-vehicle business access** | NL: paid lookup, per plate, with theft and export status | §39 StVG for legal claims only | Vehicle Enquiry Service (closed to new registrations, Oct 2026); MOT API | Approved NMVTIS providers; state contracts |
   | **Personal data** | NL: never for commercial use.
   | **Change feed** | NL: none; diff snapshots.
   | **Transaction channel** | NL: synchronous messages via providers under dealer recognition and certificate.
   | **What the mirror can hold** | NL: almost all vehicle facts.
===== specification-and-equipment 52
   | **Total** | **28.5 million** | **41%** repurchases of a VIN bought before; **6%** concurrent duplicates |
   | **Make** | The brand | Volkswagen | Provider make ids; World Manufacturer Identifier (WMI, VIN positions 1–3) at manufacturer level |
   | **Model** | The commercial model line | Golf | Provider model ids |
   | **Generation** (model series) | A design period of the model | Golf 8, 2020 onward | German *Baureihe*; Auto Trader "generation" |
   | **Body** | Body style and doors | Variant (estate), 5 doors | Body code |
   | **Version** (derivative) | Engine, power, gearbox, drivetrain, and usually the edition | 1.5 eTSI 110 kW DSG Style | cap Code (UK), German type key numbers (HSN/TSN), Schwacke code, Auto Trader derivative id, provider version ids |
   | **Edition (trim)** | The named equipment level the manufacturer sells | Life, Style, R-Line | Often part of the version; sometimes a separate line (*Ausstattungslinie*) |
   | **Factory option and pack** | Equipment ordered on top of the edition, alone or in a bundle | Panoramic roof; a driver-assistance pack | Manufacturer option codes, provider feature ids |
   | **As-built** | What one VIN actually received | This car: Style, panoramic roof, towbar, metallic paint | Per-VIN build record |
   - An **edition (trim)** is the named equipment level of a version: the standard content the manufacturer bundled under a marketing name.
   - A **factory option** is an item of equipment fitted at the factory on top of the edition's standard content, ordered alone or in a **pack** whose content can change over time.
   - The **specification catalog** is the platform's versioned reference data of makes, models, generations, versions, editions, standard equipment and available options, with the crosswalk to every provider's and portal's keys.
   - **One provider can return two layers.** DAT (Deutsche Automobil Treuhand) Austria's March 2026 guide to its SilverDAT VIN query names about 40 makes whose equipment comes live "from the connected manufacturer databases", and a second group (including Honda, Suzuki and Tesla) whose equipment comes from DAT's own manufacturer-standardized database ([DAT Austria, 2026](https://www.datgroup.com/fileadmin/at/VIN_Abfrage
   - **Products separate the layers commercially.** JATO describes VINView as catalog-level standard specification from a VIN or UK registration mark, and VINView Pro as showing "what was actually installed at the factory", available in eight European markets including the Netherlands, Belgium, Germany and the UK ([JATO, n.d., accessed 2026-10](https://www.jato.com/our-solutions/vinview)).
   - **Some providers state their confidence.** The US developer API auto.dev sells "OEM Build Data" with factory option codes and "a match confidence score" at $0.10 per call on its Growth plan and $0.08 on Scale ([auto.dev, n.d., accessed 2026-10](https://www.auto.dev/oem-build-data)).
   | **Fact store by layer** | Holds every specification fact with layer, source, source key, retrieval time, licence class and provider confidence | Register, catalog, as-built and asserted facts per vehicle key | Identity (owned by the identity graph) |
   | **Specification catalog and crosswalk** | Versioned reference data and the mapping between provider keys (provider version ids, HSN/TSN, cap Code, type approval, variant and version) and internal catalog ids | Catalog versions, standard content, option and pack definitions, generic feature taxonomy | Per-VIN facts |
   | **Resolver** | Produces candidates, scores them, applies precedence per attribute, sets the resolution state and writes an explanation | The resolved view, versioned by rule set and catalog version | The decision to pay |
   | **Purchase gate** | Decides whether to buy as-built data for this request, from the decision context, candidate value spread, coverage and budget | Purchase policy and the request ledger | Prices to clients (owned by [[billing-and-metering]]) |
   | **Build-data adapters** | One per provider: request, idempotency key, coverage check, response normalization, cost record | Provider contracts as code | Licence decisions (owned by Charter, see [[data-licensing-and-governance]]) |
   | **Dealer confirmation** | Captures dealer answers to specific questions as asserted facts with author and time | Confirmations | Overwriting provider facts |
   | **Portal mappers** | Translate catalog equipment ids into each portal's codes | Mapping tables per portal and version | Channel delivery (owned by [[listing-syndication]]) |
   The **resolver never writes into the fact layers**: a resolved view is a derived product, rebuildable when rules or the catalog change.
   | **Identified** | The vehicle key is known; nothing resolved yet | Nothing; transient |
   | **Candidates** | Technically possible versions are known | Show make, model, engine; ask for nothing yet |
   | **Edition unknown** | Several editions remain and no as-built data exists | Valuation uses the candidate range and widens its interval; the advert omits edition and options; the dealer gets one question |
   | **Edition resolved** | One edition, from a unique candidate or as-built match | Standard equipment can be shown; options still unknown unless confirmed |
   | **Dealer-confirmed** | The dealer answered the resolver's question | Shown with "dealer-supplied" wording; valuation uses it with lower confidence than as-built |
   | **As-built confirmed** | Edition and factory options from the build record | Full equipment list; highest confidence |
   | **Disputed** | Sources contradict each other on an attribute that matters | Products show the last agreed view and flag it; a data-operations queue reviews it |
   - **Option value is uneven.** Most options add little to a used car's price; a few (panoramic roofs, larger batteries, higher-power versions, certain driver-assistance packs) add a lot.
   - **Option value commonly decays with age and mileage.** An option that adds a meaningful share of value to a three-year-old car may add little to a twelve-year-old one. [[valuation-engine]] owns the adjustment models; the resolver supplies the option list and its confidence.
   - **Edition errors are asymmetric.** Overstating the edition in an advert creates a misdescription the dealer is liable for; understating it in an appraisal leaves margin on the table or loses the trade-in to a competitor.
   - **mobile.de** describes equipment as named fields with reference-data values, separate fields for model range (*Baureihe*) and trim line ("a.k.a feature sets (Ausstattungslinie)"), a free-text `modelDescription` used as the advert title, German HSN/TSN fields, and a Schwacke code.
   - **AutoScout24** takes equipment as an array of integer ids from its References API, with mutual-exclusion rules: only one automatic climate control variant per listing.
   - **Auto Trader UK** uses its derivative taxonomy and features with OEM factory codes, as above.
   - **Marktplaats** enriches car adverts with a valid plate "with data from a third party partner after posting", asynchronously, and lets dealer-supplied values take precedence ([Marktplaats API, n.d.](https://api.marktplaats.nl/docs/v1/advertisement.html)).
   | **Duplicate purchases**: two products request the same VIN at once, or a timeout leads to a retry | Request ledger keyed by AVK, provider and licence scope; reconcile against the provider's monthly statement | Single-flight lock per key; retries reuse the idempotency key; duplicates found at reconciliation go to the provider as credit claims |
   | **Conflict between layers**: as-built says 96 kW, the register says 110 kW | Precedence rules detect disagreement on attributes both layers carry | Register wins for the legal value; the record moves to **Disputed** and data operations check for a wrong VIN, a conversion or a provider error |
   | **Wrong vehicle**: build data for a different car | As-built make, model or first-registration window contradicts register facts | Treat as an identity problem first ([[vehicle-identity]]); never attach the record; the Cloned Plate is the precedent |
   | **Foreign specification**: a re-import built for another market | Build data or the provider's market flag shows a non-domestic build; catalog candidates do not match | Resolve against the source market's catalog; label equipment names as source-market names; adverts say "imported" |
   | **Incomplete record**: catalog-only answer, null `factoryFitted`, unknown pack content | Result class and per-attribute nulls | State stays **Edition unknown** or **Edition resolved**; never convert "unknown" into "not equipped" |
   | **Provider outage or slow response** | Adapter timeouts and error rates per provider | Return the catalog-level answer with candidates within the latency budget; queue the as-built upgrade; products update when it arrives |
   | **Catalog correction**: a provider reissues a version's standard content | Catalog version diff | Re-resolve affected VINs; emit specification-changed events; valuations and adverts that used the old view republish |
   | **Dealer overrides the evidence**: picks a higher edition than the as-built record | Confirmation contradicts as-built | Keep both; the advert uses the as-built edition unless data operations accept the dealer's evidence (a conversion, a mis-keyed build record) |
   | **Dealer picks from the catalog** | Plate gives model and engine; dealer chooses edition and ticks options | Cheap; fast; no supplier cost | Spec Guesswork at scale; liability for misdescription; valuations inherit guesses | Very small platforms, or markets and ages where option value is negligible |
   | **Buy as-built for every lookup** | Every resolution calls the build-data provider | Highest coverage of options where build data exists | Cost grows with lookups, not cars; still catalog-only for uncovered makes; Borrowed Uptime on the provider | High-value segments, or when the client pays per as-built call |
   | **One provider's resolved answer as truth** | Store the provider's version and equipment as the record | Simple; one contract | Lock-in to its keys; its catalog/as-built mix is hidden; no losing values | Single-market products with one dominant provider |
   | **Layered resolver with purchase gate** (recommended) | Register and catalog narrow; gate buys as-built for decisions; one record per VIN | Pays per car and per decision; explainable; provider-neutral | Needs a catalog, crosswalk, licence negotiation and a data-operations team | Multi-product groups where several products reuse the same car's specification |
   **What is common**: the layered fact model, the catalog with a crosswalk, the resolver and its states, the purchase gate, the licence-aware store and the mapping pipeline.
   **What is a market adapter**: the input key (plate in the Netherlands and the UK, VIN in Belgium and the US, VIN plus HSN/TSN in Germany), the register adapter and the facts it can supply, the provider set and its coverage list, and the portal mappers.
   **What is a rule set**: the purchase-gate thresholds.
===== vehicle-history 56
   A **history event** is one dated observation about one vehicle, from one source: "on 22 May 2025, an inspection recorded 168,950 km"; "on 3 March 2022, an insurer declared the vehicle a total loss"; "on 9 September 2024, the vehicle was first registered in the Netherlands after import from Germany".
   | **Odometer reading** | A distance shown on a given date | Inspections, registers, statutory mileage registries, workshops, manufacturers, auctions, adverts, appraisals | Permanent; may be corrected | Low |
   | **Inspection** | A periodic test with result, defects and reading | APK (NL), MOT (UK annual roadworthiness test), *Hauptuntersuchung* (DE) | Permanent | Low |
   | **Damage or claim** | A loss, estimate, repair or damage status | Insurers, repairers, auctions, damage-data aggregators, registers' damage statuses | Permanent; often incomplete | Low |
   | **Title brand or write-off** | An official or industry label on the vehicle's legal status | US state titling agencies via NMVTIS; UK insurers' write-off categories | Permanent by design | Low |
   | **Theft** | Reported stolen, or recovered | Police systems, registers, commercial theft checks | Reversible | **High** |
   | **Registration change** | First registration, owner change, import, export, plate change | Registers | Permanent | Medium |
   | **Recall** | A campaign applies, or a remedy was done | Regulators, manufacturers, register flags | Status changes | Medium |
   | **Service** | Maintenance or repair performed | Manufacturer digital service records, workshops, statutory registries | Permanent | Low |
   | **Commercial observation** | Listed, appraised, auctioned, sold, with mileage and photos | The platform's own dealer SaaS, portals, auctions | Permanent | Low |
   - **Official** sources record under a legal duty and are audited: a register, an inspection body, a statutory mileage registry such as Belgium's Car-Pass.
   - **Manufacturer** sources record from the car or the franchised network: connected-car odometer transmissions, digital service books.
   - **Commercial** sources record for their own business: insurers, repairers, auctions, portals, aggregators such as Axleby's fictional supplier Kilometra.
   - **First-party** sources are the platform's own users: a dealer's appraisal, a stock-in, an advert's mileage field.
   An **odometer reading** is an event.
   An **odometer judgement** is a conclusion computed over the series of readings: is the series consistent with a vehicle that was driven normally and never had its odometer turned back?
   A **title brand** is a permanent label on a vehicle's legal status, such as salvage, flood, rebuilt, junk or "not actual mileage".
   | **Source connectors** | One per source: authentication, quotas, parsing, unit normalization, mapping to event types, licence class | Source contracts and raw deliveries | Which vehicle an event belongs to (Anchor decides) |
   | **History event store** | Append-only events per vehicle key, with source, dates, licence and corrections | History events | Person data; specification; valuations |
   | **Judgement engine** | Computes odometer judgement, damage summary, brand summary and recall status from events, per rule version | Derived views and their rule versions | The register's own judgements, which it stores as events |
   | **Assembler** | Serves a request: reads events, runs live checks, applies entitlement and licence filters, builds the timeline | Request-time composition and the hit class | Rendering rules per product |
   | **Report renderer and snapshot store** | Renders the report for a product and market, stores inputs and output under a report id | History reports | Billing; delivery to clients |
   - **The isolated outlier.** One reading sits above both neighbors, and the series is monotonic without it.
   - **The unit artifact.** A reading in miles stored as kilometers is about 0.62 of the true value; the reverse is 1.61 times.
   - **The country hop.** The last reading abroad is higher than the first reading at home.
   - **The sustained step-down.** Readings fall and the following readings continue from the lower value, from independent sources.
   A **history report** is a rendered, dated snapshot of a vehicle's events and judgements for one product, market and requester.
   - **Defensibility.** A dealer can show a buyer, or a court, exactly what the platform knew at sale time.
   - **Correction reach.** When an event is corrected, the store can list every report that used it.
   - **Supersession.** A corrected report is a new version with a link to the old one, never an edit in place.
   | **Hit** | At least one event beyond the basic register facts, in a section the product sells | Yes |
   | **Partial** | Some sections answered, at least one paid section unavailable because a source failed | Yes, with the gap stated; credit on request *(policy choice)* |
   | **No hit** | Vehicle resolved, no events beyond basic register facts in any sold section | No |
   | **Failed** | Vehicle not resolved, or the assembly failed | No |
   | **Duplicate events** | The same reading or claim arrives from a register and an aggregator, or twice from one source with different dates | Dedup key (AVK, type, value, date window); per-source idempotency on `source_ref` | Collapse into one event with several attestations; never count twice in a rule |
   | **Event on the wrong vehicle** | Clone or mis-keyed plate attaches another car's damage | Implausible series (two mileage tracks, two colors, two locations); identity split in Anchor | Re-attach events to the corrected AVK; recompute views; supersede affected reports |
   | **Conflicting events** | Register says "logical", Tracewell says "rollback suspected"; insurer total loss, later clean registration | Disagreement checks between official and derived judgements | Show both with sources; never overwrite an official event; brands stay permanent |
   | **Incomplete history** | Import with no foreign readings; source covers only some years | Coverage map per source and country | State the gap; judgement "not assessable" for that period |
   | **Unit or format error in a batch** | Readings off by a factor of 1.609 across many cars from one delivery | Ratio checks against neighbors; per-batch anomaly rate | Quarantine the batch; retract its events; recompute and supersede |
   | **Retries and late arrivals** | A source redelivers, or an older reading arrives after a report | `recorded_at` after report time | Events are idempotent; judgements recompute; reports are superseded only when a judgement changed |
   | **Partial failure in fan-out** | One live source times out | Per-source timeout and error metrics | Retry once; render with stated gap or decline; hit class reflects it |
   | **Source outage or expired credentials** | A supplier's feed stops; an API key is revoked after 90 days unused, as the UK MOT history API does | Freshness SLO per source; credential-expiry monitoring | Serve stored events with gaps marked; never imply recency the store does not have |
   | **Correction from the register** | The RDW corrects an earlier reading after a documented request, within ten working days of approval | Register mutation feed | Append a correction event; recompute; supersede affected reports |
   | **Pass-through assembly** (query every source per request, store only the report) | Freshest; simplest licensing; no long-term storage of licensed data | Availability is the product of all sources; cost per report includes every upstream call; no cross-source judgement over time; corrections cannot be traced | A small product reselling one or two sources |
   | **Event store** (ingest and store every event) | Judgements over the whole life; fast; survives outages; corrections and supersession possible | Needs storage rights for every source; ingestion and matching cost; risk of serving stale volatile facts | Sources that license storage; the platform's own first-party data; long-lived judgements |
   | **Hybrid** (store what the licence allows; query live what is volatile or licensed per call) | Matches each source's licence and volatility; stored events answer during outages | Two code paths; coverage and hit class must merge both; harder to test | Most multi-source history services, Axleby included |
   | **Register judgement only** | Authoritative; legally recognized; no false accusations from the platform | Blind to commercial, foreign and first-party readings; not available outside NL | Products that must not add interpretation |
   | **Own judgement only** | Uses all readings; works in every market | Competes with the register's; its errors are the platform's | Markets without an official judgement |
   | **Both, labeled** | Combines authority and coverage; differences are explained | More complex wording; needs governance of disagreements | Markets with an official judgement and rich commercial data |
   | **Official odometer record** | NL: RDW, mandatory registration since 1 January 2014; takes over Belgian (2025) and German (2026) readings for imports.
   | **Buyer-facing odometer document** | NL: register judgement per plate, free vehicle report for the holder.
   | **Damage and total loss** | Mostly commercial and incomplete; register damage status (WOK) not in open data | Insurance notices in the central register (ZFZR); no public damage history found | Insurer write-off categories via paid checks | Title brands from states; insurers report total losses to NMVTIS monthly |
   | **Theft** | NL: in paid business register access, not in open data | Theft notices in ZFZR | Police National Computer via commercial checks | Sometimes in NMVTIS reports; commercial checks |
   | **Recalls** | NL: open-recall indicator per plate in open data | No public per-vehicle recall flag found in this research | MOT history may show recalls | OEM VIN lookup free for 15 years (49 CFR 573.15) |
   | **Main history gap** | Imports: years abroad | No odometer register for buyers; damage data commercial | Write-off and finance data behind paid checks | Unreported accidents; state brand vocabularies differ |
   | **Report expectation** | NL buyers expect the register's judgement; BE buyers expect a Car-Pass | Commercial reports; dealer warranties | Finance, write-off and theft checks with guarantee | Title brands, accident records, a history score |
===== ingestion-and-entity-resolution 69
   **Ingestion** is how those facts enter the platform; **entity resolution** is how the platform decides which physical car each fact is about; **survivorship** is how it decides, attribute by attribute, which fact to believe.
   | **Register mutation feed** | Dutch register changes that Turnstile derives from daily snapshots; UK MOT (annual roadworthiness test) daily deltas | Change events, ordered per plate, at the source's daily rhythm | Plate; registration dates | A missed or partial snapshot; changes between two snapshots are invisible |
   | **Register bulk data** | The Dutch register's open dataset of registered vehicles | Full dataset, refreshed by the publisher | Plate; no VIN | No guarantee of availability or currency; schema changes without notice |
   | **Partner files** | Kilometra readings; insurer damage files | Nightly or weekly files, full or delta | VIN usually; sometimes only a foreign plate | Semantic drift (units, codes), duplicates, truncated files |
   | **Partner APIs on demand** | Build data per VIN for Specbook (about **€0.46** a request); UK MOT history by plate or VIN | Request and response | VIN or plate in the request | Per-call cost, quotas, outages; licence limits on caching |
   | **Adverts** | Pulse: about **2.3 million** harvested or portal-fed adverts a day | Daily snapshots or portal feeds | Often no VIN; plate sometimes masked | Duplicates across portals; dealer-typed specifications |
   | **Dealer and user input** | Lotwise stock-ins (**1.9 million** a year), DMS stock exports, dealer corrections, Odolo complaints | Interactive writes; periodic DMS files | Plate typed or scanned; VIN when the dealer has it | Typos; options claimed, not evidenced |
   | **Ingestion pipeline** | The path every external fact takes into the platform: receive, land raw, validate, normalize, resolve to a vehicle, store as a per-source record, and publish a change.
   | **Landing zone** | Immutable storage of exactly what arrived (file, message or API response), with a batch or message id, a content hash, the arrival time and the source.
   | **Quarantine** | A holding area for records or whole batches that failed a contract check.
   | **Source record** | One source's statement about one vehicle at one time, normalized, linked to its landing batch and to the vehicle it was resolved to. |
   | **Entity resolution** | Deciding which real-world entity, here a physical vehicle identified by its Axleby Vehicle Key (AVK), a source record is about.
   | **Match decision** | The stored outcome of resolution for one source record: the AVK, the rule or model and version that decided, the score, and who confirmed it if a human did. |
   | **Survivorship rule** | A per-attribute rule that chooses the current value from competing source records: precedence by source class, recency, plausibility and tie-breakers, with a version number. |
   | **Golden record** | The resolved current view of one vehicle: for each attribute, the surviving value plus a pointer to the source record it came from.
   - **Batch**: a bounded set of records (a file, a dataset snapshot) arrives and is processed as a unit, with a natural "complete" signal: the manifest says 410,212 rows, and 410,212 rows were read.
   - **Micro-batch**: small batches on a short schedule, typical of dealer management system (DMS) stock exports every 15 minutes that must be diffed against the previous snapshot to find what changed.
   - **Event-driven**: change events are pushed as they happen (a register change, a price edit, a lead).
   - **Request-time lookup**: the platform asks the source at the moment a decision needs the fact (build data for one VIN, a theft check before purchase).
   | **Freshness** | Bounded by the schedule: a nightly file gives data 2–26 hours old *(assumption)* | Seconds to minutes after the source changes | The product's need **and the source's own freshness**: a stream over a source that updates nightly is still nightly |
   | **Volume** | Efficient for large, periodic volumes: 410,000 readings in one 40-minute load | Efficient for steady, small changes: Lotwise's 1.9 million stock-ins a year, plus their edits, arrive one at a time through the working day | Peak shape matters more than total: month-end peaks in register transactions, about 4× the daily average, raise change volume and need consumer headroom |
   | **Cost** | Low fixed cost: object storage plus a scheduled job; supplier files are often in the base licence | Always-on consumers and broker capacity; suppliers may charge more (Kilometra's quote: €95,000 a year) | Marginal value of the freshness gained, per product |
   | **Replay** | Natural: keep the file and reload it.
   | **Completeness check** | Easy: manifest row count, checksum, expected arrival time | Hard: gaps need sequence numbers per partition and periodic reconciliation against a snapshot | Whether the source can provide sequence numbers or snapshots |
   | **Ordering** | Within the file, by observation date; across files, by batch | Per key if partitioned by vehicle; across partitions, none | Whether consumers need per-vehicle order (yes, for odometer and status) |
   | **Blast radius of a bad source** | One batch, held as a unit | A trickle, already consumed by the time a pattern is visible | Whether plausibility checks need the whole set (unit drift is visible as a pattern, not in one record) |
   | **Delivery** | Manifest row count matches rows read; file sequence has no gap; arrived before 04:00 | Batch | Hold the whole batch; alert the integration owner |
   | **Schema** | Columns, types, required fields, code lists (`unit` in {km, mi}) | Record | Quarantine the record |
   | **Semantics** | Reading not lower than the previous official reading; reading not above a plausible ceiling for the vehicle's age; VIN check-sum valid where the VIN format has one | Record, compared with history | Quarantine the record; flag as a rollback candidate only if the source is official |
   | **Distribution** | Median reading per age band and origin within 0.85–1.15 of the trailing 30 days (the Kilometra **batch gate**, [[observability-and-data-operations]]); share of records failing semantic checks per supplying workshop chain below a threshold; volume within the usual range | Batch segment | Hold the segment; release the rest |
   - **Identifiers**: VINs to upper case, with spaces and separators removed.
   - **Units and quantities**: odometer to kilometers with the original value and unit kept; power to kilowatts; prices to amount, currency, and whether VAT is included.
   - **Codes and catalogs**: fuel, body, color and damage types map to Axleby's code lists; make, model and version strings map to Specbook's catalog ([[specification-and-equipment]]) where possible and stay unmapped, with a reason, where not.
   - **Time**: every record gets an **observation time** (when the fact was true or observed, as the source states it) separate from the **ingestion time**.
   **Normalization must never destroy information**: the Odometer Correction is exactly the case where someone later needs to know what the source actually said.
   **Dealers and rooftops** are matched across portal accounts, DMS ids and company numbers ([[tenancy-and-dealer-groups]]); **consumers** in leads are constrained by the General Data Protection Regulation (GDPR) and belong to [[leads-and-crm]].
   - **Single-valued attributes** (first-registration date, fuel type, current odometer, edition, color) have one current value.
   - **Event sets** (damage events, inspections, ownership changes, readings) accumulate.
   The **golden record** is the result of applying these rules to all source records of one vehicle.
   - **Rules can change.** When version 3 replaced version 2, Inlet recomputed the golden record for all 41 million vehicles in a background job and emitted change events only where values changed.
   - **Sources can be withdrawn.** A superseded batch or an ended licence marks source records inactive, and the affected golden records are recomputed.
   - **Disputes can be answered.** Why does the platform say the car was first registered in 2018?
   - **Auto Trader UK** described in August 2026 an in-house customer data platform built in 2021 that ingests behavioral events through Kafka.
   - **AutoScout24** described DataRunner in September 2023 as built on AWS Glue, Athena, Airflow and PySpark, integrated with DataHub and Great Expectations, to support "decentralized ownership" and "data as product" ([AutoScout24, 2023](https://tech.autoscout24.com/blog/posts/datarunner/)).
   - **Cox Automotive** chose an identity graph on Amazon Neptune in 2019 for consumer "householding and lead mapping".
   **A distribution check is only as sharp as its segments**: the narrower the segment matches how a source can fail (one export, one workshop chain), the smaller the error it can see.
   **The outcome**: an alert at about 02:50, a redelivery the next night, and no valuation, report or advert reading the wrong mileage.
   | **Duplicate delivery** (same file twice, stream redelivery) | Double counts; repeated events in history | Content hash on landing; idempotency key on source records | Ignore the duplicate; no downstream effect |
   | **Truncated or partial file** | Fewer vehicles updated; missing readings | Manifest row count and checksum | Hold the batch; request redelivery; earlier data stays current |
   | **Missing batch** | Nothing arrives; stale data that looks fine | Freshness check per source against the expected arrival time | Alert at the SLO, not at job failure; products show the age of the data |
   | **Schema drift** (new column, renamed code) | Records fail schema checks | Schema layer | Quarantine; update the adapter; replay from landing |
   | **Semantic drift** (unit, code meaning, scale) | Plausible-looking wrong values | Semantic and distribution layers | Hold the segment; supplier redelivers; supersede, do not edit |
   | **Conflicting sources** | Two values for one attribute | Survivorship sees both | Rule decides; the loser is kept and shown where the conflict matters to a buyer |
   | **Incomplete records** (no VIN, no date) | Records cannot be resolved or ordered | Resolution tier 5; missing observation time | Keep unattached; retry when Anchor changes; never invent a date from arrival time without marking it |
   | **Wrong merge** (two cars as one) | Contradictory history; a complaint | Identity conflict checks; audits of match decisions | Reverse the match decisions; re-resolve; recompute affected golden records; reissue reports ([[vehicle-identity]]) |
   | **Source outage** (a snapshot not published, or Turnstile's feed stalled) | Gap in the mutation feed | Missing snapshot version; heartbeat missing | Keep the previous day with its age shown; the next snapshot diff catches up; a full reconciliation against the snapshot finds what the feed missed |
   | **Retry storm or backfill overload** | Live ingestion slows behind a replay | Lag per source; queue depth | Separate replay capacity from live capacity; rate-limit backfills |
   **Reconciliation against a snapshot** is what makes an event-driven feed trustworthy.
   | **Overwrite in place** | Loaders update one current table | Simplest; fast reads; no extra storage | No replay; no explanation; corrections are patches; source withdrawal impossible | Never for data that is sold or that drives prices; acceptable for internal caches that can be rebuilt from elsewhere |
   | **Per-source records plus computed golden view** | All source records kept; golden record recomputed by rules | Explainable; reversible; rules can change; supports bitemporal history | More storage; recompute cost; rule governance needed | The default for vehicle facts that feed valuations, reports, adverts or contracts |
   | **No golden record** (consumers resolve) | Consumers read source records and choose | Maximum flexibility; no central rules | Every product resolves differently; adverts, reports and valuations disagree (**Spec Guesswork**) | Only for analytics teams studying the sources themselves |
   | **Deterministic only** (VIN, plate-at-date) | Explainable; no false merges from scoring | Leaves adverts and dealer records without VIN unresolved | Sources that drive history and reports |
   | **Probabilistic for all** | Highest match rate | False merges inside the history of real cars; costly reviews | Never as the first tier for vehicles |
   | **Tiered** (deterministic first, probabilistic for the remainder, review band) | Match rate where it is cheap to be wrong, certainty where it is not | Two mechanisms to govern; thresholds to tune | The default; set thresholds per consumer, stricter for Tracewell than for Pulse |
   | **Register data shape** | NL: the RDW (Netherlands Vehicle Authority) publishes an open bulk dataset under CC0 without VIN, plus paid per-plate business access (*OVI-zakelijk*) at €0.07–0.18 a lookup; BE: no open bulk register examined | Central register at the Kraftfahrt-Bundesamt (KBA, the federal motor transport authority) not open for individual vehicles | DVLA bulk anonymized data under contract with "reasonab
   | **Odometer sources** | NL: register judgement per plate, not readings; BE: Car-Pass, the statutory mileage register, which received 345.5 million readings in 2025 | Inspection readings at the KBA, not public; commercial sources | Driver and Vehicle Standards Agency (DVSA) history of MOT (annual roadworthiness test) readings by API: 500,000 requests a day, 15 a second on average | Title odometer statements via NMVTI
   | **Usual matching key** | Plate (a Dutch plate usually stays with the car); type-approval number, variant and version | HSN/TSN (*Herstellerschlüsselnummer* and *Typschlüsselnummer*, manufacturer and type key numbers) for the model; plates change on re-registration | Plate, but personalized and retained plates move between cars | VIN, present almost everywhere |
   | **Resolution difficulty** | Low for domestic cars; high for imports | Moderate: VIN often present in commercial data, plate unreliable | High for plate-only sources because plates move | Low for VIN-bearing sources |
   | **Natural ingestion mode** | Snapshot diffs turned into a change feed, plus live lookups at decision time | Partner files and request-time lookups | API lookups within quota; partner files | Partner and aggregator files; request-time lookups |
===== provenance-and-data-quality 65
   | **Provenance** | "Information about entities, activities, and people involved in producing a piece of data or thing, which can be used to form assessments about its quality, reliability or trustworthiness" (W3C PROV data model, [2013](https://www.w3.org/TR/prov-dm/)).
   | **Provenance envelope** | The fields stored with every fact, as part of the fact: source, source class, source record and batch, observed-at, recorded-at, licence, confidence, derivation, status and version. |
   | **Fact version** | One immutable version of one fact.
   | **Valid time** | When the fact was true in the world: the date the odometer showed 41,220 miles.
   | **Transaction time** | When the platform recorded, and so believed, that version of the fact.
   | **Bitemporal fact** | A fact stored with both times, so the platform can answer what was true at any date, as known at any date. |
   | **Freshness SLO** | A target for the age of a fact at the moment a decision uses it, set per data class and decision context, with a defined behavior when it is breached. |
   | **Data contract (vehicle)** | The agreement with a data supplier, part commercial and part machine-readable, that fixes the schema, the semantics (units, codes, time zones), the provenance fields the supplier must deliver, delivery times, quality thresholds, correction duties and the licence reference. |
   | **Source scorecard** | A periodic, per-source measurement of delivery, contract compliance, accuracy against ground truth, coverage and correction behavior. |
   | **Confirmed** | From an official source for that fact | Dutch register first-registration date; a UK MOT (annual roadworthiness test) reading from the Driver and Vehicle Standards Agency (DVSA) | State it as fact, with the source |
   | **Corroborated** | Two independent sources agree within tolerance | Kilometra reading within 2% of an MOT reading taken within 30 days | State it as fact; name both sources on request |
   | **Asserted** | One non-official source | A single workshop reading; a dealer's "towbar fitted" | Show it attributed: "reported by a workshop"; may feed a valuation, which is then marked provisional |
   | **Suspect** | Failed a check, or disputed | A reading lower than the previous official one | Never state it as fact; feeds judgements such as the odometer judgement in [[vehicle-history]] |
   | **New observation** | New, usually recent | Now | A new MOT reading | New fact; may change the current value |
   | **Late observation** | In the past | Now | A UK workshop reading from January delivered in May | New fact slotted into history; may change judgements for past dates |
   | **Correction** | Unchanged | Now; previous version closed | The redelivered reading, miles not kilometers | New version supersedes the old one; everything that used the old version is affected |
   | **Retraction** | Unchanged | Now; version closed, no successor | A reading attached to the wrong vehicle by plate | Fact withdrawn; the subject's derived facts recompute |
   - **What is the current odometer?** The newest valid reading, as known now: k2, 66,337 km.
   - **What did Axleby tell Rijnstaal on 17 May?** As known on 17 May: k1, 41,220 km.
   - **What do we now believe the odometer showed on 17 May?** At least 66,337 km, from k2 on 18 January.
   - **Why did the 14 May valuation come out high?** The valuation ledger cites k1.
   - **Source lag**: from observation to the source publishing it.
   - **Pipeline lag**: from publication to the fact being recorded and usable.
   - **Age at use**: from recording to the decision.
   | **Immutable build facts** | As-built edition and options, first registration | Appraisal, advert, valuation | None; recorded once with source and date | Not applicable; re-fetch only on dispute |
   | **Registration state** | Owner-change date, export, inspection expiry | Dossier display, Axleby Trade checks | Dutch register changes in Anchor within an hour of the daily snapshot's publication; mirror coverage-through within 3 days of today *(assumption)* | Label "register status as of …"; never block display |
   | **Decision-critical status** | Theft, awaiting-inspection, transfer possible | Purchase confirmation, register transaction | Live at the decision; never from a copy | Block the decision, keep the work saved ([[reliability-and-dependencies]]) |
   | **Accumulating history** | Odometer readings, MOT results, damage | Reports, valuations, odometer judgement | Supplier file recorded before 03:00; MOT daily delta applied by 10:00 CET *(assumption)* | Report states coverage-through; Vantage marks affected valuations provisional |
   | **Market observations** | Adverts, price changes, sale signals | Pricing signals, valuation training | Daily per portal ([[market-data]]) | Segment flagged; pricing signals withheld for that segment |
   | **Derived judgements** | Valuation, odometer judgement | Offers, prices, reports | Model at most 7 days old (weekly retraining); inputs within their own SLOs | Valuation carries a stale-input flag in its ledger entry ([[valuation-engine]]) |
   **A breach changes behavior, not just a dashboard**: each class has a defined degraded answer.
   | **Completeness** | Did we get every record and every required field? | Manifest counts; share of records with VIN, unit, observation date | Inlet, at the door |
   | **Validity** | Are values well formed and in range? | Schema, code lists, VIN structure, plausible ranges | Inlet, at the door |
   | **Consistency** | Do the facts agree with each other and with history? | Reading not below the previous official one; fuel type matches register | Inlet and survivorship |
   | **Accuracy** | Is the fact true of the car? | Agreement with **independent ground truth** on an overlapping sample | Data quality team, monthly |
   | **Timeliness** | Does it arrive when the contract says? | Arrival against window; coverage-through against SLO | Monitors ([[observability-and-data-operations]]) |
   **Accuracy cannot**: a valid, consistent reading can still be wrong about the car.
   - **Registers expose provenance at field level.** The DVSA's MOT API carries unit, read status and data source per test, and publishes daily delta files and a weekly bulk file ([DVSA, n.d., accessed 2026-10](https://documentation.history.mot.api.gov.uk/mot-history-api/download-vehicle-mot-history-data/)).
   - **US title law fixes some freshness and permanence.** States must report title data to NMVTIS "at a frequency of once every 24 hours" ([28 CFR 25.54](https://www.law.cornell.edu/cfr/text/28/25.54)); insurers and salvage yards report monthly ([28 CFR 25.55](https://www.law.cornell.edu/cfr/text/28/25.55), [25.56](https://www.law.cornell.edu/cfr/text/28/25.56)).
   - **History providers disclose source limits.** Carfax states that a report "is based only on information supplied to CARFAX" ([Carfax, archived December 2025](https://web.archive.org/web/20251219084511/https://www.carfax.com/company/about)): a coverage disclaimer, not field-level provenance.
   **Facts from the batch**: `batch_ref = 2027-05-13` returned 2,870 superseded readings for 2,870 vehicles, each with its k2 successor.
   **Answers served to Rijnstaal**: Meterline usage events for Rijnstaal's billing account with `fact_version` in that set.
   **What Axleby believes now**: `as_known_at(now)` for the same vehicles and dates.
   | **Duplicate fact from one source** (redelivery, replay) | Two identical versions | Idempotency on source, `source_record_id` and source version | Keep one version; no new transaction time |
   | **Same fact from two sources** | Two agreeing readings | Survivorship sees both | Keep both; tier becomes **Corroborated**; never count the car twice in a scorecard |
   | **Conflicting sources** | Two readings a week apart that disagree by a factor of 1.6 | Consistency check | Keep both; lower one becomes **Suspect**; disputed wording in reports |
   | **Correction without a reference** | Supplier resends a value with a new record id | Same subject, same `observed_at`, same chain, different value | Hold as a candidate correction; ask the supplier; never let both stand as observations |
   | **Correction for an unknown or already superseded version** | Reference points to nothing, or to an old version | Lookup on `corrects_record_id` | Park until the original arrives, or chain it after the latest version; [[journey-data-correction]] owns the ordering cases |
   | **Incomplete envelope** (no `observed_at`, no licence) | Fact cannot be placed in time or shown | Envelope validation at write | No observation time: store with a "no later than" bound, exclude it from judgements.
   | **Time zone or date-format ambiguity** | Readings land a day early, or with day and month swapped | Contract requires zone or date-only; distribution of `observed_at` per chain | Hold the segment; supplier redelivers |
   | **Lineage emitter outage** | Gaps in OpenLineage runs | Daily reconciliation against Inlet's batch log | Re-emit from the batch log; loads never wait for lineage |
   | **Source outage** | Coverage-through stops advancing | Freshness monitor | Products show the coverage date; decision-critical facts block per the SLO table |
   | **Retroactive change to a past answer** | Client disputes a report or a call | `as_known_at` query | Show what was served and what is known now; never rewrite the served version |
   | **Per table or dataset** | Source and load time per table | Cheapest; easy to add | Cannot separate sources inside a table; no answer per fact | Internal analytics on single-source data |
   | **Per fact, inline** | All envelope fields on every row | Self-contained; simple queries | Storage about six times the reference model *(assumption)*; licence text duplicated and hard to change | Small, high-value stores such as the valuation ledger |
   | **Per fact, batch fields by reference** | Fact-level fields inline; source, licence and batch once | Complete answers at modest cost; licence changes in one place | A join on reads; batch records must be retained | **Default** for vehicle fact stores |
   | **Overwrite plus audit log** | Current value; changes only if someone reads the log | Log drifts from data; past answers need forensics | Caches that can be rebuilt from elsewhere |
   | **Transaction time only** (system-versioned, as in SQL Server temporal tables) | What we believed at any time | Cannot tell a late observation from a correction | Records that are not observations: configurations, prices set by people |
   | **Valid time only** | History of the world as now known | Cannot show what was served before a correction | Analytics that never answer disputes |
   | **Bitemporal** | Both, for any pair of dates | Query discipline; storage; erasure handling | **Default** for facts that are sold, judged or disputed |
   | **Official ground truth for odometer** | NL: register judgement per plate, not readings, in open data; BE: Car-Pass, the statutory mileage register, fed by garages, inspection, manufacturers (including connected-car readings quarterly) and the RDW for Dutch imports | Inspection readings held by the Kraftfahrt-Bundesamt (KBA, federal motor transport authority), not public | DVSA MOT readings with unit and data sourc
   | **Freshness signal from the source** | RDW dataset `Last-Modified` header; observed lag of a few days for owner changes | None public for the register; commercial providers' own | Daily MOT delta files from 08:00 plus weekly bulk | States report within 24 hours; insurers and yards monthly |
   | **Attribution rule** | RDW open data: may not state the data come from the RDW | Per commercial licence (DAT, Autovista) | MOT documentation under the Open Government Licence; data reuse terms to be checked per agreement | Per approved-provider agreement |
   | **Rectification duty when facts are personal data** | General Data Protection Regulation (GDPR) Art. 16 and Art. 19 | GDPR Art. 16 and Art. 19 | UK GDPR equivalents | No general federal right; brands are permanent by law |
   | **Hardest provenance gap** | Readings behind the Dutch judgement are not available to platforms | No official odometer ground truth for platforms | Commercial readings for vehicles before import | Coverage varies by state and reporting entity |
===== part-3 1
   The **EV Overvaluation** is the thread.
===== market-data 45
   | **All** | **2.30 million** | **61%** | |
   | **Market observation** | One sighting of one advert or market event from one source at one time, with provenance (source, method, observed-at, terms version).
   | **Listing** | One advert on one channel under one listing id, from first to last observation.
   | **Market vehicle** | A cluster of listings believed to be the same physical car on offer: across portals, across relists, across rooftops of one group.
   | **Market data lake** | The store of market observations and everything derived from them (listings, market vehicles, inferred sales, segment statistics), with per-observation provenance and rights tags so that any figure can be traced to, and rebuilt without, any source. |
   | **Sale detection** | Inferring that a market vehicle left the market because it was sold, with an outcome state, a confidence and a confirmation source where one exists. |
   | **Stand-time** | Days from the first observation of a market vehicle on any channel to its inferred sale.
   | **Market days' supply** | Market vehicles currently on offer in a segment divided by the average daily inferred sales in that segment over a trailing window, usually 30 days. |
   | **Segment** | A versioned grouping of vehicles for statistics: make, model, generation, fuel, body, power band, age band, mileage band and region, arranged as a hierarchy with fallbacks. |
   | **Coverage** | The share of the real market a statistic is based on, estimated per segment, market and source. |
   | **Harvested public adverts** | Most retail supply on public portals | Plate or VIN sometimes; often neither | Asking price | Database right, terms of use, competition and privacy law; see next section |
   | **Portal data agreements** | One portal's adverts, often with history and de-listing dates | Better than harvesting; portal ids | Asking price | Contract: purposes, redistribution, retention |
   | **First-party dealer stock** (Lotwise, Showroomly) | The platform's own dealers' stock, costs, price changes and sales | VIN, plate, stock id | Asking price **and** realized sale price | Dealer contract; competition law limits on sharing |
   | **Register ownership changes** | That a vehicle changed keeper, and when | Plate (NL), VIN in some feeds | None | Register terms; per-vehicle access varies sharply by country |
   | **Auction and B2B results** | Wholesale and trade clearing prices | VIN | Transaction price, as-is | Auction or exchange contract |
   | **Aggregate statistics** | Counts of transfers by segment and month | None per vehicle | None | Publisher's terms |
   **Source concentration rises**: one portal group's terms of use, API changes or commercial decisions can remove a large share of a market's observations overnight.
   | **Collectors and feed adapters** | Harvest public adverts, receive portal feeds, take first-party stock events, read register snapshot diffs | Nothing durable; writes to landing | One adapter per source with a completeness check per run |
   | **Observation store** | Append-only market observations with provenance and rights tags | Market observations | Never update in place; partition by source and day; purge by source |
   | **Listing lifecycle builder** | Turns observations into listings: first seen, price changes, last seen, gone | Listings | A listing is "gone" only after two complete harvests without it |
   | **Market vehicle resolver** | Clusters listings across portals, relists and rooftops; attaches clusters to AVKs | Market vehicles | Deterministic links first; probabilistic clustering with thresholds; cluster ids are stable |
   | **Sale inference** | Assigns outcome states with confidence; confirms against register changes and first-party sales | Inferred outcomes | Versioned classifier; outcomes revisable; precision measured against ground truth |
   | **Segment statistics** | Supply, inferred sales, stand-time, days' supply, price distributions per segment, with coverage and evidence counts | Segment statistics | Minimum-evidence rule and fallback hierarchy; every row stamped with window and classifier version |
   **Intra-group transfers**: when a car moves from one rooftop of a dealer group to another, its listing usually ends and a new one starts at the new rooftop.
   **Relists**: dealers re-advertise cars to appear fresh in "newest first" sorting.
   - **Cox Automotive** estimates US retail used-vehicle sales from inventory movement: its January 2026 report says the estimates "are based on observed unit changes tracked by vAuto", with full-year volume "based on registrations and other sources".
   - **Auto Trader UK** bases its Retail Price Index on "circa 800,000 daily pricing observations across the whole retail market" ([Auto Trader Group, July 2023](https://plc.autotrader.co.uk/news-views/press-releases/retail-price-index-june-2023/); the release is more than three years old, and the index's method may have changed since).
   - **Indicata**, Autorola's market-data product, was described in 2017 trade press as tracking price development, stocking days and market days' supply from overnight sweeps of online used-car listings ([AM Online, 2017](https://www.am-online.com/news/technology/2017/09/11/autorola-s-used-vehicle-management-software-indicata-receives-uk-launch)).
   **Days' supply barely moved, because the two errors cancelled**: duplicates inflated supply by a factor of 1.6 and de-listings inflated sales by 1.47.
   | **Harvest public adverts** | Broad and quick to extend | Weak outside markets that show plates | Asking | Database right, terms, concentration on few portals | As a complement, for statistics that do not substitute for the portal, under counsel |
   | **Portal data agreements** | One portal deep | Better; portal ids and history | Asking | Contract dependence on a competitor-supplier | Where one or two portals dominate a market |
   | **First-party dealer data** | Your dealers only | Exact | Asking and realized | Competition law on exchange; dealer trust | Always, as ground truth and core signal; aggregated and delayed outward |
   | **Register changes** | Whole national fleet | Plate or VIN | None | Register terms and purpose rules | Where per-vehicle data is legally available (NL) |
   | **Auction and B2B results** | Wholesale only | Exact | Transaction | Contract | Where the platform owns or partners with auctions |
   | **Buy a market-data product** | Provider's | Provider's | Provider's | Licence limits on redistribution | Markets where you have no first-party base |
   | **Portal structure** | Consolidating: AutoScout24 took over AutoTrack and Gaspedaal.nl (Nov 2025); Marktplaats | 2dehands/2ememain (same API as Marktplaats); AutoScout24.be | mobile.de and AutoScout24 compete; AutoScout24 can export to mobile.de; dealers multi-home (Bundeskartellamt 2026) | One dominant portal, Auto Trader, which also sells valuations and metrics | Several marketplaces; inventory reaches them mostl
   | **Per-vehicle sale confirmation** | Register open data: date of registration in the current name, export indicator, per plate | Not verified as available to market-data providers | None; KBA publishes monthly aggregate keeper changes (6.03M cars, 2023) | None for market use; SMMT quarterly transactions from DVLA data (7.81M, 2025) | Auctions see wholesale; retail inferred from dealer inventory (Cox, vAuto) |
   | **Identity in adverts** (Axleby's observation for Europe) | Plate often readable; resolves through the register mirror | Rarely plate or VIN | Rarely plate or VIN | Registration mark in first-party stock; varies in harvested adverts | VIN commonly published (industry practice) |
   | **Transaction prices** | First-party dealer data | First-party dealer data | First-party data; DAT reports manufacturer and customer transaction values (2024) | First-party data; portal's own trade valuations | Wholesale auctions (Manheim index on 5M+ transactions) |
   | **Legal frame for harvesting** | EU database right; *Innoweb* and *Ryanair* came from Dutch references | EU database right | EU database right | UK database right (Reg. 16, SI 1997/3032); post-2020 CJEU rulings not binding | No database right (*Feist*, 1991); contract and access law (*hiQ*, 2022) |
   | **Competition law on dealer data** | EU Horizontal Guidelines 2023 | Same | Same | UK competition law (not reviewed here) | US antitrust (not reviewed here) |
   **The source policy**: which sources are licensed, harvested or excluded per market; the concentration limit per portal group; the response when a portal objects.
   **The outward rule for first-party data**: minimum aggregation per published cell, delay for dealer-derived prices, and the dealer contract clause that permits it.
   **The sale definition**: the outcome states, windows and classifier, versioned, with precision published internally per market.
   **The coverage contract**: what every statistic must carry, and the thresholds below which consumers such as Vantage must not use it.
===== valuation-engine 65
   | **Retail (asking)** | Dealer advertises to consumer | Reconditioned, retail-ready | Dealer advert prices | Auto Trader "Retail"; KBB Typical Listing Price |
   | **Retail (transaction)** | Consumer pays dealer | Reconditioned | Dealer sale records | KBB Fair Purchase Price |
   | **Trade-in (part-exchange)** | Dealer pays consumer | As appraised | Auctions, appraisals, offers | KBB Trade-In Value; Auto Trader "Part-exchange" |
   | **Trade (wholesale)** | Dealer to dealer, auction | As is, not reconditioned | Auction and B2B prices | Manheim Market Report; KBB Auction Value; Auto Trader "Trade" |
   | **Private** | Consumer to consumer | As is, no warranty | Private adverts | KBB Private Party; Auto Trader "Private" |
   | **Dealer selling / purchase value** | Dealer to consumer / consumer to dealer | Per DAT condition entry | Transactions reported by manufacturers and customers, market research | DAT *Händlerverkaufswert* and *Händlereinkaufswert* |
   | **Maximum purchase price** | The dealer's own ceiling | The dealer's reconditioning plan | Retail target minus costs and margin | Not published; computed in dealer tools |
   | **Future (residual)** | Lessor, lender | Projected mileage, average condition | Depreciation history, supply forecasts | Auto Trader Future Valuations (up to six years) |
   **A valuation without its value type is dangerous**: a lessor that reads a retail value as a trade value sets reserves that never clear.
   The **maximum purchase price** (MPP, sometimes called the buy box) is the most a dealer can pay for a car and still earn its target margin.
   **Valuation confidence** is the engine's statement of how far a value can be trusted, returned with the value.
   A **range** is an interval expected to contain the realized price with a stated probability; an 80% range should contain 80% of outcomes.
   - **Licensed guide values** come from providers such as DAT, Autovista (owned by J.D.
