   - **Own valuations** are what Vantage computes.
   - **Decisions** are what a dealer did: the offer made, the MPP accepted or overridden, the retail price set.
   | **Valuation API** | One contract for every caller: Lotwise, Tradefloor, Odolo, JSON API clients | Returns the typed answer; never a bare number |
   | **Context assembler** | Gathers identity (Anchor, keyed by the Axleby Vehicle Key, AVK), specification (Specbook), odometer and damage (Tracewell), condition report, battery certificate | Records the version of every input fact it used |
   | **Market surface** | Nightly base values and segment statistics from Pulse, with an intraday overlay | Stamped with the observation window it was built from |
   | **Model service** | Scores the base value; holds the champion model and any challengers | Model version on every score |
   | **Adjustment engine** | Applies option, condition, battery and region lines | Versioned tables; each line overridable with a reason |
   | **Provider gateway** | Licensed guide values per country | Licence checks in the request path (Charter); no caching beyond licence terms |
   | **Valuation ledger** | Immutable record of every valuation and the decisions and outcomes linked to it | Append only; written before the answer is returned |
   | **Model operations** | Training, back-testing, release gates, drift monitors, segment circuit breakers | Per-segment metrics; leading indicators |
   The **valuation ledger** is the immutable record of every valuation the engine issued, with its inputs, the versions of those inputs and of the model, the answer, who saw it, and links to the decisions and outcomes that followed.
   - **Request:** caller, tenant and user, purpose (appraisal, API, report, consumer), value type requested, idempotency key.
   - **Context:** AVK, the mileage reading id and value, specification version and edition confidence, condition report id, battery certificate id, location.
   - **Answer:** point value, range, confidence flag, basis, adjustment lines, comparable observation ids (ids, not copies, where licences forbid copying), market-surface version, model and adjustment-table versions.
   - **Segment key:** fuel type, age band, price band, body type, market.
   - **Outcome links,** appended as events arrive: offer made and accepted, purchase price, MPP override and reason, reconditioning cost, list price and price changes, sale price and date, or trade clearing price on Tradefloor.
   | **Expected retail** | **€22,500** | 80% range €21,300–€23,700; `normal` |
   - **Median absolute percentage error** and the share of valuations within ±5% and ±10% of the realized price.
   - **Signed bias**: an unbiased engine with a 7% error loses some deals and wins others; a +9% bias overpays on every purchase.
   - **Range coverage:** whether 80% ranges contain about 80% of outcomes.
   - **Rolling-origin evaluation**, which re-runs the model as of each past date and never lets later data leak into an earlier valuation.
   - **Separate scores against transaction prices and against final asking prices.** Asking-price targets inherit dealer pricing habits; transaction prices are the truth but arrive later and less often.
   - **Asking-price movement** in the segment, from Pulse, against the movement in Vantage's own outputs for the same segment.
   - **Stand-time and price-cut rate** on dealer stock: cars that sit and get cut are priced above the market.
   - **Supply growth** in the segment (Auto Trader's June 2023 release reported EV supply up 174% year on year).
   - **New-car list-price changes**, which Auto Trader names as an input to its future valuations (help center, January 2024).
   - **Override rate**: dealers overriding the value downward in one segment.
   - **Clearing and no-sale rates** on trade channels: Fleetmoor's no-sale rate rising from 18% to 41% was a market signal, not just a complaint.
   - **Model card** per release: training window and weights, features, segments covered and excluded, back-test results per segment, known weaknesses.
   - **Back-test gate:** a challenger must match or beat the champion on signed bias and range coverage in every segment with enough data, not only on global error.
   - **Shadow period:** the challenger scores live traffic for two weeks without being shown, as unbilled ledger entries with a `shadow` purpose.
   - **Staged release** by market and segment, with rollback to the previous model version by configuration.
   - **Applicability contract** published to API clients: segments valued, segments refused, flags and their meanings.
   - **Manheim (Cox Automotive), US.** Beyond the MMR method described above, the MMR now carries an EV Battery Health score from 0 to 100 (help page, 2026).
   - **Kelley Blue Book, US.** KBB's value definitions (n.d., accessed 2026-10) separate trade-in, private party, fair purchase price, typical listing price and auction value.
   - **Cox Automotive scale.** Launching its DRiVEQ data brand in February 2022, Cox reported about **18 billion** valuation requests a year across its businesses: valuation as the common currency of a portfolio ([[case-cox-automotive]]).
   - **Auto Trader UK.** Valuations became a retailer module in April 2023 (FY2023 results, June 2023).
   | **Duplicate requests** (app retries, double taps, client retry loops) | Idempotency key per request | Same answer and same ledger entry returned; one billable event ([[billing-and-metering]]) |
   | **Conflicting inputs** (dealer types 48,000 km; Tracewell's latest reading is 61,000 km) | Lotwise checks the entry against Tracewell before it is stored | Vantage values from Tracewell's derived current odometer, not the raw entry, and lowers confidence when the odometer judgement is *rollback suspected* or *not assessable* ([[vehicle-history]]); both reading ids go to the ledger |
   | **Incomplete records** (edition unknown, no condition report, no battery certificate) | Edition confidence and missing-input checks | Value from the base derivative with a wider range and a named flag; or "no valuation" outside the applicability contract |
   | **Thin evidence** (rare derivative, few comparables) | Minimum-sample rule per segment | Fallback hierarchy (derivative → model → segment) with `fallback_model`; refuse below the floor |
   | **Model service or cloud link down** | Health checks and timeouts on the scoring call | Serve from the cached nightly surface on AWS with `stale_market`; appraisal continues |
   | **Market surface not rebuilt overnight** | Surface version older than 24 hours | Serve yesterday's surface with its date; stock recomputation waits rather than repricing on stale data |
   | **Nightly stock recomputation partially fails** | Per-car status in the batch run | Retry failed cars; dealers see per-car value dates, not a silent mix |
   | **Provider gateway outage** (licensed guide value unavailable) | Gateway circuit breaker | Answer without the guide-value input, flagged; never serve an expired cached guide value beyond its licence |
   | **Corrected input fact** (Odometer Correction) | Correction event names the fact versions it replaces | Ledger query finds affected valuations; re-value; notify dealers whose offers, MPPs or prices used them; [[journey-data-correction]] |
   | **Segment drift** | Segment monitor | `watch` or `restricted` status; retrain; model card updated |
   **Never return a degraded value without saying so**: every fallback has a flag, and every flag is in the ledger.
   | **License guide values only** | Accepted by dealers, lenders and sometimes tax authorities; no model team | Per-call cost; redistribution limits; no control over drift response; no differentiation | Valuation is a supporting input, not a product |
   | **Comparables only** | Explainable; dealers can check every line | Breaks in thin segments; licence limits on showing portal adverts; noisy | Dense markets with licensed advert data; dealer-facing pricing tools |
   | **Own ML model with adjustment lines** | Differentiating; fast response if monitored; can use the platform's own outcomes | Needs a model team, a ledger, back-testing and governance; liability for errors | Valuation is sold as a product and the platform has outcome data |
   | **Hybrid: own model, licensed guides as inputs, comparables as explanation** | Combines accuracy, acceptance and explainability | Most complex; licence terms must allow guide values as model inputs | A group that sells data and runs dealer workflows, as Axleby does |
===== condition-and-damage 59
   - **Inspection.** One capture session on one vehicle, keyed by the Axleby Vehicle Key (AVK, the internal identity key from [[vehicle-identity]]), with a purpose (appraisal, stock-in, lease return, trade listing, pre-delivery), a location, an inspector, a device and a start and end time.
   - **Observation.** A raw piece of evidence from an inspection: a photo, a video, a measurement (tread depth, paint-layer thickness, odometer reading), a checklist answer or a diagnostic readout.
   - **Finding** (damage item).
   - **Condition report.** A versioned, read-only document derived from one inspection's confirmed findings for a stated purpose and audience: a trade report for buyers, a return report for the lessee, an appraisal report for the dealer's file.
   - **Condition grade.** A summary score computed from findings by a published rubric.
   - **Damage estimate.** A priced repair plan for a set of findings: operations, parts, labor time, paint and their prices, produced from a repair-cost catalog or by an assessor.
   - **Reconditioning cost** (recon cost).
   | **Tractable** | Image-based damage assessment for insurers, repairers, recyclers, fleets and dealers; "certainty scores accompany every estimate, factoring in visibility, image quality, and damage severity" | [Tractable](https://tractable.ai/), n.d., accessed 2026-10 |
   | **DAT FastTrackAI** | Photo-based damage detection with "automated recording of component, type of damage and severity" and transfer into an initial damage calculation, in DAT's software "from September 1, 2026"; also offered inside the weDAT platform | [DAT AI Bundle](https://www.dat.de/ai-bundle-en/), n.d., accessed 2026-10; [DAT Austria, weDAT](https://www.datgroup.com/de-at/produkte/wedat/), n.d. |
   | **Solera Qapter** | An AI-powered modular suite for insurers and repairers with "damage detection algorithms" and "photo-based estimating" | [Qapter](https://www.qapter.com/), n.d., accessed 2026-10 |
   | **UVeye** (with Cox vAuto) | Drive-through inspection of exterior, underbody and tires, integrated into dealer appraisal tools | [Cox Automotive](https://www.coxautoinc.com/press-releases/cox-automotives-vauto-and-uveye-bring-ai-powered-vehicle-inspections-to-market/), January 2026 |
   | **ProovStation** | Drive-through scanning that detects, locates, measures and labels damage, compares with a previous scan, and blurs faces for the EU General Data Protection Regulation (GDPR) | [ProovStation](https://proovstation.com/), n.d., accessed 2026-10 |
   | **Lotwise Inspect** (at return centers, in Appraise and the stock-in check) | Guided capture on phones and tablets; offline queue; panel-map tagging | Mandatory shot list per purpose; capture metadata and device hash on every observation |
   | **Scanner adapters** | Drive-through and lane scanners at partner sites | Scanner images and detections enter as observations and machine findings with device and model version |
   | **Evidence store** | Originals in object storage; metadata, hashes and lineage in PostgreSQL | Write once; originals never modified; derived renditions stored beside them |
   | **Image pipeline** | Renditions for screens, plate and face blurring for shared views, quality checks (blur, exposure, framing) | Runs asynchronously; quality failures go back to the inspector while the car is still there |
   | **Detection gateway** | Calls the damage-detection provider or model; normalizes output to the panel map | Detections stored as unconfirmed findings with confidence and model version |
   | **Findings and review** | The finding lifecycle; review queues by confidence and consequence | Only confirmed findings feed views; every state change has an actor and reason |
   | **Rubric engine** | Computes grades, lessee charges and report contents from confirmed findings | Rubrics are versioned content (return standards, trade grades, buyer scales), never code branches per customer |
   | **Estimating** | Damage estimates via a licensed repair-cost catalog; reconditioning estimates via each dealer's rate card | Catalog calls only where the decision needs them; recon rate cards owned by the tenant |
   | **Condition API and events** | Reports, findings and estimates to Vantage, Lotwise, Tradefloor, Odolo and API clients | Consumers reference finding and report ids, never copies; `condition.report.issued`, `finding.disputed` and `recon.actual.recorded` events |
   - **Originals are evidence; renditions are for screens.** Keep originals write-once with their hash, in the region of the tenant's cell ([[tenancy-and-dealer-groups]]).
   - **Blur before sharing.** Photos taken in yards capture other cars' plates, people and occasionally addresses.
   - **Tier by age, delete by purpose.** Hot storage while the car is in stock or a dispute window is open, then archive tier.
   - **Recon variance** per line, per finding type, per rooftop and per segment: actual minus estimate.
   - **Rate-card calibration**: when PDR on doors runs 30% over the card at a rooftop for a quarter, the card is wrong, not the inspector.
   - **Finding closure**: a repair order closed against a finding moves it to "superseded by repair", so the dossier and any later trade report show both the damage and its repair.
   - **The report shows findings with photos, not only a grade.** A trader bids on the photos of the damage he will repair.
   - **Tires, paint readings and warning lights are mandatory sections.** These were 61% of Fleetmoor's Q1 claims between them; a report that omits a section must say "not inspected", never leave it blank.
   - **The report names its rubric and its inspection date.** A buyer claim is judged against what was disclosed then, under the rubric then.
   - **Claims attach to findings.** A claim says "this finding was missing" or "this finding was understated", with the buyer's own photos as observations.
   - **Manheim, US (2014).** AutoGrade computed a standardized grade from damage data submitted by auctions; ADESA's Paul Lips said it would "calculate a consistent objective grade the same way, every time" ([Manheim, 2014](https://press.manheim.com/2014-01-17-Aasc-Announces-Standardized-Vehicle-Condition-Grading-Technology)).
   - **Cox Automotive and UVeye, US (January 2026).** Drive-through scanning integrated into vAuto's appraisal, pricing and inventory tools for service-lane acquisition, with the release citing 125 users and more than 850 vehicles acquired in the first wave ([Cox Automotive, 2026](https://www.coxautoinc.com/press-releases/cox-automotives-vauto-and-uveye-bring-ai-powered-vehicle-inspections-to-market/)).
   - **DAT, Germany and Austria (2026).** FastTrackAI detection feeds an initial damage calculation inside DAT's existing software and weDAT ([DAT](https://www.dat.de/ai-bundle-en/)).
   - **CarMax and Carvana, US (2026).** CarMax inspects "the engine and all major systems" and uses stand-alone reconditioning centers ([CarMax 10-K, 2026](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1170010/000117001026000021/kmx-20260228.htm)); Carvana's inspection covers "controls, features, brakes, tires, and cosmetics" ([Carvana 10-K, 2026](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1690820/000169082026000009/cvna-2025123
   | **Total** | **€260** | **€1,930** | **€1,290** | |
   - **The respray became a finding with evidence.** The coating-gauge readings and close-ups were observations, captured and hashed at return.
   - **The tires were measured, not described.** 2.3 mm is inside Fleetmoor's return standard, so the lessee owes nothing, and outside most dealers' retail standard, so the recon estimate includes two tires.
   - **The insurer-grade estimate stayed out of the buying decision.** At €1,930 it would have underbid the car by about €640 against the dealer's own rate card.
   - **The dealer's €1,290 matched the estimate.** The Zwolle dealer's February surprise was the €680 gap between his €610 guess and his €1,290 bill.
   | **Duplicate uploads** (app retries after a dropped connection) | Idempotency key per observation plus content hash | Second copy acknowledged and discarded; one observation |
   | **Duplicate findings** (inspector and model record the same dent; two close-ups tagged twice) | Same panel, zone and type within one inspection | Merged into one finding with both sources linked; the reviewer sees the merge |
   | **Conflicting inspections** (return report says tires 3.1 mm; buyer measures 2.3 mm a week later) | A new inspection's findings differ from the last issued report on the same AVK | Both kept with dates and inspectors; the dispute workflow decides; neither overwrites the other |
   | **Incomplete inspection** (mandatory shots missing, no paint readings) | Shot-list and section checks before completion | Inspection cannot complete; if issued anyway by override, the report says "not inspected" per missing section and the grade is withheld |
   | **Wrong vehicle** (photos taken of the neighboring car) | Vehicle identification number (VIN) plate photo or scan mismatch with the inspection's AVK; scanner plate reading | Inspection quarantined; findings not issued until the VIN is confirmed ([[vehicle-identity]]) |
   | **Detection provider outage or timeout** | Gateway timeouts and error rates | Inspection proceeds on inspector findings; detection queued and run later; the report never waits for the model |
   | **Detection model update shifts rates** | Detection and confirmation rates per model version and damage type | Pin the version per inspection; review a new version on a shadow sample before switching |
   | **Partial upload** (inspection marked done on the phone, half the originals missing on the server) | Server-side completeness check against the device manifest | Inspection stays "uploading"; the inspector is alerted while the car is still on site |
   | **Catalog or rate card changes mid-dispute** | Version recorded on every estimate | Disputes are settled on the version in force at issue; recompute only for new reports |
   | **Recon actual never arrives** (repair done outside the DMS) | Recon lines open after the car is sold | Excluded from variance metrics rather than counted as zero; the rooftop sees the gap |
   **The car moves on, so capture must finish while it is there**: every check that can fail should fail on the phone, not in the back office a day later.
   | **Inspector with free-form app or paper** | Cheap to start; flexible | Not comparable across inspectors; weak evidence; grades are opinions | Low volume, no disputes, no trade use |
   | **Guided capture on a phone** (fixed shot list, panel map, measurements) | Comparable; defensible; works anywhere, offline | Needs training and discipline; longer than a glance | Appraisals, stock-ins, lease returns, trade listings: the default |
   | **Drive-through scanner** | Seconds per car; consistent images; underbody and tires | Fixed sites; capital cost; no interior or mechanical | High-volume return centers, auctions, large service lanes |
   | **Self-inspection by driver or seller** | No inspector cost; early warning | Self-interested evidence; poor capture | Pre-return estimates and consumer trade-in offers, always followed by a verified inspection before money changes hands |
   | **Licensed repair-cost catalog** (manufacturer parts and labor) | Accepted by insurers, assessors and courts; precise per operation | Per-call cost; overstates retail recon for cosmetic damage | Lessee charges, insurance, tax evidence |
   | **Dealer rate card per finding type** | Reflects actual methods and prices; cheap | Only as good as its calibration; differs by dealer | Recon estimates in buying decisions |
   | **Model trained on recon actuals** | Learns hidden costs per segment | Needs linked findings and actuals at volume; drifts with labor prices | A group with years of linked recon data |
   | **Flat allowance by age and segment** | Trivial | Wrong for every individual car | No inspection available; must be labeled as such |
===== ev-battery-health 45
   **State of health (SoH)** is the ratio of a battery's current ability to store energy or charge to its ability when new, as a percentage.
   - **Energy or capacity.** Capacity is charge, in ampere-hours; energy is capacity times voltage, in kilowatt-hours.
   - **Gross or usable.** Manufacturers keep a buffer the driver cannot use; the ID.3's "58 kWh" is usable energy, and the gross pack is larger.
   - **Reference.** "Original" can mean the nominal rating, the certified value at type approval, or the first measurement of this pack.
   A **battery certificate** is a document issued by a named party that states a battery's SoH for a specific vehicle at a specific time, with the method used.
   The **battery passport** is the electronic record that the EU Battery Regulation (Regulation (EU) 2023/1542) requires for every electric-vehicle battery "placed on the market or put into service" from **18 February 2027** (Article 77(1)).
   | **BMS readout** | Read the car's own SoH or capacity estimate over the diagnostic port or the manufacturer's API | Seconds to minutes | Cheap; no driving; works at scale on a lot | Manufacturer's algorithm, not disclosed; not every make exposes it; can be recalibrated by software | Manheim's LotVision devices (Cox Automotive, 2024) |
   | **Short diagnostic test** | Read BMS data plus pack and cell parameters (cell voltage spread, energy counters), apply the vendor's statistical model | 2–5 minutes | Scalable for remarketing; vendor issues a certificate | Model-dependent; coverage varies by make and model year | Aviloo FLASH (3 minutes); Moba (under 2 minutes, vendor-stated) |
   | **Full cycle test** | Record a complete charge or discharge and measure the energy | Hours to a day | Most direct measurement of energy | Needs time, a charger or a drive; temperature-sensitive | Aviloo PREMIUM |
   | **Modeled estimate** | Predict SoH from make, model, age, mileage and climate | None | Needs no access to the car | Hides outliers by construction | Make-and-model averages in many valuation tools |

   | **EU Battery Regulation, Article 77 and Annex XIII** | Battery passport for each EV battery placed on the market or put into service; SoH, cycles and negative events in the legitimate-interest tier | From **18 February 2027** | A pack identifier and, if access is granted, a regulated history; access for valuation depends on the implementing act |

   | **UN GTR No. 22** | Defines SOCE; passenger-car MPR 80% to 5 years or 100,000 km and **70%** to 8 years or 160,000 km | Established 2022 | The common definition; the floors the US adopted |

   | **Battery intake** | Accept results from integrated diagnostic devices, vendor APIs, uploaded PDFs, QR codes and, later, passport and manufacturer data; normalize to observations | Source adapters, document extraction, idempotency | Accept a value without method and date |
   | **Certificate verifier** | Confirm each certificate with its issuer (API, QR landing page or signed document) and record the result | Verification status and evidence of the check | Treat an unverifiable certificate as false, or a verified one as current |
   | **Battery observation store** | Hold every observation, append-only, keyed to the Axleby Vehicle Key (AVK) and, where known, the pack identity | Observation records, pack-to-vehicle edges | Overwrite or average observations |
   | **Expected-SoH model** | Estimate the SoH band expected for an edition, age, mileage and climate | Segment reference curves and their version | Fill a car's SoH field |
   | **Evidence selector** | Choose the observation to use for a given purpose, or return "unknown" | Selection rules per purpose, validity windows | Hide which observation it chose |

   | **Advert and Tradefloor listing** | Latest **verified** certificate from a short test or full cycle, at most **180 days** and **7,500 km** old *(Axleby policy)* | No figure; "no battery certificate" |
   | **Valuation (Vantage)** | Highest-ranked observation in validity: full cycle, then verified short test, then BMS readout; uncertainty passed with it | "Battery unknown": no battery line, wider range |
   | **Consumer report (Odolo)** | Latest verified certificate with its date, method and issuer, at any age; older ones listed below | "No battery test on record" |
   | **Appraisal screen (Lotwise Appraise)** | All observations, newest first, with method and status; expected band for the segment shown separately | Prompt to run a test |

   | **Duplicate results** (device retries, the same PDF uploaded twice, the vendor webhook and the device both report) | Idempotency key from issuer and certificate id; content hash for documents | One observation; later copies linked as duplicates, not new evidence |
   | **Conflicting observations** (BMS 94%, short test 87%) | Difference larger than the combined stated uncertainty, per method pair | Keep both; selector applies method ranking; dossier shows the conflict; a conflict rate per make and method feeds the vendor review |
   | **Incomplete records** (no odometer, no certificate id, unknown unit, no battery size) | Intake validation | Stored as `incomplete`; usable for the appraisal screen, never for adverts or valuation; prompt to retest |
   | **Device offline in a yard without signal** | Device queue age | Store-and-forward with the original timestamp; observation date is the test date, not the upload date |
   | **Vendor verification API down** | Verification timeouts | Status `pending`; adverts publish without the figure; retry with backoff; dealers see "verification pending", not an error |
   | **Forged or edited certificate** | Issuer record mismatch | Status `mismatch`; figure withdrawn from every channel by event; case to the dealer and, for repeat cases, to account management |
   | **Revoked certificate** (vendor withdraws a result after finding a model fault) | Issuer revocation feed or periodic re-verification | Status `revoked`; affected valuations found through the valuation ledger's evidence id and re-run; adverts updated |
   | **Pack replaced** | Pack serial or passport id changes; sudden SoH jump of more than 8 points *(assumption)* | `battery.pack.replaced` event; new pack edge; older observations stay with the old pack |
   | **Software update moves the buffer** | Step change in BMS readouts across many cars of one model within days | Flag the model; do not treat as degradation or recovery; prefer test methods for that model |
   | **Wrong vehicle** (device read another car) | Device VIN differs from dossier VIN | Reject; route to the appraiser |
   | **BMS readout only** (generic device) | Lowest | Varies by manufacturer algorithm | Wide but uneven; some makes expose nothing | Lot-wide screening, internal triage, not adverts |
   | **One integrated test vendor** | Device plus per-test fee | High within one scale | Limited to the vendor's supported models | A single market with one dominant vendor |
   | **Several vendors, normalized by method** (Axleby's choice) | Dealer chooses | Comparable within method, not across | Widest | A multi-market platform whose dealers already own different devices |
   | **Platform's own test** | Platform builds hardware and models | Highest control | Must be built make by make | A group with auction-scale volume of its own cars, like Manheim |
   | **Modeled estimate only** | None | Consistent, and blind to outliers | Universal | Never as a car's figure; only as a reference band |
===== connected-vehicle-data 43
   **A connected car reports on itself**: its odometer, its warning lamps, its battery, sometimes its position, every day or every few minutes, through the manufacturer's backend.
   **Connected-vehicle data** is data a vehicle generates about its use and condition and sends through an electronic communications service or exposes on a physical port.
   | **Odometer value** | In scope: pre-processed data (para 34) | Very high: history, valuation, lease mileage | Low alone; higher at daily granularity, because it reveals use |
   | **Fault codes and malfunction lamps** | In scope (para 34) | High: condition at appraisal and return | Low |
   | **Battery level** and component wear (brake pads) | In scope (para 34) | Medium; state of health belongs to [[ev-battery-health]] | Low |
   | **Time or distance to next service** | In scope unless predicted (para 34) | Medium: reconditioning cost | Low |
   | **GNSS location** (global navigation satellite system) | In scope (para 34) | Low for history and valuation | **High**: the European Data Protection Board (EDPB) calls location "particularly revealing of the life habits of data subjects" |
   | **Raw sensor and CAN-bus signals** | In scope: raw data (para 33) | Low for this platform | Varies |
   | **Driving or eco-scores**, crash-severity analysis | Out of scope: inferred or derived (para 35) | Tempting for risk products | High |
   **Driving scores are out of scope**: they are the manufacturer's derived data and the Data Act gives nobody a right to them.
   - **User**: a person or organization that "owns a connected product or to whom temporary rights to use that connected product have been contractually transferred" (Art. 2(12)).
   - **Data holder**: the party that has the right or obligation to use and make available the product data, in practice the manufacturer or its backend.
   - **Third party** or data recipient: the party the user chooses to receive the data.
   - **Readily available data**: data the data holder "lawfully obtains or can lawfully obtain ... without disproportionate effort" (Art. 2(17)).
   - **Consent grant (vehicle)**: this book's term for the platform's record of one right to receive one vehicle's data.
   | **Manufacturer backend or data portal** | The vehicle talks only to the manufacturer's server; third parties call the manufacturer's web services.
   | **Aggregator, consumer-consented** | A developer API across many brands; the driver logs in with their manufacturer account and grants scopes | Smartcar describes "an OAuth 2.0 consent flow that lets your users link their vehicles", with permissions such as `read_odometer` ([Smartcar, n.d.](https://smartcar.com/docs/getting-started/introduction)) | Seconds to minutes for an on-demand read | Smartcar states "over 40
   | **Aggregator, fleet-cleared** | A business registers VINs; the manufacturer approves each one | High Mobility documents an asynchronous activation with states `pending`, `approved`, `rejected`, `revoking`, `revoked` and others that "can take several days or more", and rejection when the vehicle's "privacy mode is enabled" ([High Mobility, n.d.](https://docs.high-mobility.com/docs/getting-started/fleet-clearance/act
   | **Aftermarket dongle or OBD reader** | A device on the OBD port reads the car directly | ePrivacy consent of the person who controls the car at that moment | Immediate, while plugged in | Any car with an OBD port; depth varies by brand | Appraisal and return inspections |
   | **Statutory channel** | Manufacturers report readings to a public registry | By law | Quarterly where it exists | All connected cars of reporting manufacturers | Reaching connected readings through a register |
   **Consumer consent** is a person granting a scope for a car they drive, in one session.
   **Fleet clearance** is a business registering a car it owns, approved by the manufacturer over days.
   **Mandate** is the consent and data-rights registry: one record per consent grant, with its state.
   | **Mandate** | Records consent grants; runs their state machine; sends activation and revocation to routes; answers "is there an active grant for this vehicle, recipient, purpose and category?" | Consent grants, disclosure texts shown, grant events | Source licences and client purposes (Charter); person data beyond the grantor reference |
   | **Signalyard connectors** | One per manufacturer backend or aggregator: authentication, activation calls, webhooks, polling, the route's data dictionary, mapping to canonical signals | Route contracts and raw deliveries | Which vehicle a VIN belongs to (Anchor) |
   | **Telemetry store** | Canonical signals per vehicle, tagged with grant id, signal time and receipt time; deletion by grant | Raw and normalized telemetry | History events; anything after the grant's retention date |
   | **Promotion service** | Selects readings the grant allows to become history, at the granularity the grant allows, and writes them as history events | Promotion rules and their versions | The odometer judgement (Tracewell's rules) |
   | **Tracewell** | History events and judgements | Promoted readings as history events, with source class `manufacturer` | Raw telemetry |
   - **A statutory registry already uses connected readings.** Car-Pass, which takes manufacturers' quarterly connected readings, reported **345.5 million** odometer readings received in 2025 ([Car-Pass, 2026](https://www.car-pass.be/en)).
   - **The manufacturer is the consent bottleneck, and the car can refuse.** High Mobility's documentation shows activation taking days, depending on software updates, and failing when privacy mode is on ([High Mobility, n.d.](https://docs.high-mobility.com/docs/getting-started/fleet-clearance/activation-process.md)).
   - **Enrolment at the point of sale carries enforcement risk.** In January 2026 the US Federal Trade Commission (FTC) finalized an order against General Motors over allegations that it used "a misleading enrollment process" for OnStar and Smart Driver and sold drivers' geolocation and driving-behavior data.
   | **Route** | Direct manufacturer integrations | Aggregator | A few brands make up most of the fleet and volumes justify per-brand work | Many brands, small volumes, or a consumer flow that needs one consent screen |
   | **What to store** | Full telemetry kept long-term | Purpose-scoped telemetry plus coarse promoted history | Never for a third party: Art. 6(1) requires erasure when the purpose ends | Always, with promotion rules per grant |
   | **Granularity of history** | Every reading | One per month plus event dates (sale, return, inspection) | The user's own service needs daily data | Shared history: enough for the odometer judgement, far less revealing |
   | **Consent unit** | Per vehicle | Per fleet contract | Consumer and dealer-stock grants; anything that can end with a sale | Never alone; a fleet contract is the legal frame, but each VIN still needs its own state |
   | **Appraisal read** | Remote read via consumer consent | OBD reader on the forecourt | The customer has the manufacturer app and is present | No app account, older car, or no route for the brand; needs ePrivacy consent too |
   | **Revocation trigger** | Platform sales events | Register ownership changes plus nightly reconciliation | Never alone | Always; sales events can be an early warning |
   | **Legal right of the user to car data** | EU Data Act, applicable since 12 September 2025 | EU Data Act | No Data Act; access is contractual | No federal equivalent; access is contractual |
   | **Consent to read the car** | National implementations of ePrivacy Art. 5(3); EDPB 01/2020 | TDDDG (*Telekommunikation-Digitale-Dienste-Datenschutz-Gesetz*, the German telecommunications and digital services data protection act) § 25: access to terminal equipment only with consent, based on "clear and comprehensive information" ([TDDDG, accessed 2026-10](https://www.gesetze-im-internet.de/ttdsg/__25.html), translat
   | **Statutory connected-odometer flow** | BE: manufacturers' quarterly connected readings to Car-Pass.
   | **Pending change** | EU roadworthiness revision: quarterly connected readings to national registers (Council text, 2025; in negotiation) | Same | Not applicable | Not applicable |
   | **Security-related repair access** | SERMI, mandatory | SERMI, mandatory | SERMI voluntary, reported live from 1 April 2026 | No federal scheme; state right-to-repair laws (not verified for this chapter) |
   | **Main enforcement risk** | Purpose creep under Data Act Art. 6 and the GDPR | Same, with active state data protection authorities | UK GDPR and PECR; Information Commissioner's Office (ICO) guidance on vehicle identifiers under review | Deceptive-practice enforcement (FTC, state attorneys general) over enrolment and resale |

===== vehicle-acquisition 56
   - **17%** had no valuation id: the dealer had typed a retail figure from memory or from a portal.
   - **31%** had no reconditioning estimate to compare with actual cost.
   - **38%** of appraisals started on a phone, and about one in 16 of those lost photos or notes when the session timed out in an auction hall or a farm yard.
   | **Trade-in** (part-exchange) | Consumer buying another car from the dealer | Appraisal and offer, negotiated inside the sale | Margin scheme | Guided appraisal, MPP, offer, link to the deal |
   | **Consumer purchase** ("we buy your car") | Consumer not buying | Online estimate, then inspection and a firm offer | Margin scheme | Conditional online offer, inspection, final offer |
   | **Auction** (physical or online) | Lessors, rental fleets, dealers | Bidding | Usually VAT-qualifying from businesses | Lane mode: scan, MPP, bid limit |
   | **Business-to-business (B2B) trade** | Dealers, traders | Asking price or offer | Depends on the seller's VAT status | Trade listings, MPP, offer ([[b2b-trade-and-remarketing]]) |
   | **Lease and rental returns** | Lessors | Tender, auction or buy-back terms | VAT-qualifying | Condition report, MPP, bid |
   | **Import** | Foreign dealers, auctions | Asking price plus landed cost | Cross-border rules | Landed-cost MPP including registration tax ([[registration-and-trade-services]]) |
   An **appraisal** is the dealer's structured assessment of one car for a possible purchase, in five steps:
   A **trade-in** is a car the buyer hands over as part of buying another car from the same dealer.
   **It belongs to the dealer**: target margin, holding cost, expected discount from asking price and reconditioning rates are the dealer's settings; guide books publish inputs, not this number.
   **It has a point in time**: it uses today's market surface and expected days to sell ([[market-data]]).
   A **purchase offer** is the price a dealer commits to pay for a specific car, to a specific seller, under stated conditions, until a stated limit.
   - **Validity.** In its 2025 annual report (February 2026), AutoNation describes its "We'll Buy Your Car" guaranteed offer as "honored for 7 days or 500 miles at any of our locations" ([AutoNation 10-K, 2026](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/350698/000162828026007800/an-20251231.htm)).
   - **Conditions.** KBB's offer is redeemable after the dealer verifies condition and features.
   - **A basis.** The offer assumes a mileage, a condition and a specification.
   | **Appraise app** | Capture in desk or lane mode: scan, odometer, offer presentation; guided photos and checklist through the Lotwise Inspect component; offline outbox | Local drafts until synced |
   | **Appraisal service** | Appraisal and offer aggregates, their state and versions; composition of facts | Appraisal, offer versions, decision snapshot |
   | **MPP calculator** | Pure function: valuation, costs, policy and VAT basis in, MPP lines out | Nothing; deterministic and versioned |
   | **Buying policy** | Target margin by band, holding cost, negotiation allowance, recon rates, offer validity, approval limits | Policy versions per group, rooftop and user (in Baseline, [[onboarding-and-configuration]]) |
   | **Vehicle context** | Identity, register status, specification, history | Facts with provenance |
   | **Gauge** (condition service) | Inspection evidence, findings, reconditioning estimate from dealer rate cards | Condition report |
   | **Vantage** | Typed valuation with range, comparables, segment status | Valuation ledger |
   | **Dossier** | Stock item from the accepted offer | Stock item, cost layers, VAT regime |
   **The MPP calculator is a pure, versioned function**: the same valuation id, condition report, policy version and VAT basis always give the same lines, so it is testable, replayable after a correction, and identical in every channel.
   | **Acceptance** before expiry moves to purchase only after a handover check: odometer reading, quick condition confirmation, documents | A car that arrives with new damage goes to re-inspection, not to stock |
   | **Expiry** is a scheduled transition, and an expired offer cannot be accepted | The dealer may re-offer, but a new offer means a new version based on today's valuation |

   - **Background synchronization cannot be assumed.** MDN marks the Background Synchronization API as "not Baseline because it does not work in some of the most widely-used browsers" ([MDN, n.d., accessed 2026-10](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Background_Synchronization_API)).

   - **The VAT basis comes from the seller.** Lessors sell with VAT; dealers may sell under either regime.
   - **Condition comes from someone else's report**, with its own grading scale ([[condition-and-damage]]).
   - **The commitment is the bid.** The appraisal records the bid limit and the bids placed, so a buying desk can see how often its buyers paid above their own limits.
   | **Duplicate appraisals** | Offline submission retried; two salespeople, or two rooftops of one group, appraise the same car | Client-generated appraisal id; open offers indexed by tenant and AVK, checked on sync | Retries return the existing record; a second appraiser sees the first offer and its expiry before issuing; group policy decides whether to match it |
   | **Conflicting offer edits** | Salesperson and manager revise on two devices | Offer versions with optimistic concurrency | Second revision fails with "v2 issued by the sales manager at 14:02" and must supersede v2 explicitly |

   | **Market moves while offers are open** | Segment moves to `watch` | Segment status change event | Open-offer exposure report per segment; existing offers honored; new offers on the cautious point and shorter validity |

   | **Accepted offer, car differs at handover** | New damage, missing key, 900 km over the limit | Handover check against the offer's conditions | Re-inspection; a new version or withdrawal per the stated conditions; reason recorded |
   | **No MPP; value only** | Retail and trade values | Simple; dealer decides | Discipline depends on the person; no comparable record | Single-owner independents who buy every car themselves |
   | **MPP with lines** (Axleby default) | MPP and every line, offers up to MPP freely | Explainable; disputes are about a line; consistent across channels | Exposes margin targets to staff | Most independents and small groups |
   | **Suggested offer only; MPP hidden** | An opening offer and a ceiling that requires approval | Protects negotiation room | Staff cannot reason about the number; approval traffic | Groups with central buying desks |
   | **Automated binding offer** | Nothing; the system offers online | Scale; speed | Balance-sheet risk; needs segment circuit breakers and fast offer adjustment | Only with risk limits, as [[valuation-engine]] warns |

   | **Register action at purchase** | NL: stock-in to trade stock (*bedrijfsvoorraad*) with the dashboard odometer reading; seller receives the *vrijwaringsbewijs*; no completion during an outage (RDW).

===== inventory-and-vehicle-dossier 63
   - **38%** of history reports and **44%** of register transactions could be linked to a stock item only by plate and date heuristics, because the products shared no vehicle key.
   A **vehicle** is the physical car, identified by its vehicle identification number (VIN) and, in Axleby, by the **Axleby Vehicle Key (AVK)** in the identity graph Anchor ([[vehicle-identity]] owns both).
   A **stock item** is one tenant's ownership of one vehicle for one **stay**: from the moment the dealer commits to buy it until the moment the dealer no longer carries its risk.
   A **vehicle dossier** is the stock item together with everything the dealer needs to manage that stay: references to vehicle facts and to the versions used for decisions, documents, the results of module actions, and a timeline of what happened.
   | **What it is** | The physical car | One tenant's stay with that car | Stock item plus facts by reference, documents and timeline |
   | **Key** | AVK (resolves to VIN) | Stock item id; tenant; AVK; stay sequence | Same as the stock item |
   | **Owner** | Data half (Anchor, Specbook, Tracewell) | Dealer tenant, through the dealer product | Dealer tenant |
   | **Lifetime** | Decades, across owners | Days to months | Stay plus statutory retention |
   | **Shared across tenants?** | Yes, within licence | Never | Never |
   | **Display identifier** | Plate (time-bounded), VIN | Dealer stock number | Dealer stock number |
   - **Current facts are referenced** by AVK and read through the owning context: specification from Specbook, history from Tracewell, registration status from the register gateway.
   - **Decision inputs are snapshotted by identifier and version.** The Octavia's appraisal used one valuation from the Vantage valuation ledger, with its model version and timestamp, and the purchase was made against version 3 of its history report.
   - **Dealer assertions are the dealer's own facts.** "Two keys present", "service book complete", "tow bar fitted after first registration" are stock-item attributes, labeled as dealer-asserted, and never written back into the shared vehicle record without going through the data half's ingestion rules ([[ingestion-and-entity-resolution]]).
   The **VAT regime** is a stock-item attribute fixed at purchase: under the EU margin scheme, a car bought from a private seller is later sold with VAT due only on the margin, and the margin is the selling price minus the purchase price, not minus reconditioning ([Directive 2006/112/EC, Arts. 313–315](http://publications.europa.eu/resource/celex/32006L0112)). [[deals-and-sales-documents]] owns the margin scheme; the st
   **Target margin** is the planned front-end gross for this car, set at purchase from the appraisal's maximum purchase price ([[vehicle-acquisition]]) and revised by pricing ([[used-car-pricing]]).
   The **stock lifecycle** is the state machine behind those words, and the first design decision is that **they are not all states of the same machine.**
   | **Appraised** | An offer exists; the dealer does not own the car | Appraisal complete with valuation reference | appraisal-recorded |
   | **Purchased** | The dealer has committed to buy; cost and risk start | Accepted offer or purchase invoice; VAT regime set | stock-item-created |
   | **In transit** | Owned but not on site | Transport booked | location-changed |
   | **In reconditioning** | On site, being prepared | Arrival confirmed | preparation-started |
   | **Ready** | Retail-ready and available | Preparation track done, or dealer override with reason | stock-item-ready |
   | **Reserved** | Held for one buyer | Sale contract signed, or deposit or manager hold with expiry ([[deals-and-sales-documents]]) | stock-item-reserved |
   | **Sold** | The sale is binding and complete | The deal's conditions are met (payment or financing confirmed) and the invoice is issued, or delivery, where the dealer marks sold on delivery ([[deals-and-sales-documents]]) | stock-item-sold |
   | **Delivered** | Handed over; risk passed | Handover recorded; register or keeper change done where required | stock-item-delivered |
   | **Written off** | Left stock without a sale | Reason (total loss, theft, scrapped, returned to supplier) | stock-item-written-off |
   | **Preparation** | not started, inspection, awaiting approval, mechanical, body, detail, photos, done | Reconditioning workflow ([[condition-and-damage]]) | Starts at purchase; "done" is the guard for Ready |
   | **Advertising** | not advertised, desired live, live on N channels, withdrawing, withdrawn | Syndication ([[listing-syndication]]) | Reserved, Sold and Written off force "withdrawn" |
   | **Register** | e.g. in trade stock, registered to buyer, exported, deregistered | Register transactions ([[registration-and-trade-services]]) | Delivered requires the market's register step |
   | **Financing** | not floorplanned, floorplanned, curtailment due, paid off | Dealer finance, often the DMS | Sale triggers payoff; curtailment dates feed pricing |
   | **Dossier service** | Stock item lifecycle, guards, cost layers, documents, timeline | Stock item, dossier, timeline | Vehicle facts, price advice, channel listings, deals |
   | **Dossier BFF** (backend for frontend) | Composes the dossier view: stock item, vehicle facts with provenance, module status | Nothing | Any state |
   | **Module orchestrator** | Turns dossier actions ("order history report", "register to trade stock", "publish") into module commands; records outcomes on the timeline | Action records | The modules' results |
   | **Pricing and signals** | Price advice, repricing rules, stand-time signals ([[used-car-pricing]]) | Price proposals, signals | The asking price once accepted (the dossier owns it) |
   | **Stock list projection** | Searchable, group-wide stock view for desks and websites | Nothing (rebuildable) | Any decision |
   | **DMS adapter** | Maps DMS records to the dossier and back ([[dms-integration]]) | Cross-reference table | Fields the dossier owns |
   The **timeline** is an append-only list of what happened to the stock item: state transitions, price changes, cost lines, documents attached, module actions requested and their results, notes, DMS updates, and corrections to vehicle facts the dealer relied on.
   | **Location move** | Breda lot to Velto's central preparation hub | Location | None | Same stock item; location-changed event; transport cost line |
   | **Rooftop move, same legal entity** | Velto Tilburg to Velto Breda, both owned by Velto Nederland B.V. (one of Velto's five Dutch legal entities; name illustrative) | Selling rooftop, often location | None: same owner | Same stock item; rooftop-changed event; adverts re-pointed to the new rooftop's channel accounts |
   | **Entity move inside a group** | Velto Nederland B.V. to Velto België NV | Owner | A sale between companies: invoice, VAT, register transactions in both countries | Close the stock item as Sold (disposition: intra-group); create a new stock item linked to it |
   | **Sale to another dealer** | Velto to an independent trader | Owner | Normal trade sale | Sold; the buyer's stock item, if on Lotwise, is a separate tenant's record |
   - **Optimistic concurrency on the aggregate.** Every command carries the version the caller read; a stale transition fails with the current state and who changed it ("reserved by Breda at 10:02").
   - **Field ownership limits the conflict surface.** The DMS adapter writes only DMS-owned fields, pricing only the price; writes to disjoint fields are merged without a version conflict and both recorded.
   - **Transitions are commands, not field writes.** Nobody sets `status = sold`.
   - **Idempotency by source identity.** Every external writer supplies an idempotency key: the DMS source version, the register transaction id, the module action id.
   - **Out-of-order updates are resolved by source version, not arrival time.** A polling DMS adapter with an overlap window can deliver an older "in stock" after a newer "sold"; the dossier keeps the last applied source version per field group and ignores older ones.
   - **Offline capture creates proposals.** An appraisal captured offline proposes a stock item when it syncs; if the same car was created meanwhile, the dossier matches by AVK and tenant and a human merges the two.
   - **Cost per car.** AutoNation's specific-identification costing and its list of capitalized costs (2026 filing, above) is the accounting form of the cost layers in this chapter.
   - **The inventory system starts at preparation.** Carvana's 2025 annual report (published February 2026) says each acquired vehicle is transported to a reconditioning site, "at which point the vehicle enters our inventory management system" ([Carvana 10-K, 2026](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1690820/000169082026000009/cvna-20251231.htm)).
   - **Validate before resync.** After the June 2024 CDK Global ransomware attack, Sonic Automotive told the US Securities and Exchange Commission (August 2024) that it resumed DMS transactions on 30 June only after "internal risk assessments and data validation procedures" ([Sonic 10-Q, 2024](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1043509/000104350924000065/sah-20240630.htm)).
   - **Statutory stock records.** HMRC's guidance (2021) requires "a stock book that tracks each item sold under the margin scheme individually" ([HMRC, 2021](https://www.gov.uk/guidance/using-the-vat-margin-scheme-for-second-hand-vehicles)); US dealers keep odometer statements for five years (see the market table below).
   | **Capitalized cost** | **€15,513** | |
   | **Gross after margin VAT** | **€1,786** | |
   | **Status column on a stock table** | One row per car, one status field, audit log on the side | Simple; every developer understands it | Tracks collide in one field; transitions are field writes without guards; audit is optional | A single-rooftop product with no syndication or DMS |
   | **Aggregate with state machine, tracks and timeline** (recommended) | Stock item root, guarded commands, coupled track changes, timeline in the same transaction | Invariants enforced; history complete; current state cheap to read | More design work; tracks need clear owners | Multi-module dealer SaaS with syndication, DMS and registers |
   | **Full event sourcing** | State rebuilt from an event stream | Perfect history; temporal queries | Schema evolution of events; harder migration from a monolith; human notes and receipts fit awkwardly | A greenfield team with event-sourcing experience and no legacy store to migrate |
   | **Dossier for everything** | One record; no sync | Competes with the DMS; duplicates accounting | Independents without a DMS |
   | **DMS for everything; dossier mirrors** | No ownership arguments | Dealer product stops when the DMS does; sold-state lag causes zombie listings | Rarely; only for read-only analytics products |
   | **Field-level ownership** (recommended) | Each side owns what it does well; dossier can run alone | Ownership must be configured, documented and enforced per DMS | Franchised dealers and mixed groups |
   | **Register status of stock** | Dealers recognized for trade stock (*erkenning bedrijfsvoorraad*) register bought cars to trade stock; such cars owe no motor-vehicle tax, have no periodic inspection (APK) obligation and may not be parked on the public road ([Ondernemersplein, n.d.](https://ondernemersplein.overheid.nl/wetten-en-regels/erkenning-bedrijfsvoorraad-voor-voertuigen-aanvragen/)) | No trade-stock register 
   | **Moving unregistered stock** | Trade plates for test drives (same source) | Dealer plate (*handelaarsplaat*), Z prefix, valid one year, for vehicles the dealer owns "with a view to promotion and sale" ([Vlaanderen.be, n.d.](https://www.vlaanderen.be/handelaarsplaat)) | Red plates ("06") for test and transfer drives, with one page per vehicle in the vehicle booklet and trip records kept one year ([FZV §41](https://
   | **Documents the dossier must hold** | Register proofs (indemnification, registration in the buyer's name), odometer judgement (*Nationale AutoPas*, NAP) | Car-Pass odometer certificate, which a seller must give a private buyer ([Car-Pass, n.d.](https://www.car-pass.be/en/faq)) | Registration certificate part II, warranty-shortening agreement where used (§476 BGB) | V5C reference, margin-scheme records | Odometer st
   | **Statutory stock record** | Margin-scheme records under national VAT law | As NL | As NL | Stock book tracking each margin-scheme vehicle individually (HMRC, 2021) | No VAT; state sales tax at registration |
   | **DMS landscape that shapes ownership** | Local vendors for independents; brand-approved DMSs for franchised dealers | Not verified; importer influence likely | Brand-shaped; OEM-affiliated DMSs common | Keyloop, Pinewood for large groups | Concentrated: CDK, Reynolds, Tekion and others with certified programs |
===== used-car-pricing 61
   - An **age-step rule**: cut 2.5% at 30, 45 and 60 days in stock, then 1.5% a week from day 75 until the price reached cost plus €300, and offer the car on Tradefloor, Axleby's trade exchange, at day 90.
   The **asking price** is the price at which the dealer offers a car to consumers, on its own website and on portals.
   The **valuation** is Vantage's estimate of what the car is worth, with a value type, a range and a confidence flag.
   The **target margin** is the planned front-end gross for this car, set at purchase from the maximum purchase price ([[vehicle-acquisition]]).
   The **transaction price** is what the buyer finally pays after negotiation and trade-in, recorded on the deal ([[deals-and-sales-documents]]).
   **Price position** is the asking price divided by a **market reference value** for the same car, expressed as a percentage: a car at 97% is asked 3% below the market reference.
   **Stand-time** is how long a car has been for sale; [[market-data]] owns it as a market measure, computed from adverts across all dealers.
   A **stand-time signal** is a per-car alert that a car has stood longer than it should, **given its segment, its price position and the market's current pace**.
   | **Above market** | Position above 104% and outside the reference range, reference confidence `normal` | Valuation, asking price |
   | **Below market** | Position below 96% with at least average demand: gross may be left on the table | Valuation, leads and views |
   | **Stand-time** | Days since first advert beyond the 70th percentile of expected days-to-sell for the segment and position | Market stand-time, dealer history |
   | **No response** | Fewer enquiries in 14 live days than a quarter of the segment median | Leads ([[leads-and-crm]]), advert views |
   | **Stale price** | Price unchanged for 21 days while the reference moved more than 3% | Valuation history |
   | **Financial deadline** | Curtailment or internal age limit within 10 days | Stock item funding |
   A **repricing rule** is a versioned, declarative instruction that, for cars matching a scope and a signal, computes a proposed new asking price, subject to guardrails, and either proposes it to a person or applies it.
   | **Scope** | Velto group, all NL and BE rooftops, retail stock, not reserved, not in the EV segment while its status is `watch` or `restricted` |
   | **Trigger** | Above-market signal, or stand-time signal with position above 100% |
   | **Target** | Move toward a target position by tier: high potential 100%, standard 98%, low potential 96% |
   | **Guardrails** | Floor and ceiling, maximum step 4%, at most one change per car per 7 days, no change under €100 |
   | **Mode** | Propose to the desk (default) or apply automatically (only for named, low-risk rules) |
   | **Version and owner** | Version 7, effective 18 June 2027, owner: Velto pricing desk; approved by Mirjam Kuipers |
   | **Reference provider** | Supplies each stock car's market reference: Vantage retail asking value with range, confidence and segment status; licensed portal ratings where available | Nothing; reads [[valuation-engine]] | The valuation |
   | **Signal generator** | Computes price position and the six signals per car, nightly and on relevant events (new cost, new price, new lead) | Signals with their inputs and dates | Prices |
   | **Rule engine** | Evaluates repricing rules from Baseline against signals; produces proposals; runs simulations | Proposals, rule runs | Rules' content (Baseline), prices |
   | **Pricing desk workspace** | Lists signals and proposals by group, region and rooftop; bulk approve, edit or reject with a reason | Approval decisions | Who may approve (authorization) |
   | **Dossier service** | Applies a price change conditionally on the stock-item version; records price history; emits the price event | Asking price, price history | Signals, proposals |
   | **Outcome service** | Joins price history to leads, sales, gross and days; compares treated and control rooftops | Outcome metrics per rule version | Decisions |
   **Delete-and-recreate is never a pricing tool**: it resets the portal's price history and days on market ([[listing-syndication]]).
   **A price change is a local decision that must not wait for remote systems**: the dossier accepts it, and the remote effects follow with visible status.
   - **A VAT-aware floor.** The floor is the lowest price at which front-end gross after VAT meets the rule's minimum.
   - **A ceiling**, usually the top of the reference range plus a margin, so a rule cannot raise prices beyond what the evidence supports.
   - **Step and frequency limits**: a maximum change per step (4% at Velto), at most one rule change per car per 7 days, and no change below €100 or 0.75%.
   - **Reference quality gates**: rules act only on references flagged `normal`; `thin_sample`, `fallback_model`, `spec_uncertain` or `stale_market` downgrade an automatic action to a proposal.
   - **Blast-radius stop**: a run that would change more than 25% of a tenant's stock, or move the average price by more than 2%, pauses for named approval.
   - **State exclusions**: reserved, sold, in a deal, or locked by the desk.
   - **Simulation before activation.** Every new rule version is first run against current stock and shown as a list: "this version would change 412 prices today, average −2.1%, 31 blocked by floor".
   - **Central pricing in groups.** AutoNation's 2025 annual report (February 2026) describes "our One Price used vehicle centralized pricing and appraisal strategy" ([AutoNation 10-K, 2026](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/350698/000162828026007800/an-20251231.htm)).
   - **Algorithmic pricing per car.** CarMax's description (2026, quoted above) is the clearest public statement that pricing is per vehicle, balanced between turn and gross, and adjusted for "pricing elasticity".
   - **From calendar to potential.** Pollak's 2019 and 2023 interviews (above) document the change from age-based cuts to per-car potential tiers inside the company that popularized velocity pricing.
   - **Portals as price authorities.** Auto Trader UK's FY2026 results (May 2026) describe tools for retailers covering "pricing, retail rating, market health, demand, supply, vehicle marketplace performance and predicted days to sell", and price flags built on its specification-adjusted valuations ([Auto Trader Group, 2026](https://plc.autotrader.co.uk/media/usjleen0/full-year-press-release-fy26.pdf)). mobile.de expose
   - **The dealer tool as a market instrument.** Cox Automotive states (January 2026) that its monthly US retail used-sales estimates "are based on observed unit changes tracked by vAuto", from vAuto Live Market View data over the most recent 30 days ([Cox Automotive, 2026](https://www.coxautoinc.com/insights/used-retail-vehicle-sales-december-2025/)).
   **Relay's Thursday peak disappeared**: Velto's weekly price changes fell from about 2,260 to about 800, and the remaining ones were spread across the week.
   **Pricing design is also capacity design**: frequency limits and minimum changes remove load no adapter tuning would.
   | **Duplicate application** (run retried after a timeout, user double-submits) | Idempotency key: run id + stock item, or command id | Absolute target price makes a replay harmless; dossier returns "duplicate, no change" |
   | **Conflicting updates** (rule versus salesperson, desk versus rooftop, car reserved meanwhile) | Version-conditional write; desk lock flag on the stock item | Rule re-reads and re-evaluates; a manual price wins over a rule for 7 days unless the desk lifts it; reserved cars are skipped |
   | **Incomplete records** (no cost, no VAT regime, edition unknown) | Missing floor; reference flagged `spec_uncertain` | No automatic action; task to the rooftop to complete the record; position shown with a wide range |
   | **Untrustworthy reference** (thin sample, stale surface, segment `watch` or `restricted`) | Reference flags and segment status on every signal | Automatic actions become proposals with a warning; runs never start on a surface older than 24 hours |
   | **Runaway rule** (wrong threshold, unit error, bad index) | Simulation diff; blast-radius stop; maximum step | Run pauses for approval; rollback by re-applying each car's previous price as a new event with reason `rollback`, never by editing history |
   | **Partial failure** (price applied, some channels failed) | Desired-state lag per channel listing ([[observability-and-data-operations]]) | Relay converges each channel to the latest price; the dossier shows "price live on 4 of 5 channels" |
   | **Corrected fact** (wrong mileage or edition behind the reference) | Correction event names the fact versions it replaces; price events carry the reference snapshot | Find every price set against the wrong reference, re-propose, and tell the dealer which adverts showed the wrong price ([[journey-data-correction]]) |
   | **Portal rejects the price** (below a portal minimum, missing mandatory price fields) | Channel error classified as permanent | Parked without retry; desk sees the rule and the channel's reason |
   | **External outages** (Vantage, Pulse or Relay unavailable) | Health checks; surface age | Manual pricing continues; signals show their date; channel updates wait in desired state and send the latest price once on recovery |
   | **Manual, by experience** | Captures local knowledge; no rule risk | Inconsistent; does not scale to a group; no record of reasons | Small independents with a few dozen cars |
   | **Age-step cuts (velocity)** | Simple; guarantees movement; easy to explain | Cuts cheap cars, ignores expensive young ones, gives gross away; abandoned by its own author | As a last-resort backstop for a financial deadline, not as the method |
   | **Market position target** | Market-anchored; explainable with comparables | Only as good as the reference; pushes everyone toward the same price | Dense segments with a trustworthy reference |
   | **Per-car potential or return tiers** | Matches price effort to each car's gross and turn potential; current documented direction | Needs good reference, demand and cost data; harder to explain | Groups with central desks and complete cost data |
   | **Fully algorithmic per car** | Consistent at scale; uses elasticity and probability of sale | Model risk, drift, a hard explanation problem; needs a pricing science team | Large retailers that own data and outcomes, as CarMax describes |
   | **Advice only** | Signals and suggested prices; people decide | Low; signals ignored | Independents; first rollout |
   | **Propose and approve** | Rules produce proposals; desk approves in bulk | Approval fatigue; rubber-stamping | Group desks; new rule versions |
   | **Automatic within bands** | Small in-band moves applied; the rest proposed | A wrong reference moves many prices quietly | Proven rule versions on `normal` segments, with blast-radius stops |
   | **Fully automatic** | Rules apply all changes | Runaway rules; drift spreads straight to every advert | Rarely; only with a control group and fast rollback |
===== merchandising 66
   **Merchandising** is the first sale: producing complete, accurate and attractive adverts for every car in stock, quickly, and keeping them true while the car and its facts change.
   | **Stock-in to first live advert** | **7.0** |
   | **Stock item** | One stay of one car in the dealer's stock, with cost, state and location | Dossier ([[inventory-and-vehicle-dossier]]) | Stock-in to delivery or write-off |
   | **Advert** | The channel-independent offer content for one stock item: title, description, equipment text, ordered media, price presentation, disclosures, availability | Merchandising (this chapter) | First draft to withdrawal |
   | **Channel listing** | One advert on one channel, with the portal's id and desired and actual state | Syndication ([[listing-syndication]]) | Insert to confirmed withdrawal |
   | **Referenced facts** | Make, model, edition, engine, first registration, mileage, color, CO2, odometer judgement, history summary | Anchor, Specbook, Tracewell, the dossier | No; corrections go to the fact's owner, which records the dealer as a source | Advert re-renders |
   | **Derived content** | Equipment list, highlights, generated title and description, availability text | Rendered from facts | Only through stored, checked overrides | Re-rendered; overrides re-checked |
   | **Commercial terms** | Asking price, price-reduction claim, financing example, warranty, delivery terms | Pricing ([[used-car-pricing]]), lender rate cards ([[deals-and-sales-documents]]), dealer settings | Through their owning modules | Re-rendered; disclosures recomputed |
   | **Dealer-authored content** | Personal note, photos, video, photo order | The dealer | Yes | Checked for stale numbers and unsupported claims |
   | **Disclosures** | Financing warning and example, energy data, mileage certificate link, prior price on reductions | Per-market rules evaluated on the other four kinds | No | Recomputed on every revision |
   The **vehicle media pipeline** is the path a car's photos and video take from capture to channel, with the rights and retention rules that govern them.
   The **listing quality score** is a per-advert measure of completeness and correctness, computed from explicit checks and delivered with a ranked list of actions.
   | **Advert composer** | The advert aggregate and its revisions; field-kind rules; dealer overrides; re-render triggers | Facts, price, channel payloads |
   | **Media pipeline** | Media assets, processing, classification, order, rights, retention | Per-channel image counts and renditions (syndication renders them) |
   | **Description generator and claim checker** | Fact sheets, generated text per language, claim verification, generation records | The dealer's decision to publish |
   | **Disclosure rules** | Versioned per-market rule sets and their evaluation | Legal interpretation (legal owns it; product encodes it) |
   | **Quality scorer** | Checks, score, ranked actions, calibration against outcomes | Channel acceptance rules ([[listing-syndication]]) |
   - **Background replacement** is common practice, offered by specialist vendors and some portals.
   - **Masking.** Other cars' plates and bystanders' faces are personal data in Axleby's European markets and are blurred by default ([[privacy-and-retention]]).
   - **Angle classification.** Each photo gets an angle tag.
   - **The dealer owns its photos.** Axleby holds a licence to process and publish them to the channels the dealer chooses.
   - **Photos never cross tenants.** When a car's vehicle identification number (VIN) returns to stock at another dealer, the new dealer starts with an empty set, even though Axleby holds 24 photos of that car.
   - **Market data does not see dealer photos.** Pulse, the market data lake, works from harvested adverts under portal agreements, not from Lotwise's photo store; Charter records the scope ([[data-licensing-and-governance]]).
   - **Retention follows the stay.** Masters are kept 24 months after the stock item closes, as evidence in condition disputes, then deleted.
   | **As-built confirmed** | Edition, standard equipment and factory options | "Factory-fitted: panoramic roof, electrically folding towbar" |
   | **Edition resolved** | Edition and standard equipment; no options | Options omitted; dealer prompted to confirm visible options |
   | **Dealer-confirmed** | Edition and equipment as confirmed | Listed normally; stored source "dealer-supplied" |
   | **Edition unknown** | Only equipment standard on **every** remaining candidate edition | Edition name omitted; one question to the dealer |
   | **Disputed** | The last agreed view | Flagged in the dealer's actions; no generated claims about the disputed attribute |
   **Order by what buyers value**: 140 standard items in catalog order bury the four that matter.
   **Localize by canonical id**: labels come from the canonical feature catalog in each language, never from translated dealer text, and the same ids map to portal codes in syndication ([[specification-and-equipment]] owns the mapping).
   **Prices and regulated figures never enter prose**: a monthly payment in a sentence is a disclosure without its rules and a copy that will not follow the next price change.
   **History claims are licensed phrases, not free claims**: "accident-free" is a promise no history source can make, while "no damage records found in the sources checked on [date]" states what the platform knows, and even that appears only if Charter allows Tracewell's facts for the purpose "public advert" ([[data-licensing-and-governance]]).
   **Battery claims need a certificate**: a state-of-health (SoH) figure for a battery-electric vehicle enters the fact sheet only from a verified test certificate no older than 180 days and 7,500 km, with issuer, date and method ([[ev-battery-health]]).
   The **claim checker** is the component that matters.
   - **Germany**: the Price Indication Ordinance (*Preisangabenverordnung*, PAngV) §17(4) requires an example at an APR that the advertiser can expect **at least two thirds** of resulting contracts to meet or beat ([PAngV §17, accessed 2026-10](https://www.gesetze-im-internet.de/pangv_2022/__17.html)).
   - **United Kingdom**: the Financial Conduct Authority's (FCA) Consumer Credit sourcebook, CONC 3.5.5R, requires figures the firm "reasonably expects … to be representative of credit agreements to which the representative APR applies", and its guidance applies a **51%** test to the representative APR ([FCA Handbook, CONC 3.5, accessed 2026-10](https://www.handbook.fca.org.uk/handbook/CONC/3/5.html)).
   - **United States**: Regulation Z makes "the amount of any payment" a triggering term that requires the down payment, terms of repayment and APR ([12 CFR 1026.24(d), CFPB, accessed 2026-10](https://www.consumerfinance.gov/rules-policy/regulations/1026/24/)).
   | **Identity and facts** | Edition resolved or confirmed; mileage from a dated source | Wrong facts cost sales and trust |
   | **Photos** | Required angles present (front three-quarter, dashboard, odometer); quality flags; retail photos after bodywork | Coverage, not count, predicts enquiries |
   | **Text** | Description in each advertised language; all claims supported; no stale numbers | Unsupported text is a liability |
   | **Commercial terms** | Price present; financing example valid; warranty stated | Buyers filter on them |
   | **Freshness** | Rendered from current fact versions; photos newer than the last condition change | Catches the Sticky Error |
   **The product is the ranked action list**: "Add the dashboard and odometer photos", "Confirm whether the car has a panoramic roof", "Your description repeats an old mileage", each with an estimated effect on enquiries.
   | **A fact is corrected upstream** (Odometer Correction, edition dispute) | Fact-change events; stale-content scan of dealer text | Re-render referenced and derived content; flag dealer text with the old figure; withdraw from channels if a disputed claim stays in the text past the dealer's response window |
   | **Duplicate photos** (same upload twice; one photo on two cars of a rooftop) | Content hash per stock item; perceptual hash across the rooftop's live stock | Exact duplicates collapse to one asset; a near-duplicate across two cars raises an action, not an automatic removal |
   | **Partial upload** (12 of 24 photos arrived) | Device upload manifest against received assets | Set shown as incomplete; publishing with what arrived is the dealer's explicit choice and lowers the score |
   | **Conflicting edits** (dealer edits text while a price change re-renders) | Optimistic concurrency on the revision | Price and disclosure blocks re-render without touching dealer text; a save on an outdated revision merges by content kind, not last writer |
   | **Processing or model provider outage** | Health checks; queue age per processor | Cosmetic processing replays later; generation falls back to fact templates; no advert waits for either |
   | **Rate card stale or lender service down** | Rate-card age; quote errors | Monthly payment and example removed until a valid card returns |
   | **Rule input missing** (no first-registration date) | Evaluation error | Publication blocked in that market with a specific action; a missing input is never a reason to skip the rule |
   | **A generated claim slips through** | Buyer complaint linked to the revision | Generation record identifies fact-sheet and checker versions; the case joins the checker's regression set; texts from the same checker version are rescanned |
   | **Dealer free text** | Voice, local knowledge | Slow; copies facts that drift; unchecked claims | Small dealers who want it, with the stale-content check on |
   | **Templates from facts** | Deterministic, consistent, cheap | Repetitive; buyers discount boilerplate | Fallback; low-value cars; untested languages |
   | **LLM from raw car data** | Fluent, fast | Unsupported claims; no provenance; hard to re-check | Never for public adverts |
   | **LLM from a fact sheet with claim checking** | Fluent and provable; re-renderable; auditable | Checker and regression set to maintain; per-language evaluation | Default at Lotwise scale |
   | **In-house pipeline** | Control of rights, masking and retention; one master set for all channels | Models for background and classification to build or license |
   | **Specialist photo vendor** | Studio-grade backgrounds; capture apps | Photos, and people in them, leave the platform; vendor reuse terms; another outage source |
   | **Portal-side tools** | Free with the package | Helps one channel; the dealer website and other portals see unprocessed photos |
   | **Languages** | NL: Dutch.
   | **Financing example** | CCD2 from 20 Nov 2026: warning plus representative example; transposition varies | CCD2 and PAngV §17: example at an APR that at least two thirds of resulting contracts meet or beat | FCA CONC 3.5: representative example; 51% test for the representative APR | Regulation Z: a payment amount triggers down payment, repayment terms and APR |
   | **Energy and CO2 data** | Directive 1999/94/EC covers new cars; used cars outside it | Pkw-EnVKV: used cars exempt, "new" presumed at ≤ 8 months or ≤ 1,000 km; AutoScout24 enforces fuel and CO2 fields measured under the Worldwide Harmonised Light Vehicles Test Procedure (WLTP) from 16 Sept 2026 | Not researched for used-car adverts | Not researched for used-car adverts |
   | **Mileage evidence** | NL: public register odometer judgement (NAP) per plate.
