   | **Price-reduction claims** | EU 30-day prior lowest price (Directive 98/6/EC Art. 6a) | Same EU rule | No equivalent rule verified; general misleading-actions test (DMCC Act 2024) | Not researched |
   | **Misleading content** | UCPD as transposed | UCPD as transposed | DMCC Act 2024, from 6 April 2025 | Not researched |
   | **Portal-enforced content** | Car-Pass link (BE); plate enrichment by Marktplaats (NL) | WLTP data for near-new cars | Write-off categories A/B rejected; no "price on application" | Google requires VIN and dealer details on the landing page |
===== listing-syndication 60
   **Listing syndication** is the capability that does this: publishing one dealer's stock to many advertising channels and keeping each copy in step with the stock record.
   A **channel** is one destination where a dealer's stock is advertised under one contract: a portal in one country (mobile.de, AutoScout24.nl, Auto Trader UK), a general classifieds site's car category (Marktplaats, 2dehands), an advertising catalog (Google vehicle ads, a Meta automotive catalog), a dealer website, or a B2B exchange.
   A **channel listing** is one car on one channel: the pairing of a stock item from the dossier ([[inventory-and-vehicle-dossier]] owns the stock item) with a channel, plus the identifiers both sides use for it, the content last sent, and the state the channel reports.
   | **Platform listing key** | The dealer platform | Lotwise stock id plus channel id | The platform's own join; written into the portal's external-id field |
   | **Portal listing id** | The portal | mobile.de `mobileAdId`; AutoScout24 listing UUID; Auto Trader `stockId` | Addressing updates and deletes; reading status back |
   | **Creation key** | The dealer platform, once per creation attempt | mobile.de `X-Mobile-Insertion-Request-Id` | Making a retried create safe |
   | **Portal write API** | mobile.de Seller API; AutoScout24 Listing Creation API; Auto Trader Connect Stock API; Marktplaats/2dehands API | Per-listing REST calls, each with its own update and delete semantics | Change streams (mobile.de Ad Stream WebSocket), webhooks (Auto Trader Stock Notifications), list endpoints |
   | **File feed** | US portals through inventory-management providers; legacy FTP uploads | Full snapshot of the rooftop's stock, generated on a schedule | Usually none; sometimes an error report |
   | **Ad catalog** | Google vehicle ads (Merchant Center); Meta automotive catalog | Catalog items in a file, sheet or API; availability flags | Diagnostics in the ad tool |
   | **Portal as syndicator** | AutoScout24 `mobile_de` and `AutoTrack` channels; Auto Trader `exportAdvert` | A flag on a listing in another channel | Through the first portal, if at all |
   | **Publish rules** | Which cars go to which channels, when, with which price and content variant | The car's facts, price or photos |
   | **Pre-publish validation** | Each channel's mandatory fields, regulatory rules and policy rules, evaluated before sending | Fixing the content (that is [[merchandising]]) |
   | **Desired-state store** | One record per channel listing: present or absent, content version, deadline, three identifiers, last acknowledged version, last error | The portal's actual state |
   | **Scheduler (the publish queue)** | Which channel listings need attention, in which priority, within each channel's rate budget | What to send |
   | **Channel adapter** | Translation to one channel's contract, its idempotency, its error classification, its observers | Business rules about which cars to publish |
   | **Reconciler** | The comparison of desired and actual state, drift classification, repair and alerts | Overruling a portal lock or a dealer's edit on the portal |
   | **Transient** | Timeouts, 5xx, connection resets | Retry with exponential backoff and jitter; the channel listing keeps its place among others |
   | **Throttled** | HTTP 429, a reputation-based 403 at Auto Trader | Slow the whole channel's token bucket; do not retry the single call faster |
   | **Permanent for this content** | HTTP 400 validation errors, unknown equipment code, missing Car-Pass URL | Stop; mark the channel listing *rejected* with a reason the dealer can act on; retry only when the desired content changes |
   | **Locked or blocked** | `ad-only-modifiable-in-dealer-area`; Auto Trader deal holding a reservation | Stop; mark *locked* or *blocked*; show the dealer; reconcile later |
   | **mobile.de** | DELETE removes the ad; "All associated images will be purged as well" |
   | **AutoScout24** | DELETE; images of the deleted listing are deleted |
   | **Auto Trader** | Lifecycle states `DUE_IN`, `FORECOURT`, `SALE_IN_PROGRESS`, `SOLD`, `WASTEBIN`, `DELETED`; "all advert destinations should be first updated to NOT_PUBLISHED" before setting the later states; partners are "prohibited from updating the lifecycleState once the reservationDealId is present" |
   | **Marktplaats** | A `closeDate` on the advertisement |
   | **Meta catalog** | `availability` set to `not_available`, or `status` archived |
   | **Snapshot feeds** | Absence from the next file |
   **Reads of the portal's listings**: Auto Trader's stock search (up to 200 results per page), AutoScout24's listing endpoint with `include=overwriteProtection` (added 2026-09-03), mobile.de's seller ads.
   | **Zombie** | Desired absent, portal shows live | Re-mark for withdrawal at top priority; alert if past deadline |
   | **Missing** | Desired present, portal shows nothing | Re-mark for creation; check for a portal-side deletion first (fraud, phishing) |
   | **Stale** | Content version on portal older than desired | Re-mark for update |
   | **Orphan** | Portal listing carries no known external id | Match by vehicle identity; adopt, or flag as a possible duplicate |
   | **Foreign edit** | Dealer changed price on the portal | Apply the field-ownership rule: overwrite, adopt or flag |
   | **Locked** | Overwrite protection on | Mark *Locked*; stop writing; show the dealer |
   | **Create retried after a timeout** creates a duplicate | Orphan listings in reconciliation; same external id twice | Use the portal's creation key where it exists (mobile.de); otherwise look up by external id before re-creating; delete the newer duplicate, keep the one with history |
   | **Duplicate across channels** via a portal-as-syndicator export | Same VIN on a portal from two sources; dealer complaint | Model "reached via"; publish to the destination directly or via the syndicator, never both |
   | **Duplicate across rooftops** after a group transfer | Two stock items with one VIN in one group | One owning rooftop per channel listing |
   | **Conflicting update**: dealer edits price on the portal | Push notification with a non-platform source; stale or foreign-edit drift | Apply the field-ownership rule; show the dealer which value is live where |
   | **Concurrent writes** for one car on one channel | Lost update, version mismatch | Serialize per channel listing; send full desired state |
   | **Incomplete record**: missing mandatory field | Pre-publish validation; HTTP 400 | Mark *Rejected* with a reason the dealer can fix; retry only on content change |
   | **Unknown code** in a mapping table | Build-time mapping test; permanent error class | Block release of the catalog change until mapped; never retry |
   | **Partial failure**: one image of twelve fails | Adapter result; AutoScout24 cancels the whole call | Retry the whole convergence once the image is available; do not publish a half-updated listing |
   | **Portal throttling** | 429s; rising latency | Slow the channel's token bucket; keep the withdrawal reservation |
   | **Portal outage** | Error rate, synthetic car failure | Open the channel's circuit; desired state keeps accumulating; on recovery, converge current state rather than replaying history; tell dealers which channel is affected |
   | **Missed push event** | Reconciliation finds stale actual state | Periodic pull behind every push subscription |
   | **Portal-side withdrawal or restore** (fraud, phishing, auto-expiry) | Missing or orphan drift | Do not re-create blindly; check the portal reason; tell the dealer |
   | **Truncated DMS feed** would withdraw a rooftop | Mass-withdrawal guard | Pause withdrawals above threshold; human confirmation ([[dms-integration]]) |
   | **Withdrawal blocked** by a portal deal | *Blocked* state | Treat the portal's reservation as a stock event; withdraw from all other channels |
   | **Sync semantics** | Command queue (insert, update, delete in order) | Desired state with convergence | Few channels, low volume, all with idempotent upserts and reliable deletes | Many channels, bursts of change, unreliable channels, any need to know what the portal should show (almost always) |
   | **Queue key** | Per rooftop or dealer | Per channel listing | Never for writes; acceptable for batch file generation | Always for API channels |
   | **Update payload** | PATCH of changed fields | Full desired state | Portal PATCH is documented lossless for the fields sent, and payloads are large | Default; mandatory where PATCH is lossy (AutoScout24 financing fields) or lists are replace-only (Auto Trader) |
   | **Actual state** | Trust call responses and push | Push plus periodic pull and reconciliation | Never on its own | Always; pull frequency by channel volume |
   | **Feed style** | Per-listing API | Full snapshot file | Portal offers an API; change volume is high; latency matters | US portals via providers; ad catalogs; channels with no API; with a mass-withdrawal guard |
   | **Adapter sourcing** | In-house adapter | Aggregator or portal-as-syndicator | Channels with material lead share, regulatory rules or deal locks | Long-tail channels where the platform can accept weaker read-back |
   | **Main channels** | Marktplaats and 2dehands/2ememain (one API); AutoScout24 with AutoTrack and Gaspedaal after the November 2025 takeover; Google vehicle ads in open beta in NL (not listed for BE) | mobile.de, AutoScout24.de, Kleinanzeigen; Google vehicle ads in open beta | Auto Trader dominant; Motors, CarGurus UK, eBay Motors, Gumtree, Carwow, PistonHeads (Auto Trader's own competitive set, FY26) | Cars.com, Car
   | **Publisher contract** | REST APIs with partner configuration (Marktplaats "Car Dealer API partner"; AutoScout24) | REST APIs for TSPs (mobile.de Seller API; AutoScout24) | Auto Trader Connect APIs with integration capabilities | No public portal write API found; files through inventory-management and website providers |
   | **Withdrawal model** | Delete or close date | Delete | Rich lifecycle with deal and reservation locks and auto-transitions | Absence from the next snapshot |
   | **Vehicle taxonomy** | Portal enriches from the plate (Marktplaats); AutoScout24 integer IDs | KBA type codes (*HSN/TSN*, manufacturer and type key numbers) plus Schwacke/`natCode`; mobile.de reference data | Auto Trader derivative taxonomy plus OEM factory codes | VIN decoding |
   | **Regulatory fields enforced by portals** | BE: Car-Pass mileage URL mandatory on AutoScout24 from 2026-06-01 | WLTP fuel and CO2 data for near-new cars under the passenger car energy labelling ordinance (*Pkw-EnVKV*), enforced by AutoScout24 from 2026-09-16 | Write-off categories rejected; minimum price; no "price on application" | No portal-side regulatory validation found; FTC and state advertising rules apply |
   | **Back-channel** | Signed webhooks for leads (Marktplaats); list reads | Ad Stream WebSocket with 24-hour catch-up; Lead API | Stock and deal notifications (signed webhooks, short retry window) | Typically none beyond error reports |
   | **Consolidation risk** | Highest: two of the main portals now share AutoScout24's platform | AutoScout24 exports to mobile.de | Auto Trader exports to third parties (`exportAdvert`) | Cox owns both an inventory tool and a portal |
===== leads-and-crm 44
   A **lead** is the commercial unit of the dealer's marketing.
   | **Enquiry event** | One inbound message from a source: a webhook call, a cursor event, an ADF e-mail, a web-form post, a tracked call | The source's event id, or a hash of the raw message | Marktplaats webhook delivery of a `MessagingLead` |
   | **Lead** | A buyer's interest in a vehicle (or a kind of vehicle) at one rooftop, with a lifecycle from new to won or lost | Switchboard lead id, plus each source's lead id as a reference | "Buyer B wants the 2022 Škoda Octavia at Velto Breda" |
   | **Interaction** | Each message, call, bid, trade-in detail, callback request or status change attached to a lead | Source event id | A reply three hours later; a missed call |
   | **Prospect** | The person behind one or more leads, as known to one dealer legal entity | Per legal entity: normalized e-mail and phone, the portal's pseudonymous buyer id | The same buyer on Marktplaats and on Velto's website |
   **Lead routing** (owned here) is the assignment of a lead to the rooftop, team, queue and person who should answer it, by rules the dealer configures and the platform evaluates.
   **Lead attribution** (owned here) is the record of which channels, adverts and campaigns touched a lead and its eventual sale, and the rule that decides which of them gets credit.
   The **Auto-lead Data Format (ADF)** is an XML format for sending a car-buying lead from whoever captured it to whoever must answer it.
   - **The minimum valid lead is small**: "Date and time; Vehicle: year, make, model; Customer: name, and either phone number or email address; Vendor: name".
   - **Identity across hops** uses a repeatable `<id sequence="…" source="…">`: "Different sources may use different ids for the same data as it is passed around." Each hop should add its id and keep the others; the chain is the only trace across portal, aggregator and CRM.
   - **`status="resend"`** marks a lead sent again.
   - **The transport is e-mail.** Errors are reported by bouncing the e-mail, and there is no acknowledgement beyond a bounce.
   - **Implementations drift.** Kelley Blue Book's 2014 LeadDriver usage guide said "the original ADF 1.0 specification contradicts itself in certain places and the DTD does not validate" and filled missing contact fields with "not provided" ([Kelley Blue Book, 2014](https://ldti.syndication.kbb.com/reference/files/ADF_Usage_Guide.pdf)).
   | **mobile.de** (DE) | Lead API, REST, Basic auth; access for "Transfer Service Providers and Lead Management System partners" | **Pull** with a cursor: `GET …/sellers/:mobileSellerId/events?cursor=…` returns events and a `nextCursor` | Lead id and ordered events; pseudonymous `buyerIdentifier`; the vehicle's `internalNumber` (the dealer's stock reference) | `POST …/leads/:leadId/status`: `IN_PROGRESS`, `SOLD`, `NOT_
   | **Marktplaats / 2dehands** (NL, BE) | Lead Delivery Webhook v3.2.0; Bidirectional Communication API; Chat Message Webhook | **Push**; "we do not give guarantees on the specific order you will receive webhook messages in"; new fields may appear "even in patch version updates" | Lead id unique per (lead kind, advertisement, buyer); `seller.vestiging` "to distinguish different rooftops"; location postcode | Replies th
   | **Auto Trader** (UK) | Deals, Messages, Calls, Part Exchange and Leads APIs; Deal Notifications | Pull pages (Leads API at most 100 per request since June 2024) plus signed notifications | Deal and stock ids | Deal state updates; partners can no longer set a lead to `NEW_MESSAGE` |
   | **US portals and lead providers** | ADF e-mail to a per-rooftop address; some CRM APIs | E-mail; bounce on error | `<id source sequence>` chain; optional stock number or VIN | Typically none |
   | **Dealer website** (own product) | Form post to the platform | Synchronous capture; the platform owns the form and the consent text | Advert id and stock item known exactly | Not needed |
   | **Phone and chat** | Call-tracking numbers per advert or channel; chat widgets | Call events after the call | Tracking number → channel and advert | Not needed |
   | **Intake** | Receive each source on its terms: verify webhook signatures, answer within the source's budget, advance cursors, read rooftop mailboxes | Raw event store; per-source cursors and mailbox state |
   | **Normalize** | Map each source format to the canonical lead event; tolerant ADF parsing; keep unknown fields | Mapping versions per source |
   | **Match** | Resolve the vehicle (stock reference → stock item → Axleby Vehicle Key), the rooftop and the prospect | Match results with confidence and method |
   | **Deduplicate** | Apply the three dedup levels; attach interactions to leads | Lead and interaction records; links between leads |
   | **Route** | Evaluate the rooftop's effective rule set against the tenant directory and calendars | Routing decisions stamped with rule version |
   | **Deliver** | Put the lead in the Lotwise inbox and, where configured, send it to an external CRM or DMS as a command with a receipt | Delivery state per target |
   | **Outcome** | Collect status from the inbox and CRMs; write back to portals that accept it; feed attribution | Lead lifecycle; attribution touches |
   A **CRM** (customer relationship management system) is where salespeople work their leads and customers: tasks, calls, appointments, follow-up sequences, pipeline reports.
   | **Inbox** | Lotwise or Showroomly inbox | System of record for the lead | Independents such as Autobedrijf Brandsma |
   | **External CRM** | DriveDesk CRM, a brand CRM, a US CRM via ADF | Router and courier; inbox as fallback and audit view | Franchised groups such as Velto |
   | **Both** | Inbox for used cars, CRM for new cars, or the reverse | Router; must not let two systems both chase the buyer | Mixed groups |
   **Won** is set when a deal for the same stock item and prospect is signed in Lotwise Sales Admin or reported by the DMS ([[deals-and-sales-documents]]), which is the only reliable link from enquiry to sale.
   **Time to first human response** is measured from arrival to the first outbound message or answered call by a person, not to an auto-reply.
   | **Duplicate delivery** | A webhook retried after a slow acknowledgement; an ADF `resend`; a re-read cursor | Exact-duplicate key on event id or id chain plus hash | Drop and count; a rising redelivery rate means intake is too slow |
   | **Out-of-order events** | A Marktplaats follow-up arrives before the lead it belongs to | Follow-up references an unknown source lead id | Park for a short window *(assumption: 10 minutes)*, then create the lead from the follow-up and merge when the original arrives |
   | **Incomplete record** | ADF with "not provided" contact fields; a lead with no stock reference | Normalizer flags missing fields; matcher returns low confidence | Route to the rooftop's general queue with the gap shown; never drop a lead for missing data |
   | **Lead for a sold or reserved car** | The Zombie Golf's **37** leads after its sale ([[listing-syndication]]) | Matcher sees stock state Sold or Reserved | Route normally with a "car no longer available" banner and similar stock; raise a channel-state check for that listing in Relay |
   | **Closed or inactive target** | The Lost Leads of Velto | Directory event; simulation; queue-activity monitor | Successor or fallback routing; alert to the group administrator |
   | **Silent acceptance** | CRM returns success and discards | Read-back of owner and queue; acknowledgement monitor | Re-route to the Lotwise inbox, alert, dead-letter review ([[journey-lead-to-dms]]) |
   | **CRM or DMS outage** | The DriveDesk Outage, November 2027: about **34,000** leads went to the Lotwise inbox ([[dms-integration]]) | Circuit breaker per CRM and rooftop | Inbox becomes the working tool; queued deliveries replayed after reconciliation, with "already handled in Lotwise" marked so salespeople do not call twice |
   | **Conflicting status** | CRM says Lost, a deal for the same car and prospect is signed | Outcome stage compares sources | The signed deal wins; the conflict is logged for the attribution report |
   | **Where salespeople work** | Platform inbox as system of record | External CRM as system of record | Independents and groups without a CRM; used-car teams the platform serves end to end | Franchised groups with a manufacturer or DMS-vendor CRM, where new-car, service and finance leads already live |
   | **Where routing happens** | In the platform | In the CRM | Leads must reach the right rooftop and queue before any CRM sees them; group policies across several CRMs | One CRM per group with its own assignment logic; the platform delivers to a single group queue |
   | **Cross-source duplicates** | Link and show together | Merge into one lead | Always for portal leads the dealer pays for | Never silently; only on a salesperson's explicit merge |
   | **Outcome write-back to portals** | On, per channel | Off | Dealer wants the portal's ranking and reporting to reflect conversions | Dealer treats conversion data as commercially sensitive |
===== deals-and-sales-documents 47
   A **deal** is the commercial agreement to sell one or more vehicles to one buyer, together with everything settled with it: trade-ins, add-ons, payments and financing.
   - **Parties**: the selling legal entity and rooftop, the buyer (consumer or business, with VAT number when business), co-buyers, and the salesperson.
   - **Vehicle lines**: each stock item sold, with agreed price and a snapshot of the VAT regime and the vehicle facts that the documents state (VIN, plate, odometer reading, first registration).
   - **Trade-in lines**: each car the buyer hands in, with the agreed allowance.
   - **Add-on lines**: warranties, delivery packages, accessories and third-party products, each with its own VAT treatment.
   - **Payments**: deposit, financing payout, transfer or card payment of the balance, each with source and status.
   - **Documents**: the quote, the sales contract, purchase agreements for trade-ins, invoices, credit notes and the delivery receipt.
   | **Quote** (offer) | Priced proposal to a named buyer, with validity date | Yes, until superseded; each version kept | Usually none until accepted; some markets treat a signed offer as binding |
   | **Sales contract** (order, *koopovereenkomst*, *Kaufvertrag*) | The binding agreement: vehicle, price, trade-in, delivery terms, warranty, conditions such as financing approval | No, once signed; changes by signed addendum | Binds both parties; consumer law attaches here |
   | **Purchase agreement** | The dealer's purchase of a trade-in or other car | No, once signed | Proves the dealer's purchase price and the seller's status, which the VAT regime depends on |
   | **Invoice** | The tax document for the supply | No, once issued; corrected by a credit note | VAT liability; needs a sequential number |
   An **issued document** in this design is four things stored together: the structured data (the deal snapshot at issue), the rendered file (PDF/A) the buyer received, the template and tax-rule versions used, and a content hash.
   The **margin scheme** is the EU's special VAT arrangement for second-hand goods sold by a **taxable dealer**: VAT is charged on the dealer's profit margin, not on the full selling price.
   - **Who sold it to the dealer (Article 314).** The scheme applies when the dealer bought the car from a non-taxable person (typically a private seller or a trade-in from a consumer), from a taxable person whose own supply was exempt, from a small enterprise under the exemption, or from another taxable dealer who applied the margin scheme.
   - **No VAT on the invoice (Article 325).** The dealer "may not enter separately on the invoices which he issues the VAT relating to supplies of goods to which he applies the margin scheme".
   - **No deduction for the buyer (Article 323).** A business buyer cannot reclaim VAT on a margin-scheme purchase.
   - **Opting out (Article 319).** The dealer may apply normal VAT to a supply that qualifies for the margin scheme.
   - **Not for intra-Community zero rating (Article 139(3)).** A margin-scheme car sold to a business in another member state stays taxed, on the margin, in the dealer's country.
   - **New means of transport (Article 2(2)(b)).** A land vehicle supplied within six months of first entry into service, or with no more than 6,000 km, is "new" for intra-Community supplies and follows different rules.
   A **trade-in** (part-exchange) is two transactions that happen to settle together: the dealer buys the buyer's car, and the buyer buys the dealer's car.
   A **financing referral** is the dealer presenting a lender's credit product to the buyer and passing the application to the lender.
   | **Deal service** | The deal aggregate, its state machine, its lines, payments and links to stock items | The stock item's lifecycle (it requests transitions from [[inventory-and-vehicle-dossier]]) |
   | **Tax rules engine** | Versioned rules per country: VAT regime eligibility, margin calculation, rates, invoice wording, rounding | The VAT regime of a car (a stock fact with evidence) |
   | **Document issuer** | Templates, number series, issue, rendering, hash, archive, credit notes | Deal content (it freezes what the deal service gives it) |
   | **Finance referral** | Quotes from lenders, applications, status, commission records, disclosures given | The credit decision, the credit agreement and the payout (the lender) |
   | **Accounting export** | Mapping of issued documents and payments to the dealer's chart of accounts, per accounting package | The general ledger |
   - **Portals moving into the transaction meet dealer resistance.** Auto Trader UK reported in May 2026 that its Deal Builder (online deal and reservation) had over 6,700 retailers and 175,000 live vehicles at March 2026, with 137,000 deals with a full reservation in the year.
   - **US omnichannel retailers put the full deal online.** [AutoNation's 10-K published in February 2026](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/350698/000162828026007800/an-20251231.htm) describes customers selecting a vehicle at a guaranteed price, receiving a trade-in offer, applying for financing, selecting protection products and signing paperwork electronically. [CarMax's 10-K published in April 2026](https://ww
   - **The DMS is where deals, F&I and accounting live for franchised dealers.** CDK's fiscal 2021 annual report, its last public one (five years old), says its DMS products "facilitate the sale of new and used vehicles, consumer financing" and enable "company-wide accounting" ([CDK Global 10-K, 2021](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1609702/000160970221000058/cdk-20210630.htm)).
   | **Invoice total** | **€19,345.00** | |
   | **Balance due before delivery** | **€6,445.00** | Bank transfer |
   **Invoice** issued on delivery day, once the financing condition is met and the balance is covered, with number `LW-0412-2027-000318` from Brandsma's rooftop series.
   | **Two salespeople sell the same car** | Conditional write on the stock item version fails for the second | Second deal stays in *Quoted* with a visible conflict; it never reaches *Signed* |
   | **Duplicate invoice from a retried request** | Issue requests carry the deal id and document type as idempotency key | The retry returns the already-issued document; no second number is taken |
   | **Gap in the number series** | Series monitor compares allocated with issued numbers | Each gap has a logged cause (aborted transaction); gaps are reported, never refilled |
   | **VAT regime unknown or contradicted** | Issuer refuses; nightly check of purchase invoices showing VAT against margin sales | Fix the evidence on the stock item; if already invoiced, credit note and reissue |
   | **Incomplete buyer record** (missing VAT number for a business buyer, missing address) | Validation per document type and country before issue | Deal stays in *Signed*; the missing field is named |
   | **Lender callback duplicated or out of order** | Status updates keyed by application id and lender event sequence | Ignore older statuses; never move a deal backwards on a late "pending" |
   | **Lender unavailable** | Circuit breaker on the lender adapter | Quote without live rate (marked indicative); sign the contract with a financing condition; submit when back |
   | **E-signature provider down** | Provider health and timeouts | Print the frozen document, sign on paper, upload the scan against the same document id |
   | **Invoice issued, export failed** | Export receipts per document number | Re-export idempotently by document number; accounting packages that reject duplicates are fed the same key |
   | **DMS hand-off rejected or DMS down** | Missing receipt within the expiry window | Deal stays visibly pending; in a declared outage, issue from the fallback series (below) |
   | **Sale canceled after invoice** | User action | Credit note referencing the original; stock returns only on confirmation; linked credit agreement unwound by the lender |
   | **Customer withdraws from the credit agreement within 14 days** | Lender status | Deal reverts to *Signed* with an unmet financing condition; dealer and buyer settle payment another way, or cancel |
   | **Front end only**: quotes and contracts, hand-off to the DMS for invoices | Deal, quote, contract, finance referral | Franchised dealers and groups with a DMS that owns accounting | Dependent on the DMS for closing; needs a fallback series to avoid DMS Hostage |
   | **Document system of record**: Sales Admin issues invoices, exports to accounting | Plus invoices, credit notes, stock book, accounting export | Independent dealers without a DMS (most of Lotwise's NL and BE base) | Tax-law liability for the documents; per-country rule sets; retention |
   | **Full DMS**: receivables, general ledger, commissions, service | Everything | Only as a deliberate product strategy with years of investment | Competes with the dealer's accountant and with DMS vendors; accounting regulation per country; OEM certification for franchised brands |
===== registration-and-trade-services 57
   | **Total** | **3,100** | **214** |
   A **register transaction** is a request, submitted by a party with the legal authority to make it, that changes the official record of a vehicle in a government register and produces proof that it did.
   | **Netherlands** (RDW, *Rijksdienst voor het Wegverkeer*, the national vehicle authority) | Stock-in to trade stock (*bedrijfsvoorraad*) with release of the seller (*vrijwaren*); registration in the buyer's name (*tenaamstellen*); export notification; import registration with or without inspection; dismantling | Online message under an RDW recognition, through a provider or directly | Release certificate (*vrijwarin
   | **Belgium** (DIV, the vehicle registration directorate) | Registration of the new owner; plates follow the owner | Commonly insurer- or broker-driven electronic requests (not verified from a primary source) | Registration certificate; plate posted to the owner |
   | **Germany** (local registration offices; central register at the KBA, the federal motor transport authority) | New registration, re-registration with or without keeper change, deregistration (XKfz codes NZ, UO/UM/UI, AB) | Local office, registration services, i-Kfz (internet-based registration) portals; a machine interface for large customers (GKS) since 2023 | Registration documents and notices, delivered electron
   | **United Kingdom** (DVLA, the Driver and Vehicle Licensing Agency) | Keeper change to or from the trade on the V5C (the vehicle log book); first registration of new cars | Online keeper notifications; Register a Vehicle (RaV) for manufacturers and retailers | New V5C to the keeper; online confirmation |
   | **United States** (state Departments of Motor Vehicles, DMVs) | Title transfer, title and registration for the buyer, lien recording and release | State DMV systems, often through dealer service providers; electronic lien and title (ELT) for lenders | Title (paper or electronic), registration, lien record |
   - **They are synchronous, all-or-nothing and cannot be faked during an outage.** The RDW's information pack is explicit (in translation): "During a disruption of message traffic with the RDW you may not receive confirmation of the registration.
   - **They are state-guarded.** A stock-in consumes the transfer code; a registration in the buyer's name requires the car to be in the dealer's stock.
   **Transaction authority** is the chain of legal permissions that allows a particular submission: which legal entity acts, under which recognition, through which credential, operated by which person, on behalf of which customer.
   Since **1999** Axleby has been what this book calls an **authorized information provider** to the Dutch register: a book term, defined in [[registers-and-official-data]], for a contractual data-supply and transaction-provider relationship, not an official RDW title.
   The **proof** is the evidence that the register accepted the act: the confirmation message stored verbatim, the transaction code and timestamp the register returned, and the documents produced from them, such as the release certificate the seller takes home.
   | **Transaction orchestrator** | Validation of prerequisites, authority and fees; creating the transaction with its idempotency key | Talking to the register |
   | **Authority register** | Recognitions, certificates (serial, expiry, custody), provider route and user delegations per legal entity and rooftop | Customer mandates (stored on the transaction) |
   | **Transaction ledger** | One record per legal act: state, attempts, verbatim requests and responses, authority chain, proof references | Vehicle facts (read from Anchor and the register mirror) |
   | **Retry queue** | Ordering per vehicle, fairness per dealer, pacing per certificate and channel | Business decisions about what to submit |
   | **Turnstile** | Sessions, certificates on the wire, channel health, timeouts, the circuit to the register ([[registers-and-official-data]]) | Retry policy for legal acts |
   | **Proof store** | Immutable documents with hashes and retention class | Generating them before confirmation |
   | **Fee and tax rules** | Register fees, Axleby fees, registration-tax calculations (versioned) | Invoicing ([[billing-and-metering]]) |
   **Confirmed requires evidence from the register**: the confirmation message or a probe showing the change.
   - **One lane per vehicle (AVK).** Acts on the same car must happen in order: stock-in before registration in the buyer's name, stock-in before export.
   - **Fair scheduling across dealers.** Lanes are drawn round-robin per legal entity, so a group's batch shares capacity rather than taking it.
   - **Pacing per certificate and channel.** After an outage the queue drains at a controlled rate that Turnstile sets from channel health, not at full speed.
   - **Gross BPM is a banded CO2 table.** For 2026, a passenger car between 100 and 139 g/km pays €2,727 plus €181 per gram above 100; above 155 g/km, €14,538 plus €594 per gram above 155.
   - **The CO2 value has rules of its own.** The engine uses the value the RDW registered; for cars first admitted before 1 July 2020 the declarant may choose between values from the current (WLTP) and former (NEDC) EU test procedures in defined cases; when no value is known, default values apply (for example 550 g/km for a petrol car first admitted from July 2020).
   - **For a used car the declarant may choose the current tariff or an older one**, so the engine must hold every historic tariff year; the Belastingdienst publishes them back to 1993.
   - **Depreciation has three methods**: a trade price list (*koerslijst*), a valuation report (*taxatierapport*, only for cars with more than normal damage or not in a price list) or a fixed table (*forfaitaire tabel*). "You may choose the most favorable depreciation method.
   - **The methods have evidence rules and dates.** The price-list method uses the most recent list, one list only, the full vehicle specification including options, and a reference date of the day the RDW completed its inspection.
   - **Rule sets are data with effective dates**, released before they take effect, and the engine can evaluate any historic tariff year.
   - **Inputs carry provenance.** CO2 from the register, catalog price, specification and options from [[specification-and-equipment]], the price-list value with the list's name and edition, the odometer reading.
   - **The engine computes every permitted method and shows them side by side**, then records the declarant's choice, because the choice is final and the dealer, not the engine, makes it.
   - **Every calculation is stored with inputs, rule version and method choice** for the fiscal retention period and can be re-run exactly; a rule change lists the open cases calculated under the old rule.
   - **The tax and register cases share one case id**, because the register waits for the tax and the tax refers to the register's inspection date.
   | **Dealer double click or API client retry** | Same idempotency key | Return the existing transaction; no new act |
   | **Second transaction of the same type for the same car** | Ledger guard on open transactions per vehicle, type and entity | Refuse with a link to the open one |
   | **Ambiguous timeout** | No response within 90 s; connection lost after send | Ambiguous state; probe; never resubmit unprobed |
   | **Rejection after a retry** | Rejection code on a transaction with an earlier ambiguous attempt | Treat as probe evidence; resolve by register state, not by the code |
   | **Conflicting acts on one car** (two rooftops of a group, or a dealer and another provider) | Probe or rejection shows the car in an unexpected state | Manual; the register's state wins; the dossier shows who holds the car |
   | **Incomplete record** (missing code, reading or mandate) | Validation | Blocked with the missing field named; nothing queued |
   | **Partial failure in a multi-step case** (stock-in confirmed, registration rejected) | Case state with per-act outcomes | Each act stands on its own; the case shows what is done and what is not; no compensating register act without a person |
   | **Register outage** | Turnstile circuit open; channel health | Queue with visible status and estimated position; banner in Lotwise; paced drain on recovery |
   | **Certificate expiring or revoked** | Authority register monitors expiry 60 days ahead *(assumption)* | Alert entity admin; block submissions under that certificate before the register rejects them |
   | **Tax rule change** | New rule set effective date | Recalculate open cases; flag any declared under the old rule |
   | **Blind retry** | Resubmit after timeout | Never for state-changing legal acts | Double submissions; false rejections (Axleby, March 2027) |
   | **Client request id honored by the register** | Register deduplicates on a reference the client sends | Where the channel offers it (the German GKS request number is the model) | Nothing extra, but depends on the register |
   | **Probe before retry** | Ask the register's current state; resubmit only if unchanged | Where an information channel exists and state is observable | Extra reads; waits when the information channel is down too |
   | **Manual resolution** | A person checks and decides | Inconclusive probes; low volumes | People time at peaks |
   | **Become a register provider** (Axleby's position in NL) | Control of the channel, of transaction latency and of the dealer's experience; fees | Certificates, compliance with provider terms, support obligations, peak capacity | High volume, transactions core to the product, long horizon |
   | **Integrate with a provider** | Faster start; the provider carries the register relationship | Two hops between you and the register; outages and ambiguity are reported to you second-hand | Low volume or a new market |
   | **Hand off to the dealer or a registration service** | No liability for the act | Re-typing; no proof in the dossier; no fee revenue | Markets where the act is rare or local (Germany for Axleby today) |
   | **Who submits** | NL: recognized dealer through a provider or directly; BE: commonly the insurer or broker (not verified from primary sources); Axleby's Belgian transactions run through a partner | Local registration offices, registration services, i-Kfz; large customers (over 500 a year) by machine interface | Keeper or motor trader online; retailers through RaV for first registrations | Dealer or service provider
   | **Authority** | Recognitions, a 2-year RDW certificate, the buyer's DigiD authorization | Credentials and technical certificate; qualified-signature power of attorney for third parties | V5C document reference; motor-trader option in the online service | State dealer licence; ELT participant ids |
   | **Channel shape** | Synchronous; no completion during a disruption | Asynchronous, multi-hop; forwarded and escalated states | Online service with set opening hours | State-specific; batch and online |
   | **Proof** | Release certificate; transaction code; card mailed | Electronic documents and notices | New V5C | Title, registration, lien record |
   | **Registration tax** | NL: BPM, refund on export; BE: regional BIV (Flanders: Flemish tax service) | None at registration; annual vehicle tax *(general practice; not verified from a primary source here)* | VED first-year rate by CO2, then annual | State sales or use tax and title fees *(general practice; varies by state)* |
   | **Axleby's offer (2027)** | Full transaction service in NL; BE through a partner | None; hand-off | None; Showroomly does no register transactions | Not an Axleby market |
   **Watch four numbers**: ambiguous outcomes per day and their resolution time; transactions in Manual; rejected-at-register rate (a rising rate means validation is missing something); and days to certificate expiry across all entities.
===== dms-integration 57
   | **DriveDesk** (NL, BE) | 1,900 | Stock file export polled every 15 min; partner REST API for leads and customers (since 2025) | Stock in; leads out | DriveDesk stock number | None |
   | **Total connected** | **2,250** | | | | |
   A **dealer management system (DMS)** is the transactional core of a dealership: the software that records what the business owns, sells, repairs and owes.
   A **certified interface** is an access channel that the DMS vendor documents, licenses and tests for a named third party, under a partner agreement.
   A **DMS adapter** is the component on your side that speaks one DMS family's transport and model, translates it into your canonical model and back, and enforces that DMS's contract terms.
   | **Fortellis** (successor to 3PA and the Partner Program) | CDK Global | API directory and marketplace; an API contract can have several providers and Fortellis "routes this call through to the appropriate provider"; RESTful and asynchronous APIs announced 2023; site claims 135+ APIs and 425+ apps ([Fortellis, n.d.](https://fortellis.io/)) | 2018–2026 |
   | **RCI** | Reynolds and Reynolds | Certification since 2003; one CRM vendor describes it in 2026 as a "signed SOAP exchange", "not screen-scraping or a shared login", and lists operations it does not expose ([DealerFunnel, n.d.](https://dealerfunnel.com/integrations/reynolds-reynolds)) | 2003; n.d. |
   | **Opentrack** | Cox Automotive (Dealertrack DMS) | Certified third-party program; Motive Retail announced certification in 2024 for an "integrate once, connect to many" aggregator ([Auto Remarketing, 2024](https://www.autoremarketing.com/ar/technology/motive-retails-mix-platform-joins-dealertracks-integration-program)) | 2017–2024 |
   | **Automotive Partner Cloud (APC)** | Tekion | Partner tiers with "foundational APIs and standard rate limits" up to "additional APIs, higher rate limits"; "APIs and webhooks"; partners apply and are approved ([Tekion, n.d.](https://tekion.com/products/apc)) | n.d., accessed 2026-10 |
   | **Open Platform** | Keyloop (CDK's former international business, renamed in 2021) | "A single interface to dealer management systems" across Europe, the Middle East, Africa and Asia; endpoint availability varies by DMS; security review for partners ([Keyloop, n.d.](https://developer.keyloop.io/)) | n.d. (2022 footer) |
   | **Certified REST API** | Seconds | Low to medium; versioned | Partner agreement, fees, certification | The default where the vendor offers one |
   | **Vendor events or webhooks** | Near real time | Medium: at-least-once, unordered | As above | Stock status, deals, customer changes where offered |
   | **SOAP or XML message exchange** | Seconds to minutes | Low; rigid | Certification and recertification | Older programs (RCI, STAR web services) |
   | **Batch file extract** | Hours | Medium: schema drift, partial files | Usually allowed by the dealer; vendor-specific | Smaller DMS products; stock and history backfill |
   | **Aggregator ("integrate once")** | Varies; adds a hop | Medium | Extra contract; inherits each DMS's terms | The long tail you cannot justify building |
   | **Screen scraping or shared credentials** | Minutes to hours | High: breaks silently on any UI change | Breaches "closed" DMS licences; blocked by CDK and Reynolds since 2015 | Never as a product foundation |
   **Scraping fails in the way that hurts most**: silently, all at once, on a date chosen by someone else, with a legal question attached.
   | **DMS adapter** (one per DMS family) | Transport, authentication, rate limits, polling watermarks, mapping to and from canonical model, contract-scope enforcement | Adapter version tied to the vendor's certification; per-rooftop cursors |
   | **Cross-reference (xref) map** | Maps (DMS, rooftop, external id) to Axleby ids: stock item, Axleby Vehicle Key (AVK), customer, deal | The mapping and its history, including stock-number changes |
   | **Upsert service** | Applies inbound canonical changes to the dossier idempotently, by field ownership | Field-ownership rules per rooftop; inbound change log |
   | **Command queue** | Sends outbound changes as commands, tracks receipts, retries idempotently, expires stale commands | Command state: pending, acknowledged, rejected, expired |
   | **Reconciler** | Compares Lotwise and DMS per rooftop and entity; raises discrepancies as tickets | Reconciliation runs and their results |
   | **Authorization and scope register** | Records which dealer authorized which integration, for which rooftops and scopes, under which contract, until when | The dealer's authorization as data |
   | **Health monitor** | Tracks freshness and error rates per DMS and per rooftop; opens circuit breakers; drives degraded mode | Integration health state |
   - **The vehicle key is the VIN, resolved to an AVK.** The DMS stock number identifies a *stock unit at a rooftop*, not a car.
   - **The xref map is time-bounded.** Each (DMS, rooftop, stock number) edge has a valid-from and valid-to, like plate assignments in [[vehicle-identity]].
   - **The DMS customer id is per rooftop or per legal entity, never global.** Do not merge DMS customers across Velto's Dutch and Belgian entities just because the names match; the dealer is controller of each entity's customers.
   - **Deal and invoice numbers are DMS documents.** Lotwise stores them as references and never generates competing numbers for a rooftop whose DMS issues invoices.
   - **Sonic Automotive** said its dealerships stayed open "utilizing workaround solutions", and resumed DMS transactions on 30 June only after "internal risk assessments and data validation procedures" ([Sonic 8-K, July 2024](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1043509/000104350924000060/sah-20240705.htm); [Sonic 10-Q, August 2024](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1043509/000104350924000065/sah-20240630.htm)
   - **Lithia Motors** severed "business service connections" between its systems and CDK's as part of its incident response ([Lithia 8-K, June 2024](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1023128/000102312824000079/lad-20240619.htm)).
   - **AutoNation** reported its DMS and core functions restored on 29 June, while "certain ancillary systems and integrations, such as those that help automate ordering, scheduling, payment, and reporting processes, were restored by the end of July"; it attributed an estimated 5% decline in second-quarter gross profit to the outage ([AutoNation 10-Q, August 2024](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/350698/000035069
   - **Group 1 Automotive** said its UK dealerships "do not use CDK's dealers' systems and are therefore not impacted" ([Group 1 8-K, June 2024](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1031203/000103120324000048/gpi-20240619.htm)).
   | **Duplicate inbound rows** | Polling overlap, webhook retries, a DMS re-export | Version guard on (DMS, rooftop, external id) | Ignore lower or equal versions; log |
   | **Duplicate stock units for one car** | Transfer or re-stocking creates a new DMS stock number | Two open xref edges for one VIN at one group | Close the old edge; one stock item per VIN per rooftop |
   | **Conflicting updates** | Both sides changed a field | Field-ownership rule; reconciler | Owner wins; non-owner change recorded as observation; ticket if material |
   | **Incomplete records** | No VIN, invalid VIN, missing cost | Validation in the adapter | Quarantine with visible reason; never key by plate |
   | **Ambiguous write timeouts** | DMS times out after accepting the change | Missing receipt | Read DMS state before retry; additive commands to manual review |
   | **Silent acceptance** | DMS or CRM returns success and discards | Read-back of the created record; queue activity monitors | Re-route; alert rooftop; dead-letter review ([[journey-lead-to-dms]]) |
   | **Partial batch files** | Truncated or half-written extract | Row count and checksum trailer; file-size baseline | Reject the whole file; keep previous watermark |
   | **Schema drift** | DMS release renames or reformats a field | Contract tests on every file and response; unmapped-field counter | Adapter fix; replay from stored raw input |
   | **Credential revocation or expiry** | Vendor or dealer disconnects | Authentication failures per rooftop | Degraded mode; out-of-band re-authorization only |
   | **Vendor outage** | DMS unreachable for minutes or weeks | Health monitor per DMS and rooftop | Circuit breaker; degraded mode; reconcile before resume |
   | **Certification change** | Vendor narrows scope or reprices | Partner-management calendar; contract register | Adapter versioning; feature flags per scope |
   | **Build a certified adapter yourself** | Full control of mapping, monitoring and reconciliation; direct vendor relationship | Fees, certification time, one team's attention per DMS | The DMS covers a large share of your connected rooftops (DriveDesk at Axleby) |
   | **Use an aggregator** | One contract and one model for many DMS products | Extra hop, extra contract, the aggregator's outages and terms, less control of reconciliation | The long tail, where each DMS has few of your rooftops |
   | **File extract** | Works with small DMS products that have no API; dealer controls it | Hours of latency; schema drift; partial files | Stock backfill and daily sync where latency does not matter |
   | **Dealer-authorized scraping** | Fast to start | Fragile, contractually exposed, widens the attack surface | Only as a dated bridge where the licence permits and an exit is planned |
   | **Do not integrate** | No cost, no dependency | Double keying; the product loses franchised dealers | The rooftop has no DMS, or the DMS covers too few of your customers to justify any option above |
   | **DMS owns stock existence and status; SaaS owns market fields** | Matches the ledger and the manufacturer's reporting; fewer disputes with dealers' finance teams | Sold status depends on the DMS reaching you; outages need a degraded mode | Franchised dealers and groups with a DMS (Axleby's default) |
   | **SaaS owns stock; DMS receives purchases and sales** | Fast, market-led workflow; the SaaS works alone | The ledger depends on your write-back; finance teams resist | Independents with light DMS or accounting packages |
   | **Both write, last write wins** | Simple to build | Overwrites; untraceable disputes; zombie listings | Never |
   - **Scope**: entities and operations, read and write, per rooftop; how scope changes are approved.
   - **Fees**: per rooftop, per call or per transaction; who pays (Axleby or the dealer); price-change notice.
   - **Certification**: initial test, recertification triggers and lead time; which Hitch changes require it.
   - **Data use**: what Axleby may store, for how long, and whether it may use DMS-sourced data beyond serving that dealer (it may not use it for market data or valuations without the dealer's separate permission; [[data-licensing-and-governance]] records this in Charter).
   - **Incidents**: notification time, credential rotation, who decides when to reconnect, who pays for degraded operation.
   - **Termination and data return**: what happens to the xref map and DMS-sourced copies when a dealer leaves either product.
===== b2b-trade-and-remarketing 55
   | **Total** | **14,000** |
   | **Trade exchange** (fixed price, offers, tenders) | Buy-now click or accepted offer; tender at close | Seller, until collection | Dealers with aged stock or unwanted trade-ins, lessors | Ayvens Carmarket "car auctions for dealers" (Ayvens site, 2026) |
   | **Online auction** | Hammer at close, under the auction's conditions | Seller or the auction's compound | Lessors, rental companies, dealer groups | Autorola: open, tender, sealed-bid, "Fleet Owner Selection" and damaged-car sales, "over 70,000 active buyers" (Autorola site, read October 2026) |
   | **Physical auction** (often simulcast online) | Hammer in the lane | Auction compound | Fleet, rental, manufacturers | Manheim UK lists physical and virtual auctions and "defleet services" (Manheim UK site, read October 2026) |
   | **Export buyer** (direct or through an exchange) | Contract, usually prepaid | Seller, until collection | Dealers and lessors with stock that sells better abroad | Manheim Express, a B2B cross-border platform started 1 January 2025 (AUTOHAUS, 2025) |
   A **de-fleet** is the planned disposal of fleet vehicles at contract end or at the end of a holding period.
   - **It is known in advance.** Contract end dates are in the lessor's system months ahead.
   - **The result is measured against a promise.** Remarketing is judged by sale price against residual value.
   - **Three parties have a stake in condition.** The driver (or client company) pays for damage beyond fair wear and tear.
   A **digital return** is the handover of a lease or rental car recorded electronically at the moment it happens: identity check (plate and VIN), odometer, keys, documents and accessories, guided photographs, findings against a published return standard, battery state of health for an electric car, the lessee's acknowledgement, and the settlement of any charge.
   A **trade listing** is an offer of one vehicle to a defined professional audience, on trade terms.
   An **auction hand-off** is then precise: the transfer of the right to sell one vehicle to an auction partner, with the evidence the partner needs, a reserve, a time limit, and an obligation on the partner to report status and the result.
   | **Return pipeline** (lessor-facing, in Lotwise) | Expected returns from the lessor's contract feed, return appointments, channel plan per car | The lease contract and driver (lessor's own system) |
   | **Handback** (return and settlement workflow) | Return appointment, handover checklist (keys, documents, accessories), lessee acknowledgement, charge letter, charge disputes and their deadlines, release to remarketing | Inspection capture (Lotwise Inspect), findings, rubrics and charges computed from them (Gauge, [[condition-and-damage]]) |
   | **Sale-right register** | One active right-to-sell lease per vehicle and seller; grant, renew, release, expire; fencing tokens | What the channel does with the lease |
   | **Trade listing service** | Trade listings, audiences and tiers, offers, tenders, buy-now | Stock state (Lotwise Dossier), valuation (Vantage) |
   | **Buyer matcher** | Standing buyer searches ("buy boxes") and alerts | Buyer identity and verification (Axleby ID) |
   | **Auction hand-off adapters** | One adapter per auction partner: lot creation, status, results, withdrawals | The auction itself |
   | **Trade deal service** | The deal record from acceptance to collection: price, VAT basis, payment state, documents, register actions, release conditions, disputes | Invoices (document issuer in [[deals-and-sales-documents]]); register transactions (Axleby Trade back office, [[registration-and-trade-services]]) |
   The **stock item stays the seller's**: the buyer receives a copy of agreed dossier facts at sale, never a live reference (the cross-tenant rule in [[tenancy-and-dealer-groups]]).
   The **right to sell is not a field on the listing**: it lives in its own register, so a car that is not on Tradefloor at all (an export sale Fleetmoor makes by phone) can still be locked.
   | **Lessee damage charge** | Findings beyond the lessor's return standard (for example, fair wear and tear thresholds), priced from the lessor's tariff | Lessor's rubric version in Gauge | Lessee (usually the driver's employer) and driver |
   | **Buyer condition report** | All findings, including wear, with photos and a grade; battery figure only from a verified certificate ([[ev-battery-health]]) | Trade-report rubric in Gauge | Trade buyers |
   | **Reserve and channel plan** | Reconditioning estimate from the findings, with confidence | Gauge and Vantage ([[valuation-engine]]) | Lessor's desk |
   - **Tiers are part of the listing, not a filter in the UI.** A lessor may give its franchise partners a first look, then all Lotwise dealers, then exporters, as US off-lease remarketing commonly gives the dealer where the lease ends the first option.
   - **Alerts are rate-limited per buyer and fair across buyers.** A buyer with 200 buy boxes must not get a car minutes before a buyer with one.
   - **Concentration of off-lease supply on private-label platforms (US).** OPENLANE's 2025 annual report (February 2026) describes software-as-a-service remarketing for "the majority of commercial off-lease sellers in North America" and more than 40 private-label applications.
   - **Formats as product.** Autorola's site (read October 2026) lists open auctions, closed-bid tenders, sealed bids, buy-now, damaged-car sales and a "Fleet Owner Selection" format.
   - **Retail versus wholesale disposition is a recorded decision.** Hertz said in its 2025 annual report that it "prioritizes retail as our primary disposition channel" and uses "dealer direct wholesale channels, retail and auction"; CarMax sends cars that miss its retail standard to its own auctions.
   - **Battery health at the auction lane.** Cox Automotive reported in March 2024 that Manheim's LotVision devices, installed at check-in at 76 US locations, capture an EV battery health score "in minutes" ([[ev-battery-health]]).
   - **Data and marketplace ownership diverge.** VWE sold ATC to Autorola in 2026, and Autorola also owns the Indicata pricing-analytics business.
   | **Duplicate sale across channels** | Two binding acceptances for one car | Impossible by design if all channels hold tokens; for channels outside the register (seller's phone sales), the deal service checks the lease before release | First valid token wins; the other acceptance is refused before a contract forms.
   | **Partner reports a sale after lease expiry** | Late hammer result on an expired lease | Adapter flags results on expired leases | Accept the result if no other lease was granted since; otherwise escalate to the seller, because two contracts may exist.
   | **Duplicate result messages** | Callback and daily export report the same lot | Idempotency key: partner, lot id, result version | Second message is a no-op; a changed result (for example, a canceled hammer) is a new version with a reason |
   | **Incomplete return record** | Missing odometer photo, missing VIN check, handover unsigned | Handback refuses to close the return without required fields; "driver absent" is an explicit state | Return closes as `unacknowledged`; the charge letter says so, and the dispute window is longer |
   | **Conflicting condition evidence** | Buyer claims a defect not in the report | Claim attached to a finding, within the dispute window counted from collection | Re-inspection if the photos are inconclusive; settlement recorded on the deal and the outcome on the finding in Gauge, never edited into the report |
   | **Payment received but register action fails** | Buyer paid; the register's transaction channel is unavailable | Release condition stays open | Car is not released; the buyer sees "awaiting register".
   | **Ambiguous timeout on lease grant or acceptance** | Channel does not know whether it holds the lease or the sale went through | Client request id on every grant and acceptance | Retry with the same request id returns the original outcome rather than a second lease or deal |
   | **Auction partner outage** | No callbacks, no daily export | Adapter health check and missing-export alert | New hand-offs pause; existing leases are extended, not expired, while the partner is down; reconciliation runs before any lease is released |
   | **Valuation drift on a segment** | Reserve prices miss; no-sale rates rise | No-sale and clearing-rate monitor per segment ([[valuation-engine]]) | Segment flagged `restricted`; reserves for that segment need desk approval |
   | **Buyer default** | Buyer does not pay or collect | Payment deadline on the deal | Deal canceled with reason; lease returns to the seller; buyer's trading status reviewed |
   The **off-platform sale** is the leak in any lock.
   | **Sequential channels, manual withdrawal** | Desk withdraws from one channel before offering in the next | Low volume, one person per car | Fails on rota changes, as in June 2027 |
   | **Withdraw-then-offer automation** | Platform sends withdrawals, then offers elsewhere | All channels confirm withdrawals reliably | Withdrawal confirmations from partners can lag or fail; gap between channels lengthens time to sale |
   | **Exclusive lease with fencing token** (chosen) | One holder at a time; acceptance requires the current token | Binding channels, several partners, high volume | Every channel adapter must carry the token; partner contracts must include withdrawal confirmation |
   | **Simultaneous offering with first-acceptance-wins** | Car in several channels; the first binding acceptance cancels others | Seller owns every channel and can cancel sales | Not possible when a partner's terms make its sale binding; produces canceled contracts by design |
   | **Own auctions** (compounds, auctioneers, logistics) | Hammer prices for valuation; full control of formats | Capital, logistics, a marketplace business with its own liquidity problem ([[liquidity-and-network-effects]]) | The group sees itself as a marketplace operator; Cox with Manheim is the documented case |
   | **Partner auction hand-off** (Axleby's choice) | No compounds; several partners compete; focus on data and workflow | No hammer prices unless licensed; partner contracts; integration per partner | The group is a data-and-software business; VWE's move after the ATC sale points the same way |
   | **Exchange only, no auctions** | Simplest | Sellers leave for auction groups for the cars the exchange cannot sell | Dealer-to-dealer aged stock only, no lessor volume |
   | **Where wholesale stock comes from** | Lease returns are a major source; VNA counted 1.37 million leased cars and vans in NL at end 2025 (VNA, 2026) | Lessors, manufacturer banks and B2B auctions *(industry practice; no primary share found)* | Lessors and rental de-fleets through auction groups and digital remarketing (Manheim UK "defleet services"; Cox's Dealer Auction) | Off-lease supply concentrated on private-l
   | **Main auction and exchange players named in sources** | ATC (Autorola since February 2026), Autorola, lessor platforms such as Ayvens Carmarket | Autorola, Manheim Express, manufacturer and lessor platforms | Manheim UK, Dealer Auction, Autorola | OPENLANE, Manheim, Carvana's ADESA (56 sites, 2022 purchase) |
   | **Register step in a trade sale** | NL: dealer-to-dealer stock transfer through the provider's "trade purchases" function, without documents if stock types match (RDW, n.d.); export notification with plates cut and paper Part II (RDW *informatiemap*, 2026).
   | **Return and condition standard** | Lessor-specific return rules; no single published standard found | Lessor-specific; assessor organizations used *(industry practice)* | BVRLA fair wear and tear guides for cars, vans and goods vehicles | Auction condition grades; National Auto Auction Association (NAAA) 0–5 scale with 5 best, reported as the basis of Manheim's AutoGrade (Manheim, 2014; trade reports) |
   | **VAT or tax on the trade sale** | EU VAT; margin scheme or normal; intra-EU exemption with buyer VAT number (Directive 2006/112/EC, Art. 138) | Same EU rules | UK VAT margin scheme with its own vehicle stock-book rules (HMRC, 2021) | No VAT; sales tax on retail, typically not on dealer-to-dealer resale *(industry practice)* |
   | **Cross-border pattern** | Strong export flows to and through neighbors; BPM refund on export of used cars exists (Belastingdienst) | Large export and import market inside the EU | Exports to the EU are customs exports after Brexit | Exports leave through customs; the National Motor Vehicle Title Information System (NMVTIS) reports salvage and junk vehicles |
===== part-5 0
===== tenancy-and-dealer-groups 74
   - **Lotwise Classic**, the PHP and MySQL monolith in Ghent, keys everything by a `dealer_id` that means one rooftop.
   - **Showroomly**, the UK product on Rails and PostgreSQL, keys by an `account_id` that is sometimes a rooftop and sometimes a whole group, depending on how the customer was sold.
   - **Axleby Portal accounts** in Connect XML, used by Axleby Data and Axleby Trade, are keyed by the customer's legal entity, because that is who signs the data licence and pays the invoice.
   | **Organization (tenant)** | The contracting party: an independent dealer, a dealer group, a lessor, a data client | Contract, master data, identity provider settings, group policies, the boundary of data isolation | Velto Mobility Group |
   | **Legal entity** | A company with its own registration and VAT number | Invoices, VAT, sales documents, register authority, data-protection controller role | Velto's 7 operating companies, 5 Dutch and 2 Belgian |
   | **Region or cluster** | An optional grouping used for management | Reporting, permissions, lead routing, price bands | 9 regional hubs from September 2027 |
   | **Rooftop** | One physical dealership location | Stock, adverts, leads, opening hours, franchises, DMS site, subscriptions | Velto Breda, Velto Gent-Zuid, … (46) |
   | **Department** | A function within a rooftop | Lead queues, approval rights, workshop and sales split | Used sales, new sales, workshop |
   The **rooftop** is what most dealer software is built around: stock sits there, portal accounts and franchise agreements name it, and subscriptions are priced per rooftop (Lotwise's Stock & Pricing module is €189 per rooftop per month, an Axleby illustration).
   **Users** are not a level of the hierarchy.
   A **tenant** is the unit of data ownership and isolation: the boundary inside which data may flow freely subject to permissions, and across which data may flow only through explicit, audited features.
   - **The vehicle.** The Axleby Vehicle Key (AVK) in Anchor, the specification in Specbook and the history in Tracewell describe a physical car, and many tenants will hold that car over its life.
   - **Usage and audit records about the tenant.** These belong to Axleby's control plane, are attributed to the tenant and are shown to it, but the tenant cannot delete them.
   **Tenant context** is the verified answer, for one unit of work, to "on behalf of which tenant, and which node within it, is this happening?" It consists of the tenant id, the active hierarchy node (usually a rooftop), the acting user or client, and the hierarchy version used to resolve them.
   - **Control plane** (shared across the group, one instance): the **tenant directory** (organizations, legal entities, regions, rooftops, departments, external identifiers, lifecycle state, hierarchy versions), the **cell map** (which deployment serves which tenant), Axleby ID (identity, built on Keycloak with organizations), Gatekeeper (relationship authorization on OpenFGA), Meterline (usage metering) and the subscr
   - **Application plane** (one or more **cells**): Lotwise, Showroomly, Switchboard, Relay and the data APIs, each holding tenant-owned data and trusting the control plane for who the tenant is.
   A **cell** is a complete deployment of the application plane serving a defined set of tenants, with its own databases, queues, credentials and network boundary.
   Microsoft calls this pattern **deployment stamps** and describes a tenant-mapping layer that routes each request to the stamp holding its data, with tenants movable between stamps ([Microsoft, 2025](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/architecture/guide/multitenant/considerations/tenancy-models)).
   | **Edge** | Access token from Axleby ID with organization id; active rooftop selected in the session | Gateway validates the token, checks the rooftop is in the tenant at the current hierarchy version, and issues a short-lived signed internal context |
   | **Service calls** | Signed internal context header | Services reject calls without it; a library exposes it, so handlers never parse ids from payloads |
   | **Caches** | Cache client library | Keys are built only by a library that prefixes the tenant id and refuses to build a key without context; a cached value carries its tenant id and is checked on read |
   | **Database** | Per-transaction setting | Application filter plus database-enforced row-level security where the engine supports it |
   | **Search indexes** | Query builder | Mandatory tenant filter in the query library; one index per cell, tenant id on every document |
   | **Events and jobs** | Event envelope | CloudEvents extension attributes for tenant and rooftop; consumers set context from the envelope, not the payload |
   | **Object storage** | Path and encryption key | Tenant id in the object key prefix; per-tenant data key; signed URLs scoped to one object |
   | **Telemetry** | Span and log attributes | Tenant id on traces and logs, tenant tier only on metrics |
   | **Egress** | Outbound calls to portals, DMS, suppliers | Strip internal context and baggage at the egress proxy |
   - **Silo**: "tenants are provided dedicated resources", but "a silo environment still relies on a shared identity, onboarding, and operational experience".
   - **Pool**: tenants share resources, "the more classic notion of multi-tenancy".
   - **Bridge**: "some microservices in your architecture might be implemented with silo and others might use pool", decided by "the regulatory profile of a service's data and its noisy neighbor attributes".
   **Data model**: tenant = organization in Axleby ID; legal entity, region, rooftop and department are directory nodes; every tenant-owned record, event, object and cache entry carries a mandatory tenant id and, where meaningful, a rooftop id.
   **Cells**: one UK cell (Showroomly, AWS eu-west-2) and, from 2028, two EU cells for Lotwise in eu-west-1, assigned by tenant, never by rooftop, with separate AWS accounts and credentials.
   **Silo-clone tier**: a dedicated cell for one tenant is a priced option for any tenant with more than 100 rooftops or a regulatory requirement it can name.
   **Evidence**: per-tenant isolation evidence (leak-test results, access logs, key ids) is a standard export, because R4 and R5 are mostly satisfied by evidence, not by dedicated hardware.
   - **One customer is a large share of the base.** If one contract (say, a manufacturer's whole dealer network) is 20% of load, a dedicated cell for it is cheap per unit and lowers everyone's risk.
   - **A regulator or contract names the isolation.** A contractual requirement for dedicated infrastructure is met with a priced silo-clone cell.
   - **Data residency splits the base.** A US expansion would be a new cell (or region of cells) for legal and latency reasons, not a silo per tenant.
   - **The data engine lacks row-level controls and the code is untrusted.** Lotwise Classic on MySQL is Axleby's weak spot until dossier data moves to PostgreSQL services; its leak tests run hardest there.
   | **Group stock view, central pricing** | Within (across rooftops) | Nothing crosses a tenant boundary | Permissions (Gatekeeper) decide who sees which rooftops; regions are just groupings |
   | **Stock transfer between rooftops** | Within | Stock item moves rooftop; dossier and history move with it | Effective-dated move; adverts withdrawn and republished per rooftop channel accounts |
   | **Trade between dealers on Tradefloor** | Across | A **listing** the seller chose to publish; at sale, a **copy** of agreed dossier facts | Explicit publish by the seller; buyer receives a copy, not a reference; personal data of the previous retail customer never crosses ([[b2b-trade-and-remarketing]]) |
   | **Lessor returns to a dealer** | Across | Return condition report, agreed documents | Contracted data-sharing agreement between the two tenants; scoped, expiring access |
   | **External members** (agency, manufacturer field manager, DMS support) | Into a tenant | Access, not data | Expiring membership invited by a tenant admin; every action audited under the person's own identity |
   | **Market benchmarks** built from many tenants' stock and prices | Across, aggregated | Aggregates only | Contract permits it; minimum cell sizes; never per competitor ([[data-products-and-insights]]) |
   | **Shared vehicle facts** (identity, specification, history) | Not tenant data | — | Licensed reference data, governed by [[data-licensing-and-governance]] |
   - **`org.id` (the tenant) and `rooftop.id` on every trace and log line**, set from the signed context, never from the request.
   - **Only tenant tier and product on metrics.** Seven thousand tenant values as a metric label, multiplied by endpoints and status codes, is a cardinality bill; per-tenant views come from traces and logs ([[observability-and-data-operations]]).
   - **Baggage stripped at egress** to portals, DMS vendors and data suppliers.
   - **No consumer identifiers in telemetry**: plates tied to a consumer, names and phone numbers are hashed or omitted ([[privacy-and-retention]]).
   | **Cross-tenant exposure** | The Cross-Tenant Lead | Response-side assertion drops and alerts; canary-tenant markers; leak tests in CI | Kill switch per feature; identify exposed records from tenant-tagged logs; breach assessment within the legal window; notify affected tenants |
   | **Duplicate tenants** | Velto exists as 46 Lotwise accounts, 7 Axleby Portal customers and later a Showroomly account | Directory match on company registration and VAT numbers, DMS site codes, portal account ids | Merge saga with a successor pointer; old ids keep resolving; manual review for weak matches |
   | **Missing tenant context** | A background job or consumer runs without context | Libraries throw; database denies by default; a metric counts rejected calls | Fail closed; the job retries per item with that item's context |
   | **Conflicting hierarchy changes** | Two admins move the same rooftop at once | Optimistic concurrency on hierarchy version | Second write rejected with the current version; admin retries |
   | **Incomplete hierarchy** | A rooftop without a legal entity, so no invoice and no register authority | Directory validation on activation | Rooftop stays in Onboarding until complete |
   | **Partial restructuring** | Rooftop moved in Lotwise but channel accounts and billing still point at the seller | Saga state per step; daily reconciliation of directory against Gatekeeper, Relay, Meterline | Retry the failed step; reconciliation report lists drift for an operator |
   | **Stale permission after a move** | A salesperson who moved to a competitor still sees old leads through a cached decision | Consistency token on membership changes; cache invalidation on `membership.ended` | Short cache lifetimes; revocation is pushed, not polled |
   | **Control-plane outage** | Tenant directory or Axleby ID unavailable | Health checks; synthetic logins | Existing sessions continue on signed tokens and cached hierarchy; new logins and structural changes wait |
   | **Noisy neighbor** | A group's DMS re-sync of 2,000 cars slows appraisals for everyone | Per-tenant queue depth and latency by tenant tier | Fair scheduler throttles the tenant's bulk queue; interactive pool is unaffected |
   **Tenant-tagged logs are what make that possible**: in June 2027 the first hour went on finding out whom to tell.
   | **Legal entity identifiers** | Chamber of Commerce number (KvK) in NL; enterprise number (KBO/BCE) in BE; VAT number | Commercial register entry (Handelsregister) and VAT number | Companies House number; VAT number | State registration and federal employer id; dealer licence per state |
   | **Register authority on the hierarchy** | Axleby Trade's register transactions need the dealer's company authorization from the Dutch register, held by a legal entity for its locations; Belgian transactions run through a partner.
   | **Breach notification after a cross-tenant leak** | GDPR Art. 33: supervisory authority within 72 hours (Autoriteit Persoonsgegevens; Belgian Data Protection Authority) | GDPR Art. 33; state data-protection authorities | UK GDPR: ICO within 72 hours | FTC Safeguards Rule: notify the FTC when at least 500 consumers' unencrypted information is involved, within 30 days, plus state breach laws ([FTC, n.d.](https://www.
   | **Customer compliance pulled into the platform** | GDPR processor terms | Same | UK GDPR processor terms | Most dealers that arrange financing are covered by the Safeguards Rule, which requires multi-factor authentication and user-activity logging and oversight of service providers "by contract" ([FTC, 2025](https://www.ftc.gov/business-guidance/resources/automobile-dealers-ftcs-safeguards-rule-frequently-asked-que
   | **Per-user activity data inside a tenant** | NL: works-council consent for systems suitable for monitoring staff ([WOR art. 27(1)(l)](https://wetten.overheid.nl/BWBR0002747/)) | Works-council co-determination over technical devices that monitor employees ([BetrVG §87(1) no. 6](https://www.gesetze-im-internet.de/betrvg/__87.html)) | No equivalent veto | No equivalent veto |
   | **Switching and export at offboarding** | EU Data Act switching rules apply from 12 September 2025; switching charges end 12 January 2027 ([Regulation (EU) 2023/2854](http://publications.europa.eu/resource/celex/32023R2854)) | Same | EU Data Act does not apply to UK customers | No equivalent law |
   - **The definition of tenant.** Decided once, at group level, by the Chief Architect with product and finance.
