   - **The isolation requirements.** Written as requirements (R1–R8 above) and owned jointly with the security lead and the Data Protection Officer.
   - **The silo-clone tier's price and threshold.** A commercial decision with an architectural floor: the same release, the same team, no forks.
   - **Cross-tenant features.** Each new one (a benchmark, a trade exchange, a lessor portal) goes through a short review that names the explicit act, what is copied, and how access ends.
   - **Context coverage**: share of services, queues, caches and tables that enforce tenant context in a library or the database, by product.
   - **Leak-test coverage and canary alerts**: zero canary alerts is the target; zero leak tests is the warning sign.
   - **Duplicate-tenant backlog**: customers still represented by more than one record across products.
   - **Restructuring lead time**: days from a signed rooftop sale to a reconciled transfer.
   - **Noisy-neighbor incidents** by tenant tier, and **per-tenant cost** against R2.
===== identity-access-and-entitlements 61
   An **identity** is a person or a piece of software that can authenticate.
   - A **user** is a person.
   - An **API client** is a registered piece of software that belongs to one organization and calls Axleby on its own behalf or on a dealer's.
   | **Tenant boundary** | Does this request belong to this tenant at all? | Platform | Tenant context from a verified token ([[tenancy-and-dealer-groups]]) | Never within a request | A Showroomly user's request for another dealer's lead |
   | **Relationship** | Is this identity related to this object in a way that allows the action? | The customer's administrators | Gatekeeper relationship tuples | Staff join, move, leave; groups reorganize | A Breda salesperson opening a lead assigned to a Tilburg colleague |
   | **Entitlement** | Has this tenant or rooftop bought the feature, and within which limits? | Commercial (contract) | Entitlement service, derived from subscriptions | Contract signed, upgraded, cancelled | Ordering a valuation at a rooftop without Lotwise Appraise |
   | **Register authority** | Is the legal entity recognized by the register for this transaction, with a valid credential, and has the customer authorized it? | The register and the dealer | Authority register (kept by Axleby Trade's register-transactions context, read here as a gate), fed by Turnstile's certificate monitor | Recognition granted or withdrawn; certificate expiry; customer authorization | Registering a c
   | **Licensed purpose** | May this data be used for this client's purpose under the source licence? | Data governance | Charter ([[data-licensing-and-governance]]) | Contract and licence changes | A lead-generation client requesting full history data for resale |
   | **Role-based (RBAC)** | The user's role | "Used-car manager" may reprice stock | Role explosion: "used-car manager at Breda and Tilburg" is a new role, and a regional reorganization rewrites hundreds of assignments |
   | **Attribute-based (ABAC)** | Attributes of user, object and context | May approve a deal if the discount is under the user's approval limit and the deal is at one of the user's rooftops | Policies become hard to audit: nobody can list who *can* approve, only test a given request |
   | **Relationship-based (ReBAC)** | A graph of relationships between identities and objects | User is manager of region Noord-Brabant, which groups rooftop Breda, which holds this stock item | Needs a relationship store, a consistency model and tooling to answer "who can?" |
   An **entitlement** is a right a tenant or rooftop has bought: a module, a feature, a quota or an allowance, with effective dates and the contract it came from.
   **Register authority** is the legal capacity of an organization to perform an official transaction with a vehicle register, together with the credential that proves it and, where required, the vehicle owner's authorization for the specific transaction.
   | **Axleby ID** (Keycloak 26 with organizations) | Authenticate users and API clients; federation per organization; MFA and passkeys; token issuance; SCIM endpoint; legacy account linking | Credentials, sessions, linked legacy accounts, IdP settings per organization | Permissions, hierarchy, entitlements |
   | **Tenant directory** | Hierarchy of organizations, legal entities, regions, rooftops, departments; lifecycle events | Hierarchy and memberships' placement ([[tenancy-and-dealer-groups]]) | What members may do |
   | **Gatekeeper** (OpenFGA) | Relationship tuples and the authorization model; `check` and `list objects` answers | Who relates to what | Commercial rights, legal authority |
   | **Entitlement service** | Derive entitlements from subscriptions and contract overrides; publish changes | Effective-dated entitlements per tenant and rooftop | Prices and invoices ([[billing-and-metering]]) |
   | **Authority register** (owned by Axleby Trade's register-transactions context; consumed by the access path as a gate) | Recognitions per legal entity and rooftop, certificate validity, designated register agents, customer authorizations per transaction | Evidence of legal authority | The transactions ([[registration-and-trade-services]]) |
   | **Charter** | Licensed purposes per client and source; request-path purpose check | Licence and purpose register | Identity |
   | **Decision log** | Append-only record of significant authorization decisions and administrative changes | Audit evidence | Business events |
   **Rooftops are not Keycloak organizations**: the hierarchy lives in the tenant directory and relationships live in Gatekeeper, so Keycloak never has to model regions or departments.
   - **Regions are optional and replaceable.** When Velto reorganized 46 rooftops into 9 regional hubs in September 2027, the change was 46 `rooftop#region` tuples and 9 `region#manager` tuples.
   - **Leads are private by default within a rooftop.** Salespeople see their own leads, managers all of the rooftop's: leads are sensitive between rooftops and between colleagues competing for commission.
   - **Register agents are designated per rooftop and expire.** A person may submit register transactions for a rooftop only if the dealer has designated them, for a period.
   - **Approval limits stay out of the graph.** A discount limit of €1,500 for a sales manager is an attribute of the membership checked in the product (or with a condition), not a relationship.
   | **Static API key** | A long-lived secret sent with each request | Sandbox, trials, low-value public data | Leaks into code, logs and browser apps; no expiry unless enforced; one key often shared by every system at the client | Sandbox only: test vehicles, 30-day expiry, per-key and per-network rate limits *(Axleby choices)* |
   | **OAuth 2.0 client credentials with a shared secret** | Client exchanges id and secret for a short-lived token | Server-to-server production calls | The secret can still leak, but tokens expire and scopes apply | Allowed until 2028 for small clients |
   | **Client credentials with a private-key JWT or mutual TLS** | Client proves possession of a private key | High-volume or high-value production clients | Key management burden on the client | Default for JSON API v2 production clients |
   | **Username and password in a SOAP header, IP allow-list** | Legacy | Nothing new | Credentials shared across a client's systems; no scopes | Connect XML only, frozen until its sunset on 31 December 2029 |
   **Cox Automotive's Bridge ID** is the most detailed public description of identity unification across an acquired dealer-software portfolio.
   **CDK Global's CDK Unify** is described as "a secure, cloud-based single sign-on" with multifactor authentication and "role-based dashboards" ([CDK Global, n.d., accessed 2026-10](https://www.cdkglobal.com/cdk-unify)); CDK publishes no further detail.
   | **Duplicate persons** | One salesperson with a Lotwise login, an Axleby Portal login and a Showroomly login under three e-mail addresses | Matching on verified e-mail, phone and IdP subject; duplicate-person report per tenant | Link to one Axleby ID person; never auto-link on an unverified e-mail, which is an account-takeover path |
   | **Conflicting updates** | Velto's SCIM removes a user from Breda while a Velto administrator adds them in the Axleby console | Source flag on each membership: SCIM-managed or console-managed | SCIM wins for SCIM-managed memberships; the console shows them read-only |
   | **Incomplete records** | SCIM creates a user with no rooftop group; a legal entity without recorded recognition | Validation on provisioning and on activation | User exists with no access (default deny) and appears on the admin's to-do list; registrations blocked with a clear reason |
   | **Retries** | SCIM retries a create after a timeout | Idempotency by SCIM `externalId` | Second create returns the existing user |
   | **Partial failure** | A subscription cancelled in billing, but the entitlement event lost | Hourly reconciliation of entitlements against subscriptions | Re-derive from subscriptions; billing and access agree again within the hour |
   | **Stale permission** | A salesperson who left for a competitor still sees leads through a cached decision | Membership-ended events; consistency mode after changes; canary checks | Evict caches; refuse refresh; review the decision log for the window |
   | **Expired authority** | A company's register certificate expires or a recognition is withdrawn | Turnstile's daily certificate check; register rejection codes | Block submissions for that entity; notify the dealer 60, 30 and 7 days before expiry; queued transactions wait, never re-route under another company's credential |
   | **Customer IdP outage** | Velto's Entra ID unavailable on a Saturday | Federation errors per organization | Two break-glass local accounts per organization, phishing-resistant MFA, every use alerted to the customer |
   | **Axleby ID outage** | Keycloak cluster unavailable | Synthetic sign-ins per cell | Existing tokens remain valid until expiry; products keep working; new sign-ins wait |
   | **Gatekeeper outage** | OpenFGA unavailable or slow | Latency and error SLOs | Cached decisions serve views for up to 30 seconds; writes, exports and register transactions fail closed |
   | **Leaked API credential** | A client's key posted in a public repository | Secret scanning feeds; unusual source networks; enumeration patterns | Revoke and reissue within minutes; client-scoped blast radius because the credential belongs to one API client with limited scopes |
   | **Register authority** | NL: RDW recognitions per company; a two-year RDW certificate; replacing a lost one requires eHerkenning level 3 ([RDW, 2026](https://www.rdw.nl/-/media/rdwnl/documenten/bedrijf-met-rdw-erkenning/toezichtbeleid/tbb-en-informatiemap-2026/informatiemap.pdf)).
   | **Customer authorization per transaction** | NL: buyer's *machtiging* via DigiD or eHerkenning, valid 3 months | Power of attorney (*Vollmacht*) for registration services; qualified signature on the GKS route | Keeper details and V5C log book; no per-transaction digital mandate in Axleby's scope | State title and power-of-attorney forms; odometer disclosure on transfer |
   | **Platform credentials to registers** | Provider channel with certificates | KBA credentials and technical certificate (not an Axleby service) | DVLA VES: one key per company, registration closed; DVSA: OAuth client credentials, two-year secrets, 90-day inactivity revocation | Approved providers for title-history data; DPPA limits on personal data |
   | **Purpose as an authorization input** | GDPR purpose limitation; RDW provider terms | §39 of the Road Traffic Act (StVG): keeper data only for a shown legal claim ([StVG §39, accessed 2026-10](https://www.gesetze-im-internet.de/stvg/__39.html)) | Keeper data only with "reasonable cause" | DPPA permissible uses and five-year redisclosure records |
   | **MFA required by law for dealers** | None dealer-specific; GDPR Art. 32 security measures | Same | Same | Federal Trade Commission (FTC) Safeguards Rule: MFA "for any individual accessing any information system" for most dealers that finance or lease ([16 CFR 314.4](https://www.law.cornell.edu/cfr/text/16/314.4)) |
   | **Per-user decision logs** | NL: works-council consent for systems suitable for monitoring staff ([WOR art. 27](https://wetten.overheid.nl/BWBR0002747/)) | Works-council co-determination over monitoring devices ([BetrVG §87](https://www.gesetze-im-internet.de/betrvg/__87.html)) | No equivalent veto | Safeguards Rule requires logging authorized users' activity |
   **What is common** is the whole architecture.
   **What is a market adapter** is register authority: its credential types, the customer-authorization evidence and the transaction channel differ completely.
   **What is a rule set** is purpose: §39 StVG, the DPPA and GDPR purpose limitation all become purposes recorded in Charter and checked per request.
   - **One identity, owned by the group.** The decision to retire product logins is the Chief Architect's and the CTO's, with a dated plan.
   - **The five gates and their owners.** Written down once: commercial owns entitlements, customers' administrators own relationships, the register and the dealer own authority, data governance owns purposes, the platform owns the tenant boundary.
   - **The authorization model.** Gatekeeper's schema is a shared contract like an API: versioned, reviewed by the platform team and covered by the leak tests of [[tenancy-and-dealer-groups]].
   - **Credential policy for machines.** Which credential types are allowed for which data, their lifetimes and the trial-key rules.
   - **Shared-login indicators**: logins used from many devices or places at once.
   - **Legacy accounts not yet linked**, per product and per tenant.
   - **Leaver latency**: minutes from an IdP deactivation to the last successful request by that person.
   - **Static keys in production**, by client and data value, trending to zero outside the Connect XML estate.
   - **Entitlement–subscription mismatches** found by reconciliation.
   - **Authority expiry horizon**: legal entities whose certificates or recognitions expire within 60 days.
===== onboarding-and-configuration 73
   | **Time to first advert** (rooftop provisioned → first advert confirmed live) | **36 days** | **11 days** | |
   **Onboarding** is everything between a signed contract and a customer using the product for its core job without the vendor's help.
   | **Contract** | Legal entities, rooftops, modules and prices recorded as data, not as a PDF | Sales, billing | — |
   | **Account** | Tenant, legal entities, rooftops and administrators exist; login works | Platform | Customer's identity provider (if federated) |
   | **Verification** | Each legal entity is a real trade business (company register number, VAT number) | Customer operations | Company register |
   | **Authority** | Each legal entity's register recognition, the rooftops (locations) it covers and the users allowed to transact are recorded | Axleby Trade | The register |
   | **Integrations** | DMS and other adapters approved, credentialed and passing a sync test | Partner integrations | DMS vendor |
   | **Migration** | Stock, history and customers imported and reconciled; dealer signed off | Implementation | Previous vendor |
   | **Channel activation** | Each portal account points at the new feed; the old feed is off | Partner integrations | Portals, previous vendor |
   | **Configuration** | Pricing policy, templates, routing and roles set at group and rooftop level | Customer, with implementation | — |
   | **Training** | Staff with roles have done the task once on their own stock | Customer success | — |
   | **Go-live** | The rooftop runs its core job on the platform; the previous tool is read-only or off | Customer | — |
   The **register recognition** is a Dutch example of an authority stage with legal weight.
   **Time to first advert** is the elapsed time from the moment a rooftop is provisioned on the platform to the moment its first advert is **confirmed live** by a channel, meaning the portal has acknowledged the insert or the advert has been observed on the portal, not merely that the platform queued it.
   - **Time to full stock live:** until 95% of the rooftop's eligible stock is live on every contracted channel.
   - **Time to first register transaction:** for Dutch rooftops, the first registration in a new name (*tenaamstellen*) from the dossier.
   - **Time to first production call:** for data clients, the first billable API call with a production key.
   | **Current stock** | The rooftop cannot advertise without it | All units, with photos, prices, costs and channel status | VIN; dealer stock number as alias |
   | **Sold history** | Stand-time, margin and pricing analytics; buy-back and warranty questions | Commonly two to three years | VIN plus sale date |
   | **Customers and open leads** | Follow-up and repeat business | Open and recent only | Varies; personal data rules apply ([[privacy-and-retention]]) |
   **Tenant configuration** is the set of typed values, rules and templates that change a tenant's product behavior inside limits the product defines, and that the product stores, validates and versions as data.
   **Customization** is any change to behavior the product did not anticipate.
   An **extension point** is a documented, versioned place where code the vendor does not own may change or observe behavior, without running inside the vendor's process: a webhook on a domain event, a partner API call, a callout to a customer-hosted rule, a document template.
   A **fork** is vendor code that runs only for one customer: a branch, a tenant-conditional code path, a customer-specific build.
   | **Settings** | Values of predefined options | None | Default margin target, working hours, VAT scheme default |
   | **Policies** | Settings fixed at group level, locked for rooftops | None | Minimum advertised margin; mandatory history report before purchase |
   | **Rules** | Declarative conditions and actions the product evaluates | Low, if the rule language is versioned | Lead routing by brand; reprice suggestion after 45 days |
   | **Templates** | Documents, e-mails, advert text blocks | Low | Brand-specific offer PDF; Belgian French and Dutch versions |
   | **Custom fields** | Schema extension stored as metadata | Low to medium | "Warranty provider" on the stock item |
   | **Workflow steps** | Approvals and required steps | Medium | Purchase above €40,000 needs the regional manager |
   | **Extension points** | Webhooks, partner API, customer-hosted callouts | Medium (contract-versioned) | Push sold cars to the group's data warehouse |
   | **Forks** | Vendor source code | Very high, permanent | None, by policy |
   - **Idempotent and re-runnable.** Each record is upserted under a key of (tenant, rooftop, source system, source record id), with the VIN resolved through Anchor as the vehicle key.
   - **Identity first.** Every stock item is resolved to an Axleby Vehicle Key (AVK) through Anchor before it becomes a stock record, as [[vehicle-identity]] requires.
   - **Provenance kept.** Imported facts carry their source ("previous vendor export, 3 August 2026") so that a later conflict with DriveDesk or the register can be explained ([[provenance-and-data-quality]]).
   - **Reconciliation the dealer signs.** The report compares three counts per rooftop: units in the export, units in the DMS stock feed and units in Lotwise, with every difference listed by VIN.
   - **Typed and validated on write.** A routing rule that names a rooftop, a queue or a salesperson is checked against the tenant directory and the integration registry when it is saved, and again when the target changes state.
   - **Versioned and effective-dated.** Every change creates a new version with author, reason and an effective time.
   - **Stamped on decisions.** A price suggestion, a routing decision or a generated document records the configuration snapshot version it used.
   - **Simulated before applied.** A rule change can be previewed against current stock or the last month's leads: "this repricing rule would change 312 prices today", "this routing change would move 18% of Breda's leads". [[used-car-pricing]] describes simulation for pricing rules; the same mechanism serves every rule.
   - **Inherited with locks.** The group sets defaults and locks what it wants uniform (minimum margin, legal texts, mandatory checks); rooftops override what is delegated (opening hours, local templates, who receives leads).
   - **Separate from entitlements and release flags.** What a rooftop has bought lives in the entitlement service; what a feature's rollout state is lives in the flag service; what the rooftop has chosen lives in Baseline.
   **Market packs** are configuration the provider owns: Dutch registration-tax (*BPM*) fields, Belgian language and e-invoicing rules, German registration data, UK document formats.
   - **Webhooks** on domain events (stock item changed, advert published or removed, lead received, deal signed), with the delivery semantics of [[events-webhooks-and-sync]]: signed, retried, idempotent by event id.
   - **The partner API** for reads and writes the customer's own systems need, governed like any API client ([[vehicle-data-apis]]).
   - **Customer-hosted callouts** for a small number of decision points (a purchase approval, a price check) with a strict timeout and a defined default when the callout fails.
   - **Templates** with a sandboxed template language and no access to data outside the document's context.
   | **Duplicate records in the export** | 212 VIN pairs from internal transfers | VIN resolution in staging finds two source records for one AVK | Merge by rule (latest rooftop owns, transfer kept as event); ambiguous pairs to the work queue |
   | **Duplicate imports** | A batch re-run after a timeout | Upsert key (tenant, rooftop, source, source id) already present with the same hash | No-op; report counts unchanged records separately |
   | **Incomplete records** | Stock without VIN, photos missing, no specification | Validation in staging | Resolve through Anchor; otherwise hold out of stock and queue for the dealer; never invent a VIN or a specification |
   | **Conflicting values** | Import says €18,950, DriveDesk says €19,450 | Reconciliation compares the three sources per VIN | Rule agreed with the dealer before import (Velto: DMS wins for price and cost, the import wins for photos and advert text); remaining conflicts listed, not overwritten |
   | **Changes during coexistence** | A car sold in DriveDesk after the export | DMS sold event arrives for a staged record | DMS events are applied after import by event time; the import never resurrects a car the DMS has sold |
   | **Double feed at hand-over** | 418 cars advertised twice | Relay finds adverts it did not create on a handed-over account | Ask the previous vendor and the portal to stop the old feed; Relay keeps its adverts; daily verification until clean |
   | **Dark period at hand-over** | Old feed stopped before the new one was accepted | Advert count on the portal falls to zero | Restore the old feed if still possible; otherwise prioritize the new account with the portal; record the hours offline |
   | **Partial wave failure** | 7 of 9 rooftops ready on the wave date | Runway states per rooftop | Go-live is per rooftop; the other two wait without blocking the wave |
   | **External outage during cut-over** | DMS offline (the DriveDesk Outage, November 2027, lasted 12 days) | DMS adapter circuit breaker | Do not hand over channels while the DMS is down; complete migration from the export, freeze go-live, reconcile against the DMS on recovery before resuming |
   | **Bad configuration change** | A routing rule pointing at a closed rooftop | Validation on save and on target state change; simulation | Reject the save, or mark the rule invalid and route to the group's fallback queue; roll back to the previous version by id |
   | **Onboarding mode** | Self-service | Assisted project | One rooftop, standard modules, file or known-vendor import | Groups, DMS integration, several legal entities or countries |
   | **Rollout of a group** | Big bang (all rooftops on one date) | Pilot and waves | Small groups (under about five rooftops *(assumption)*) with one DMS | Larger groups; any group with mixed countries, brands or DMSs |
   | **History depth** | Current stock only | Two to three years of sold history | Dealer has no analytics need; previous data is poor | Pricing and stand-time analytics from day one; buy-back and warranty questions |
   | **Previous tool after go-live** | Switched off | Read-only for a period | Clean export and signed reconciliation | Open deals and warranty claims still reference it |
   | **Customer special request** | New configuration for everyone | Extension point for this customer | Others will want it; it fits the product's model | It is about the customer's own systems (warehouse, ERP, intranet) |
   | **Group policy** | Lock at group | Delegate to rooftop | Legal texts, margin floors, mandatory checks | Opening hours, local templates, lead assignment |
   | **Configuration store** | Central service (Baseline) | Each product keeps its own settings | More than one product shares a tenant; groups want one policy | A single product with no group features |
   | **Customer code** | Webhooks and partner API outside the process | In-process scripts or plug-ins | Thousands of tenants; strict isolation; one release train | A few very large tenants paying for a dedicated deployment |
   | **Business verification** | Company register and VAT numbers; Belgian enterprise number | Commercial register (*Handelsregister*) and VAT number | Companies House and VAT registration | State dealer licence per location; federal tax id |
   | **Register authority to record** | NL: the register's trade-stock recognition per legal entity, the rooftops it covers and the users allowed to transact; BE: access to the Belgian register through a partner | Registration through local registration offices; authority is not a single national dealer recognition *(judgement)* | Dealers notify the DVLA online as motor traders when a keeper sells to them | Title and re
   | **DMS approval** | Long tail of local and brand-approved DMSs; per-vendor partner terms | Most manufacturer-shaped market; brand-captive DMSs hold large networks | Keyloop, Pinewood and a tail of smaller vendors | Certified integration programs with fees and certification |
   | **Channel hand-over** | Marktplaats, AutoScout24, brand portals; feed supplier set per account | mobile.de, AutoScout24 | Auto Trader UK dominant; one feed supplier per account is common practice *(judgement)* | Many portals and manufacturer programs |
   | **Market pack contents** | BPM fields (NL); NL and FR templates and e-invoicing in Belgium (mandatory B2B since 1 January 2026) | Registration data; e-invoice issuing obligations phasing in from 2027 *(widely reported)* | V5C registration certificate and MOT (annual roadworthiness test) data; e-invoicing for VAT invoices from 2029 announced | State forms, sales tax, Buyers Guide |
   | **Employee monitoring** | NL: works-council consent for systems suitable for monitoring staff performance | Works-council co-determination over monitoring devices | No equivalent veto | No equivalent veto |
   | **Switching law** | EU Data Act switching rules; no switching charges from 12 January 2027 | Same | Not in scope for UK customers | No equivalent law |
   | **MFA expectation** | Customer policy and GDPR security duty | Same | UK GDPR security duty | FTC Safeguards Rule requires MFA for covered dealers |
===== billing-and-metering 72
   **Subscriptions** are priced per rooftop per month and change mid-month.
   **API calls** are tiered by volume and free when nothing is found.
   **Transaction fees** are only earned when an external register confirms the transaction.
   | **Empty history** | A plate found in the register mirror but with no history events returned "no records" and was not billed | The same case returned a report with an empty event list and was billed as a hit at €4.95 | €97,400 |
   | **Partial specification** | A plate with register data but no build data from the licensed build-data provider was billed only if build data came back | Billed at €0.09 whenever the register part of the response was filled | €51,300 |
   | **Retried calls** | Billed per distinct client request reference | Billed per HTTP request; a client retry after a gateway timeout produced a second billable call | €37,300 |
   | **Total** | | | **€186,000 to 41 API clients** |
   | **Module subscription** | Rooftop per month | Lotwise Stock & Pricing €189; Syndication €129; Website & Leads €149 | Mid-month changes, rooftops opening, closing and changing owner, group discounts |
   | **Subscription with allowance** | Rooftop per month, plus included units | Lotwise Appraise €99 with 25 history reports a month | Counting against the allowance across products, pooling across a group |
   | **Per-report or per-call fee** | Report or API call, outcome-dependent | Business history report €4.95; valuation call €0.18 tiered down to €0.06; plate-to-specification €0.09 with no-hit-no-fee | Outcome classification, retries, volume tiers, free re-opens |
   | **Transaction fee plus pass-through** | Completed external transaction | Registration in a new name €6.50 plus the register's own fee | Billing only on confirmation, separating Axleby's fee from the third party's fee |
   A **usage event** is an immutable record that one billable thing happened, written by the service that did it, at the moment it happened. "Billable" is decided by the product catalog, not by the price: a no-hit history lookup is a usage event with a price of zero, because the event is evidence that the call was made and was free.
   The **business moment** is the point at which the platform has done the thing it will charge for: the report is delivered, the register has confirmed the transaction, the subscription period has started.
   The **outcome** is the service's classification of the result, from a closed list the catalog defines per product: `hit`, `partial`, `no_hit`, `failed`, plus the billing-only `cached_reopen`.
   **No-hit-no-fee** is the commercial promise that a lookup returning nothing useful is not charged.
   The **rating engine** turns usage events into **rated usage**: priced lines, each referencing its events, the price-book version, the contract terms and every rule that changed the price.
   The **price book** is the versioned, effective-dated catalog of list prices: SKU, unit, currency, market, tiers, and the outcome-to-price map.
   A **billing account** is who receives the invoice and pays it, usually one legal entity with a VAT number, a payment mandate and an invoice address.
   A **tenant** is the contracting organization whose data is isolated ([[tenancy-and-dealer-groups]] owns the term).
   | **Product service** (history assembler, Specbook, Vantage, Axleby Trade back office, Lotwise) | Classify the outcome and emit a usage event at the business moment | Outcome classification for its SKUs | Compute prices or apply discounts |
   | **Outbox** (per service) | Make "the report was delivered" and "the event was recorded" one transaction | Delivery to Meterline, at least once | Drop events when Meterline is slow |
   | **Meterline** | Accept, validate, deduplicate and store usage events; serve them by account, period and business key | The usage log and its retention | Change an event after acceptance |
   | **Subscription service** | Hold subscriptions per billing account and rooftop with effective dates; emit period-start, change and cancel events | Subscription state | Decide what features a rooftop may use (entitlements do that) |
   | **Price book and contract store** | Versioned list prices and customer overrides | Prices, tiers, commitments, effective dates | Be edited in place |
   | **Rating engine** | Price events; apply allowances, tiers, no-hit, re-open, minimums and credits | Rated usage with references to events and versions | Read product logs or databases |
   | **Invoice run** | Assemble rated usage and subscription charges into draft invoices, hold them for review, finalize | Invoice documents and numbering | Re-rate silently after finalization |
   | **Receivables ledger** | Record invoices, credit notes, payments, returns and write-offs | Balances per billing account | Hold usage detail |
   - **The event id is derived, not generated.** A random id created at send time makes every retry a new event; a hash of the client's request reference, SKU and billing account collapses a retried "buy report" into one, whichever stack it arrived through.
   - **The business key is the vehicle and the artifact, not the plate.** Plates change over a car's life and may be personal data ([[vehicle-identity]], [[privacy-and-retention]]); the AVK and the report id identify what was sold.
   - **Corrections are new events.** A reclassification is a reversal event plus a corrected event, both referencing the original.
   **Metering must never sit in the critical path of the thing it meters**: if Meterline is down, dealers still get reports and the outboxes catch up.
   - **The subscription change is an event**, rated like usage; a proration line references it, rather than a diff of two subscription snapshots.
   - **Entitlements and billing read the same subscription**, so a module switched on is a module charged from the same date.
   A **credit** is a negative rated line with a reason code (`no_hit_misclassified`, `data_correction`, `service_outage`, `goodwill`), an actor, an approval and links to the disputed events, never a quiet edit of an invoice.
   - The **SEPA B2B** scheme is for business payers only; payment-provider guides describe it as removing the refund right for authorized collections and requiring the debtor's bank to hold the mandate before the first collection ([GoCardless, n.d., accessed 2026-10](https://gocardless.com/en-us/guides/sepa/timings)).
   - **Margin per client and per SKU**: revenue from rated usage minus upstream cost recorded on the same events.
   - **Pass-through**: for fees Axleby recharges at cost (the register's fee on a registration in a new name), the charge line is generated from the cost recorded on the event, so the two cannot diverge.
   - **Pricing rule cost**: the no-hit rate per client, multiplied by the upstream cost of no-hits, is the price of the no-hit-no-fee promise.
   - **Usage to date by SKU, rooftop and outcome**, provisional during the period, with the allowance consumed and remaining.
   - **Line drill-down** from every invoice line to its events: time, user or client, outcome, vehicle and rules applied.
   - **A daily usage export** with the event id and the client's own request reference, for automatic reconciliation.
   - **Spend alerts and caps** per API client: an alert at a threshold and an optional hard cap.
   - **Per-location subscriptions in the United States.** Mobility Global, Carfax's parent as of 2026, states in its August 2026 quarterly report that Carfax segment revenue "is primarily driven by the number of dealer locations enrolled in dealer subscription products ... the average monthly price per location ... and the number of BIG customers" (its banking and insurance group) ([Mobility Global Form 10-Q, 2026](http
   - **Subscriptions plus integration fees in DMS pricing.** CDK Global's last public annual report (fiscal year to June 2021) defined "average revenue per DMS customer site" (DMS: dealer management system) as including "DMS monthly fees, layered applications and data integration fees" ([CDK Global 10-K, 2021](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1609702/000160970221000058/cdk-20210630.htm)).
   - **Outcome-dependent fees in the Netherlands.** VWE's public pricing combines monthly modules ([Occasion Manager](https://vwe.nl/occasion-manager/), n.d., accessed 2026-10) with per-report fees, including the no-damage-no-charge report above.
   - **Metering products describe two architectures.** Stripe's billing meters aggregate events on ingest, and adjustments can cancel only events "sent to Stripe within the last 24 hours" ([Stripe docs, n.d., accessed 2026-10](https://docs.stripe.com/billing/subscriptions/usage-based/meters/configure)).
   | **Axleby charges** | | | **722.80** |
   | **Duplicate events** | Client retry after a timeout; outbox relays twice; replay after a Meterline restore | Derived `event_id` collides | Deduplication store drops the second; replay is safe by design |
   | **Missing events** | A product deploy disables event emission for one SKU | Daily completeness check: gateway request counts and product delivery logs vs Meterline events per SKU and client, with tolerance bands | Backfill from the product's outbox or delivery log as events with a `backfilled` flag; review before rating |
   | **Incomplete events** | Event without `rooftop_id` because a Lotwise screen did not pass it | Schema validation at ingestion | Accept into a quarantine partition, never drop; resolve from the user's session or rate at billing-account level with a flag |
   | **Misclassified outcomes** | History service returns `hit` for an empty report after a refactor | Outcome mix per SKU compared with its baseline; rising dispute credits | Fix classification; emit reversal and corrected events for the affected window; credit automatically |
   | **Conflicting price changes** | A contract amendment signed on the 20th, effective from the 1st | Contract store records signature date and effective date | Re-rate the open period; if the period is closed, credit or debit as catch-up lines in the next invoice, never by editing a finalized invoice |
   | **Late events** | Outbox backlog after a Meterline outage spills past the 48-hour cut-off | Event `occurred_at` in a closed period | Catch-up lines in the next period, labeled with their original dates |
   | **Billing on ambiguous external outcomes** | A register submission times out; the retry queue resubmits | The register transaction id and the register's confirmation | Emit the usage event only on confirmation, keyed on the register transaction; a double submission that needs correction produces no second fee |
   | **Partial invoice-run failure** | 300 drafts fail on a tax-rule error | Run report per account; every account must reach Draft or Hold | Re-run only failed accounts; the run is idempotent per account and period |
   | **Collection failure** | SEPA return for insufficient funds; Core refund request after a dispute | Bank return and refund messages | Post to receivables ledger, re-open the receivable, notify, re-present or route to credit process |
   | **Data correction after billing** | The Odometer Correction of May 2027: 6,140 valuations computed on wrong mileage | `fact_version` on valuation usage events joined with the correction's affected vehicles | A credit policy decided per product and customer type, applied to exactly the affected events |
   **Meterline outages** are absorbed by the outboxes, sized to buffer **72 hours** of peak usage *(Axleby target)*; the customer-visible effect is a stale usage portal and spend caps running on the gateway counter alone.
   | **Where to classify outcomes** | In each product service | At the gateway from responses | Always for outcome-priced products; only the service knows what "found" means | Pure request-counted products with no outcome rule |
   | **Aggregate on ingest vs store raw** | Counters per account and SKU (Stripe-meter style) | Raw immutable events, rated by query (Orb-style) | Very high volume, simple prices, no per-vehicle disputes | Outcome-dependent prices, per-vehicle disputes, frequent price changes; Axleby's case |
   | **Build vs buy the rating engine** | Buy a usage-billing product (Metronome, Orb, others) | Build rating in-house over Meterline | Prices fit the product's model; volumes fit its limits; team is small | Pass-through fees, register confirmations, pooled group allowances and market-specific rules the product cannot express |
   | **Rating timing** | Continuous (streaming) | Batch at close | Customers need live spend and caps | Monthly invoices only; but then the usage portal lies until month-end |
   | **Allowances** | Per rooftop | Pooled per billing account | Independents; groups that want rooftop accountability | Groups with uneven rooftops; but needs billing accounts that model the group |
   | **Usage pricing** | Postpaid in arrears | Prepaid credits or commitments | Established dealers with mandates | New API clients, trial keys, consumer-facing resellers; caps credit risk |
   | **Usual pricing shape** | Separate modules per rooftop plus per-report and per-transaction fees (VWE's public prices are an example) | Data clients on licences and per-call fees; dealers on per-rooftop software with data from valuation specialists | Data products often bundled into the advertising package (Auto Trader's package staircase) | Per-location subscriptions (Carfax), DMS per-site pricing including integra
   | **Collection** | SEPA direct debit, Core for small dealers, B2B for groups and data clients | SEPA direct debit; invoice and transfer common for large data clients | Bacs Direct Debit under the Direct Debit Guarantee | Automated Clearing House (ACH) debits and cards; invoice terms for groups *(industry practice)* |
   | **E-invoicing** | Belgium: structured B2B e-invoices mandatory since 1 January 2026, Peppol-based ([FPS Finance, n.d.](https://einvoice.belgium.be/en)); Netherlands: no domestic B2B mandate found | Receiving EN 16931 e-invoices required since 1 January 2025; issuing required from 2027 above €800,000 prior-year turnover and from 2028 for all ([IHK Stuttgart, n.d.](https://www.ihk.de/stuttgart/fuer-unternehmen/recht-
   | **Tax on the platform's own fees** | VAT, reverse charge for cross-border B2B within the EU | VAT | VAT, separate post-Brexit regime | State sales tax on SaaS and data varies by state; remote sellers can be required to collect since *South Dakota v.
   | **Pass-through fees** | Register fees on Dutch transactions passed through at cost; Belgian registrations through a partner | Few register pass-throughs; build-data and valuation licence costs are upstream costs | Register data under per-key API terms; few per-transaction pass-throughs | Title and registration fees handled by dealers and state agencies; history data licensed per report |
   | **Currency** | EUR | EUR | GBP | USD |
   **The migration path**: shadow rating, classified differences, a written cut-over criterion, and the order of retirement.
   The **credit rate by reason code** is the single best early warning: it is how the No-Hit Credit would have been caught in its first month instead of after half a year.
===== data-licensing-and-governance 54
   A **source licence** is the set of conditions under which the platform obtained a fact.
   A **licence class** is the platform's label for a family of identical conditions.
   A **purpose** is an enumerated reason for using data, such as `claims-fraud-check`, `insurance-quote-prefill`, `trade-in-appraisal`, `consumer-history-report`, `fleet-administration` or `dealer-lead-qualification`.
   - The **licence register** is the system of record for source licences, licence classes, purposes and client grants, each stored as a machine-readable, effective-dated rule linked to its contract clause.
   - The **purpose check** is the per-request decision: given caller, product, purpose and market, which sources and fields may be returned and which obligations travel with them.
   - The **access audit** is the append-only record of decisions and deliveries: who received what, for which purpose, under which decision and rule versions.
   | **Licence register** | Source licences, licence classes, purposes, client grants; each rule effective-dated and linked to its clause | The rules and their versions | Contracts themselves (legal holds them); who the caller is ([[identity-access-and-entitlements]]) |
   | **Policy compiler** | Turns register rules into signed, versioned policy bundles; rejects conflicting rules before publishing | Bundle versions | Evaluation |
   | **Embedded decision engine** | Evaluates a request locally against the latest bundle and returns allowed sources and fields, obligations and a decision id | Nothing persistent | Fetching data |
   | **Enforcement points** | Ask before reading or returning licensed data; apply field filters and obligations; write audit records | Their own outputs | The rules |
   | **Access audit** | Append-only, tamper-evident record of decisions and deliveries | Recipient evidence | Operational debugging logs |
   | **Misuse monitors** | Compare each client's traffic with its grant's expected profile | Alerts and cases | Blocking traffic (they suspend through the console) |
   | **Compliance console** | Licences, purposes, grants, DPIA links, audit queries, supplier audit packs, cases | Workflow state | Legal judgement |
   | **Volume against declared need** | Calls per month against the grant's stated volume and the client's business size | From 2,300 to 16,000 a month with the same 85 partner dealers |
   | **Vehicle mix** | Distribution of makes, ages and private versus trade vehicles against the purpose's profile | Shifted toward older private-sale cars, typical of consumer buyers, not trade-ins |
   | **Time pattern** | Hour-of-day and weekday curve | Evening and weekend peaks; dealer purposes peak on weekday working hours |
   | **Follow-through** | Share of lookups followed by the expected next step (a valuation, an appraisal) | Fell from 64% to 9% |
   | **Repeat plates** | The same vehicle looked up by the same client repeatedly | Low, consistent with one-time consumer checks |
   | **External sighting** | Axleby wording or trap records found outside | The page markup carried a supplier reference code |
   - **Watermarked rendering.** Rendered reports and PDF outputs carry a per-client reference and small, harmless variations in the order of non-material fields.
   - **Trap records.** Bulk and file deliveries ([[data-products-and-insights]]) include a few records, unique to each recipient, for plates the register has never issued, in the tradition of map makers' trap streets.
   | **Recipient audit** | Decision id, time, client and grant, end customer, purpose, audience, AVK, sources and licence classes returned, fields withheld, rule and bundle versions, response hash | Suppliers, registers, regulators, disputes | 5 years *(Axleby rule, matching its longest supplier clause and the DPPA period)* |
   | **Operational log** | Request traces, errors, latency | Engineers | 30 days, with plates and vehicle identification numbers (VINs) redacted ([[observability-and-data-operations]]) |
   **No grant without a clause.** **No local exceptions in enforcement points**: a team that needs a different answer changes the reviewed rule, never adds an `if client == …` in its code, the licensing form of the no-tenant-conditional-code rule in [[onboarding-and-configuration]].
   - **Registers inspect their recipients.** The KADOE contract lets the DVLA "check the electronic trail relating to any activity" (D11.3), and the DVLA says it monitors new customers during probation.
   - **Access is tiered by sensitivity and audience.** The RDW separates open, per-plate business and personal data.
   - **Purpose-bound data is becoming a legal category.** Under the Data Act, connected-vehicle data received at a user's request serves only the purpose agreed with that user.
   | **February** | Architecture forum decision; Femke de Boer's team starts the clause inventory |
   | **May** | Licence register and console live: 46 source agreements, 311 rules, 7 licence classes, 16 purposes |
   | **July** | Decision engine in the JSON API composer (field-level) and Tracewell's assembler; build-data life-of-vehicle rule live in Specbook; recipient audit starts |
   | **September** | Pitwall support views audited through Charter |
   | **October** | Connect XML checked at the edge proxy in front of the Utrecht data center, at **category-block level** because the legacy responses cannot be filtered by field; misuse monitors live |
   | **December** | Every external data API call passes Charter; Pulse and insight exports gated |
   | **Duplicate decisions and records** | A client retries after a gateway timeout; the composer evaluates twice | Decision id derived from client request reference and grant, as Meterline derives its event id | Audit deduplicates by decision id; one delivery record per response actually sent |
   | **Conflicting rules** | A supplier amendment allows consumer display for one purpose while the original clause forbids it | Policy compiler rejects the bundle | Steward resolves with legal; previous bundle stays live; most restrictive rule wins until resolved |
   | **Conflicting licences on one fact** | The same odometer reading came from a public source and from a restricted partner | Two attestations on one event ([[vehicle-history]]) | Serve under the least restrictive valid attestation; record which one was used |
   | **Incomplete records** | Event without licence class; grant without retention | Enforcement point sees missing class; compiler refuses incomplete grant | Not served (fail closed); backfill queue; console shows count per source |
   | **Partial failure** | Data assembled, audit write fails | Outbox write error | Restricted classes not delivered; open parts returned with a withheld marker |
   | **Stale policy** | Bundle distribution stalls in one cluster | Bundle age metric per enforcement point | Fail open for open and derived classes up to 24 h; fail closed for restricted |
   | **Late-discovered breach of terms by Axleby** | A cache kept per-request partner data beyond the request (found by a nightly retention scan) | Retention scanner compares store contents with rules | Purge; incident record; supplier notice where the contract demands it |
   | **Supplier terms change** | Partner shortens client retention from 30 to 14 days | New contract version | Effective-dated rule; responses after that date carry the new `retain_until`; earlier deliveries keep their terms |
   | **Register outage** | Licence register database unavailable | Health checks | Enforcement points continue on their last bundle; edits wait; suspensions go out through an emergency deny list pushed with the bundle |
   | **Contract only** (purpose in the PDF, periodic review) | Cheap; no engineering | Misuse found by complaint; no evidence; Lintvelt | Few sources, few clients, all low sensitivity |
   | **Gateway rules per client and product** | Simple; stops wrong products | Cannot filter sources inside a response; licence knowledge copied into gateway config | Products map one-to-one to licence classes |
   | **Embedded purpose check with field filtering** (Charter) | Per-source and per-field decisions; evidence; fast | Needs licence classes on every fact; rule translation effort | Mixed-source responses, display-limited suppliers, regulated data |
   | **Central decision service** | One place to change and observe | On the critical path of every product; latency and outage coupling | Low volume, or decisions that need fresh external context |
   | **Data-level enforcement only** (separate stores per licence class) | Strong isolation | Duplicated data; every cross-source product becomes a join problem | One very restrictive source, such as keeper data, kept physically apart |
   | **Register data terms** | NL: open data under CC0, with the rule not to attribute it to the RDW; paid per-plate access to non-sensitive data; personal data never for commercial use.
   | **Statutory recipient audit** | None specific to vehicles; GDPR accountability and register contracts | None specific; GDPR | Contractual (KADOE: 2-year evidence) | DPPA: 5-year recipient and purpose records |
   | **Connected-vehicle data** | EU Data Act: purpose-bound, erase when no longer needed, no onward transfer without a user contract | Same | Not bound by the EU Data Act | Contractual; FTC's 2026 GM order requires express consent before sharing connected-vehicle data ([FTC, 2026](https://www.ftc.gov/node/327916)) |
   | **Database protection** | EU sui generis database right (Directive 96/9/EC) | Same | Retained UK database right *(not checked against the current UK text)* | No database right; facts are not copyrightable (*Feist*, [1991](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/499/340)); protection rests on contract and terms of use |
   | **Personal-data status of plates** | Treated as personal by default; the RDW keeps holder data apart ([[privacy-and-retention]]) | Same, under GDPR | Information Commissioner's Office (ICO): a plate is personal data "in most circumstances" | DPPA "personal information" covers names and addresses, not vehicle attributes as such |
   **Where decisions are evaluated**: embedded engines with pushed bundles, and the fail-closed policy per licence class, written down before the first outage forces a choice.
   **What the audit records and for how long**: recipients and purposes for the longest contractual or statutory period, and operational logs kept short.

===== vehicle-data-apis 49
   **Most of them are other software companies**: claims platforms, lease-management systems, dealer software, portals.
   | **Lookup** | A plate (with country) or VIN in; selected facts out, in milliseconds | Claims, quoting, appraisal and dealer software | Per call or per block, outcome-dependent | This chapter |
   | **Report** | A request in; an immutable, stored document out (history, valuation certificate, battery report), retrievable later by id | Dealers, lessors, consumers through apps | Per report | [[vehicle-history]], [[consumer-vehicle-checks]] |
   | **Batch** | A list of vehicles in; results out, asynchronously | Fleet audits, insurer portfolio re-checks | Per record, with surcharges for paid upstream misses | [[scale-and-cost]], [[data-products-and-insights]] |
   | **Change feed** | A subscription in; events out when a vehicle's facts change | Lessors, insurers, dealer groups | Subscription | [[events-webhooks-and-sync]] |
   A **category block** is the unit of a vehicle data response that is sold, licensed, sourced and failed independently: identity, registration, specification, history, valuation, market, tax, battery health.
   The **response composer** is the stateless service that turns one client request into one answer.
   The **API contract registry** is the system of record for what the API promises: block definitions, canonical schemas (JSON Schema 2020-12 inside OpenAPI 3.1), dated versions with the change class of every difference, the XML serializations for the legacy base, each client credential's version pin, deprecation dates, and per-client usage of every block and deprecated field.
   - **Outcome**: `hit`, `partial`, `no_hit` or `failed`.
   - **Freshness**: `current`, `stale` or `unavailable`, the labels [[reliability-and-dependencies]] uses for degraded answers.
   | **API gateway** | Authenticate the client; attach client id, contract version pin and request id; enforce rate limits, quotas and spend caps; reject malformed requests | Admission decisions, protective counters | Classify outcomes or bill; read vehicle data |
   | **Response composer** | Resolve identity, check licences per block, fan out to contexts with per-block deadlines, assemble the canonical answer, emit usage events, store the idempotency record | Assembly rules and idempotency records | Store facts, apply business rules of a context, guess a missing value |
   | **Owning contexts** | Serve their block with outcome, sources, observation times and fact version | Their facts and hit classes | Shape responses for a particular client |
   | **Charter** | Decide which blocks and fields the client may receive for its stated purpose; record the decision | Licence and purpose register, access audit | Be bypassed by a cache or a renderer |
   | **Renderers** | Turn the canonical answer into JSON (current and older dated versions) or Connect XML (frozen XSD profiles) | Version-change transforms | Add or drop facts the canonical answer did not contain |
   | **Contract registry** | Hold definitions, versions, change classes, pins, deprecations and usage | The published contract | Be edited without a review and a changelog entry |
   **The gateway does not decide what is billable**: it counts requests for capacity and quotas, while Meterline counts outcomes decided by the contexts.
   **The renderer does not decide what is true**: in 2026 Connect XML computed its own "edition" field; in v2 XML and JSON clients asking the same question at the same moment get the same facts, and a golden test proves it on every release.
   **Protective rate limits**: requests per second per client and globally, to keep the composer and the upstream budget healthy.
   **Commercial quotas**: lookups per month in the client's contract, as entitlements ([[identity-access-and-entitlements]]).
   **Spend caps**: upstream spend per client, fed by the upstream budget broker of [[scale-and-cost]].
   | **Major version in the path** | `/v1`, `/v2`; no breaking change within a major; beta channels | Google AIP-185 and AIP-180 ([Google, 2024](https://google.aip.dev/185)) | Simple; obvious to clients | Each major is a migration for every client |
   | **Date-pinned versions per account** | Account pinned to the version current at its first call; header override; server-side "version change modules" transform responses backward | Stripe, 2017 ([Stripe, 2017](https://stripe.com/blog/api-versioning)) | Clients upgrade when they choose; the provider ships continuously | A permanently growing chain of transforms |
   | **Named releases on a cadence** | Breaking changes only twice a year in a named release; monthly additive releases | Stripe since October 2024 ([Stripe, 2024](https://stripe.com/blog/introducing-stripes-new-api-release-process)) | Predictable for clients' planning | Breaking work waits for the window |
   | **Required date-valued parameter** | `api-version=YYYY-MM-DD`, previews retired after at least 90 days' notice | Microsoft Azure guidelines ([Microsoft, n.d.](https://github.com/microsoft/api-guidelines/blob/vNext/azure/Guidelines.md)) | Explicit on every call | Clients must choose; no default |
   - **Semantic changes.** Moving the hit definition for history (the No-Hit Credit fix), changing how odometer judgements are worded ([[vehicle-history]]), or switching a valuation from asking-price to transaction-price basis changes no field and breaks every client's interpretation.
   - **Code-list changes.** Fuel types, body types and equipment codes are enumerations that grow.
   - **Privacy removals.** Auto Trader's scheduled removal of `vehicle.previousOwners` from Stock and Search in favor of `history.previousOwners` ([[case-auto-trader-uk]]) is the pattern: personal-data and licence decisions move fields between blocks.
   **Registers publish narrow, quota-bound APIs**: the UK's Driver and Vehicle Standards Agency (DVSA) MOT (annual roadworthiness test) history API allows 500,000 requests a day, a burst of 10 and an average of 15 a second, and restricts a key "for 24 hours" after the quota is exceeded ([DVSA, n.d., accessed 2026-10](https://documentation.history.mot.api.gov.uk/mot-history-api/rate-limits/)); the DVLA issues one key per
   **A marketplace publishes a partner platform**: Autotrader Connect, with capabilities, sandbox, signed webhooks, change policy and changelog, used by more than 220 technology partners according to its FY2026 results ([[case-auto-trader-uk]]).
   **A national data provider publishes schemas**: VWE's per-licensor XSDs ([[case-vwe]]).
   | **Lookup total** | | | | **€5.22** |
   | **Duplicate requests** | Client retries after a gateway timeout | Same `Idempotency-Key` (JSON) or client reference element (XML) within 72 h | Return the stored answer; no new usage event; a key reused with a different body gets 422 |
   | **Duplicate vehicles** | Plate on two vehicles on the lookup date (Cloned Plate) | Anchor returns `ambiguous` | Identity block only; ask for VIN; nothing paid served or metered |
   | **Conflicting facts across blocks** | Register says plug-in hybrid, build data says BEV | The specification context flags a conflict in its resolution state | Show the resolved value with the conflict flag and both sources; the composer never picks between blocks |
   | **Incomplete records** | History source times out; specification edition unknown | Block outcome `partial`, coverage list, resolution state | Answer with the gap; client's `partial_policy: decline` turns the block into `failed` and free |
   | **Upstream outage** | Register channel down for hours | Turnstile's session monitor; freshness class exceeded | Serve mirror facts labeled `stale` with age; theft status never from cache |
   | **Retry storms** | Many clients retry a slow composer | 503 rate and retry ratio at the gateway | `Retry-After`; shed batch lane first; documented jittered back-off in the client guide |
   | **Renderer drift** | XML and JSON disagree after a change | Golden comparison on every release | Release blocked; fix in the renderer, not in a context |
   | **Transform bug for old pins** | A version-change transform drops a field for clients pinned to 2027-10-01 | Contract tests per supported pin, replaying recorded requests | Roll back the transform; affected clients notified with request ids |
   | **Corrected facts already served** | Odometer Correction reaches API answers | `fact_version` per block, stored with the usage event | List affected client answers by fact version; notify through `vehicle.fact.corrected` from Q4 2028 ([[events-webhooks-and-sync]]) |
   **Conflicts stay inside the block that owns them**: a composer that reconciled blocks would become a resolver without tests, owner or provenance.
   | **Per-source schemas** (one schema per licensor, as VWE documents) | Licence scoping is trivial; adding a source touches nothing else | Every client harmonizes; source changes are client changes; no cross-source resolution | Clients already map those sources and value raw provenance over convenience |
   | **Endpoint per internal service** (Axleby's JSON API v1) | Cheap to build; teams ship independently | Clients compose; internal boundaries become public contract; each team invents metadata and errors | Internal APIs only |
   | **Harmonized vehicle resource with category blocks** (v2) | One model; licence and price per block; partial failure is local; usage evidence from block selection | The provider owns harmonization and per-block metadata; composer is a critical service | Several hundred external clients, mixed licences, outcome-based pricing |
   | **Query language with field selection** (for example GraphQL) | Clients ask for exactly what they use | Licence, price and freshness are per block, not per field; cost per query is hard to predict; caching and metering get harder | Internal product front ends over owned data; Axleby uses it for none of its external APIs |
   | **Keep the legacy stack until sunset** | No risk to legacy clients | Two classifications, two data paths, two meters for years | Sunset is months away, not years |
   | **Facade over the new composer** (Axleby) | One truth, one meter; legacy wire format unchanged | Serialization mapping and golden testing cost; old quirks become documented behavior changes | Long sunset, many clients who cannot move soon |
   | **Forced migration** | Shortest dual running | Breaks clients on the provider's schedule; churn and contract disputes | Few clients, or a contract that permits it |
