   **The reconciler was doing the event path's job**: this was the Zombie Golf's quieter cousin, not a queue blocked behind failures but a message that never existed.
   A **domain event (vehicle)** is an immutable statement, published by the context that owns a fact, that something happened to one business entity: a vehicle, a stock item, a channel listing, a lead, a deal or a register transaction.
   | **Domain event** | A committed business fact about one entity | The owning context | *stock item sold*, version 41 |
   | **Command** | A request to another system to do something; it can fail or be rejected | The sender, with a receipt from the receiver | Hitch's "set DriveDesk stock status to reserved" ([[dms-integration]]) |
   | **Change-data event** | A row-level insert, update or delete captured from a database log | The database schema, which is to say nobody | `UPDATE stock SET status=5` |
   | **Flow event** | A record that a unit of work passed a stage, for monitoring | [[observability-and-data-operations]] | "withdrawal acknowledged by channel 14" |
   | **Usage event** | A billable business moment, with outcome | [[billing-and-metering]] | "history report delivered, `hit`" |
   An **outbox** is a table in the producing service's own database.
   A **webhook subscription** is a registered, authorized request by an external party to receive selected event types at an HTTPS endpoint, with a secret, a filter, a delivery state and a delivery log.
   A **reconciliation job** is a scheduled comparison of what one system believes with what another system actually holds, for a defined set of entities, that produces discrepancies as tickets or corrective events and never overwrites silently.
   **The nightly restock signals** (about **350,000** recomputed stock items) are published as a dataset, because 350,000 "signal changed" messages at 02:00 would bury the transitions that matter.
   | **Outbox** (per service) | Make "the change happened" and "the event exists" one commit | Unpublished events, their order per entity |
   | **Relay** | Read outboxes by log tailing, publish to topics, preserve per-key order | Relay offsets |
   | **Internal topics and schema registry** | Durable, keyed, versioned event streams; compatibility checks | Topic configuration, schema versions |
   | **Consumer inbox** (per consumer) | Deduplicate by event id; apply effects with version guards | Processed-event log |
   | **Public event gateway** | Map internal events to the public catalog; filter by tenant, entitlement ([[identity-access-and-entitlements]]) and licence ([[data-licensing-and-governance]]) | The public catalog and its versions |
   | **Event feed API** | Cursor-based pull of a subscriber's events, 30 days back | Per-subscriber cursors |
   | **Webhook dispatcher** | Sign, send, retry, record, disable failing endpoints | Webhook subscriptions, delivery log |
   | **Reconciliation jobs** | Compare desired and actual state per external system; emit corrections or tickets | Runs, discrepancies, their resolution |
   - **Relay's RabbitMQ queue** stays until Relay is rebuilt as desired-state sync.
   - **Connect XML** (the 2006 SOAP platform on SQL Server) uses SQL Server **change tracking**, which records only the primary keys of changed rows: "Only the fact that a row has changed is required, not how many times the row has changed or the values of any intermediate changes" ([Microsoft Learn, 2025](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/sql/relational-databases/track-changes/about-change-tracking-sql-server)).
   - **At least once, unordered, with versions.** Every webhook carries `webhook-id` (the CloudEvents `id`, stable across retries), the entity key and `entityversion`.
   - **Acknowledge fast.** A 2xx within 10 seconds counts as delivered; the receiver should queue and process later.
   - **Retries for three days**: at 5 seconds, 5 minutes, 30 minutes, 2 hours, 5 hours, 10 hours, then every 12 hours until 72 hours.
   - **Replay and the feed.** Every event the subscriber was entitled to stays readable for **30 days** through the event feed API, by cursor.
   - **Two secrets during rotation**, and **egress through a proxy "that filters internal IP addresses"**, as Standard Webhooks recommends, because dealer-supplied URLs are attacker-controllable ([[vehicle-platform-security]]).
   **Nothing a dealer does waits on the backbone**: the reservation is synchronous inside the dossier, and the outbox holds events while the broker is down.
   **Nobody guarantees order**: Stripe, Marktplaats and Auto Trader each say so in writing.
   **Push comes with pull**: mobile.de's Ad Stream is a WebSocket whose `catchup=true` option replays events from the last disconnect, kept for **24 hours** ([mobile.de Ad Stream, n.d., accessed 2026-10](https://services.mobile.de/manual/ad-stream.html)), and Marktplaats pairs its lead webhook with a pull API for conversations.
   **DMS vendors have moved toward events**: CDK Global announced asynchronous Fortellis APIs in January 2023 "designed to meet needs for fast data updates, record synchronization and event-triggered workflows" ([BusinessWire, 2023](https://www.businesswire.com/news/home/20230123005590/en/CDK-Global-Introduces-New-Modern-Application-Programming-Interface-Tools-for-Developers-at-NADA-2023); widely reported, not fetched f
   | **Total** | | **roughly 0.6 to 0.8 million** |
   | **Duplicate events** | Relay republishes after a restart; webhook retried after a slow 2xx; replay | Event id already in the consumer inbox | Skip; version guards make re-application harmless |
   | **Out-of-order events** | Retries, parallel consumers, replays, webhook reordering | `entityversion` lower than the stored one | Record and ignore; never move state backwards |
   | **Lost events** | Dual write; consumer commits offset before effect | Reconciler finds desired and actual state apart; outbox-to-topic counts differ | Outbox removes the cause; reconciler emits a corrective event |
   | **Incomplete records** | Stock item without AVK; schema-invalid payload | Producer-side schema check | Quarantine with reason; never publish a keyless vehicle event |
   | **Conflicting updates** | DMS and dealer change the same field; portal edits a listing by hand | Field-ownership check on apply; reconciler discrepancy | Owner's value wins; the other is recorded as an observation and ticketed ([[dms-integration]]) |
   | **Poison event** | A payload that crashes one consumer every time | Repeated failure on one offset | Park it in a dead-letter topic after N attempts; alert the owning team; keep the partition moving only if the consumer's semantics allow skipping one entity |
   | **Relay stalled** | Debezium connector failure; binlog retention exceeded | Age of the oldest unpublished outbox row | Alert at 5 minutes *(assumption)*; binlog retention set above the longest tolerated stall; outbox rows deleted only after publication |
   | **Consumer backlog** | Slow consumer; replay flood | Consumer lag per group in seconds, not messages | Separate replay lane with emission caps; scale consumers per partition |
   | **Subscriber outage** | Partner endpoint down or failing | Delivery failure rate per subscription | Retry for 72 hours, disable, notify, replay from cursor |
   | **Cross-tenant delivery** | Filter bug; rooftop moved between groups | Gateway tests on tenant filters; audit sampling of deliveries | Subscriptions re-authorized on every hierarchy change; the gateway refuses an event whose `tenantid` the subscription does not cover |
   | **External outage** | Portal, DMS or register unavailable | Adapter health; circuit breaker | Desired state persists; adapters converge after recovery; reconcile before resuming write-back ([[reliability-and-dependencies]]) |
   **Poison events conflict with ordering**: skipping one breaks that entity's order; blocking stops the partition.
   | **Dual write** | Commit, then publish | Trivial | Loses events on crashes; publishes events for rolled-back changes if done in the other order | Never for business facts |
   | **Outbox with log-tailing relay** | Event row in the same transaction; Debezium reads the log | Atomic with the change; ordered per key; no polling load | Needs binlog or CDC access and a connector to operate | The default for services with a relational store |
   | **Outbox with polling relay** | Relay selects unpublished rows | No log access needed | Polling load and latency; ordering needs care | Databases where log tailing is not allowed or available |
   | **Table-level CDC as the event stream** | Publish row changes directly | No producer code change | Couples consumers to the schema; no business meaning; leaks columns | Migrations and analytics only |
   | **Event sourcing** | The event log is the store | Full history; replay by design | Large change to how services are built; queries need projections | New contexts with strong audit needs, such as Meterline's usage log |
   | **Webhooks only** | Low latency; simple for small integrators | Missed events during outages; short windows | Never alone |
   | **Event feed only** (cursor pull) | Complete; replayable; receiver controls pace | Polling latency and load | Batch-minded clients; regulated clients that disallow inbound calls |
   | **Webhooks plus feed** | Latency of push, completeness of pull | Two interfaces to document and operate | The default |
   | **Register change signals** | Dutch register open data is a plate-keyed snapshot; changes found by diffing ([[registers-and-official-data]]).
   | **Portal back-channels** | Marktplaats/2dehands: signed push webhooks (RSA HTTP signatures), 2-second deadline, no ordering; pull API for conversations | mobile.de: Ad Stream WebSocket with 24-hour catch-up; Lead API pull by cursor with status write-back | Autotrader Connect: HMAC-signed stock and deal notifications, retries over about 31 minutes, `changedFields` | Mostly files through inventory-management provider
   | **Lead transport** | JSON webhooks | Cursor-based pull | Deal-centric APIs and notifications | ADF/XML email, with the 2025 Sales Lead API of STAR (Standards for Technology in Automotive Retail) as a possible successor |
   | **DMS events** | Local vendors, mostly files (DriveDesk polled every 15 minutes in Axleby's case) | Brand-shaped DMS landscape; files and vendor APIs | Keyloop, Pinewood and a tail; vendor APIs | Fortellis asynchronous APIs (2023, widely reported), Tekion webhooks, Reynolds and Reynolds' RCI SOAP exchange |
   **Watch five numbers**: the oldest unpublished outbox row per service, consumer lag in seconds, webhook success and disabled subscriptions, reconciler discrepancy rates, and the private synchronization paths still running (six mechanisms for one event at the start).
===== data-products-and-insights 44
   | **Enrichment job** | A file of identifiers (plates, vehicle identification numbers or VINs), often with its own reference per row | The same rows with requested attributes and a status per row | Per record, tiered | Insurers, lessors, fleet managers, data companies |
   | **Insight cube** | A question: segment, region, period | Aggregated counts, shares, stand-times and prices, as dashboard, report or file | Annual subscription per scope | Importers, dealer groups, trade bodies, lessors |
   | **Propensity score (vehicle)** | Nothing, or its own customer plates | A probability band per vehicle, or an aggregate forecast | Subscription per territory or per scored vehicle | Dealers, importers |
   | **Enrichment intake** (upload, SFTP, batch API) | Accept files, validate format, normalize identifiers, dedupe, attach the client's purpose | Job record, input file | Reject the whole file only for format errors; row problems become row statuses |
   | **Enrichment job runner** | Chunk, resolve, assemble attributes, checkpoint, retry | Chunk state, result rows | Reads the mirror and local stores; live upstream calls only for attributes the client bought as live |
   | **Insight cube builder** | Build fact tables from events, conform dimensions, compute cubes per release | Cube releases | Every release is immutable and versioned; inputs are recorded by snapshot id |
   | **Disclosure control** | Apply threshold, dominance and complementary suppression; fix geographies | Suppression policy versions | One serving layer for dashboard, report and file; no direct table access |
   | **Feature snapshots and scoring pipeline** | Point-in-time features, monthly training and scoring | Score store with model version and expiry | Features as known on the snapshot date; no future data |
   | **Match-in service** | Return scores only for identifiers the client submits under a customer-relationship purpose | Match requests (transient) | Objection list applied before return; nothing returned for unknown plates |
   | **Client file exchange** | Deliver files and manifests; expire links; delete | Delivered files | Deletion 30 days after delivery for inputs and outputs; a deletion record kept |
   - **Daily figures are provisional**, labeled as such, and rebuilt each night.
   - **Monthly releases are frozen.** A correction appears in the next monthly release as a restatement, with a delta table showing the old figure, the new figure and the reason.
   - **Delivered files are listed.** The file exchange keeps, for each delivery, the release id and the recipient, so a material restatement can be announced to exactly the clients who received the affected figures.
   - **The client declares the relationship.** Each submitted plate carries a relationship type (sold by us, serviced by us in the last 24 months, leased by us) taken from the client's DMS, CRM or Lotwise sold-vehicle records.
   - **Bands, not probabilities.** The service returns one of four bands (low, normal, elevated, high) with the calibration published in the contract.
   - **Scores die.** Each score expires after 90 days, matching the three-month retention for score data.
   - **Objections are by plate and by episode.** Odolo, Axleby's consumer brand, hosts an objection form.
   - **Nothing for unknown plates.** A plate that is not in the score store or has an objection returns "no score." The response does not reveal which of the two applies.
   - **Importers get aggregates.** An importer receives expected ownership changes per dealer territory, segment and month, under the vehicle threshold of the disclosure policy.
   | **Duplicate submission** of the same enrichment file | Job idempotency key from file hash, job spec and client | Return the existing job; no second bill |
   | **Duplicate rows** within a file | Normalization and dedupe at intake | One resolution, same answer on every duplicate row, billed once |
   | **Incomplete rows** (malformed plate, foreign plate, VIN with a bad check digit) | Validation per row | Row status `invalid_identifier` or routed to the market adapter; never dropped |
   | **Worker crash mid-job** | Chunk heartbeat | Resume from the last committed chunk; usage events committed with chunks |
   | **Upstream outage** during live columns | Budget broker and circuit breaker | Job completes as `Partial`; deferred columns filled later; manifest says which |
   | **Partial register snapshot** (row count drop, schema change) | Load guards on the mirror ([[registers-and-official-data]]) | No cube release and no scoring run from it; previous release stays live, marked stale |
   | **Conflicting figures** between a client's own data and the cube | Client query cites release id | Release lineage shows snapshot ids, suppression version and source coverage; differences explained, or restated |
   | **Late correction** of register or supplier data | Correction events in Tracewell and the mirror | Restatement in the next monthly release; affected file recipients notified from the delivery list |
   | **Differencing attack** across dashboard queries | Query-pattern monitor on the serving layer | Fixed geographies; complementary suppression; account review for repeated near-duplicate queries |
   | **Score delivered after ownership change** | Registration-episode check at match time | Score void; "no score" returned |
   | **Objection list unavailable** | Health check on match-in | Fail closed: no scores returned |
   | **Model drift** | Monthly band calibration check | `watch` status, then bands withheld until retrained |
   | **Territory plate lists** | Strangers' plates with bands | Weak: no relationship, no route to a lawful contact, no chance to object | Highest, until it is challenged | Never for privately held cars in the EU, in my view |
   | **Match-in on own customers** | Bands for plates the client already holds | Defensible: relationship, objection handling, ePrivacy existing-customer rule | Lower; depends on the client's customer base | Dealers and importers with real customer files |
   | **Aggregate forecasts** | Expected changes per area and segment | Strong: no plate-level output | Planning value, not leads | Importers, groups, field sales |
   | **Clean-room join** | Bands or overlaps computed without either party seeing the other's records | Defensible if outputs are bands for own customers or aggregates above thresholds | Higher for large clients; costly to operate | Importers and lessors with their own owner data |
   | **In the serving layer over the cube** | One policy for all channels; versioned | Needs a curated query surface; no free SQL | Default for commercial insight |

   | **Enrichment sources** | NL mirror of open data; paid per-plate business access "not in bulk"; BE via a partner channel | Commercial build and valuation data; register keeper data only for legal claims (§ 39 of the Road Traffic Act, StVG) | Driver and Vehicle Standards Agency (DVSA) MOT (annual roadworthiness test) history API (500,000 requests a day); DVLA bulk anonymized data under "reasonable cause" ([DVLA INF26
   | **Labels for ownership-change scores** | NL: snapshot diffs; BE: no comparable change stream verified | None public per vehicle; dealer's own DMS and service history only | Keeper changes not public; MOT due dates are a public signal, but reuse terms for resale were not verified | Registration and DMS/CRM data under contract |
   | **Owner contact** | Never from the register for commerce; dealer's own customers under GDPR and ePrivacy | Same EU rules; register only for legal claims | DVLA anonymized data (make, model, partial postcode) "mainly for marketing"; UK GDPR and the Privacy and Electronic Communications Regulations apply | Driver's Privacy Protection Act (DPPA): bulk distribution "for surveys, marketing or solicitations" only if the 
   | **Market-area geography** | Fixed market areas from four-digit postcodes | Postcode regions (*Postleitzahlgebiete*); fixed areas | Postcode districts; corrections needed for fleets registered at head offices | ZIP-based dealer areas; DPPA excludes the 5-digit ZIP from "personal information" (§ 2725) |
   **What is common**: the enrichment job, the cube with its serving layer, the suppression policy, the restatement rules, match-in and the file-exchange retention are one design across markets.
   **What is a market adapter**: the event sources that feed the cube, the label source for scores, and the geography.
   **What is a rule set**: thresholds per measure, objection and contact rules, and which products exist at all.
===== consumer-vehicle-checks 48
   A **plate check** is a free, anonymous lookup by registration plate (or VIN, the 17-character vehicle identification number) that identifies the vehicle and shows a few basic facts.
   A **consumer report** is a paid, rendered, dated snapshot of one vehicle's identity, register status, history judgements and value range, issued to one consumer under one purpose.
   - **It is bought once, just before a decision**, by someone who cannot call an account manager.
   - **It is the platform's most visible output.** A dealer reads a business report next to their own knowledge.
   - **Its buyer has no contract with the platform beyond the purchase**, so everything the business contract would say (permitted use, liability, refunds) is set by consumer law and the terms shown at checkout.
   | **Personal data** | Does the field identify or describe a person? | Keeper count yes; keeper names, addresses, finance-holder identity never |
   | **Licence** | Does the source licence allow display to consumers, and with what attribution? | Partner damage data licensed for business use only stays out; Dutch open-data fields may be shown, but not described as coming from the RDW |
   | **Purpose** | Was the data collected for a purpose compatible with a consumer check? | A dealer's private appraisal notes stay out; the dealer's odometer reading, as a sourced event, may appear |
   | **Accuracy and coverage** | Can the platform stand behind the statement, and say what it did not check? | "No damage records found in the sources checked for 2024–2027" instead of "no damage" |
   | **Odolo apps and web** | Plate entry, camera scan, vehicle confirmation, checkout, report viewing | Nothing durable; device-local scan images | Vehicle facts |
   | **Odolo BFF** | Session, consumer account, app attestation, challenge, routing | Consumer accounts (separate from the three business logins) | Abuse policy thresholds (security) |
   | **Plate check service** | Resolve plate to vehicle through Anchor; return the public field set | The public field list per market | Register access ([[registers-and-official-data]]) |
   | **Orders and entitlements** | Quote, payment, entitlement, refund, credit, supersession notices | Orders, payment references, entitlements, refunds | Prices (price book in Meterline) |
   | **History assembler** (Tracewell) | Build the report under product `consumer-report`, market and purpose | Events, judgements, snapshots ([[vehicle-history]]) | Payment state |
   | **Charter** | Decide which sources and fields the consumer purpose may show, with attribution rules | Licence and purpose register ([[data-licensing-and-governance]]) | Rendering |
   | **Meterline** | Record each issued report and its upstream cost | Usage events ([[billing-and-metering]]) | Consumer refunds (Odolo's ledger) |
   - **No per-plate pages for search engines.** A vehicle-check site that publishes one indexable page per plate has enumerated itself and invited every crawler to copy the result.
   - **No teaser computed from paid or derived data.** "14 history records found, including 2 damage records" was Axleby's own data, and walking it was the point of the August attack.
   - **Marketing copy is part of the specification.** A landing page that promises "the complete history" raises what a consumer may reasonably expect.
   - **The snapshot is the evidence.** To carry the burden of proof for a year, Axleby must show what the report said, from which events, under which rule versions and coverage.
   - **"Brought into conformity" means supersession.** When an event is corrected, the remedy is a new snapshot linked to the old one, delivered to the holder, plus a refund or price reduction where the statement changed.
   - **Pressure on content.** A dealer harmed by a report wants it softened.
   - **Pressure on placement.** Dealers will pay to appear next to a consumer who is about to buy.
   - **Pressure on data.** A consumer checking a plate on a forecourt is a hot prospect, and the dealer would like to know.
   - **It looked like the attack.** Day one of the August attack was also a sudden rise of anonymous checks from residential addresses, and the on-call engineer called it organic.
   - **Paid reports have upstream cost.** Each report makes a live theft check and may buy build data.
   - **The payment rails have their own limits.** The PSP's checkout and the store's purchase sheet are outside Axleby's control.
   | **Duplicate payment events** (PSP webhook retries, store notification replays) | Idempotency key per order; receipt id unique per entitlement | Second event acknowledged and ignored; reconciliation job compares PSP and store reports with entitlements daily |
   | **Double purchase by the consumer** | Entitlement lookup on (account or device, vehicle, 24 hours) before checkout | Offer the existing report; automatic refund if bought anyway |
   | **Wrong vehicle** (typo, scan misread, cloned plate) | Confirmation step; VIN on report; complaints tagged "wrong vehicle" | Refund by rule; a cloned-plate complaint becomes an identity case for Anchor ([[vehicle-identity]]) |
   | **Belgian plate on its owner's next car** | Plate assignment date earlier than the car's Belgian registration | Belgian reports are bought by VIN; a plate entry asks for the VIN from the Car-Pass or registration certificate |
   | **Event corrected after issue** | Correction log lists snapshots that used the event | New snapshot; notice to every holder; refund where a statement changed; the old version stays readable with a banner |
   | **Source down during assembly** | Assembler hit class (partial, failed) | Web: issue with stated gaps or void; in-app: credit plus refund request; theft unchecked is said in words |
   | **Assembler or Tracewell outage** | Journey service-level objective (SLO) on report issue | Free check keeps working from the mirror; checkout shows "reports temporarily unavailable"; queued paid orders complete or void |
   | **PSP or store outage** | Checkout error rates per rail | The other rail continues (web stays open when a store is down); no report is issued unpaid |
   | **Anonymous volume spike** | Volume plus walk-detector signals | Waiting room for free checks; challenge; never shed paid assembly ([[vehicle-platform-security]]) |
   | **Licence change by a source** | Charter version change on a consumer-visible source | Re-render rules for new reports; existing snapshots keep what they showed, with the licence version they were issued under |
   | **Free check content** | Public fields only plus "report available" | Free check is anonymous and public (Odolo after August) | Weaker sales argument; lower conversion *(assumption: about 0.3 points)* |
   | **Hit disclosure** | Authorize, assemble, capture | Web rail with authorize-then-capture | Authorization holds visible to consumers for a few seconds |
   | **Platform reuse** | Odolo as a thin edge on shared services | Reports must agree with business reports and corrections must reach them | Odolo's roadmap depends on platform teams |
   | **Plate scanning** | On-device recognition | Default | Model quality varies by device |
   | **Dealer channel** | None | Brand trust is the whole product | Forgoes referral revenue and dealer cross-sell |
   | **Free public data to build a free check on** | NL: register open data, free and CC0, including the odometer judgement per plate; reuse may not be attributed to the RDW.
   | **Statutory buyer document** | NL: odometer judgement (the former *Nationale AutoPas*, NAP) available per plate.
   | **Plate as a key** | NL: plate stays with the car.
   | **Paid consumer products (documented)** | VWE sells Finnik reports, stickers and a vignette; Carfax Europe sells reports by Dutch plate or VIN ([carfax.eu, n.d.](https://www.carfax.eu/nl)) | Commercial VIN-based reports, such as autoDNA at €24.99 (as of 2026); Carfax Europe expected to enter Germany (Mobility Global, 2026) | HPI Check £9.99 basic, £19.99 full (as of 2026), with a guarantee up to £30,000 ([HPI, n.d.
   | **Consumer law for the report** | Digital Content Directive (conformity, one-year burden of proof); withdrawal waiver under Consumer Rights Directive Art. 16(m) | Same EU rules | Consumer Rights Act 2015 digital-content remedies; withdrawal waiver under reg. 37 of the 2013 Regulations | State consumer-protection law; the Driver's Privacy Protection Act (DPPA) restricts personal information from motor-vehicle record
   | **Personal data in reports** | GDPR; RDW gives no personal data to market parties for commercial purposes | GDPR | UK GDPR; the ICO treats a plate as personal data in most circumstances | DPPA: personal information from state motor-vehicle records only for permitted uses |
===== part-7 0
===== reliability-and-dependencies 55
   **Its most valuable facts come from authorities and suppliers it cannot replace**: the register, build-data providers, workshop aggregators, portals and DMS products.
   A **journey SLO** is an objective set on an end-to-end dealer or client journey rather than on a service.
   A **dependency budget** is the share of a journey's error budget allotted to one external dependency, together with the rule that limits how many synchronous dependencies a critical journey may carry.
   A **degraded mode** is a named, tested way for a journey to keep working, or to fail honestly, while a dependency is impaired.
   **The dependency had a slow state that nothing measured**: the monitor saw errors, not latency, so the queue kept submitting into a brownout for 45 minutes.
   A **dependency** is any system outside the team's control that a journey relies on: a register, a data supplier, a portal, a DMS, a payment provider, another cloud, or another team's product.
   | **Tier 1: blocks a legal or commercial act** | The dealer cannot complete the act without the dependency; no copy can substitute | Register transaction channel for registration at delivery |
   | **Tier 2: degrades a decision** | The journey can continue with older, partial or labeled data | Build-data provider for an appraisal; register information channel for a valuation |
   | **Tier 3: delays a background flow** | Work queues and catches up; the dealer notices only if the delay is long | Portal adapters for price updates; DMS file imports; supplier batches into Tracewell |
   A **service level indicator (SLI)** is "a carefully defined quantitative measure of some aspect of the level of service", and an SLO is a target for it ([Google, *Site Reliability Engineering*, 2016](https://sre.google/sre-book/service-level-objectives/)).
   A **degraded mode** is named, specified per journey and dependency, visible to the user and exercised on a schedule.
   | **Labeled copy** | The last known fact, with source and observation time | Register facts, specification, history, market data | Facts whose staleness is dangerous (theft status at purchase) |
   | **Partial answer** | Available sections, with the missing ones marked unavailable and why | Reports and lookups composed from several sources | Hiding a missing section as "nothing found" |
   | **Honest queue** | The act is accepted, ordered and shown as pending at the authority | Register transactions, DMS write-backs, portal operations | Telling the user it is done |
   | **Local system of record** | The platform takes ownership of a field until the owner returns, then reconciles | DMS-owned stock status during a DMS outage | Fields the platform has no right to own (the dealer's ledger) |
   | **Fail fast with a reason** | A clear refusal, so the user can choose another path | Acts that need a live answer and cannot wait | — |
   **Deadlines propagate**: a lookup with a 2-second budget that has spent 1.4 seconds must not give the build-data call a fresh 3-second timeout ([Google, 2016](https://sre.google/sre-book/addressing-cascading-failures/)).
   - **Success**: the counterpart confirmed it, and the evidence is stored.
   - **Definitive failure**: the counterpart refused it with a reason.
   - **Ambiguous**: the call timed out, the connection broke after sending, or the response could not be parsed.
   - **Is the channel up?** It checks connection success, latency and the error mix on real traffic and on scheduled probes ([[observability-and-data-operations]]).
   - **Will it stay up?** It tracks credential expiry and quota consumption.
   - **Is it planned to be down?** RDW publishes its major maintenance on Sundays from 07:00 to 11:00, listing eleven such Sundays for 2026, and announces other planned work on the same page ([RDW, onderhoud en storingen, n.d., accessed 2026-10](https://www.rdw.nl/paginas/onderhoud-en-storingen)).
   - **Per register channel**: separate connection and worker pools for each Dutch channel, the Belgian partner, DVLA and DVSA.
   - **Per portal channel**: Relay gives each of the 61 channels its own queue and concurrency limit, and withdrawals go in a lane ahead of updates.
   - **Per DMS family and per rooftop**: Hitch isolates DriveDesk from the German DMS adapters and keeps a breaker per rooftop, so one rooftop with revoked credentials does not stop the others.
   - **Per traffic class at shared upstreams**: the broker separates interactive lookups from client batches ([[scale-and-cost]] describes the lanes).
   - **AutoNation** said all its locations stayed open, operating "through manual and alternative means and processes, albeit with lower productivity" ([AutoNation Form 8-K, June 2024](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/350698/000035069824000078/an-20240619.htm)).
   - **Sonic Automotive** kept dealerships open "utilizing workaround solutions" and resumed DMS transactions on 30 June only after "internal risk assessments and data validation procedures" ([Sonic Form 10-Q, August 2024](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1043509/000104350924000065/sah-20240630.htm)).
   - **Lithia Motors** severed "business service connections" between its systems and CDK's as part of its own incident response ([Lithia Form 8-K, June 2024](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1023128/000102312824000079/lad-20240619.htm)).
   | **Total from dependencies** | | | **0.83%, nearly three times the 0.3% line** |
   - **Measure each dependency yourself, from your side.** Flow records and probes ([[observability-and-data-operations]]) give the availability that goes into the budget, not the contract's.
   - **Weight by when it matters.** A supplier at 99.9% a month that loses its 0.1% at month-end has failed the business more than one that loses 0.3% on Sunday nights.
   - **Bring the measurement to the supplier review.** Hendrik's quarterly supplier reviews use Axleby's numbers, and renewals ask for incident notification times and data-validation duties after restores.
   | **Ambiguous write outcome** | Timeout after sending a register transaction, DMS command or portal delete | Outcome class "ambiguous" in the queue | Hold that vehicle's queue; read the counterpart's state; resubmit only on a clear "not done" |
   | **Duplicate submissions** | Two transactions or commands for one act | Idempotency key per business act; read-back | Mark the duplicate; correct proof and fee lines; report the count per incident |
   | **Retry storms** | Many callers retry a slow dependency at once | Retry ratio per dependency above budget | Single retry point; token-bucket retry budget; full jitter |
   | **Recovery surge** | A queue drains into a dependency that just came back | Recovering state; error rate on trial submissions | Paced drain below the counterpart's normal rate; revalidate prerequisites before each submission |
   | **Stale queued work** | Prerequisites changed while the act waited | Revalidation before submission | Reject with a reason to the dealer; expire commands that newer state has superseded |
   | **Incomplete data after a restore** | The counterpart restores from an older backup | Read-only reconciliation before write-back resumes | Discrepancy tickets; the field owner decides |
   | **Quota exhaustion** | A shared key hits its daily limit; all tenants lose the source | Quota meter in the session monitor | Reserve headroom for interactive traffic; batch lane pauses first |
   | **Monitor wrong** | The session monitor says healthy while submissions fail | Flow monitor disagrees with the health state | Flow evidence wins; page on the disagreement |
   | **Synchronous call with timeout** | The freshest answer; simplest to build | Inherits the dependency's availability and latency; spends the budget | The act needs a live answer (purchase gate, register transaction) |
   | **Mirror or push-fed copy, labeled** | Availability independent of the source; low latency | Licence limits; storage; freshness to monitor; stale facts if the feed stops | Information dependencies the licence lets you keep (register facts, MOT bulk files) |
   | **Cache within the licence, with soft and hard TTL** | Good hit rates on repeated vehicles; serves the last good value | Cold-cache surges; licence retention limits; negative caching needed | Paid per-request sources with retention rights ([[scale-and-cost]]) |
   | **Asynchronous queue with receipts** | The user keeps working; order and retries are controlled | Pending states to show; reconciliation; expiry rules | Writes: register transactions, portal operations, DMS write-backs |
   | **Second provider as runtime fallback** | A different source when the first fails | Different answers from different models; rarely exercised; double contracts | Only when the second path also carries live traffic every day |
   | **Registration write channel** | NL: RDW channel through a listed provider or directly, with an RDW certificate.
   | **Behavior during an outage** | NL: registration cannot be completed until the disruption ends (RDW, 2026) | GKS is asynchronous: requests are *Weitergeleitet* (forwarded for manual handling) or *Eskaliert* (escalated) when a portal gives no response "within five minutes" (KBA brochure, 2024) | DVLA's online sale and purchase service publishes opening hours (Monday to Friday 7am to 9pm, weekends 7am to 8pm) | Per s
   | **Information read path** | NL: open register snapshot (no VIN, plate-keyed) can be mirrored; change detection by diffing or a provider stream | No open vehicle-level register; reads under §39 StVG need a claim-related purpose | DVSA MOT bulk and daily delta files can be mirrored; DVLA's vehicle enquiry API closed to new registrations (October 2026) | Title and brand data through NMVTIS-approved providers; licensed
   | **Published limits and windows** | RDW Sunday maintenance windows, published per year | KBA names the software provider as first-level support for GKS customers | DVSA: 500,000 requests a day, 15 a second, 24-hour restriction after exceeding quota | Program-specific; not studied here |
   | **DMS concentration** | Long tail of local vendors (DriveDesk is fictional) | Brand-shaped DMS landscape | Several vendors cover large groups | High: CDK alone served nearly 15,000 locations in 2024 |
   **What is common** is the reliability model: the dependency map, journey SLOs with three outcomes, per-dependency budgets, one retry point, breakers driven by a shared health state, and the split between labeled reads and honestly queued writes.
   **What is a market adapter** is the shape of the write channel.
   **What is a rule set** is which facts may be mirrored: open Dutch register data and DVSA bulk files can be, while German register data generally cannot, so a German lookup has fewer labeled-copy options and needs partial answers sooner.
===== scale-and-cost 51
   | **Cost** | | **€105,490** |
   | **Result** | | **about −€9,000** |
   A **workload shape** is the profile of one kind of demand over time.
   A **unit cost** is the cost of one unit of a business output: a lookup, a report, a publish, a rooftop-month.
   **Upstream data**: fees paid per request to build-data providers, history sources, valuation inputs and registers, including fees for requests that return nothing.
   **Amortized licences**: fixed fees for bulk datasets and register access, spread over the units they serve.
   **Shared platform and people**: gateway, identity, observability, on-call, support and the legacy data center, allocated by a stated rule.
   **Cost per lookup** is the fully loaded unit cost of one billable data call, reported as an average and, more usefully, **per call path**.
   **Cost to serve** is the fully loaded cost of one customer over a period: a rooftop-month, an API client's year, a dealer group's contract.
   A **pass-through cost** is an upstream fee that the platform passes on to the client explicitly, as a surcharge or a separate line, instead of absorbing it in a blended price.
   | **Interactive lookups** (dealer screens, partner software) | Business hours | 2.6 million billable lookups a day | Weekday peak hour about 310 calls/s, ~10× the daily average | Sub-second | Build data on miss; history sources; register APIs |
   | **API client batch bursts** | Client's job scheduler | Rijnstaal: 1.1 million calls in 41 minutes | 450 calls/s, unannounced | Minutes to hours, if the client is told | High: older and rarer vehicles miss the cache |
   | **Nightly recomputation** | Clock | Pricing signals for about 350,000 Lotwise stock vehicles | A 01:00–05:00 window *(assumption)* | Done before dealers open | Low: internal market data |
   | **Monday-morning stock edits** | Week start | 352,000 stock vehicles; 1.4 million channel operations a day | About 3× the hourly average, 08:00–11:00 Monday *(assumption)* | Minutes (publish lag) | Portal rate limits |
   | **Month-end register peaks** | Calendar, sales targets | 1.25 million register transactions a year | About 4× the daily average at month- and quarter-end | Same day | Register transaction channel |
   | **Consumer spikes** | Media coverage, weekends, attacks | Odolo: about 2.1 million free checks a month | Enumeration in August 2027: about 9 million requests in 6 days, ~20× normal | Seconds | Register mirror (cheap); paid sources on full reports |
   | **Market-data ingestion** | Harvest and partner feeds | About 2.3 million adverts a day | Spread over the day; reprocessing runs are large *(assumption)* | Hours | Partner feed limits |
   The **upstream budget broker** is the component most platforms lack.
   - **Paces** requests per upstream with a token bucket sized to the contract or the published limit.
   - **Reserves** headroom for interactive traffic, so the batch lane can use only what the interactive lane leaves unused.
   - **Counts spend** per client and per product as it happens and enforces **spend caps**.
   - **Coalesces** concurrent requests for the same vehicle into one upstream purchase.
   | **Owned or open fact** | Dutch register open data in the register mirror; vPIC decode tables | None beyond the source's terms; NHTSA offers downloadable vPIC databases ([NHTSA, n.d.](https://vpic.nhtsa.dot.gov/api/)) | Lookup cost is compute only |
   | **Licensed fact with retention** | Build data per VIN | Contract sets retention (Axleby until 2027: 30 days) and permitted reuse | Each expiry is a repurchase |
   | **Rented content** | Third-party history records in a report | Often per-request use only *(assumption: no public clause)* | No reuse; cost is per report |
   | **Negative answer** | "Plate not found", "no MOT record" | Short time-to-live (TTL) | Stops repeated no-hits becoming spend |
   | **Direct average** | 100% | | **€0.0240** |
   | **Fully loaded average** | | | **€0.031** |
   | **Renegotiate build-data retention** | Retain resolved build data per VIN for the life of the vehicle within Axleby products; the provider prices the first fetch higher (about €0.58) | Removes repurchases: about −€0.0034 |
   | **Coalesce concurrent fetches** | The upstream budget broker holds one in-flight purchase per VIN and source; other requests wait for it | Removes duplicates: about −€0.0006 |
   | **Negative cache and no-hit pre-checks** | Cache "not found" answers for 24 hours *(assumption)*; check the register mirror before paying for a plate that does not exist | Halves paid no-hits: about −€0.0018 |
   | **Batch lane with pass-through on misses** | Batch endpoint with a completion window; clients see a quote of the expected misses, and misses above a stated rate carry a surcharge | Protects margin on batches; no change to the average |
   | **Total** | | **about €49** |
   **Cost per publish** is the cost of one channel operation (insert, update or delete of an advert on one portal).
   **Cost per report** is dominated by sources.
   | **Vehicle images** | 1.9 million stock-ins a year × about 30 photos × originals and renditions | Roughly 250 TB a year *(assumption: 4.5 MB per photo with renditions)* | Business need: adverts end, dossiers keep a few photos | Tier or delete after sale; keep renditions only where reused |
   | **History events** (Tracewell) and the register mirror | New events per VIN; 41 million vehicles | Moderate, steady *(assumption)* | Product value; privacy rules in [[privacy-and-retention]] | Columnar storage; bitemporal compaction |
   | **Ledgers and usage events** (valuation ledger, Meterline, access audit) | 2.6 million billable lookups a day plus reports and transactions | Hundreds of GB a year raw, far less compressed *(assumption)* | Dispute windows, fiscal retention, audit | Columnar store; never delete inside retention |
   | **Fetch per request, short retention** | Buy build data or history per call; keep it for the licence window | Low and unpredictable volume; the licence is cheap per call | Repurchases; cost tracks traffic; batches lose money at flat prices |
   | **Fetch once, retain per VIN** | Negotiate long retention within the platform's own products | High repeat demand for the same vehicles; facts that do not change | Higher first-fetch price; retention must be enforced as a licence rule |
   | **Prefetch on first registration** | Buy data for new vehicles as the register mirror shows them | Most lookups concern vehicles registered in the platform's markets | Pays for vehicles nobody asks about; imports still miss |
   | **Flat-fee or tiered bulk licence** | Pay a fixed fee for unlimited or banded use | Volume is high and stable | Commitment risk if volume falls; harder exit |
   | **Derive from own catalog** | Infer specification from register type data and the platform's catalog | Facts the register carries well | **Spec Guesswork** where edition and options matter |
   | **Blended per-call price** | One list price per call type | Cost per path is predictable and the mix is stable | Batches and rare vehicles erode margin silently |
   | **Pass-through or miss surcharge** | Charge misses, or upstream fees, separately | Batch clients; contracts that already price upstream | Complex invoices; a meter that knows paths is required |
   | **Batch lane with completion window** | Accept batches on a separate endpoint, paced by budget | Insurers, lessors, fleet enrichment | Clients must accept delay; needs quoting and status |
   | **Register data for lookups** | NL: RDW open data can be mirrored, so status lookups cost compute ([RDW open data, accessed 2026-10](https://opendata.rdw.nl/resource/m9d7-ebf2.json)).
   | **Main upstream cost driver** | Build data on misses; register transaction fees (passed through) | Specification and valuation inputs from commercial providers on more calls | Quota rather than fees on public APIs; paid history sources | Per-report history licences |
   | **Caching posture** | Mirror open register data; license build-data retention | Retention set entirely by commercial contracts | Respect per-key quotas; no bulk mirror where terms forbid | Decode tables cached freely; report content rented |
   | **Calendar peaks** | Month-end and quarter-end register transactions (Axleby: ~4×); year-end tax changes *(assumption)* | Month- and quarter-end sales targets *(assumption)* | Plate-change months, March and September, are commonly the busiest *(industry practice)* | Month- and quarter-end retail pushes; weekends *(assumption)* |
   | **Shared-quota risk** | Low for mirrored data | Contract rate limits per provider | High: one key per company, daily lock-out | Provider rate limits |
===== observability-and-data-operations 54
   **Publish lag** is the time from a stock change in the dealer system to its confirmed state on a channel.
   The **support console** is the tool that shows a support agent the evidence about one vehicle, advert, lead or transaction.
   | **Service health** | Are our components up, fast and not saturated? | Latency, traffic, errors, saturation per service; queue depth; consumer lag | Engineers, within minutes |
   | **Flow health** | Do the outcomes dealers and clients pay for actually happen, end to end, in time? | Publish lag, lead acknowledgement, register transaction outcome, report delivery | Dealers and clients, hours or days later |
   | **Data health** | Are the facts we hold and sell fresh, complete, plausible and correctly attributed? | Source freshness, batch volume, value distributions, match rates, conflict rates | Consumers and data clients, weeks later |
   **Outcome mix**: confirmed success, confirmed failure, still pending, and **unknown**, meaning the platform cannot tell.
   **Publish lag** is the time from the moment a stock change is committed in the dealer system of record (Lotwise at Axleby) to the moment the platform has **evidence** that the channel shows the new state.
   | **Confirmed state** | A callback or status API reporting the advert's processed state | A portal partner API with stock notifications | True end-to-end lag |
   | **Accepted** | A synchronous acknowledgement that the request was received | HTTP 202, or a 200 on an asynchronous pipeline | Lag to acceptance only; the rest is inferred |
   | **File delivered** | A feed file was transferred; the portal imports it on its own schedule | Nightly or hourly XML or CSV over SFTP | Lag to transfer only |
   | **Read-back** | The platform reads the advert back from the channel and compares it | Sampled lookups through a partner API, where the channel's terms permit them | Real state, on a sample |
   - **Freshness**: the age of the newest record from a source.
   - **Volume**: records per batch or per day against a trailing baseline per source.
   - **Distribution**: whether values still look like they did, per segment.
   - **Linkage**: whether records still resolve to vehicles.
   **OpenTelemetry** (OTel) is the instrumentation standard to use; it graduated in the Cloud Native Computing Foundation on **11 May 2026** ([CNCF, n.d., accessed 2026-10](https://www.cncf.io/projects/opentelemetry/)).
   - **Backends for frontends (BFFs)** start the trace and attach tenant context from the authenticated session: organization, rooftop, user role, product.
   - **Asynchronous hops** break the parent-child chain.
   - **Adapters at the edge** record the request, the external system's response, and the **interpreted outcome**: confirmed, accepted, rejected with reason, or ambiguous.
   | **Metrics** | `market`, `tenant.tier` (independent, group, enterprise, API client) | `product`, `channel`, `operation`, `outcome` | Bounded cardinality: alerting and SLOs run here |
   | **Traces and logs** | `org.id`, `rooftop.id`, `client.id` (API clients), `user.role` | `avk` where a vehicle is involved, `advert.id`, `lead.id` | High cardinality is cheap per record; per-tenant views come from queries |
   | **Flow events** | `org.id`, `rooftop.id` | `avk`, `channel`, `operation`, timestamps per stage, evidence level | Per-tenant and per-channel monitors and the support console |
   | **Never in telemetry** | — | Plates and VINs linked to a private owner, buyer names, e-mail addresses, phone numbers | Personal data; use the Axleby Vehicle Key (AVK) or a token |
   | **Per workshop chain**: readings lower than the vehicle's previous reading | Above 0.5% of the chain's records *(assumption)* | **Quarantine** that chain's segment; release the rest of the batch |
   - **Register channels.** Turnstile, Axleby's register gateway, already monitors sessions, certificates and quotas ([[reliability-and-dependencies]] covers the session monitor).
   - **Transaction channels** cannot be probed with real transactions.
   - **Portals.** A probe checks each channel's API endpoints and authentication, and the read-back sampler doubles as a correctness probe.
   - **Axleby's own public surfaces.** The JSON API, Connect XML and Odolo's free check are probed from outside AWS, so a regional or DNS failure is seen the way clients see it.
   **Probe traffic is real traffic**: it consumes register quotas and must be excluded from usage metering and market statistics, so probes run under a dedicated internal client identity that Meterline (usage metering) and Pulse ignore ([[billing-and-metering]]).
   | **Vehicle dossier timeline** | Dossier events (stock-in, price changes, reports, valuations, register transactions), keyed by AVK | "What happened to this car in our systems, and when?" |
   | **Channel status** | Publish-lag flow records with evidence level per channel | "Is this advert live, withdrawn, or unknown on each portal, and since when?" |
   | **Lead trail** | Lead flow records | "Where did this lead go, who acknowledged it, and when did a human respond?" |
   | **Transaction status** | Register transaction outcomes | "Was this registration submitted, did it succeed, and is the outcome ambiguous?" |
   | **SEV1** | A paid flow is broken for a market or more, or a confirmed data exposure | Register transaction channel unavailable at month-end; cross-tenant data shown |
   | **SEV2** | A paid flow is broken for a dealer group, a channel or a major API client, or a data defect has reached customers | One portal not accepting withdrawals; a supplier batch with wrong values published |
   | **SEV3** | Degraded flow within SLO error budget, or a data defect held at the gate | Elevated publish lag on file-based channels; a quarantined batch |
   - **Duplicate flow events.** The outbox relay delivers at least once, so a flow record can arrive twice.
   - **Conflicting evidence.** An adapter reports "accepted", and the read-back shows the advert still live a day later.
   - **Incomplete records.** A flow record without a start timestamp, or a span without a tenant tag, is not dropped.
   - **Retries that mask failure.** An operation that succeeds on its fifth attempt is a success for the dealer and a warning for the platform.
   - **Partial failures.** Portals and DMS products often accept a batch and reject some items inside it.
   - **Monitoring outage.** If the telemetry backend's intake is delayed, dashboards show calm, not alarm.
   - **External outage noise.** During a register or portal outage, flow monitors fill with Unknown and queued records.
   | **Per-tenant visibility** | Tenant ids as metric labels | Tenant ids on traces, logs and flow events; metrics by tier | Fewer than a few hundred tenants, or one critical metric | Thousands of rooftops (Axleby: 8,550 across Lotwise and Showroomly) |
   | **Telemetry backend** | Hosted vendor (Axleby: Datadog) | Self-hosted open-source stack (Prometheus, Loki, Tempo, Grafana) | Small SRE team; fast start; monitoring must survive own outages | High volumes where vendor pricing dominates; strong platform team; data-residency demands |
   | **Flow evidence** | Hop acknowledgements | Confirmed state via callbacks and read-back | Channels with trustworthy synchronous semantics | Asynchronous channels and any withdrawal flow |
   | **Flow monitoring method** | Event-based stage tracking | Periodic reconciliation of desired versus actual state | Need for per-unit latency and support detail | Channels with no events at all; catching drift that events miss |
   | **Data checks** | Blocking gate (quarantine) | Warn and publish | Sources whose errors spread to paid outputs (history, odometer, register) | Low-impact enrichment where delay costs more than error |
   | **Trace sampling** | Head sampling at a fixed rate | Tail sampling keeping errors, slow traces and flagged tenants | Low volume; simplicity | Production at scale; investigations of single tenants |
   | **Registers to probe** | NL: RDW information and transaction channels (certificate-based); RDW open data carries a last-modified header.
   | **Channel evidence** | Typically a mix of portal partner APIs and feed files (industry practice); file-based channels need read-back | Typically partner APIs at the large portals and file feeds at smaller channels (industry practice) | Auto Trader UK publishes per-message notifications with retries (n.d., accessed 2026-10) | Lead delivery commonly by ADF/XML (Auto-lead Data Format) e-mail; acknowledgements need CRM
   | **Breach and incident notice** | GDPR Art. 33: 72 hours to the authority; processors notify controllers without undue delay | Same GDPR rule | UK GDPR, same 72-hour rule; notification to the ICO | Federal Trade Commission (FTC) Safeguards Rule: dealers notify the FTC within 30 days for events involving at least 500 consumers (in force since May 2024); state breach laws vary |
   | **Monitoring of employees** | NL: works council consent for systems "suitable for" observing presence, behavior or performance (Works Councils Act, art. 27(1)(l)) | Works council co-determination for technical systems that monitor employee behavior or performance (Works Constitution Act, §87(1) no. 6) | No equivalent co-determination right; data-protection rules apply | No federal equivalent |
   | **Customer audit demands** | Dealer auditors ask about processor access | Same, plus works-council questions about per-salesperson metrics | Similar to EU | Safeguards Rule requires dealers to "monitor and log the activity of authorized users" (16 CFR 314.4(c)(8)); dealers ask vendors for user-activity logs |
===== vehicle-platform-security 61
   **Enumeration defense** is the set of controls that make walking an identifier space (plates, VINs, stock ids, lead ids) expensive, slow, visible and low-yield; a **scoped credential** binds one client, environment, set of purposes and spend limit, and is revocable on its own.
   | **Derived vehicle data** | History signals, damage counts, valuations, decoded editions and equipment | Resale; building a competing check or valuation service |
   | **Licensed and official data** | Build data from a commercial provider; register data; workshop odometer readings | Resale without the licence; the platform's licence is at risk if it leaks |
   | **Personal data held for dealers** | Leads, buyer details, finance applications, sales contracts | Fraud, identity theft, extortion |
   | **The ability to act** | Publish or withdraw adverts; change prices; submit registration in a new name (*tenaamstellen*) or indemnification (*vrijwaren*) at the Dutch register through Axleby Trade | Fraud; theft of a car's legal identity; sabotage of a competitor |
   | **Availability** | Lotwise for 7,200 rooftops; Showroomly for 1,350; the data APIs for 640 clients | Extortion: ransomware works because dealers cannot sell without the system |
   - **Authorization by object**: the caller may reach only objects it has a relationship with (its stock item, its lead).
   - **Licensed lookup of any object**: the caller is *allowed* to ask about any plate, because that is the product.
   | **Plate and VIN enumeration** | Odolo free check; trial and leaked API keys | Derived and licensed data; upstream spend | The August attack | Enumeration defense; scoped credentials; spend caps | This chapter; [[consumer-vehicle-checks]] |
   | **BOLA in dealer APIs** | Lotwise and Showroomly APIs with sequential ids | Other tenants' stock, leads, deals | Sequential integer ids in Lotwise Classic | Object checks; tenant context; non-guessable ids | [[tenancy-and-dealer-groups]] |
   | **Credential stuffing at dealer logins** | Lotwise and Axleby Portal login pages | Leads, finance applications, register transactions | No MFA on Lotwise and Axleby Portal logins; shared logins | MFA; breached-password checks; step-up for high-risk actions | [[identity-access-and-entitlements]] |
   | **API key leakage by integrators** | Public repositories, mobile apps, logs | Whatever the key's scope allows | 210 Connect XML clients with passwords; static v1 keys | Scoped credentials; secret scanning; holder binding | This chapter |
   | **Ransomware** | Phished staff, exposed admin paths, a compromised partner | Everything the attacker reaches | The DriveDesk Outage (November 2027) at a partner | Segmentation; phishing-resistant MFA; immutable backups; restore drills | This chapter; [[reliability-and-dependencies]] |
   | **Supply chain through adapters** | 61 channel adapters, DMS adapters, supplier feeds, webhook URLs | Partner credentials; internal network | Adapters share a runtime and a secrets namespace | Adapter isolation; per-partner credentials; egress control | This chapter; [[dms-integration]] |
   | **Insider misuse** | Support staff, engineers, dealer staff | Customer lists, leads, report data | Support access without tickets before Pitwall | Ticket-bound access; masking; access audit | This chapter; [[observability-and-data-operations]] |
   | **Register-transaction fraud** | A dealer account with register authority | A car's legal registration | Shared logins submitted 61% of Velto's Q1 transactions | Person-level authority; step-up MFA; anomaly rules | [[registration-and-trade-services]] |
   | **Edge** (web application firewall, bot management, challenge) | Is this a browser or app session that behaves like one? | Crude bots, known-bad networks, high volume per address | Residential proxies with real browsers; authenticated API clients |
   | **Gateway** (scoped credential) | Is the caller known, and is the credential valid for this environment and product? | Unauthenticated calls; sandbox keys on production; expired keys | A valid leaked credential, within its scope |
   | **Charter** (purpose check) | Is this product allowed for this client's licensed purpose? | Calls outside the licence ([[data-licensing-and-governance]]) | Misuse inside a licensed purpose |
   | **Quota and spend meter** | Has this caller exceeded its contract quota or its daily upstream spend? | Bulk extraction beyond contract; cost runaways | Slow extraction within quota |
   | **Response composer** | Which fields does this caller's tier receive? | High-value fields reaching anonymous or trial callers | Nothing; it limits the yield |
   | **Upstream budget broker** | May this request spend money upstream? | Paid fetches triggered by trial or anonymous traffic ([[scale-and-cost]]) | — |
   | **Walk detector** | Does this caller's traffic look like a walk? | Sequential series; no-hit rate far above normal; plate-format coverage; lookups with no follow-on action | Walks spread over many accounts that each look normal |
   **BOLA on the dealer side** is a matter of discipline in every handler: the object must belong to the caller's tenant and be reachable through the caller's relationships. [[tenancy-and-dealer-groups]] describes the tenant context, the response-side assertion and the leak tests.
   - **Cells are security boundaries, not only capacity units.** Each cell has its own deployment credentials, databases and per-tenant data keys wrapped by a cell key ([[tenancy-and-dealer-groups]]).
   - **The adapter zone holds partner credentials and nothing else.** The 61 channel adapters and the DMS adapters run in their own accounts, with one secret path per partner and per rooftop connection.
   - **All outbound traffic leaves through an egress proxy.** It allow-lists partner hosts per adapter, strips internal context and, as the Standard Webhooks specification recommends for webhook senders, "filters internal IP addresses", because a customer-supplied URL is attacker-controlled input ([Standard Webhooks, n.d., accessed 2026-10](https://github.com/standard-webhooks/standard-webhooks/blob/main/spec/standard-w
   - **The register gateway is its own zone.** Turnstile holds the certificates and keys that let Axleby act at the Dutch register, and the DVLA key that cannot be replaced while registration is closed ([[registers-and-official-data]]).
   - **No flat network from dealers to the platform.** Dealers reach Axleby over the internet with user authentication, never through site-to-site VPNs.
   **Backups** are designed against an attacker who already holds production credentials.
   **Inventory with owners**: every adapter has an owner, a partner contract, a credential list with scopes, and the rooftops it serves.
   **Inbound data is untrusted input**: schema and size limits, no active content in descriptions, quarantine on anomalies ([[ingestion-and-entity-resolution]]).
   - **Measures.** Article 21 includes incident handling, business continuity "such as backup management and disaster recovery", **supply chain security**, vulnerability handling, access control, cryptography and multi-factor authentication where appropriate.
   - **Reporting clocks.** Article 23 requires an early warning within 24 hours of becoming aware of a significant incident, an incident notification within 72 hours, and a final report within one month.
   - **Fines.** Maximums of at least €10 million or 2% of worldwide turnover for essential entities, €7 million or 1.4% for important ones.
   | **Distributed walk below every static limit** | August 2027: 3,800 addresses, under 400 requests each per day | Walk detector on outcomes per session cluster and per credential, not per address | Challenge and low-yield responses for anonymous tiers; suspend credentials; canary evidence for follow-up |
   | **Conflicting revocation and use** | A key is revoked while 2,000 requests are in flight | Gateway revocation cache under 60 s | In-flight requests complete; usage after the cut-off is not billed and is logged as a security event |
   | **Incomplete inventory** | An adapter credential nobody owns; a beta host without rate limits (OWASP API9) | Quarterly inventory reconciliation against the secrets service and DNS | Assign or revoke; retire unlisted hosts |
   | **Partial failure of a control** | The walk detector is down for a day | Heartbeat and an alert on missing signals | Static quotas, spend caps and response tiers still apply; the detector backfills from usage events |
   | **Partner compromise** | The DriveDesk Outage: ransomware at a DMS vendor | Partner notice; adapter health; anomalous inbound data | Kill switch; credential revocation; degraded mode; out-of-band re-keying; reconciliation before write-back ([[dms-integration]]) |
   | **Own compromise in one cell** | Deployment credentials of one cell stolen | Deployment audit; unexpected changes; canary tenants | Isolate the cell; rotate its keys; restore from the vault if integrity is in doubt; other cells keep serving |
   | **Per-address rate limits only** | Limit requests per IP address at the edge | Small sites; unauthenticated content of low value | Fails against residential proxies; blocks shared offices and mobile networks |
   | **Authentication for every lookup** | No anonymous lookups at all | Pure B2B data APIs | Kills consumer acquisition; Odolo's free check exists to sell reports |
   | **Tiered responses** | Anonymous callers get public fields; paid or contracted callers get derived and licensed fields | Mixed consumer and business products on one data platform | Product must accept a less attractive free tier; field-level design per tier |
   | **Behavioral walk detection** | Score outcomes per caller and per session cluster | Any endpoint with significant volume | Tuning effort; false positives against legitimate batch clients, who need a batch lane |
   | **Challenges and app attestation** | Proof of a real browser or genuine app | Consumer traffic | Friction for real users; attackers can pay for solved challenges |
   | **Traceable answers (canaries)** | Unique synthetic records reachable only by walking | Data whose resale would harm the business | Must never mislead a real user; needs disciplined handling |
   | **Cybersecurity law for a SaaS vendor** | NIS2; Dutch *Cyberbeveiligingswet* in force 15 August 2026 ([NCSC, accessed 2026-10](https://www.ncsc.nl/cyberbeveiligingswet)); Belgium's transposition not verified for this chapter | NIS2 through the amended BSI Act; the BSI's page states the statutory registration deadline has passed and directs entities to register ([BSI, accessed 2026-10](https://www.bsi.bund.de/DE/The
   | **Incident reporting** | NIS2: 24 h / 72 h / 1 month; GDPR Art. 33: 72 h | Same | UK GDPR: 72 h; NIS Regulations | FTC: 30 days for 500+ consumers; state breach laws |
   | **Assurance customers ask for** *(industry practice)* | ISO/IEC 27001 | ISO/IEC 27001; the BSI publishes NIS2 guidance alongside it | ISO/IEC 27001; Cyber Essentials (Plus) in supplier requirements | SOC 2 Type 2 |
   | **Plate as sensitive data in APIs** | Register vehicle data is open; keeper data never released for commerce | Keeper data only for legal claims (§ 39 StVG) | ICO treats plates as personal data in most cases; DVLA requires plates in POST bodies | Vehicle data not personal under the Driver's Privacy Protection Act; DMV personal data restricted |
   - **The response tiers.** A product decision with a security floor set by the Chief Architect and the CISO: anonymous tiers get nothing not already public.
   - **The credential policy.** One policy for every product (scope, lifetime, holder binding, sandbox isolation, suspension); products do not issue their own keys.
   - **Cells as security boundaries.** The cell map in [[tenancy-and-dealer-groups]] is also the incident-isolation map.
   - **Restore objectives per product**, approved by the CTO, with drill results reported to the board quarterly.
   - **The regulatory position.** Whether the group is in NIS2 scope, and as what, is a legal judgement made once, written down and revisited when products change.
   - **Walk-detector precision**: confirmed walks against suspensions reversed for legitimate clients.
   - **Credential hygiene**: share of production credentials scoped, holder-bound and under a year old.
   - **MFA coverage** of users who can submit register transactions, read finance applications or export lists.
   - **Time to revoke**: from leak report to suspension, measured on every report.
   - **Restore drill results** per product, and **adapter credentials** with a named owner.
===== privacy-and-retention 65
   The writer, a consumer in Heerenveen whom this chapter calls the requester, asked Axleby to "delete everything you have about me and my cars" and to stop "selling my number plate to car dealers."
   | **Odolo** | Account, a saved "my car" watch on her current plate, **3** paid full reports (2024) with their purchase and payment records, **2** support tickets | Controller |
   | **Pulse** (market data) | **2** harvested private-seller adverts for her 2017 Toyota Yaris from early 2024; one had her mobile number in the free-text description | Controller |
   | **Lotwise**, Velto Mobility Group's Zwolle rooftop | Appraisal and purchase file for the Yaris (March 2024): her name, address, bank account, a copy of her identity document | Processor for Velto |
   | **Lotwise**, Autobedrijf Brandsma | Customer record, **3** portal leads, the deal and sales invoice for a 2021 Kia Niro (April 2024) | Processor for Brandsma |
   | **Horizon** (Axleby Insight's trade-in propensity scores) | **14** monthly scores on the Kia's plate back to 2024, computed on the customer list Brandsma uploads and kept without expiry, as all scores were before the 2027 redesign ([[data-products-and-insights]]) | Processor for Brandsma |
   | **Snowflake** (Lotwise copy) | Copies of all of the Lotwise records above | Same as the source |
   | **Tracewell** (history) | **17** odometer, inspection and ownership-change events on the two cars, with no person fields | Controller |
   | **Backups** | All of the above, in 35-day rolling backups | Same as the source |
   | **Telemetry** | Nothing: the 2027 tagging standard keeps private plates and contact data out of logs ([[observability-and-data-operations]]) | — |
   - **The warehouse ignored retention.** Lotwise deleted leads after the dealer's retention setting in MySQL, but the nightly copy to Snowflake had never deleted anything.
   - **Free text defeated minimization.** Pulse's harvester dropped seller contact fields, but not phone numbers typed into advert descriptions.
   - **Deletion would not have stuck.** Deleting the requester's Horizon scores would have done nothing.
   | **UK Information Commissioner's Office (ICO)** | "In most circumstances, a VRM is personal data.
   | **DVLA** (Driver and Vehicle Licensing Agency, UK) | Treats registration number and VIN as sensitive information and requires them in the request body, not the URL, "to reduce the chance of it appearing in web server logs" ([DVLA KADOE API, n.d.](https://developer-portal.driver-vehicle-licensing.api.gov.uk/apis/kadoe/kadoe-description.html)) | A transport rule worth copying for every API |
   | **RDW** (Dutch vehicle authority) | Vehicle data "can be viewed for free by everyone"; data about the persons linked to vehicles go only to the holder and parties named in law; "market parties such as garage businesses never receive personal data from the RDW for commercial purposes" (translated) ([RDW privacy statement, n.d.](https://www.rdw.nl/over-rdw/privacy-en-security/privacyverklaring/gegevens-die-de-rdw-reg
   - **Vehicle facts**: identity, specification, odometer readings, inspections, damage, registration events, valuations, market observations.
   - **Person facts**: names, contact details, identity documents, bank accounts, finance applications, messages.
   - **Links**: "this person was the seller of this vehicle on this date", "this lead was about this stock item".
   - **Reports describe the car.** Odolo and business reports show the number of previous keepers, ownership-change dates at month precision and the country of earlier registration.
   - **Harvesting keeps facts, not sellers.** Pulse stores price, mileage, equipment, dates and a seller *type* (private or trade).
   - **Events are thin.** `lead.received` and `deal.signed` carry ids, not contact details, so subscribers fetch personal data through an authorized API call ([[events-webhooks-and-sync]]).
   - **Identifiers travel in bodies, not URLs**, following the DVLA practice; telemetry carries tenant ids but never private plates or contact data.
   A **retention rule** is a period, a trigger and an action.
   - **Retention runs.** Each night Tidemark asks every registered store's adapter for records past their trigger date and issues delete, anonymize or aggregate commands.
   - **The erasure ledger** is an append-only log of every erasure, keyed by a salted hash of the subject key (an e-mail address, phone number, tenant customer id or plate), with the controller, scope, reason and the stores confirmed.
   - **The suppression list** is the part of the ledger that recomputation jobs must consult before they produce output: Horizon scoring, harvest-to-Pulse matching, marketing audiences.
   - **Restores replay the ledger.** Any restore from backup, any warehouse rebuild and any replay from the landing zone applies the erasure ledger before the restored data becomes readable.
   | **Duplicate identities** | The requester used two e-mail addresses; a lead had a mistyped phone number | Locator matches on several identifiers; low-confidence hits shown, not acted on | Confirm with the requester before erasing a weak match |
   | **Duplicate requests** | The same request arrives by form and by e-mail | Case dedup on verified identity | Merge cases; one answer |
   | **Conflicting rules** | Erasure requested while an invoice is in its fiscal period, or a record is under legal hold | Tidemark evaluates holds and fiscal rules before deleting | Restrict instead of erase; tell the requester why and until when |
   | **Erasure versus correction** | A correction to a vehicle fact arrives for a person-linked record already erased | Ledger lookup in the correction path ([[journey-data-correction]]) | Correct the vehicle fact; do not re-link the person |
   | **Incomplete request** | A request names only a plate, with no proof of keepership | Verification step | Ask for proof; answer only about data linked to the verified person |
   | **Retries and partial failure** | The Snowflake delete fails for one partition | Per-store confirmations missing past the target | Idempotent retry; case stays open until all stores confirm |
   | **Consumer missed the event** | The search index was down when `party.erased` was published | Consumer lag alarm; nightly reconciliation of the index against the ledger | Replay from the ledger |
   | **Resurrection** | A restore or a landing-zone replay brings deleted leads back | Restore runbook requires a ledger replay step | No restored store opens until the replay completes |
   | **Recomputation** | Next month's Horizon run rescores a suppressed plate | Job reads the suppression list at start; fails closed | Suppressed outputs never leave the job |
   | **Dealer does not answer** | A forwarded request sits unanswered at a small dealer | Case clock; reminder at 5 working days | Escalate to the dealer's account owner; document Axleby's assistance |
   | **Unregistered store** | A team adds a new cache or bucket with personal data | Deployment check against the processing register; periodic scan for unregistered buckets | Block the deployment; register the store with a retention class |
   The **unregistered store** is the failure that undoes all the others.
   | **Targeted deletion** | Delete or update the rows in each store | Exact; works with retention rules | Must reach every copy; slow in columnar stores and backups | Default for individual erasure and retention runs |
   | **Anonymization** | Remove the link to the person, keep the vehicle fact | Keeps value for history, valuation and statistics | Must truly cut the link; free text and rare combinations leak | Expired appraisals, deals after fiscal period, adverts |
   | **Restriction** | Keep the data, block all use but the legal one | Meets legal-retention conflicts | Needs access control on the restricted fields | Invoices in fiscal period, legal holds |
   | **Crypto-shredding** | Encrypt per key; destroy the key | Reaches backups and copies at once | Per-person keys are hard to manage; useless for data copied in clear | Tenant offboarding with per-tenant keys ([[tenancy-and-dealer-groups]]) |
   | **Suppression** | Keep a hashed identifier that blocks future use | Stops recomputation and re-collection | Keeps a minimal record of the person | Objections to marketing; recomputed products |
   | **Central hashed-identifier index** | Minutes per request; one place to test coverage | Is itself a store of personal data (hashed) that needs protection and retention |
   | **Is a plate personal data?** | GDPR and the CJEU's relative test (*Breyer*, *Scania*, *SRB*); no squarely deciding Dutch court ruling found | Same CJEU test | ICO: "in most circumstances" yes; guidance under review after the Data (Use and Access) Act 2025 | No general concept; under the Driver's Privacy Protection Act (DPPA) "personal information" means data identifying an individual and excludes vehicle attribute
   | **Keeper data from the register** | RDW never supplies personal data for commercial use; Belgian DIV access through a partner *(not researched in depth)* | § 39 StVG: keeper details only to pursue legal claims from road traffic ([StVG § 39](https://www.gesetze-im-internet.de/stvg/__39.html)) | DVLA releases keeper data for "reasonable cause", purpose-bound and audited ([DVLA INF266, 2022](https://assets.publishing.
   | **Regulator** | Autoriteit Persoonsgegevens; Belgian Data Protection Authority | Federal and state data protection authorities | ICO | FTC, state attorneys general, California Privacy Protection Agency |
