   | **Request deadline** | One month, extendable by two (GDPR Art. 12(3)) | Same | Same under UK GDPR | California: 45 calendar days, extendable by 45 ([California AG, 2026](https://oag.ca.gov/privacy/ccpa)) |
   | **Right to delete** | GDPR Art. 17 with Art. 19 recipient notices | Same | UK GDPR, same structure | State laws such as the California Consumer Privacy Act (CCPA, amended by CPRA); no federal right |
   | **Fiscal retention of sales records** | NL 7 years; BE set by Belgian VAT law | 8 years for accounting vouchers (§ 147 AO) | 6 years for VAT records | State and lender rules |
   | **Transfers out of the market** | EU–US Data Privacy Framework adequacy decision of 10 July 2023, upheld by the General Court on 3 September 2025 ([Curia press release 106/25](https://curia.europa.eu/jcms/upload/docs/application/pdf/2025-09/cp250106en.pdf)); EU adequacy for the UK renewed 19 December 2025 ([European Commission, accessed 2026-10](https://commission.europa.eu/law/law-topic/data-protection/internation
   | **Documented enforcement on vehicle data** | — | — | — | FTC's January 2026 order against GM and OnStar requires affirmative express consent for connected-vehicle data and bans disclosure to consumer reporting agencies for five years ([FTC, 2026](https://www.ftc.gov/node/327916)); a 2026 DPPA class action against Carfax over crash-report data survived a motion to dismiss (widely reported, [Robinson+Cole, 2026](http
   **A US entry would not be a new rule set but a new obligation type**: the DPPA's five-year recipient record is a per-disclosure audit that Charter's access audit can hold, but only if DMV-sourced personal data is tagged at ingestion.
   - **The default stance on identifiers.** Plates and VINs are personal by default; exceptions per component are written, reviewed by the DPO and kept in the processing register.
   - **The controller map.** Approved by the DPO and legal, reflected in dealer contracts, and enforced by architecture: dealer data crosses to controller-side stores only through registered flows.
   - **The retention schedule.** The DPO owns the periods; product owners own the implementation in their stores; the Chief Architect owns the rule that **no store exists without a retention class**, checked at deployment.
   - **The bounds on dealer settings.** A product decision with legal input: defaults, minimums and maximums per data class and market.
   - **Registration coverage**: share of stores, topics, buckets and warehouse tables with a retention class, and share feeding the locator.
   - **Records past retention**: count per store per week.
   - **Request cycle time** and **processor-forward time**, against the one-month clock and the 2-day target.
   - **Erasure propagation lag** to warehouse, search and caches.
   - **Suppression hits** in recomputation jobs: evidence that the list works.
   - **Personal data found in free text** by sampling: Pulse adverts, lead messages, dossier notes.
===== part-8 0
===== journey-inventory-to-portal 18
   **The only synchronous hops are local ones**: stock item, price and reservation commit inside the dossier, and everything remote follows with a visible status.
   **Two new problems.** First, **the reason went to the wrong person**: Lotwise showed Sietse "Rejected on AutoScout24: equipment not accepted", and he called support at 10:40 to ask which option to remove.
   **Rejections are classified by owner**: content the dealer controls (a missing Car-Pass link in Belgium) is a dealer action; platform mapping, configuration or adapter faults are an integrations incident, shown to the dealer as "we are fixing this".
   | **When to advertise** | From intake photos, "available from" date | Only when *Ready*, with retail photos | Margin-scheme stock with holding cost, buyers who accept a date; channels that allow it | Ad catalogs with strict image rules; dealers whose intake photos show damage; association portals that list only deliverable cars |
   | **Who coordinates the journey** | One orchestrator that drives every hop as a saga | Each hop reacts to the previous hop's event; a journey monitor measures end to end | Short, transactional flows with one owner (a register transaction) | Long flows across teams and weeks, where each hop has its own owner and failure modes (this journey) |
   | **Gate publication on all channels** | Publish nowhere until every chosen channel validates | Publish per channel as each validates | Never for consumer channels; a buyer on Marktplaats should not wait for Google | Default; show per-channel status to the dealer |
   | **Who creates the stock item for DMS rooftops** | The DMS row, through the adapter | The Lotwise appraisal, with the DMS row matched to it | When stock arrives without an appraisal (auction lots, transfers) | When the appraisal happened in Lotwise; the appraisal id is the better key |
   | **What the dealer sees after Publish** | "Published" | Per-channel evidence: live, accepted, sent, rejected with owner | Never, after the Golf Variant | Always |
   | **Handover to first live** | Purchase signed → first channel evidence | Median under 2 hours for cars advertised from intake *(assumption)* | Slow capture, slow composing |
   | **Desired to evidence, per channel** | Channel listing becomes desired present → channel evidence | p95 under 30 min for API channels *(assumption)*; file feeds at their schedule | Adapter, portal and mapping faults |
   | **Stock-in to first advert** | Stock item created → first live advert | 3.4 days average (Q4 2027) | Preparation and photo bottlenecks |
   | **Update convergence** | Price or content change → evidence on all channels | 95% within 15 min | Queueing, throttling |
   | **Withdrawal convergence** | Reserved, sold or written off → absence on all channels | 99% within 10 min; p99 14 min in September 2027 | The Zombie Listing |
   | **Sold-but-live** | Cars reserved or sold more than 30 min ago and live anywhere | Zero | Lost events, races, outages |
   | **First-time acceptance** | Share of creates accepted on the first attempt, per channel | Tracked per channel *(assumption: alert below 97%)* | Mapping and validation gaps |
   | **Rejections by owner** | Open rejections, split into dealer-owned and platform-owned, by age | Platform-owned older than 4 hours: zero *(assumption)* | Feed Roulette, misrouted reasons |
   | **Identifier integrity** | Stock items with AVK; channel listings with portal id; leads matched to a stock item | 100%, 100%, tracked | Broken chain, unmatched leads |
   **Measure every clock from a business event to evidence, never to a send.** And **split each measure by cause**: time spent waiting for a rule (the catalogs waiting for *Ready*) is a dealer decision, while time spent on a rejection is a platform defect.
===== journey-data-correction 46
   | **Fact version** | One recorded state of one vehicle fact of one Axleby Vehicle Key (AVK, the internal vehicle id), with its own id, valid time, recorded and superseded times, source and status ([[provenance-and-data-quality]]).
   | **Disputed** | A status on a fact version that is suspected wrong but not yet corrected.
   | **Correction case** | The unit of work and of accountability.
   | **Correction event** | `vehicle.fact.corrected`: an immutable, per-vehicle event saying that fact versions were superseded or retracted, published by the context that owns the fact.
   | **Impact edge** | A record, written by a consumer when it uses a fact version, that links the version to the artifact built on it, and the artifact to its recipient. |
   | **Impact graph** | All impact edges taken together.
   | **Issued artifact** | Anything that left the platform's control or was relied on: a valuation returned to a caller, a report delivered, an API answer, an offer made, a monthly file delivered.
   | **Live artifact** | Something the platform still renders and can change: a current odometer, an advert, a dossier view, a price suggestion.
   | **Inlet**, ingestion pipeline ([[ingestion-and-entity-resolution]]) | Data platform | Lands the corrected batch; checks `corrects_record_id`; holds segments |
   | **Tracewell**, history store ([[vehicle-history]]) | History team | Appends corrected versions, marks superseded or disputed ones; recomputes odometer judgements; emits `vehicle.fact.corrected` through its outbox |
   | **Retrace**, correction cases and impact | Data operations (Priya Raman) | Opens cases, runs the impact query, applies the per-class policy, tracks completion and residuals, assembles the audit pack |
   | **Vantage** and the valuation ledger ([[valuation-engine]]) | Valuation team | Re-values affected entries as linked successors; reports which ones led to actions |
   | **Report store** in Tracewell | History team | Supersedes report snapshots; triggers reissue notices |
   | **Lotwise Dossier**, advert composer, pricing ([[inventory-and-vehicle-dossier]], [[merchandising]], [[used-car-pricing]]) | Lotwise | Re-renders adverts, flags dealer text, re-proposes prices, notifies dealers |
   | **Public event gateway**, Charter, Meterline ([[events-webhooks-and-sync]], [[data-licensing-and-governance]], [[billing-and-metering]]) | Platform, governance, billing | Computes the external audience from the access audit; sends thin webhooks; credits re-fetches |
   | **Pulse** and insight files ([[market-data]], [[data-products-and-insights]]) | Market data | Recomputes affected statistics; restates frozen monthly releases |
   **An adapter that cannot answer reports a gap, never zero**: adverts rendered before October 2027 have no version edges, so their adapter queries by vehicle and date window and labels the answer *approximate*.
   **The event carries version ids, not values**: consumers re-read the fact through the owning API with its licence decision, and external subscribers get the same shape, filtered to vehicles they received ([[events-webhooks-and-sync]]).
   - **Consumers.** A paid history report is digital content.
   - **Personal data.** This book treats plates and VINs as personal data by default ([[privacy-and-retention]]).
   - **Data clients.** Axleby's terms with Rijnstaal and other API clients cap liability and describe a correction duty on both sides.
   | **Duplicate correction** | Supplier redelivers the redelivery; replay of the case | Idempotency on (source, record id, source version); notices keyed (case, artifact, recipient) | No new versions; no second notice.
   | **Conflicting corrections** | Two corrections of one record disagree, or the supplier's value contradicts an official reading | Two successors for one version; semantic check against official readings | Version becomes **disputed**; report wording says so; data operations decides with evidence, as the registers do |
   | **Incomplete correction** | Supplier corrects part of the batch, or cannot say which records were wrong | Case scope vs corrected count | Uncorrected versions stay disputed and excluded; the case stays open with a named waiver if they never resolve |
   | **Partial fan-out failure** | One consumer (Relay, the report store) fails mid-way | Completion per consumer in the case | Consumer replays from its topic offset; case cannot close while any consumer is incomplete |
   | **Adapter blind spot** | A store kept no version edges (pre-October 2027 adverts) | Adapter returns *approximate* | Query by vehicle and window; widen the residual sweep for that class |
   | **Residual copies** | Dealer text, PDFs, exports, a client's warehouse | Residual sweep: stale-content scan, search for superseded values in text fields | Flag to owner; for live adverts, block republication; record what cannot be reached |
   | **Recipient beyond reach** | Car sold; buyer has no relation with Axleby | Outcome link shows sale | Notice to the dealer with the corrected report; the dealer decides whether to tell the buyer |
   | **Erasure meets correction** | The person linked to an affected record was erased | Erasure ledger lookup ([[privacy-and-retention]]) | Correct the vehicle fact; never re-link the person to send a notice |
   | **Owning store down during fan-out** | Tracewell or Vantage unavailable | Backbone lag; case completion stalls | Events wait on topics; containment already holds; the gateway suppresses disputed attributes in the meantime |
   | **Dispute on pattern evidence** (recommended) | Stops spread within an hour | Some provisional values; a false alarm must be lifted | A batch-level pattern or an official contradiction exists |
   | **Write-time impact edges per store** (recommended) | Exact, fast, owned by each store | Every new consumer must record versions; storage | Facts feed issued, paid or relied-on artifacts |
   | **Thin notice, free re-fetch** (recommended) | Licence-safe; re-fetch is audited and credited | Client must act; two calls | Licensed data; data clients with varied rights |
   | **Detection latency** | Bad version recorded → case opened | 8 days | Batch errors at load; disputes within 1 business day |
   | **Containment time** | Case opened → versions disputed | Never contained | Under 1 hour |
   | **Exposure** | Artifacts built on the version, per day of exposure | ~560 valuations, 37 reports, 107 adverts a day | Zero for batch errors |
   | **Internal fan-out time** | Correction recorded → all internal consumers complete | 11 days, manual | Under 1 hour (dry run: 38 minutes) |
   | **Impact completeness** | Residuals found after closure ÷ impact set | 307 advert texts missed; 41 live after three weeks | Zero live residuals at closure |
   | **Client acknowledgement** | Notified clients that re-fetched or acknowledged within 7 days | Unknown | 90% *(assumption)*; rest escalated |
   | **Consumer reissue time** | Correction → corrected report sent | Weeks | Same business day |
   | **Evidence time** | Client asks "what did you tell us?" → per-call answer | 6 working days | Under 1 hour (Nov 2027 re-run: 40 minutes) |
   | **Official odometer source corrects itself** | RDW: last reading by the business, older by RDW within 10 working days.
   | **How Axleby learns of it** | Changed judgement or reading in register data and the snapshot diff | Supplier redelivery under contract | Changed test record in DVSA daily files *(inference: DVSA does not document how amendments appear)* | Title brand from providers of NMVTIS, the National Motor Vehicle Title Information System |
   | **What a correction can do** | Supersede a reading; recompute the judgement beside the register's own | Supersede commercial readings | Supersede; official wins over commercial | **Cannot remove** a title-level odometer brand; history must show both |
   | **Duty to tell recipients** | GDPR Art. 19 | GDPR Art. 19 | UK GDPR Art. 19 | No GDPR equivalent; California consumers have a right to correct under the California Consumer Privacy Act (CCPA) |
   | **Consumer remedy for a wrong report** | Digital Content Directive, national law | Same Directive | Consumer Rights Act 2015 | State consumer law; providers' buyback guarantees |
===== journey-lead-to-dms 46
   **Lead acknowledgement** is the evidence, from the system where people work, that a lead reached a person.
   **Dead-letter review** is the governed process for every lead that could not be routed, delivered or confirmed.
   | **Portal** (Marktplaats, AutoScout24, mobile.de) | The buyer's account, the conversation, the delivery contract, its own lead count | Where the lead goes after delivery |
   | **Axleby** (Switchboard, Baseline, tenant directory, Hitch) | Intake, matching, routing decisions, delivery and its evidence, the lead store, attribution | The CRM's queues, users and housekeeping |
   | **DMS vendor** (DriveDesk) | The CRM record, queues, work lists, its API's semantics | Which queue a lead should go to |
   | **Dealer group** (Velto) | Its organization, routing policy, CRM queue setup, the people who answer | The behavior of either vendor's software |
   **Lead acknowledgement** is evidence, produced by the system where people work, that a lead reached a person.
   | **A0 Accepted** | 2xx from the target's API; SMTP 250 for e-mail | The target's transport | None: the lead stays *Routed* | The target received the bytes |
   | **A1 Delivered** | Read-back: record id, queue id, queue active, queue in an active work list | Hitch adapter; Switchboard's own write for the Lotwise inbox | *Delivered* | A person *could* see it |
   | **A2 Acknowledged** | Assignment, open, or status change by a user | CRM activity via Hitch; inbox events | Timestamp on the lead | A person *did* see it |
   | **A3 Answered** | Outbound message or answered call by a person, not an auto-reply | Inbox, CRM activity, call tracking | *Contacted* | The buyer got an answer |
   | **Total** | **1,460** | |
   A **dead-letter review** is the governed handling of every lead that the journey could not route, deliver or confirm.
   | **Silent acceptance by the CRM** | H6–H7 | Read-back fails (no record, inactive queue, no work list); rooftop silence at H8 | Lead stays in shadow inbox; dead-letter case; re-drive to valid queue |
   | **Rooftop closed after restructuring** | H2–H4 | `rooftop.closed` event; rule validation; simulation before activation | Successor routing with an alert; rule marked invalid until fixed |
   | **Duplicate lead from two channels** | H3, H6 | Prospect link within the legal entity; xref shows an open CRM lead for the prospect and car | Second lead linked; delivered to the CRM as an activity on the first CRM lead, not as a new lead |
   | **Lead for a sold car** | H2 | Stock state Sold or Reserved at match time (from the nightly export in 2027) | Route normally with a "sold" banner and alternatives; raise a channel-state check in Relay |
   | **CRM outage** | H6 | Circuit breaker per CRM and rooftop in Hitch | Lotwise inbox becomes the working copy; on recovery, open leads delivered with their Lotwise history, closed ones as history only |
   | **Ambiguous timeout** | H6 | Create call times out with no response | Read before retry: search the CRM by Switchboard lead id in its external-reference field |
   | **Consent missing or unrepresentable** | H2, H6 | Normalizer finds no consent data; Hitch mapping test finds no consent field at the target | Deliver with marketing blocked by default; set the CRM's no-marketing flag where it exists |
   | **Follow-up before its lead** | H1–H3 | Follow-up references an unknown source lead id (Marktplaats does not guarantee order) | Park for 10 minutes *(assumption)*, then create the lead from the follow-up |
   - **Unordered delivery at intake.** Marktplaats gives no "guarantees on the specific order you will receive webhook messages in" and expects a response within 2 seconds ([Marktplaats, n.d.](https://api.marktplaats.nl/docs/v2/lms/index.html)).
   - **Outcome write-back as a contract.** mobile.de's Lead API (version 2026-06-25) lets the receiver post `IN_PROGRESS`, `SOLD`, `NOT_INTERESTED` or `SPAM` with an optional `occurredAt` "in case of asynchronous communication" ([mobile.de, 2026](https://services.mobile.de/manual/lead-api.html)).
   - **The CRM as part of the DMS.** In the June 2024 CDK Global outage, Sonic Automotive told the SEC that its CRM was among the affected systems ([Sonic Automotive 8-K, July 2024](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1043509/000104350924000060/sah-20240705.htm)).
   - **The journey as the unit of measurement.** In 2024 AutoScout24 described defining service level objectives on critical user interactions, including "Dealer is contacted by a Buyer about a car" ([AutoScout24, 2024](https://tech.autoscout24.com/blog/posts/slos-for-critical-user-interactions/)).
   - **The last hop fails too.** Pied Piper's 2025 study of 4,023 US dealerships found that 19% did not personally respond to a web enquiry within 24 hours ([Auto Remarketing, 2025](https://www.autoremarketing.com/ar/retail/dealerships-responsiveness-to-internet-leads-hits-record-high-again)).
   | **Delivery evidence** | Read-back per lead after create | Trust 2xx, rely on activity monitoring | The target has a queryable API and any lead loss is costly (always for paid portal leads) | The target cannot be queried (files, e-mail); then keep the platform inbox as the working copy |
   | **Shadow copy in CRM mode** | Always write a copy in the platform inbox | No copy; the CRM is the only store | A fallback must exist during outages and investigations | Never fully; at most hide the copy from salespeople to avoid two systems chasing one buyer |
   | **Invalid target at routing time** | Route to successor or fallback and alert | Hold the lead until the rule is fixed | Always: the buyer must be answered today | Never for leads; holding is acceptable only for marketing exports |
   | **Late recovery** | Redeliver to the dealer's queue, deduplicated, with context | Hand the dealer a list | The dealer's CRM is where its people work and the batch can be marked | The batch is small and the dealer prefers manual triage |
   | **Silence alert** | Per rooftop, 4 business hours | Per lead, minutes | Groups with BDCs and works councils; few false alarms | Single-rooftop dealers who ask for a personal nudge, as an opt-in |
   | **After-hours branch** | Platform routes to the group contact center | CRM routes after hours | The group has several CRMs or rooftops on different systems | One CRM per group with its own scheduling |
   | **Source-count match** | Leads per rooftop and source in Switchboard ÷ the portal's own count, where the portal exposes one | ≥ 99.5% daily | 99.7% |
   | **Delivered with read-back** | Leads reaching A1 within 2 minutes | ≥ 99% | 99.4% |
   | **Fallback share** | Leads that reached a person only through a fallback inbox | Watched for trend; under 1% outside outages | 0.4% |
   | **Rooftop silence** | Rooftop-hours with leads arriving and no A2 for 4 business hours | Zero | 0 alerts |
   | **Time to first human response** | Median arrival → A3, inside and outside opening hours | Set by the dealer | 31 min inside / 2.1 h outside (contact center) |
   | **Duplicate CRM leads** | CRM leads for a prospect and car that already had an open CRM lead | Under 1 per 1,000 | 0.6 per 1,000 |
   | **Dead-letter age** | Age of the oldest open case | Under 1 business day | 6 hours |
   | **Won with a lead** | Share of retail sales with a linked lead | Tracked; drives attribution | 64% |
   **Source-count match** is the only check made by a party outside the journey.
   **Time to first human response** is split between the platform's segment and the dealer's, so a slow week is attributed to the right party.
   | **Netherlands / Belgium** | The baseline above: Marktplaats and 2dehands push webhooks, DriveDesk via Hitch.
   | **Germany** | mobile.de leads are pulled with a cursor, so H1 latency is the poll interval, and a lost cursor means replay rather than loss.
   | **United Kingdom** | Showroomly keeps its own lead inbox until it moves onto shared leads in 2028.
   | **United States** | Axleby has no US customers.
===== journey-sale-to-registration 27
   - **The register had registered the Niro in her name at 14:31.** From that moment she, not Brandsma, was the keeper on record, responsible for road tax, insurance and periodic inspection, for a car standing on Brandsma's lot.
   - **The "rejection" belonged to a second submission.** Axleby's worker had resent the message after a 30-second timeout.
   | **Commercial** | Deal service and stock item | Signed, finance pending, invoiced; reserved, sold, delivered | What has been agreed and paid? |
   | **Channel** | Relay, per channel listing | Live, withdrawing, withdrawn, blocked, locked | What does the market still see? |
   | **Legal** | Trade ledger, per register act | Draft, blocked, queued, submitted, ambiguous, confirmed, rejected, manual | Who does the register name, and since when? |
   - **Lane priority comes from the hand-over, not the click.** Since June 2027 every Sales Admin deal carries a collection time, and DMS-originated sales get one from a field the dealer fills when starting the registration.
   | **At signing** | Earliest legal certainty; longest margin before hand-over | The buyer is keeper of a car they have not paid for or collected; cancellations need a reverse act | Never as the default; perhaps for business buyers who prepay |
   | **At hand-over, with the buyer present** | No act for deals that fall through at the last minute | Every register delay is a delay at the desk; month-end peaks land at the counter | Low volume, or markets where the act is quick and rarely fails |
   | **By dealer click** | The dealer decides timing | Forgotten registrations; no link from deal to act | Only as a fallback; measured by sold-but-not-registered |
   | **Hold the car; buyer returns** | Hand-over blocked by the legal line; buyer messaged when confirmed | Lost goodwill, a second trip, a car on the lot that may already be the buyer's |
   | **Hand over with the act queued** | Buyer drives away; dealer trusts the queue | The car is still in the dealer's trade stock: the buyer drives a car the register does not name them for, and the dealer remains responsible for it.
   | **Hand over on paper, register later by the buyer** | Dealer issues documents for the buyer to register at a counter, where the market allows | Re-typing, no proof in the dossier, and the act may fail days later |
   | **Signature to keys** | Contract signed → *delivered*, per rooftop, median and 90th percentile | Reported per rooftop; no group target *(assumption)* |
   | **Sold to registered** | *Stock item sold* → register act Confirmed, Dutch rooftops | 95% within 15 minutes on working days *(assumption)* |
   | **Sold, not registered** | Sold stock items at Dutch rooftops with no register act and no exemption reason after 2 working days | Alert per rooftop; the forgotten-click detector |
   | **Reserved but live** | Reserved or sold cars with a channel listing not Withdrawn | 99% withdrawn within 10 minutes; alarm at 30 |
   | **Ambiguous outcomes** | Count per day and time to resolution | 41 on 30 June 2027, all resolved by probe |
   | **Contradictions** | Sold car whose register act is Rejected for a state reason | Zero tolerated; each one goes to Manual |
   | **Blocked on hand-over day** | Acts Blocked within 24 hours of the collection time | Trend to zero as validation moves to signing |
   | **Fee integrity** | Confirmed acts without exactly one fee line, and fee lines without a Confirmed act | Zero before the invoice run |
   | **Proof delivered** | Confirmed stock-ins whose release certificate reached the seller the same day | Tracked since June 2027 |
   | **History lag** | Confirmed act → ownership event in Tracewell, and → observed in open data | Seconds and days; both published |
   | **Netherlands** | The recognized dealer, through a provider or directly | The register's confirmation, synchronously | The hand-over gate on a Confirmed act works as described; no completion during a disruption |
   | **Belgium** | Commonly the buyer's insurer or broker requests the registration (not verified from a primary source); Axleby's Belgian acts run through a partner | Often only that the request was made | The seller must give a private buyer a Car-Pass, the odometer-history document; without it the buyer may have the sale annulled ([Car-Pass, n.d.](https://www.car-pass.be/en/faq)).
   | **Germany** | Local registration offices; registration services or the buyer; large customers through the KBA's machine interface, whose requests can be *forwarded* or *escalated* for manual handling ([KBA, 2024](https://www.landkreis-eichstaett.de/fileadmin/Dateien/Website/Dateien/SG_52_Verkehrswesen_KFZ/Informationsbroschuere_GKS.pdf)) | Usually nothing, unless the dealer or its service transacts | The legal line
   | **United Kingdom** | Keeper change notified to the DVLA (Driver and Vehicle Licensing Agency) online by keeper or motor trader | Online confirmation, if the dealer notifies | Vehicle tax does not transfer, and the buyer "must tax a vehicle you've bought before you drive it" ([GOV.UK, n.d.](https://www.gov.uk/sold-bought-vehicle)); the hand-over checklist adds the buyer's tax, and a 30-day right to reject keeps the 
   | **United States** | Dealer or service provider at the state DMV (Department of Motor Vehicles); lenders through electronic lien and title where states offer it ([California DMV, n.d.](https://www.dmv.ca.gov/portal/vehicle-industry-services/electronic-lien-and-title-program/)) | Varies by state; title often follows weeks later *(industry practice)* | Odometer disclosure travels on the title ([49 CFR 580.5](https://w

===== build-buy-or-partner 49
   | **Build** | Own team writes and runs it | The platform | The platform | Anchor, Tracewell, Vantage, Switchboard, Meterline, Gatekeeper's model, Charter |
   | **Adopt** | Run an open-source product in-house | The platform | The platform | Keycloak for Axleby ID; OpenFGA under Gatekeeper; PostgreSQL |
   | **Buy** | Use a vendor's software or SaaS for a function | Often the vendor, as processor | The platform, which cannot pass its customer duties to a processor | Datadog, Snowflake, Vertex AI, a tax-calculation service, a damage-detection API |
   | **License** | Pay for data under a contract that limits use | The supplier owns it; the platform holds a licensed copy | Shared: the platform for how it uses the data, the supplier for what it warrants | Build data, catalogs, international damage records, Kilometra readings, repair-cost catalogs |
   | **Partner** | Another business performs a service for a shared customer | Each party holds its part | Each party for its own act | DMS vendors, portals, auction houses, Merelstad Auto Finance (the lender), the Belgian registration partner |
   **Acquire** is a sixth route, used for Lotwise (2019), Wertlauf (2021) and Showroomly (2023); it turns sourcing into integration ([[one-platform-from-many-products]]).
   - **Sourcing decision.** A recorded choice of one of the options above for one capability, with the requirement that drove it, the assumptions under which it holds, what the platform keeps in-house to make an exit possible, the review trigger, and the decision owner.
   - **Data-rights test.** The set of questions a licensed data source, or a vendor that receives the platform's data, must answer satisfactorily before it is contracted.
   | **Supply** | Who can produce this fact or service at all? | Coverage lists; register terms; manufacturer access rules |
   | **Data rights** | What may we store, derive, show and resell? | The licence, tested clause by clause (below) |
   | **Judgement and liability** | Will a customer hold us responsible for the answer? | Contracts, product promises (no-hit-no-fee, guarantees), consumer law |
   | **Differentiation** | Do customers choose us for this, measurably? | Win/loss reasons, retention by module, pricing power |
   | **Unit cost curve** | Cost per unit at today's and the forecast volume | Supplier price, fixed team cost, crossover volume *(assumption recorded)* |
   | **Lock-in and exit** | What does leaving cost, and what must we own to leave? | Key dependence, data return terms, switching rights, integration size |
   | **Regulatory duty** | Which duty can we not hand over? | GDPR controller role, register terms, the Digital Operational Resilience Act (DORA) for financial clients, the Data Act |
   | **Dependency** | Which journeys stop if it fails? | Dependency tier per journey ([[reliability-and-dependencies]]) |
   - **The canonical model is not the supplier's model.** Specbook stores catalog, as-built and register facts as separate sourced layers ([[specification-and-equipment]]), not the provider's version codes as the vehicle's identity.
   - **Evidence and outcomes stay in-house.** A detection vendor's output enters Gauge as an unconfirmed finding with a model version ([[condition-and-damage]]).
   - **Every paid call is metered for cost as well as revenue** ([[billing-and-metering]]), so the Gate 4 crossover is a query, not an annual guess.
   | **Resell a provider's finished report** | The provider's events, judgement, wording and guarantee | Gate 3: Axleby cannot explain or correct the judgement; the No-Hit and wording rules are the provider's | A market where Axleby has no own sources and sells few reports |
   | **License events, own the event store and judgements** (Axleby's choice) | Raw events under storage rights; Tracewell judges | Needs the data-rights test per source | Axleby's markets, where it holds register data and first-party readings |
   | **Build sources** (own network of workshops and insurers) | Direct contributions | Gate 4: years of partner acquisition | Only as a long-term extension of first-party data |
   | **License guide values for all products** | ≈ €2.3M at an assumed €0.12 per value × 19M issued valuations; no European provider publishes per-call prices | Accepted by lenders and insurers; no model team | Redistribution limits to API clients; no single guide covers Axleby's four markets; drift response is the supplier's |
   | **Own models with guides as inputs** (Axleby's choice) | ≈ €1.8M for a Munich model and model-operations team of about 14 *(assumption)* plus compute | Uses Axleby's outcome data; explainable lines; segment circuit breakers | Liability for errors; needs a ledger, back-testing and governance |
   | **Integrate a partner's valuations per market** | Per dealer licence | Matches dealer expectations where one provider dominates | No control; values not resellable |
   **Owning the model was a bet on owning outcomes**: Lotwise sales, Tradefloor deals and register sale events.
   | **Image capture and evidence store** | Build (Gauge, Lotwise Inspect) | Evidence is Axleby's and the tenant's; capture rules are per purpose |
   | **Damage detection model** | Buy, behind the detection gateway | Fast-moving vendor market; no public dataset large enough to train a competitive model (CarDD has 4,000 images, 2022) |
   | **Repair-cost estimate** | License a repair-cost catalog | Manufacturer parts and labor times are only available from catalog providers; insurers and courts accept them |
   | **Findings, rubrics, recon estimates and ground truth** | Build | The lessee charge and the trade grade are Axleby's statements; confirmed findings measure any vendor |
   | **Build a full CRM** | Groups wanting one vendor | A team of 20 or more for years *(assumption)*; competes with brand-mandated CRMs franchised dealers cannot drop | Reject |
   | **White-label a CRM vendor inside Lotwise** | Independents wanting more than an inbox | Second routing engine; vendor's data rights over Axleby's leads; revenue share | Decline; revisit only for a vendor that consumes Switchboard routing |
   | **Build the inbox and router; deliver to any CRM** (Axleby's choice) | Both segments | Hitch adapters per CRM family; read-back | **Keep** |
   - **Portals.** Relay connects to 61 channels; [[listing-syndication]] keeps adapters in-house for channels carrying more than a few percent of a market's leads and accepts aggregators for the long tail only where read-back and reconciliation survive.
   - **Auctions.** Tradefloor hands about 11,500 cars a year to two auction partners ([[b2b-trade-and-remarketing]]); VWE made the same move in 2026: Autorola completed its acquisition of VWE's auction subsidiary ATC on 1 February 2026 ([Journal de l'Automobile, 2026](https://journalauto.com/distribution/autorola-reprend-une-filiale-de-vwe-automotive/)), and VWE's product page still shows auctions "from partner Automoti
   - **Lending.** Merelstad Auto Finance pays a referral share of about €180 per paid-out contract.
   | **Adopt Keycloak, self-hosted** (Axleby's choice) | ≈ €0.4M for about 2.5 engineers in Marta Nowak's platform team plus infrastructure | Organizations feature fully supported since Keycloak 26 (October 2024) ([Keycloak, 2024](https://www.keycloak.org/2024/10/keycloak-2600-released)); EU hosting; no per-user price | **Keep** |
   | **Supplier outage** | Adapter error rates; journey SLOs with upstream failures attributed ([[reliability-and-dependencies]]) | Degraded answer defined in the sourcing record (catalog-level spec, stored history events, inbox fallback, DMS-absent mode) |
   | **Price rise at renewal** | Renewal calendar 12 months ahead; cost per path in Meterline | Second-source pilot started before renewal; exit cost known from the record |
   | **Supplier acquired, possibly by a competitor** | Watch list of suppliers' ownership | Review data rights clause "change of control"; Autotelex data sits inside VWE's API while Autotelex also competes with VWE, and the ATC sale turned a VWE subsidiary into a partner |
   | **Licence terms tightened or terminated** | Charter rule change; supplier notice | Purge by lineage: every fact carries its licence ref, so the affected facts and derived outputs are findable; re-derive from other sources |
   | **Duplicate or concurrent purchases** | Purchases per distinct VIN above 1.0; reconciliation against supplier statements | Single-flight broker; duplicates claimed as credits ([[scale-and-cost]]) |
   | **Billed for incomplete answers** | Result class per paid call (as-built, catalog-only, no hit, error) | Coverage check before buying; contract clause on catalog-only billing |
   | **Partner reports success but does not act** | Read-back of owner and queue; reconciliation | Fallback channel; incident with partner; contract remedy |
   | **Vendor quality drifts** (detection, catalog) | Confirmation and rejection rates on own ground truth | Route by confidence; switch vendor at the gateway |
   - **Upstream cost share** per product and market, and **supplier concentration**: suppliers whose loss would stop a tier-1 journey.
   - **Exit readiness**: for each top-ten supplier, the time to switch, tested by a drill or a second-source pilot at least once per contract term.
   - **Repurchase rate**: paid fetches per distinct vehicle; a rise means a cache, licence or broker regression.
   - **Vendor quality on own ground truth**: confirmation rates for detection, catalog-only rates for build data, read-back failures for partners.
===== one-platform-from-many-products 41
   | **Front door** | Brand, navigation, a portal or menu that links products | One website and one support number; still several logins and invoices | A group portal with links to each product |
   | **Linked identity** | Authentication; a central login linked to per-product accounts | One sign-in; permissions and records still per product | Cox Automotive's Bridge ID (rollout 2020–2021) |
   | **Shared kernel** | One identity and tenant directory, one vehicle key, one meter, one licence check, consumed by every product | One login, one customer, one invoice per legal entity, one dossier per car | Axleby's One Dossier Year 1 |
   | **Shared capabilities** | Whole business capabilities used by several products: lead routing, syndication, configuration, register transactions | One behavior across products; brand-specific screens | Showroomly moving onto Switchboard and Relay 2 |
   | **Replatformed** | One product absorbs another's customers; the other is retired | A migration, then one product | Lotwise absorbing Axleby's earlier dealer portal in 2019 |
   **The shared kernel** is the smallest set of capabilities whose answers every product must take from one place: **who** (person, organization, legal entity, rooftop), **which car** (the vehicle key), **what was used** (the usage event) and **whether it was allowed** (entitlement, authority and licence).
   - **Link.** The product keeps its own record but stores the kernel's key beside it: a Lotwise login linked to an Axleby ID person, a stock item linked to an AVK (Axleby Vehicle Key, Anchor's opaque vehicle identifier), a billing account linked to a tenant-directory legal entity.
   - **Migrate.** The kernel becomes the source of truth and the product reads from it.
   - **Retire.** The product's own mechanism is switched off and deleted: the Lotwise password table, the plate cache, the stored procedure that billed reports.
   **The retirement date is the milestone that matters**: only retirement ends double running and the drift between the two.
   | **People** | Product logins | Axleby ID persons in an organization | Customer identity provider (IdP) with SCIM provisioning, verified e-mail | A shared login mapped to one person, so register transactions are misattributed |
   | **Customers** | Product account and billing records | Tenant-directory organization, legal entity, rooftop | Company registration number, DMS site code, portal account id, direct-debit mandate | Two legal entities merged because they share a VAT number or a trading name |
   | **Vehicles** | Stock ids, plates, report and transaction references | AVK in Anchor | VIN confirmed by the register; plate as of the observation date | A plate-keyed fact attached to the wrong car |
   | **Brand, screens and workflow** | Lotwise's independents and Showroomly's UK groups work differently; one screen for both would serve neither ([[tenancy-and-dealer-groups]]) |
   | **Release cadence** | Lotwise ships monthly; the data APIs change in April and October releases with notice ([[vehicle-data-apis]]) |
   | **External API contracts** | Data clients pin dated versions; a dealer product's internal changes must not reach them |
   | **Market-specific rules** | UK registration, VAT and portal integrations differ from Dutch ones; they live in market packs and adapters ([[onboarding-and-configuration]]) |
   | **Consumer identity** | Odolo's consumers are not business users, have different consent and retention, and stay in their own account store |
   | **People** (Axleby ID) | Linking open; Velto federated | Hartley pilot only | Portal linking open; JSON API v2 clients on OAuth | Through Axleby Portal linking | Out of scope (consumers) |
   | **Customers** (tenant directory) | All rooftops linked | Linked where Companies House matched | Linked | Linked | Business referral partners only |
   | **Vehicle key** (AVK) | Retired plate key: every stock item has an AVK | Own stock id plus plate (2028) | JSON API on AVK; Connect XML plate-keyed until facade | AVK per transaction since June 2027 | Paid reports on AVK; free check reads the plate mirror |
   | **Meter** (Meterline) | Shadow-rated since October 2027 | Not started (SaaS billing tool; last in order) | Shadow-rated since October 2027 | Fees on register confirmation since June 2027 | Not started |
   | **Licence check** (Charter) | Through the Tracewell assembler | Not applicable to its own data | Every external call since December 2027 | Not applicable | Through the Tracewell assembler |
   | **Events** (backbone) | Outbox from the first quarter of 2028 | 2028–29 | No events from Connect XML | 2028 | 2028 |
   - **Two capabilities added.** **A13 Upstream data sourcing and cost control** (supplier contracts, the build-data budget broker and cost per lookup) became a capability after the Saturday Batch of May 2027 showed that one client's batch could cost more in build-data fees than it earned ([[scale-and-cost]]).
   - **One capability merged.** **D2 File enrichment** folded into **D1 Vehicle data APIs**: JSON API v2's batch lane (`/v2/lookup-jobs`) serves enrichment files through the same composer, licence check and meter as single lookups, so a separate capability would have meant a separate meter.
   - **One scope change.** **A9 Condition and damage estimation** moved from workflow half to shared, because Gauge's findings now feed Vantage's valuations and Tradefloor's buyer claims as well as Lotwise ([[condition-and-damage]]).
   - **Three capabilities drawn at level 3**, as v1 anticipated, where parts differ in class or owner: **A1 Register access** (open-data plate lookup, commodity; validated mirror, differentiating; live status calls, differentiating); **A8 Vehicle valuation** (models and segment monitoring, differentiating; serving and valuation ledger, parity); **F2 Authorization and entitlements** (relationships in Gatekeeper, entitlem
   The **five kernel capabilities** are A2 vehicle identity resolution, F1 accounts, organizations and tenancy, F2 authorization and entitlements, F4 usage metering and billing and F5 data licensing and purpose governance.
   **B2B trade exchange became red**: the Twice-Sold Returns of June 2027, when 17 of Fleetmoor's cars were sold twice through Tradefloor and an auction partner, has an approved fix (the sale-right register) that was not yet live ([[b2b-trade-and-remarketing]]).
   | **Day 0** | Inventory: logins, customer records, vehicle keys, billing paths, data sources and their licences, event and integration paths, security baseline; placed on the capability map | All |
   | **Day 90** | Customers linked to the tenant directory; usage events emitted to Meterline in shadow; every external release of vehicle data checked by Charter; telemetry tagged with organization, rooftop and client ids | F1, F4, F5, F6 |
   | **Month 12** | Users sign in through Axleby ID; every vehicle record published to other products carries an AVK; Meterline rates the product's usage; no new tenant-conditional code | F1, F2, A2, F4, F3 |
   | **Month 24** | Shared capabilities adopted where the map marks the product's own version as a duplicate; own copies retired on a dated plan | Tier S, duplicated tier H |
   | **Always** | The brand, its screens and its market rules may stay; separability kept (per-brand data partitions, billing entities and tenant export) | — |
   **Detection** is a standing duplicate report per mapping (the duplicate-tenant backlog of [[tenancy-and-dealer-groups]] is one); **recovery** is a reviewed merge with a tombstone that redirects the old key, never a delete.
   | **Front door only** | One brand and entry point | Logins, customers, keys, invoices, data | Low; fast | A holding structure that may sell brands; products with no shared customers |
   | **Linked identity** (Cox's documented first step) | Sign-in | Permissions, customer records, keys, meters | Low to medium; months | First year after an acquisition; many products that must keep shipping |
   | **Shared kernel** (Axleby Year 1) | Customer, person, vehicle key, meter, licence | Workflows and screens | Medium; one to two years; kernel becomes tier 0 | Products sell to the same customers and about the same cars |
   | **Shared kernel plus shared capabilities** (Axleby Years 2–3) | Duplicate lead, syndication and configuration engines | Brand surfaces, market rules | Medium to high; years | Capabilities behave the same across markets apart from configuration |
   | **Full replatforming** onto one product | Everything, eventually | Nothing; the other product is retired | High; customer migration risk and churn | Products overlap almost entirely in market and function, and the absorbing product already serves the market |
===== organizing-engineering 34
   | **Stream-aligned** | "Aligned to a flow of work from (usually) a segment of the business domain" | A dealer journey (appraise, stock, price, advertise, lead, sell, register) or a data-client journey (integrate, call, reconcile an invoice) |
   | **Platform** | "A grouping of other team types that provide a compelling internal product to accelerate delivery by Stream-aligned teams" | Runtime, event backbone, identity and tenancy, configuration, metering, ingestion, register gateway |
   | **Complicated subsystem** | "Where significant mathematics/calculation/technical expertise is needed" | Vehicle identity resolution, valuation models |
   | **Enabling** | "Helps a Stream-aligned team to overcome obstacles.
   A **data-product team** is a stream-aligned team whose stream is a vehicle-data domain (specification, history, market data) and whose customers are internal products and external data clients.
   The **journey owner** is the stream-aligned team that owns a dealer or data-client outcome end to end ("a sold car is withdrawn from every channel within 30 minutes", "a lead reaches a person who acknowledges it").
   The **dependency owner** is the team that owns one external party: a portal, a register channel, a dealer management system (DMS) family, a data supplier.

   - **The promise.** Coverage, accuracy, freshness and the wording of uncertainty per market ([[provenance-and-data-quality]]).
   - **The terms.** With Femke de Boer's governance team, the licence conditions under which the product may be served, cached and combined ([[data-licensing-and-governance]]).
   - **The price and the cost.** With Lena Vos's billing team and the scale-and-cost review, the stock-keeping unit (SKU) on the price list, no-hit rules and the fully loaded cost per call ([[scale-and-cost]]).

   | **By acquired brand or product** | Fast after an acquisition; customer continuity; clear revenue accountability | Reproduces the brands in the architecture: separate logins, keys, metering (**The Acquisition Archipelago**) | The first 12–24 months after an acquisition *(assumption)*, or when the group intends to keep the products separate or sell them |
   | **By technical layer** (front end, back end, data, operations) | Deep specialist skills; easy hiring | Every feature crosses three or four teams; nobody owns an outcome | Rarely; only for very small organizations |
   | **By journey and domain** (recommended) | Owners for outcomes and for data domains; shared platforms; fewer hand-offs | Needs strong platform products and clear interaction modes; acquired teams must change mission | A group that has decided to become one platform, with enough engineers to staff platform teams (roughly 150 or more *(assumption)*) |
   | **Matrix** (brand product lines plus functional platform group) | Keeps commercial lines; builds shared services | Two reporting lines; priorities fought out between them | A transition state lasting a defined period, with a date for leaving it |

   | **On-call as working time** | EU Working Time Directive as interpreted in *Matzak* (2018) and C-344/19 (2021); Belgian implementing rules not verified here | Same EU case law | [Working Time Regulations 1998](https://www.legislation.gov.uk/uksi/1998/1833), which implemented the EU directive; post-Brexit treatment of the EU case law not verified here | Fair Labor Standards Act: on call is working time if the employe
   | **Co-determination over schedules and monitoring** | Dutch works council consent for systems suitable for monitoring staff ([WOR art. 27](https://wetten.overheid.nl/BWBR0002747/)) | Works council co-determination over working-hours schedules and over "technical devices designed to monitor the behaviour or performance of the employees" ([BetrVG §87(1) nos. 2 and 6](https://www.gesetze-im-internet.de/englisch_betrvg/
===== modernizing-the-platform 60
   - **Connect XML**: a SOAP/XML API and report back end built in 2006 on .NET Framework and SQL Server, in a colocated data center in Utrecht.
   - **Lotwise Classic**: a PHP/Symfony monolith on MySQL from 2009, moved to AWS eu-west-1 in 2020.
   | **Legacy wrong** | 64% | Connect XML sent Vantage an edition string derived from the register's model name, so the car landed in the wrong valuation segment; its own nightly register tables were days stale on 6 of the 28 nights |
   | **New wrong** | 21% | The composer dropped dealer-entered options for two makes; imported cars took the Dutch registration date instead of first registration abroad, so they looked younger |
   | **Policy undecided** | 15% | Nobody had decided whether a damage record in the history should lower the trade value automatically, or how mileage was rounded |
   - **inputs are pinned**: both paths see the same fact versions, the same valuation model version and the same upstream data, bought once;
   - **comparison rules are per output kind**: exact for identity and register fields, a tolerance band for valuations, set comparison by event id for history reports;
   - **side effects are never doubled**: register transactions, channel publications, lead deliveries, invoices and consumer payments are produced by one path only.
   | **Parallel run** | Both paths on the same request | Nobody until cut-over | Read-only answers: lookups, valuations, reports, specification |
   | **Shadow mode** | New path computes without acting | Internal reviewers | Invoices, pricing proposals, routing decisions |
   | **Dry run** | New path prepares; old path acts | Internal reviewers | Register transactions, channel publications |
   | **Canary** | New path serves a small cohort | That cohort | After a clean parallel run, for latency and load |
   | **Writer flip** | One path writes, per rooftop | Everyone in the cohort | Moving a system of record |
   | **Dual writes from application code** | Classic code writes MySQL, then calls the new service | No new infrastructure | Lost or reordered writes on crashes and deploys; two writers disagree silently | Rejected: the same pattern lost 83 sold events in October 2027 ([[events-webhooks-and-sync]]) |
   | **Change data capture (CDC) into a read model, then a writer flip** | Debezium reads Classic's MySQL binary log into the new service's read model; ownership switches per rooftop | No Classic code changes before the flip; the read model can be compared nightly; rollback per rooftop | Read model is shaped by legacy columns until the flip; binlog retention and schema changes must be managed | **Chosen** |
   | **Outbox events only** | Classic emits domain events from its outbox; the new service builds state from them | Clean contract | Needs every state change in Classic to emit a complete event first; history before the outbox is missing | Used for consumers, not for the migration itself |
   The **ownership router** is a per-rooftop setting in Baseline, the tenant configuration service ([[onboarding-and-configuration]]), with a version number.
   - **Legacy wrong**: the new answer is right.
   - **New wrong**: fix the new path and rerun the affected sample.
   - **Policy undecided**: no written rule existed.

   | **Named owner per client** | Every XML-only client has an Axleby owner (account manager for the commercial plan, engineer for the 14 large ones) | A list without names does not get worked through |
   | **Migration support** | Free v2 sandbox and comparison tool that replays a client's last 30 days of XML requests against v2 and shows field differences | The client sees its own differences before it writes code |
   | **Signals** | `Deprecation` and `Sunset` headers on every SOAP response from 2028; monthly usage reports per client; account-manager contact at 12, 6 and 3 months | SOAP clients rarely log HTTP headers, so people carry the message |
   | **Brownouts** | Scheduled failures in 2029: 1 hour on 11 September, 4 hours on 16 October, 24 hours from 13 November; dates published 90 days ahead | Finds forgotten integrations whose owners have left |
   | **Price** | From 1 July 2029, XML calls are rated at v2 prices plus 20% | Makes the last six months cost more for the client than migrating |
   | **Extension** | Paid extended support until 30 June 2030 at most, only with a signed migration plan and a test date | Prevents an open-ended second life |
   | **Switch-off evidence** | Per client: no calls for 30 days, or written confirmation; per operation: zero calls for 60 days | Usage data, not the calendar, closes each line |
   - **Cox Automotive** announced in April 2018 that it was going "all in" on AWS, migrating most of its brands and closing more than 40 data centers, and "modernising monoliths" with containers, functions and Aurora ([Amazon, 2018](https://press.aboutamazon.com/2018/4/cox-automotive-goes-all-in-on-aws)).
   - **Auto Trader UK** decided in 2018 to move to public cloud, chose Google Cloud and ran about a third of its services there by early 2019 ([Google Cloud, 2019](https://cloud.google.com/blog/products/application-development/how-the-uks-largest-automotive-marketplace-is-innovating-with-istio-on-google-kubernetes-engine)).
   - **Carfax** moved its blog to AWS in 2017, then its home page, used-car listings and research pages, first splitting traffic 50/50 with on-premises, according to a June 2023 vendor post ([Solo.io, 2023](https://solo.io/blog/monolith-microservices-collaboration-solo-aws)).
   **The data core moves last** is a recurring shape.
   - **CarGurus** decided in early 2019 to decompose its monolith and adopted the strangler fig in the second half of that year.
   - **CDK Global**'s Fortellis site, read in October 2026, links to migration guides from the older Partner Program for software vendors and dealers.
   | **Two writers for one record** | A stale Lotwise screen or a DMS stock import writes to Classic after the rooftop flipped | Classic rejects writes whose owner version is not current; rejected-write counter per rooftop | The write is retried against the Dossier service by the router; the import adapter in Hitch routes by owner |
   | **CDC gap or lag** | Debezium connector stalls past binlog retention during a Classic schema change | Age of the newest applied binlog position; nightly checksum per rooftop | Re-snapshot the affected rooftops; schema changes in Classic reviewed by the migration team until the last flip |
   | **Conflicting updates across the flip** | A price change queued in Classic before the flip lands after it | Owner version in the event; Dossier service rejects events stamped with an older version | Dealer sees the change as "not applied" in the timeline and repeats it |
   | **Incomplete legacy records** | Stock items without an AVK (0.7% before the backfill) or without reconditioning cost | Migration validation per field | Migrate as explicit `unknown`, never as a default value; the Margin-Scheme Error came from a default ([[deals-and-sales-documents]]) |
   | **Duplicate requests through the facade** | An XML client retries a timed-out call without a new reference | Facade derives the request reference from the client reference element | One Meterline event per reference; the No-Hit Credit's retry billing cannot recur |
   | **Partial answers the old format cannot express** | A history block is partial because a damage source timed out; Connect XML had no partial state | Facade mapping in Folio's frozen serializations | An optional status element added under the freeze's exception rule, announced as a behavior change; the client can still ignore it |
   | **External outage during a cut-over** | The register's transaction channel or a DMS fails during a wave | Journey service-level objectives ([[reliability-and-dependencies]]) | Freeze windows; waves pause automatically when a tier-1 dependency is degraded |
   | **Shadow path leaks** | Shadow valuations billed, shown or logged as deliveries | Meterline events tagged `shadow`; Charter audit category | Shadow results never written to the valuation ledger of record |
   | **Big-bang rewrite** | Build the replacement to feature parity, switch everyone at once | One cut-over; no transitional code | Parity is unknowable in advance; the cut-over risk is the whole business | Small systems with few users and a tolerable outage |
   | **Lift and shift** | Move the same code to new infrastructure | Fast; removes a data center | Changes nothing about ownership or coupling (Lotwise Classic moved to AWS in 2020 and stayed a monolith) | A data center exit is the only goal |
   | **Strangler with writer flips** (Axleby, Lotwise) | Move one system of record at a time behind seams | Value early; rollback per unit; differences found on real traffic | Years of transitional architecture; needs discipline to finish | The system of record is live, large and owned by one team |
   | **Facade and freeze** (Axleby, Connect XML) | Serve the old interface from the new core; stop changing it | One truth immediately; clients move on their own schedule | Frozen quirks must be reproduced; the facade itself needs a sunset | Many external clients the provider cannot force |
   | **Keep it forever behind a facade** | No client lost | Old credentials, no new blocks, a permanent second contract | A few high-value clients and a cheap facade |
   | **Sunset with policy** (Axleby) | Predictable for clients; evidence decides each switch-off | Expensive in account management; some long-tail churn | Hundreds of clients and a contract that permits notice |
   | **Hard cut-off** | Short | Breaks clients on the provider's schedule; disputes | Few clients, or a security reason that overrides the commercial one |
   | **Legacy API client base** | Most of Connect XML's 210 XML-only clients; garage-software vendors with SOAP integrations | Wertlauf's data clients, served from Vantage's API rather than Connect XML (Axleby illustration) | Showroomly has no legacy data API; its migration is identity and syndication | Axleby not present; CDK's Partner Program and Fortellis show an interface owner moving integrators (no public timetabl
   | **Register operations during a cut-over** | Dutch register transactions are certificate-based and cannot be submitted twice; dry run only.
   | **Invoicing cut-over constraints** | Belgium: structured B2B e-invoices (Peppol-based) mandatory since 1 January 2026, so the new invoice service must issue them from day one; Netherlands: no domestic B2B mandate found ([[billing-and-metering]]) | E-invoice issuing required from 2027 above €800,000 turnover and from 2028 for all, so the rating and invoicing move must handle EN 16931 | Bacs Direct Debit mandates mov
   | **Public migration evidence** | None found for Dutch or Belgian vendors | Kleinanzeigen (Adevinta) described a Debezium-to-Kafka migration pipeline at 3,000 events per second (January 2025) | Auto Trader's 2018–2024 cloud move | Cox (2018 AWS), Carfax (2017 onward), CarGurus (2019 onward) |

   **Not every automation is AI**: Velto's repricing rule `vel-core` in [[used-car-pricing]] is a versioned rule, and easier to govern for it.
   - **Principal.** The named person the agent acts for: a Velto buyer, Sietse Brandsma.
   - **Agent.** A registered client in Axleby ID, Axleby's identity platform, with its own client id, and a **version** that bundles the model, the prompt template and the tool manifest.
   - **Delegation.** The token by which the agent acts for the principal.
   - **Envelope.** The written limits on tier-3 actions: which actions, which segments, maximum amount, maximum count per day, maximum open exposure, expiry, and the person who signed it.
   | **Agent runtime** | The agent loop: reading events, calling tools, assembling proposals; one run per principal and task | Any business state; any credential beyond the run's delegated token |
   | **Model gateway** | Calls to foundation models through EU-region endpoints; prompt and model version pinning; redaction; per-tenant cost metering; Charter purpose checks on what enters a prompt | Which data a tool may return (that is the product's gates) |
   | **Tool layer** | Typed tools in three classes: read, propose, commit; each tool a thin wrapper over an existing product API or command | Business rules; tools never bypass product validation |
   | **Proposal store and approval inbox** | Proposals as records (input fact versions, fact sheet, text, proposed action, expiry, state); the principal's queue to approve, edit or reject with a reason | The decision itself (that is the principal's) |
   | **Envelope service** | Envelope evaluation at commit time against Baseline settings, open exposure and daily counts | The rights themselves (Gatekeeper and the entitlement service) |
   | **Product commands** | Tradefloor offers, Lotwise price changes, inbox replies: each validated, authorized through the five gates and idempotent | Knowledge of whether the caller is a person or an agent, beyond the token's actor claim |
   | **Decision log** | Every proposal, approval, rejection, commit and denial, with actor, principal, agent version and gate outcomes | — |
   | **Read** | `search_trade_listings`, `get_condition_report`, `get_valuation`, `get_stock_gaps` | Any agent with the principal's read rights | None; reads are logged when they touch personal data or licensed data |
   | **Propose** | `create_buying_proposal`, `draft_price_change`, `draft_reply` | Agents registered for the use | A record in the proposal store; nothing outside it changes |
   | **Commit** | `place_offer` | Only with an approval id from the inbox, or within a signed envelope | A product command that may bind the principal |
   The **Model Context Protocol (MCP)** is an open protocol through which an AI application discovers and calls tools and data on a server.
   - **The MCP server is a façade over JSON API v2 and Lotwise commands**, with no business logic and no data the APIs do not already serve ([[vehicle-data-apis]]).
   - **External agents get read and propose tools by default.** A proposal from a dealer's own agent lands in the same approval inbox as the buying assistant's.
   - **Data clients' licences apply unchanged.** An insurer's agent calling plate-to-specification through MCP is the insurer calling the API, with the same Charter purposes, metering and no-hit rules ([[billing-and-metering]]).
   - **Rate and spend caps per agent client**, because agents retry and fan out; the Plate Enumeration Attack of August 2027 showed what an unconstrained automated caller does ([[vehicle-platform-security]]).
   - **Auto Trader UK** reported in May 2026 that Co-Driver "anecdotally reduces the average time taken to list a vehicle from 28 minutes to 5 minutes", and described "more than 50 proprietary AI and machine learning models", including a Buying Signals model that flags high-intent enquiries ([Auto Trader Group, 2026](https://plc.autotrader.co.uk/media/usjleen0/full-year-press-release-fy26.pdf)).
   - **mobile.de** reported in July 2026 that its Lead Manager's AI writes "45 percent of all written replies", and that a call-assistant pilot with 19 Dutch dealers found most answered calls came outside opening hours ([mobile.de, 2026](https://newsroom.mobile.de/plattform-update-sommer-2026/), translated from German).
   - **Cox Automotive** announced on 28 January 2026 a VinSolutions Virtual Assistant described as "an agentic AI solution capable of autonomously handling sales, acquisition, and Instant Cash Offer leads", "creating tasks, appointments and updating notes autonomously" and "alerting staff when human handoff is needed", alongside vAuto acquisition workflows inside the CRM ([Cox Automotive, 2026](https://www.coxautoinc.co
   - **Score per listing, not per buyer.** One fact sheet and one valuation per listing version, shared across principals; fit to each principal's stock gaps is computed by rules.
   - **Event-driven, not polled.** A listing is scored when it is released to a tier or changes, which is about 1,200 times a day.
   - **The model writes only what a person will read.** Rationales are generated for the proposals that pass the rules, about **620 a week**.
   - **Screening never buys data.** Specbook, Axleby's specification resolver, already refuses to buy build data in decision contexts that do not justify it ([[specification-and-equipment]]); "agent screening" was added to those contexts.
   | **Conflicting actions**: agent and human act on one listing | One-open-offer rule in Tradefloor | The later command replaces or is rejected; both are logged |
   | **Incomplete facts**: no Gauge report, edition unknown, no battery certificate | Fact-sheet completeness flags | Proposal marked incomplete; never eligible for the envelope |
   | **Model provider outage or timeout** | Gateway health and latency | Second provider, then template rationale; proposals still flow, since rules decide fit |
   | **Principal leaves or loses rights** | SCIM deprovisioning; membership-ended event | Grant revoked; open proposals expire; open envelope offers withdrawn |
   | **Runaway cost** | Per-agent and per-tenant spend meter with daily cap | Agent suspended for that tenant at the cap; the inbox shows why |
   - **Talking to consumers.** Article 50(1) requires providers to ensure that AI systems "intended to interact directly with natural persons" inform them "that they are interacting with an AI system, unless this is obvious".
   - **Generated text.** Article 50(2) requires providers of systems generating synthetic text to mark outputs "in a machine-readable format and detectable as artificially generated", except where the system performs "an assistive function for standard editing" or does not substantially alter its input.
   - **High-risk boundaries.** Annex III lists, among others, AI used "to evaluate the creditworthiness of natural persons" (point 5(b)) and AI used "to monitor and evaluate the performance and behaviour" of workers (point 4(b)).
   - **Dates.** The Digital Omnibus on AI, Regulation (EU) 2026/1744 of 8 July 2026, published on 24 July 2026, moved the high-risk obligations to **2 December 2027** for Annex III systems and **2 August 2028** for systems under Article 6(1).
   | **No agents; AI only drafts (tiers 0–1)** | Simple liability; no new authorization surface | Leaves screening and routine actions manual | The platform lacks idempotent commands or a decision log |
   | **Propose-only agents (tier 2)** | Most of the time saving; a person decides everything | Approval fatigue; proposals expire unseen | Default for every workflow with money or legal effect |
   | **Envelope agents (tier 3)** | Speed on routine, low-value, well-valued actions | Envelope design, monitoring and a signatory per tenant | Segments where valuation is back-tested and the downside per action is capped |
   | **Autonomous binding (tier 4)** | Scale | Balance-sheet risk; correlated errors across tenants | A board decision with risk limits, not a product feature |
   | **Platform builds agents** | Controls, data and gates designed together | Model and evaluation team to fund | The workflow depends on the platform's own data |
   | **Dealers' own agents through MCP** | Dealers choose their tools; no model cost to the platform | Less control over what the agent says; support burden | Read and propose by default; commit only by dealer-signed envelope |
   **Offers bind**: a seller's acceptance creates a contract.
   **The valuation is the weak link**: an agent's quality is capped by Vantage's in that segment, and segment status is the only guard that moves with the market.
   | **AI-specific law** | EU AI Act; Article 50 since 2 Aug 2026 | Same | No AI Act; consumer and data-protection law apply (my reading of the 2026 record) | No federal AI statute; California's bot-disclosure law, in force since 1 July 2019, applies to bots used "to incentivize a sale" without disclosure ([Perkins Coie, 2019](https://perkinscoie.com/insights/blog/i-am-robot-californias-new-law-requires-disclosure-use-b
   | **What a reply may claim** | Unfair Commercial Practices Directive as transposed | Same, plus German price-indication rules for credit examples ([[merchandising]]) | Digital Markets, Competition and Consumers Act 2024 misleading-actions rules, in force since April 2025 | Federal Trade Commission Act section 5; state dealer-advertising rules |
   | **Languages for evaluation** | Dutch; French for Belgium | German | English | English; Spanish common in dealer marketing |

===== evolving-the-vehicle-platform 45
   **A vehicle data and dealer SaaS platform sells decisions about one physical car**: what it is, what happened to it, what it is worth, what to pay, what to ask, where to advertise it and who now owns it.
   **The two halves share what describes the car and the customer**: the vehicle key, the dossier, accounts and entitlements, the meter, licence rules and events.
   **Every external party is a dependency with its own clock**: registers, portals, DMS vendors and data suppliers go down, change terms and change owners, so the platform degrades honestly, reconciles afterwards and never lets one of them stop the dealer.
   - **The facts depend on parties the platform does not control.** A register can close a channel, a portal can be bought by a competitor, and a DMS vendor can change its access terms, as CDK did in 2015 ([[case-cdk-global]]).
   - **Regulation arrives on fixed dates with uncertain scope.** The EU Data Act has applied since 12 September 2025, its access-by-design duty reaches cars placed on the market after 12 September 2026, and its unfair-terms rules reach older long-term contracts on 12 September 2027 ([Regulation (EU) 2023/2854, 2023](http://publications.europa.eu/resource/celex/32023R2854)).
   - **Prices move by segment, not on average.** The Manheim Used Vehicle Value Index reached a record 236.2 in December 2021, up 46.6% in a year ([Cox Automotive, January 2022](https://www.coxautoinc.com/news/manheim-forecast/)).
   - A **stressor** is anything that could happen to the business or its environment: a law, a competitor's move, a supplier's failure, a price shock, a sale.
   - An **attractor** is the state the business is pushed into when the stressor hits: how dealers, partners and the market will actually behave.
   - A **residue** is what remains of the architecture after the stressor, and the structural change that would let the platform survive it.
   - The **naive architecture** works under normal conditions; the **residual architecture** is the naive design plus the residues that survive the stressors considered.
   | **Registers and regulation** | Daan Visser, Femke de Boer | Register outage or new terms; registration reform; privacy rulings; Data Act duties; tax rule changes |
   | **Market and prices** | Priya Raman | Segment price collapse; market-wide spike; free data commoditizing basic lookups |
   | **Channels and partners** | Hendrik Maes | Portal consolidation; DMS concentration, attack or terms change; auction partner change |
   | **Data supply** | Priya Raman, Marta Nowak | A licensed source withdrawn or repriced; a supplier error; new connected-car data |
   | **Ownership and capital** | Lena Vos | An acquisition to integrate; a brand to sell; a change of owner |
   | **Technology and adversaries** | Marta Nowak | AI agents between dealer and buyer; scraping; ransomware on Axleby itself |
   | **R1 One vehicle key** | AVK in Anchor, time-bounded plates, merge and split events | S1, S5, S7, S8, S10, S11, S13, S14 | 8 | Every new stock item carries an AVK; Pulse match 64% |
   | **R2 Provenance and bitemporal facts** | Envelope v1, Inlet, Tracewell events | S5, S9, S10, S11, S13, S14 | 6 | Live July 2027; older facts marked reconstructed |
