   | **R7 Tenant directory and one identity** | Axleby ID, Gatekeeper, tenant directory, Baseline | S7, S8, S12, S16 | 4 | Velto live; Hartley pilot |
   | **R8 Versioned rules and models** | Vantage segment status, Baseline market packs, tax rule sets | S4, S5, S6, S9 | 4 | Segment status live 2027 |
   | **R9 Separability** | Per-tenant keys, UK cell, product-scoped stores, contract inventory | S8, S16 | 2 | Partly by accident (UK cell, KMS keys) |
   **Axleby will not build a DMS**: every DMS vendor would become a competitor ([[dms-integration]]).
   **It will not own auctions**: wholesale prices are valuable ([[case-cox-automotive]]), but buying an auction house is a capital decision, not an architecture option.
   **It will not enter the US before 2030**: title, NMVTIS (the National Motor Vehicle Title Information System) and a concentrated DMS market need a different data spine.
   | **Year 1 (2027–28): one dossier, one account** | One login per person; history, valuation, condition and register transactions in the Lotwise Dossier; one invoice that explains each line | Axleby ID and Gatekeeper; AVK as the only vehicle key; Meterline; Charter in every data path; Inlet and the provenance envelope | No stock item without AVK; Meterline two consecutive clean months and cut over; 100% of external da
   | **Year 3 (2029–30): market-driven automation and event APIs** | Automatic in-band repricing behind guardrails; digital lessor returns (Handback); JSON API v2 webhooks (live from Q3 2028) and `vehicle.fact.corrected`; Showroomly on shared identity, leads and syndication | Event backbone with outbox; Relay 2 desired state and reconciliation; Vantage segment status in every automated decision; tenant export | Correcti
   | **Year 5 (2031–32): assisted used-car business and embedded data utility** | Buy recommendations from trade stock and predicted stand-time per car; self-balancing group stock; components embedded in partners' software; consented connected and battery data per VIN; cross-border categories for Belgium and Germany | Agent permission model; Mandate at scale; passport identifiers; market adapters for a second register |
   **Year 3 waits for Year 1's gates**: repricing on a dossier without AVKs, or webhooks before events carry entity versions, would automate the Cloned Identity and the Sticky Error.
   **Retirements are dated and public**: a Connect XML sunset that can slip keeps 210 XML-only clients from moving ([[vehicle-data-apis]], [[modernizing-the-platform]]).
   **Twelve is a ceiling**: a scorecard of fifty measures has no owners, and the board learns to read only the colors.
   | **The stressor list goes stale** | Written once, admired, filed | No stressor added or retired in a year; an incident that matches no stressor | Yearly review starting from the year's incidents and counterparties' ownership changes ([[vehicle-data-ecosystem]]) |
   | **Residues erode under delivery pressure** | A plate-keyed cache "for speed"; a product reading Tracewell without Charter; an adapter without a contract test | Build-time rules (no vehicle record without an AVK, no data-serving service without Charter's library), checked like ArchUnit tests | Block the change, or register an exception with an expiry date approved by the Group Chief Architect |
   | **Options are bought and never watched** | Contract tests stop running; a trigger passes unnoticed | A quarterly line per option in the board pack | Exercise it or delete it |
   | **Fitness functions become targets** | Publish lag counted only on channels that confirm state; after-hours leads excluded | Definitions versioned and reviewed by the owner of the failure pattern, not of the number | Restate history under the corrected definition, as Pulse did when only 68% of de-listings proved to be sales ([[market-data]]) |
   | **The program outlives its purpose** | Year 3 gates met, budget renewed anyway | Gate status against budget | Dissolve the program into platform teams' roadmaps ([[organizing-engineering]]) |
   | **A combined shock arrives before the residue** | Some will | Journey SLOs go degraded across several dependencies | The residue of last resort: labeled copies for reads, visible queues for legal writes, built beforehand |
   - **Own the stressor list and run it yearly.** The Group Chief Architect owns it; each family has a business owner who drafts it.
   - **Protect the residues as products.** R1 to R4 have named owners, the slowest change process in the group and build-time rules that stop erosion.
   - **Report fitness functions to the board, not milestones**, and keep the option register short and watched: delete any option whose trigger cannot be observed.
   - **Design for leaving as well as joining.** Every integration decision asks: what would it take to undo this for one brand or one tenant?
   - **Decide what not to become.** Not a DMS, not an auction house, not a US title business before 2030.

===== case-cox-automotive 27
   **The author's interpretation** is marked "my reading" or sits in the Chief Architect's view.
   A **DMS** is the dealer's operational system of record for deals, accounting, parts and service ([[dms-integration]]).
   **F&I** is the dealer's finance and insurance desk, where credit applications and contracts are prepared.
   **Floorplan finance** is credit that funds a dealer's stock.
   | **Platform identity** | Who is this person?
   | **Tenancy and authorization** | Which dealer group, rooftops, products and roles does this person have? | Not documented; relinking per product suggests it stays local | One organization model (group → rooftop → user → role) that every product consumes ([[tenancy-and-dealer-groups]]) |
   A **vehicle identification number (VIN)** is the 17-character identifier assigned at manufacture ([[vehicle-identity]]).
   **An index with a published method.** The **Manheim Used Vehicle Value Index (MUVVI)** is a monthly measure of US wholesale used-vehicle prices.
   **What the public record does not show** is how KBB, vAuto or the Manheim Market Report compute per-vehicle values, how they are back-tested, or how quickly they react to a shock like the 2020 fall.

   | **Link identities rather than replace user stores** | Bridge ID: one account per product attached to a central login | Many products must keep shipping; authentication is the urgent pain | Dealer-group administration stays fragmented; each acquisition restarts the work |

===== case-cdk-global 11
   **My interpretation** is marked as such or sits in the Chief Architect's view.
   - **The injunction and its reversal (2017).** A Wisconsin district court ordered CDK and Reynolds in July 2017 to let Authenticom keep using dealer credentials.
   - **The consolidated litigation (2017–2025).** The cases were consolidated in Illinois.
   - **The FTC and Auto/Mate (2018).** On 20 March 2018 the Federal Trade Commission challenged CDK's purchase of Auto/Mate, a smaller DMS vendor that had won business with lower prices, flexible contracts and "modest fees to integrate third-party applications".
   | **Close the system of record to credential-based access** | Lock-out in 2015; security argument accepted as real by the court record; litigation for a decade | Shared logins are a genuine security risk and a sanctioned path exists at cost-based terms | The sanctioned path is priced at what the market will bear in a concentrated market |
   | **Run a certified access program** | 3PA, then the Partner Program; certification and fees per vendor per dealer | Many vendors of uneven quality write into the system of record | Certification becomes a way to decide who may compete with the owner's own applications |
   | **Turn the program into a contract-first marketplace** | Fortellis: contracts, multiple providers, routing, tokens, metering | The owner wants other systems to supply the same contracts and can show neutral terms | The owner is the only provider in practice and the terms stay private |
   | **Divest everything except the system of record** | Digital marketing (2020) and international (2021) sold; online retail bought (2021) | The system of record is the defensible asset and the capital is better spent near it | Value and customer risk concentrate in one system |
   | **Run one hosted system for a large share of a market** | About 15,000 dealers affected in 2024 | Operations at scale are cheaper and more consistent than thousands of on-premises installs | Blast radius is not bounded, so one incident is an industry outage |
   | The **Zombie Golf** (February 2027): a sold status from DriveDesk failed to sync and a Golf stayed live on **4** portals for **4 days** | Dealers resumed only after validating their data | A per-rooftop reconciliation of stock status against the DMS would have caught the missing sold event within a day; it is the same control Sonic used after the outage |
   | The **Lost Leads of Velto** (September 2027): an inactive DriveDesk CRM queue returned success and discarded **1,460** leads | Lithia severed connections deliberately; integrations must tolerate disconnection | An acknowledgement from a DMS or CRM is not proof of delivery.
===== case-auto-trader-uk 23
   **The author's interpretation** is marked "my reading" or sits in the Chief Architect's view.
   The unit that matters is **average revenue per retailer (ARPR)**, the monthly revenue per retailer forecourt, a forecourt being one advertising location, close to what this book calls a rooftop.
   | **Retail Essentials** | April 2022 | "Uses our proprietary taxonomy data to ensure that vehicles are well described" and "enables real-time stock management … on Auto Trader and all other digital channels" (FY2023) | Vehicle lookup by registration; dealer stock from dealer software |
   | **Valuations** | April 2023 | "Specification adjusted valuations", based on "c.500,000 obervations [sic] that we see each day" (FY2023); "specification and condition adjusted" (FY2024) | Taxonomy derivative; advert and price observations |
   | **Trended Valuations, enhanced Retail Check** | April 2024 | Forward-looking retail values; over 70% of retailers used trended valuations each month in FY2025 | Valuations plus time series of the same observations |
   | **Co-Driver** | FY2025; in packages from 1 April 2025 | "An umbrella brand for a range of AI-enabled products": image ordering, generated descriptions, vehicle highlights | Specification, features, images, history fields of the stock record |
   | **Deal Builder** | Trial 2022; core package from May 2025 | Buyers value a part-exchange, apply for finance and reserve a car | Stock, valuations (part-exchange), lender integrations |
   | **Buying Signals** | January 2026 | A model "trained on verified but anonymised sales transactions and consumer interactions" that flags high-intent leads | Deals, enquiries, browsing events |
   **My reading** is that the order was not accidental.
   **My reading** is that this is a relationship-based entitlement model exposed to partners: *partner P may perform action A on advertiser R's stock because R's package includes feature F and R has authorized P.* It is the shape [[identity-access-and-entitlements]] recommends, and it is the reason a bundled business can still differentiate packages.
   **My reading** is that the stock record is a **desired-state record with per-channel status**, the design [[listing-syndication]] argues for: the dealer's software says which channels a car should be on, and the platform reports, per channel, whether it is. `CAPPED` turns the commercial contract (how many cars the package pays for) into a state the dealer's software can see, which is the stock lever of the business m
   **My reading** is that 30 days is short for an ecosystem in which, by the platform page's own account, a technology partner "usually manages the integration".
   **My reading** is that Auto Trader sells **a family of related values, each tied to a decision**: what to pay at part-exchange, what to ask on the forecourt, what the car will be worth in 60 days, and how likely it is to sell in the dealer's target time.
   **My reading** is that this is a deliberate control boundary: the model proposes and the retailer, or the retailer's software, commits.
   **My reading** is that two status fields, one per party, are the most important modeling choice here.
   **My reading** is that the dispute was less about a feature than about **who controls the availability of the dealer's stock.** A reservation is a state on a car that the dealer owns, sells in other channels and records in its own DMS.
   **My reading** is that two practices transfer: a small central delivery-platform team serving hundreds of developers ([[organizing-engineering]]), and **one definition of a governance rule compiled into every engine that needs it**, so batch and streaming agree on which events count, as billing must agree on which calls count ([[billing-and-metering]]).
   | **Bundle data products into the advertising package** | Valuations, Co-Driver and Deal Builder added through April pricing events | One dominant channel; products add information to the dealer's existing workflow | The product changes the workflow or shifts control of stock; dealers cut paid stock |
   | **Build valuations from the marketplace's own observations** | c.500,000 observations a day (2023); price flags on the consumer site | The portal sees most of the market's retail adverts | The market is split across portals; the model shapes the prices it learns from |
   | **Publish a partner API with per-advertiser capabilities** | 15 production APIs; capabilities tied to subscriptions; 220+ partners | Most dealers integrate through vendors; packages must differ | Capabilities diverge from what billing and the UI believe |
   | **Expire stale stock on timers** | Documented lifecycle timers | Partners and DMS links are unreliable about sold events | Timers are mistaken for freshness; a sold car stays live for weeks |
   | **AI proposes, client commits** | Co-Driver returns text and image order; the client writes them | Responsibility for advert content stays with the dealer | Partners write AI output unreviewed, and the boundary exists only on paper |
   | **30-day notice for breaking changes** | Documented policy and changelog | Few, active, well-staffed partners | A long tail of partners ships slowly and retailers discover the break |
===== case-vwe 44
   **The author's interpretation** is marked "my reading" or sits in the Chief Architect's view.
   **A register-access business.** VWE's "About" page (read October 2026) calls it the **"largest officially recognized RDW provider"** and says it "once started as a car dealership".
   | **MijnVWE** dealer platform | Dealer workspace: stock, advertising to "ruim 150 verkoopkanalen" (more than 150 sales channels), sales, financing referral, register transactions | Register transactions (*vrijwaren*, *tenaamstellen*, plates, deregistration) from the workspace |
   | **Finnik Zakelijk** appraisal | Plate or VIN lookup with warnings for damage, recalls, "awaiting inspection" (*WOK*, *Wachten Op Keuring*) status and registration tax; register status; odometer history; price history; comparable foreign prices | Register status and odometer readings combined with other sources |
   | **Schadeverleden** damage history | International damage database plus Dutch indicators (insurance claims, *WOK* status, registration-tax changes) | Dutch indicators include register-derived flags |
   | **Marktinzicht** market insight | "Based on official RDW registers": fleet, new and used sales, inspections, export, scrapping, import; reports, a dashboard or a one-off file | Built on register statistics |
   | **Change-of-ownership lead generator** | A machine-learning model scores plates likely to change owner within one to two months | Plate-keyed; the page does not name its input sources |
   | **Finnik** consumer app | Free basic check and a paid full report by plate | Plate-keyed lookups |
   **Occasion Manager** (product page, read October 2026) is the stock and pricing module.
   **Finnik Zakelijk** (read October 2026) is the appraisal app: plate or VIN lookup including foreign vehicles, damage and recall warnings, register status, odometer history combining official readings with maintenance and international damage records, manufacturer maintenance history, factory options via a SilverDAT VIN lookup, and the remaining registration tax (*BPM*, *Belasting van personenauto's en motorrijwielen*
   **MijnVWE** (read October 2026) is the workspace that holds the modules together, with advertising to more than 150 channels (the homepage says "more than 100 platforms"), register transactions, damage history, maintenance, battery State of Health (SoH) certificates and, since 2026, weDAT damage calculation.
   **Vehicle characteristics** (English product page, read October 2026) is the data-enrichment product: lookups by plate or chassis number returning edition name, first registration, country of origin, owner history, odometer checks, standard and optional equipment, consumption and environmental data, photos and factory specifications from SilverDAT VIN, delivered through an API "per registration plate" or by enriching
   - **Interdata** is an XML interface for using vehicle data in a customer's own application: an XML request, often keyed by plate, and an XML response.
   - **XMLmode** lets garage-software vendors integrate VWE services as web services configured per integrator.
   | **Per-source schemas** (VWE's documented interface) | Licence scoping: a client gets the domains it pays for; each licensor's data stays recognizable; adding a source adds a schema without touching others | Every integrator harmonizes edition names, equipment codes and conflicting values; a source change is a client change; provenance is implicit in the schema, not explicit per field |
   | **Harmonized vehicle resource** (book's reference design) | One model for clients; conflicts resolved once; provenance per field; licence enforced as field filtering | The provider must own the harmonization, keep source lineage per field, and enforce licences at field level rather than at schema level |

   | **ATC** sold to Autorola (completed 1 Feb 2026, Journal de l'Automobile, 2026) | ATC offered around 40,000 inspected vehicles at any time to just over 8,000 selling and 7,000 buying professionals; it continues as an independent auction platform | An internal marketplace becomes an external partner; Occasion Manager still lists "auctions from partner Automotive Trade Center" |

   - **2022.** NewPort Capital took a stake in VWE Groep B.V. (VWE release, 31 August 2022); the ACM cleared the concentration on 19 September 2022, finding that "sufficient competition remains" (ACM decision ACM/22/179803, published 21 September 2022).
   - **2026.** Main Capital Partners announced on 9 September 2026 that it will acquire a **majority stake** from NewPort, with management unchanged.
   | **Autotelex** (homepage, read Oct 2026) | Rival and supplier: Autotelex data is inside VWE's Interdata (`AtdDataTypes.xsd`) | Selling and trade values, residuals, advertising system, trade-in tools, registration-tax calculator, an API |
   | **AutoScout24** with AutoTrack and Gaspedaal.nl (AutoRAI, 25 Nov 2025) | Channel and potential tool competitor: took over AutoTrack and Gaspedaal.nl; AutoTrack dealers move to AutoScout24's platform; 43,000+ connected dealers across 18 countries | Price evaluation and dealer tools on the portal side |

   | **RDW Open Data** (RDW open-data portal, n.d.) | Free substitute for basic register lookups | Plate-level technical data under an open licence |

   | **Germany** | No comparable open register access for the trade; registration through local authorities | Build and valuation data (DAT, Schwacke) and portal advert data |
   | **United Kingdom** | DVSA inspection (MOT) history through a public API with bulk files and daily changes; DVLA's vehicle enquiry API, closed to new registrations as of October 2026 | The dominant portal's own advert and buyer observations ([[case-auto-trader-uk]]) |
   | **United States** | No single register; state DMVs, a federal title-information system | A contributory history network built from many reporting sources |
   | **Build products on one national register** | Transactions, status, statistics and lead scores all tied to RDW | The register is rich, trusted and transactable; one market | Expansion abroad; register terms change; register outage on a busy day |
   | **Expose licensors' schemas directly** | Per-source XSDs for RDW, Jato, DAT, Autotelex and others | Licences require attribution; clients already map these sources | Clients need one vehicle model; a licensor changes its format |
   | **Keep the XML interface stable** | XML and SoapUI-tested web services; no public versioning | Installed base of long-lived integrations | Newer clients expect JSON, tokens, sandboxes and events |
   | **Mix subscriptions, conditional lookups and pass-through fees** | €74.95 modules, €9.95 no-damage-no-charge reports, €1.74 SilverDAT lookups | Dealers want low entry cost and to pay for what they use | Usage is not metered with its outcome; invoices cannot be explained |
   | **Output plate scores, not owners** | Lead generator returns flagged plates; dealer joins its own customers | Owner data is personal and register access is purpose-bound | Scores are treated as anonymous and leave purpose limits |
   | **Divest the marketplace, keep it as a partner** | ATC sold, still shown as partner auctions | The group's strength is data and workflow, not liquidity | The partner changes terms or joins a competitor group |
   | **Keep acquired brands separate "for now"** | XRAY keeps name, terms and agreements | Early after a deal, to protect customers | "For now" becomes permanent; customers re-type plates across brands |
   **Axleby is not a disguised VWE.** Readers who know the Dutch market will notice that Axleby Data, like VWE, holds a contractual data-supply and transaction-provider relationship with the Dutch register (what this book calls an *authorized information provider*, a book term defined in [[registers-and-official-data]], not an official RDW title), sells register transactions to the trade, runs an older XML interface bes
   | **Turnstile** register gateway with a transaction retry queue; after the Month-End Register Outage (31 March 2027: 5 hours 40 minutes, 3,100 queued, 214 submitted twice) it adds idempotency keys | Register under every product; degradation not described | The case shows how much depends on that gateway; Axleby's double submissions show that a retry queue without idempotency is not enough |
   | **Charter** licence and purpose register in the request path of every data API, built after the Licence Breach (January 2027) | Several licensed sources resold, attributable by schema | Schema-level attribution answers "where did this come from" but not "may this client use it for this purpose"; Charter answers the second |
   | **Meterline** records every billable event with its outcome, after the No-Hit Credit (€186,000 to 41 clients) | Conditional and pass-through per-lookup prices | The same pricing shape; Axleby's credit is what happens when two interfaces rate one rule differently |
   | **One Dossier**, Year 1: Axleby ID, Gatekeeper, Anchor as the only vehicle key, Meterline; €38 million over 2027–2029 | Buy-and-build stated; XRAY kept separate "for now" | Integrate the spines first and leave brands on their surfaces; the case adds that divestment needs the same separability in reverse |
   | **JSON API v2** with webhooks beside Connect XML until 2029 | XML documented; JSON mentioned but undocumented publicly | Publish the new contract fully, with versioning and events, and keep the old one stable until the installed base moves |
===== domain-coverage 25
   - **Nothing in the model is a fact about VWE.** No brick, module, store, dependency, KPI or stressor is presented as VWE's architecture or plans.
   - **Brand names are replaced.** Several brick and module names carry VWE product names.
   - **The running example is fictional.** Axleby, the fictional pan-European vehicle data and dealer software group this book follows (see [[meet-axleby]]), is not a disguised VWE.
   - **Primary**: the chapter that covers the brick in depth, owns its main terms and draws its architecture.
   - **Also**: chapters that cover one of its modules, use the brick as an input, or depart from its decomposition.
   - **Axleby**: the fictional system the chapter uses as its worked example, where one exists.
   - **`ctrv` and `mats`** are price-listed e-commerce: stickers, vignettes, plates, one-day insurance.
   - **`finc`** is treated as a referral to a lender, not as lending.
   - **`mins` `module-occasion-meter`** (a dealer's share against local competitors) is covered as a benchmark product inside [[data-products-and-insights]], whose main argument is disclosure control rather than dashboards.
   | **Ingestion and entity resolution** | `mktd` ingest and matcher, `vhis` assembler, `vidn` resolver each do their own loading and matching | One pipeline with landing zone, quarantine, idempotent loads and replay; one survivorship rule instead of four | [[ingestion-and-entity-resolution]] (Inlet) |
   | **Provenance and data-quality operations** | KPIs "Data quality incidents" and "Enrichment match rate"; source lineage in the catalog | Facts need source, observed-at, licence and confidence; corrections need bitemporal history | [[provenance-and-data-quality]] (provenance envelope v1) |
   | **Connected-vehicle data** | Battery readings by dongle only | Manufacturer APIs, aggregators and the EU Data Act change who may obtain odometer, fault and battery data, and under which consent | [[connected-vehicle-data]] (Mandate, Signalyard) |
   | **Vehicle media pipeline** | Photos mentioned in adverts and appraisals | Capture, background replacement, ordering, rights and delivery are shared by adverts, appraisals and condition evidence | [[merchandising]] |
   | **CRM integration** | Lead inbox only; a persona asks for SMS follow-ups | Dealers run external CRMs; leads must reach them, be acknowledged and report status back | [[leads-and-crm]] (Switchboard) |
   | **DMS integration** | Group persona wants "integration with the group DMS"; KPI "DMS integrations live"; no brick | Franchised dealers' stock, deals and accounting live in a dealer management system the platform does not control | [[dms-integration]] (Hitch) |
   | **Onboarding and configuration** | Year-5 milestone "self-service onboarding in one day"; group setup fees | Migration, channel linking and configuration as versioned data, instead of forks for large customers | [[onboarding-and-configuration]] (Runway, Baseline) |
   | **Events, webhooks and synchronization** | Events listed on data assets (`stock-changed`, `transaction-completed`), but every dependency is a synchronous call; "register-change webhooks" on a vendor roadmap | Sold status, corrections and usage must reach many consumers in order, once, with replay | [[events-webhooks-and-sync]] (One Dossier event backbone) |
   | **Observability and data operations** | KPIs for API availability, incident notification time and cache hit rate; "public status page" milestone; no brick | Business flows (publish lag, lead delivery, transaction success) need monitors, per tenant and per channel | [[observability-and-data-operations]] (Pitwall, publish-lag dashboard) |
   | **Lease contract administration** (contracts, maintenance agreements, claims handling; named in the model's insights but without bricks) | Lessors appear as remarketing, enrichment and API clients in [[b2b-trade-and-remarketing]], [[data-products-and-insights]] and [[vehicle-data-apis]] | Fleet and contract management is its own software category with its own vendors.
   | **Workshop, after-sales, parts and warranty** | The DMS keeps them; [[dms-integration]] marks them out of scope in its system-of-record table | They belong to the manufacturer and DMS world, not to used-car data and workflow |
   | **Consumer travel documents and short-term insurance as products** | Only the vehicle-data boundary (eligibility by emission class) in [[consumer-vehicle-checks]] | Commodity e-commerce; nothing in the design is specific to vehicle data |
   | **Lending** (credit decisions, payouts, financial supervision) | The referral and its consumer-credit rules in [[deals-and-sales-documents]] | The lender carries the credit risk and the regulatory authorization |
   | **Trade escrow, transport and auction operations** | Trust between traders and the hand-off to auction partners in [[b2b-trade-and-remarketing]] | Axleby owns no auctions; these are partner services |
   | **Accessibility of consumer apps** | Not covered | A general product duty, well covered elsewhere and not changed by vehicle data |
   | **The model's KPI values, deployment topology and tutorial walkthrough** | KPI names and tree shapes are used; Axleby has its own volumes and incidents | The values are invented seeds; the deployment is uniform ("EU cloud runtime"); the walkthrough is replaced by Axleby's worked examples |
===== toolkit 115
   - **They are maintained data, not documents.** Licence rules live in Charter, the licence and purpose register; routing rules in Baseline, the tenant configuration service; API contracts in Folio, the contract registry; decision records in one log.
   - **They reference each other by identifier.** An API contract card cites licence classes and SKUs; a decision record cites the capability rows and entities whose owner it changes.
   - **They say which half and which market.** The data half sells facts under licence; the workflow half runs dealers' work inside tenants.
   - **Each Axleby example agrees with the chapter that teaches it.** All Axleby names, figures and dates are fictional; the linked chapter works the full case.
   | **Domain and capability** | Level-1 domain; a business ability as a noun phrase | Never a system ("Specbook"), a team or a brand |
   | **Systems** | Every implementation today | Two or three implementations is the duplication signal |
   | **Class** | D differentiating, P parity, C commodity | D means a customer would leave if it were merely as good as a competitor's; require a named comparison |
   | **Condition** | Green, Amber, Red, Grey (missing) | Funding follows condition as much as class |
   | **Scope** | Shared, data half, workflow half | Shared only if both halves need **the same answer about the same car or customer** |
   | **Market flag** | Market-specific or not | A market difference is a rule set or adapter, not a separate capability |
   | **Shared promise** | For shared rows: latency, freshness, consistency, availability per consumer | Incompatible consumers get two serving paths over one model |
   | **Owner** | One accountable person | Consumers are not owners |
   | **Entity** | A business object with identity and a lifecycle |
   | **Identifier** | Opaque, never reused, minted by the owner; plates, stock and invoice numbers are labels |
   | **Source of truth** | The one context allowed to change it; its **system of record** (the store) may differ during a migration |
   | **Allowed copies** | By kind: **reference** (the key), **decision snapshot** (id and version used), **observation** (another party's claim with source), **projection** (rebuildable read model with measured lag) |
   | **Lifecycle** | States and the context that moves it through them; events published on the backbone ([[events-webhooks-and-sync]]) |
   | **Team and degraded rule** | The owning team; what consumers do when the owner is unavailable |
   | **Freshness class** | Immutable, registration state, decision-critical, accumulating, market, derived | Sets the SLO and the degraded answer |

   **Axleby example** (Kilometra workshop readings, contract v2, August 2027):
   | **Release and owner** | Model and adjustment-table versions; approver |
   | **Value types and markets** | Retail, trade-in, trade, private, future; basis (VAT scheme, registration tax included or not) |
   | **Training** | Window, recency weighting, sources and their licences' `derivatives` rules |
   | **Inputs and flags** | Fact types and confidence; flags that widen the range (`thin_sample`, `stale_market`, `spec_uncertain`) |
   | **Applicability contract** | Segments valued; segments refused, and what is returned instead |
   | **Back-test per segment** | Median absolute percentage error, share within ±5% and ±10%, **signed bias**, 80% range coverage; against transaction and against asking prices separately; rolling origin |
   | **Drift monitors** | Leading indicators per segment and the thresholds for `watch` and `restricted` |
   | **Release gates** | Challenger beats champion on bias and coverage in every segment with enough data; shadow period; staged release; rollback |
   | **Known weaknesses** | Segments with thin data or lagging ground truth |
   | **Channel kind** | Portal write API, file feed, ad catalog, or portal as syndicator |
   | **Reached via** | Other channels this one exports to, so the dealer makes one decision per destination |
   | **Operations** | Create, full update, PATCH allow-list, delete, lifecycle states |
   | **Identity and create idempotency** | Platform external id on the listing; native creation key, or look up by external id before creating again |
   | **Withdrawal saga** | Steps, deadline, how absence is confirmed |
   | **Error classes** | Each response code mapped to transient, throttled, permanent for this content, or locked |
   | **Rate budget** | Token bucket, ramp-up, reserved share for withdrawals |
   | **Observers** | Push (callbacks, streams) and pull (list reads); evidence level: confirmed, asynchronous, or file without status |
   | **Field ownership** | Per field: platform wins, portal wins, or flag |
   | **Mandatory fields per market** | With the date each became mandatory |
   | **Mapping tables** | Equipment, make and model mapping versions, keyed by identifiers, never display names |
   | **Owner and probes** | Named engineer; weekly changelog review into dated tickets; hourly synthetic car on a test rooftop |
   | **Scope and version** | Tenant, rooftops, rule-set version and effective date |
   | **Delivery mode** | Inbox, external CRM, or both (and which leads go where) |
   | **Evaluation order** | 1 lead type, 2 vehicle to rooftop, 3 group policy, 4 calendar, 5 assignment, 6 terminal fallback |
   | **Targets** | Each queue, team or CRM queue, validated against the tenant directory and integration registry |
   | **Closure handling** | Successor rooftop on `rooftop.closed`; alert to the group administrator |
   | **Delivered means** | Read-back of the CRM record's owner and queue; inbox copy until confirmed |
   | **After hours** | Contact center, auto-acknowledgement, callback slot |
   | **Consent scopes** | Answer the enquiry; marketing by channel; text version |
   | **Simulation** | Replay of the last 30 days: leads per target, targets with leads and no active users |
   | **Monitors** | Unacknowledged leads per rooftop; portal counts reconciled daily |
   | **Legal act, market, register** | The act in the local term with an English gloss; channel (direct, provider, partner) |
   | **Authority chain** | Legal entity, recognition, credential and expiry, provider route, user, customer mandate; stored as it was at submission |
   | **Prerequisites** | Each check, its source, and whether it can run from a mirror or needs a live call |
   | **Idempotency** | Key from the dealer's click to the fee line; business guard on open transactions per vehicle and type |
   | **Ordering** | Lane key (AVK); fairness across legal entities; pacing set by channel health |
   | **Timeout and Ambiguous** | Timeout; probe that asks the register what it did; outcomes matches, does not match, inconclusive |
   | **Proof** | What is stored verbatim; documents produced; retention |
   | **Fees** | Register fee (pass-through) and own fee; metered on confirmation only |
   | **Degraded mode** | Honest queue; what dealer and buyer are told |
   | **Owner** | Product owner and the on-call team |
   | **DMS family and access pattern** | Certified API, events, SOAP, file extract, aggregator; never scraping as a foundation |
   | **Partner agreement** | Scope, fees, certification and recertification triggers, data use, incident duties, termination and data return |
   | **Dealer authorizations** | Legal entity, rooftops, scopes, signer, expiry, held in the authorization register |
   | **Field ownership** | Default table plus per-rooftop overrides by configuration, never code |
   | **Identity mapping** | Time-bounded xref (DMS, rooftop, external id) → stock item and AVK |
   | **Inbound** | Upsert key, version guard, watermark overlap, quarantine for records without a valid VIN |
   | **Outbound** | Commands, receipt definition, read-before-retry, expiry per command type |
   | **Reconciliation** | Nightly per rooftop: VIN set, status, checksum over owned fields; auto-resolve rules |
   | **Health, degraded mode, game day** | Freshness per rooftop; circuit; Lotwise as local system of record; fallback invoice series; last exercise |
   **Axleby example** (DriveDesk adapter in Hitch, 2027):
   | **Hierarchy** | Organization (tenant), legal entity, region, rooftop, department; memberships, not users, in the tree |
   | **Requirements** | Isolation, cost to serve, noisy neighbors, compliance, enterprise demands, restructuring, blast radius, one product, each with a number |
   | **Per component** | Pool, silo or bridge, and why |
   | **Context propagation** | Tenant id mandatory on records, events, objects, cache keys, telemetry |

   | **Cells** | Count, regions, assignment by tenant, separate accounts and credentials |
   | **Tiers and prices** | What each tier gives and costs |
   | **Restructuring** | How a rooftop moves between tenants; target time |
   | **Reopens when** | Conditions that change the answer |
   **Axleby example** (architecture forum decision, July 2027):
   | **Block and owning context** | One owner, which decides outcomes; the composer only assembles |
   | **Schema** | JSON Schema reference; code lists documented as open |
   | **Sources and licence classes** | Which purposes Charter must find; what is `withheld` and why |
   | **Outcome rules** | `hit`, `partial`, `no_hit`, `failed`: the business definition of each, and its SKU and price rule |
   | **Freshness class** | `current`, `stale`, `unavailable` thresholds; whether a copy may ever serve it |
   | **Error semantics** | Block-level failures stay HTTP 200 with a labeled block; 5xx only when the service cannot answer |
   | **Version status** | Beta (90 days' notice), stable (12 months' notice to remove), dated version supported 24 months after its successor |
   | **Behavioral change record** | Changes to outcome rules, code lists and computation bases, shipped only in the April and October releases |
   | **Service level** | Availability of well-formed answers; per-block `current` rate |
   **Axleby example** (history block, JSON API v2, October 2027):
   | **Journey** | Where it starts and ends, in dealer or client terms |
   | **Good / degraded / bad** | Exact conditions, including "never a silent loss" |
   | **Objective and window** | Good-or-degraded target; cap on degraded; rolling window |
   | **Owner** | One named leader |
   | **Dependency budget** | Share reserved for own code; share per synchronous dependency × share of attempts touching it |
   | **Dependencies** | Tier 1 (blocks a legal or commercial act), 2 (degrades a decision), 3 (delays a background flow) |
   | **Degraded modes** | Labeled copy, partial answer, honest queue, local system of record, or fail fast with a reason |
   | **Never degrades** | The checks that must stay live |
   | **Attribution** | Every bad outcome tagged own code, named dependency or client input |
   | **Error-budget policy and game days** | What stops when the budget burns; last exercise |
   | **Capability and requirement** | Tool 1 row; the requirement the source must meet, with numbers |
   | **Gate 1: supply** | Can anyone else do it, and can anyone but us? |
   | **Gate 2: data rights** | The nine questions: store, scope, derive, redistribute, train, billing basis, evidence, exit, personal data.
   **An unanswered question is a "no"** |
   | **Gate 3: judgement or liability** | Will a customer hold us responsible for the answer?
   | **Gate 4: cost curve** | Unit cost at today's and forecast volume; crossover; team capacity *(assumptions recorded)* |
   | **Options compared** | Including "do nothing", with cost, rights, risk and verdict |
   | **Decision and key assumption** | One option; the assumption under which it holds |
   | **Owned to keep exit open** | Adapter boundary, canonical model, ground truth, ledger |
   | **Review trigger and owner** | The event that reopens it; recorded as a Tool 16 record |
   - **Review on a calendar.** Capability map and sourcing cards yearly and after every acquisition; source contracts and channel profiles at each renewal and each portal changelog entry; model cards with every release; journey SLO cards before month-end and quarter-end peaks; register transaction cards whenever a register changes its channel or rules; decision records on their review dates.
   - **Let incidents add fields.** Beyond those named in the key points, the per-chain check came from the Odometer Correction and tenant id in every cache key from the Cross-Tenant Lead.
   - **Keep the set small.** Every field should change a decision or preserve evidence.
